Proposed Revisions to the LS Power Grid California, LLC Transmission Owner
Tariff to Provide for Large Load Interconnection and Cost Recovery
Dear Secretary Reese:
Pursuant to section 205 of the Federal Power Act (“FPA”)1 and Part 35 of the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission’s (“FERC” or “Commission”) regulations,2 and in response to the Commission’s order instituting the show cause proceeding in California Independent System Operator Corp.,3 LS Power Grid California, LLC (“LS Power Grid”) submits revisions to its Transmission Owner Tariff (“TO Tariff”) to establish a framework for the interconnection of Large Loads4 and for the recovery of the costs of transmission upgrades driven by Large Loads. The revisions include (1) adding a new Section 10A and a new Appendix VI (Large Load Interconnection Procedures (“LLIP”)) to establish an interconnection process for Eligible Customers for Large Load (“ECLLs”); (2) corresponding revisions to the TO Tariff; and (3) adding a new Appendix V, the pro forma Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement (“CRA”). LS Power Grid respectfully requests that the Commission accept the proposed tariff revisions to become effective January 16, 2027, sixty-one (61) days after filing, consistent with the Commission’s prior notice requirement,5 and grant any necessary waivers.

A U S T I N   D A L L A S   D U B A I   H O U S T O N   L O N D O N   N E W  Y O R K   P A R I S   S A N  A N T O N I O   S E A T T L E   W A S H I N G T O N ,  D C
I. INTRODUCTION AND SUMMARY OF FILING
The proposed revisions establish a transparent framework for LS Power Grid, a transmission-only, non-load-serving Participating Transmission Owner (“PTO”) in the California Independent System Operator Corporation (“CAISO”), to interconnect Large Loads and to recover the costs of transmission upgrades that are driven, in whole or in part, by a Large Load (“Large Load-Driven Upgrades”). New Section 10A and the LLIP in Appendix VI govern ECLL applications, studies, interim service, and interconnection agreements. The pro forma CRA in Appendix V establishes the Eligible Customer’s twenty-year Minimum Transmission Revenue Contribution (“MTRC”) and related payment and security obligations for each Covered Upgrade. Amounts received under a CRA are credited to transmission customers through LS Power Grid’s existing Transmission Revenue Balancing Account Adjustment (“TRBAA”), and LS Power Grid’s existing Formula Rate Template already identifies plant by project and removes TRBAA credits from Formula Rate revenue credits. Therefore, those credits are not counted twice and no Formula Rate Template changes are required.

The filing responds to the Show Cause Order’s direction that respondents (1) ensure transparency concerning the network upgrade costs to provide transmission service to Eligible Customers on behalf of large loads, (2) establish a pro forma cost recovery agreement to mitigate the risk of cost shifting among transmission customers, and (3) ensure that such payments are appropriately credited toward transmission owners’ transmission revenue requirements.6 The CRA assigns the wholesale revenue-support obligation to the Eligible Customer that takes transmission service on behalf of the Large Load Facility and backs that obligation with Construction Security, Operational Security, and a Termination Fee. The framework is designed to mitigate the risk that other customers bear the costs of a Covered Upgrade if the associated Large Load is delayed, reduced, or does not materialize, while preserving LS Power Grid’s recovery of its revenue requirement through its Formula Rate and the CAISO Transmission Access Charge (“TAC”), and without changing CAISO cost allocation.

The proposed revisions do not change the cost-of-service inputs, return, depreciation, taxes, or allocators used to calculate LS Power Grid’s revenue requirement, and no changes are proposed to LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate Template (Appendix III), its Formula Rate Implementation Protocols (Appendix IV), or Appendix I of the TO Tariff. Once these revisions are accepted, a conforming, executed CRA with populated Schedules generally will not require an individual FPA section 205 filing, while unexecuted agreements, agreements containing a material deviation, and other agreements required by Commission rule or order will be filed with the Commission.7

II. BACKGROUND
A. LS Power Grid and Its Formula Rate
LS Power Grid is a transmission-only PTO in the CAISO that owns the Gates and Round Mountain projects, which CAISO selected LS Power Grid to develop through its competitive solicitation process, and is developing additional competitively selected projects. The Gates project is an approximately 850 MVAR STATCOM at the Gates Substation in Fresno County, and the Round Mountain project is an approximately 530 MVAR STATCOM and associated 500 kV switchyard in Shasta County. LS Power Grid is a non-load-serving PTO with no End-Use Customers; it neither serves retail load nor has a retail relationship with any customer seeking a Large Load interconnection. LS Power Grid’s formula rate consists of its Formula Rate Template (Appendix III of the TO Tariff) and its Formula Rate Implementation Protocols (“Protocols”) (Appendix IV of the TO Tariff) (together, the “Formula Rate”), which were originally accepted in Docket No. ER21- 195-000 and most recently revised in Docket No. ER26-2665-000. LS Power Grid’s Transmission Revenue Requirement (“TRR”) is the sum of its Base Transmission Revenue Requirement (“Base TRR”), determined under the Formula Rate, and its TRBAA, and is recovered through the CAISO TAC. LS Power Grid already uses the TRBAA to credit customers with revenue it receives outside the TAC.

B. Show Cause Order and Stakeholder Process
On June 18, 2026, the Commission issued the Show Cause Order, instituting an FPA section 206 proceeding in Docket No. EL26-71-000 directed to CAISO and its PTOs. The Commission preliminarily found that existing tariffs appear to be unjust, unreasonable, or unduly discriminatory or preferential because they lack sufficiently clear and consistent provisions to address the challenges posed by the rapid growth of large loads and co-location arrangements between loads and generating facilities.8 The Commission directed each respondent either to show cause why its tariff remains just and reasonable or to explain what tariff changes would remedy the identified concerns.9

Following the Show Cause Order, CAISO convened joint coordination meetings with the PTOs to address the Show Cause Order and CAISO’s proposed implementation framework, and LS Power Grid participated in those meetings from July through October 2026. LS Power Grid also participated in CAISO’s public stakeholder meetings and submitted written comments on CAISO’s Straw Proposal, Draft Final Proposal, and Draft Tariff Language asking CAISO to recognize non- load-serving and affected PTOs expressly and to preserve a meaningful role for those PTOs in the interconnection and study process. LS Power Grid further participated in discussions with other non-load-serving PTOs, the broader PTO group, and load-serving PTOs addressing the application and study process, information sharing, technical coordination, and the allocation and recovery

8  Show Cause Order at P 38.
9  Id. at Ordering Para. B.

A U S T I N   D A L L A S   D U B A I   H O U S T O N   L O N D O N   N E W  Y O R K   P A R I S   S A N  A N T O N I O   S E A T T L E   W A S H I N G T O N ,  D C of costs.10 Those discussions highlighted the need to ensure that an affected PTO has a meaningful role in the process even when it does not provide retail service to the requesting load. LS Power Grid’s proposal does not displace the separate arrangements that an Eligible Customer must enter into with an Affected System Owner for an Affected System Upgrade or with the owner of a regional project share owned by another entity.11

III. DESCRIPTION OF THE PROPOSED REVISIONS
A. Large Load Interconnection Framework (Section 10A and Appendix VI)
A new Section 10A of the TO Tariff establishes an interconnection process for ECLL Interconnections. An ECLL is a Utility Distribution Company or Metered Subsystem that provides retail transmission and interconnection service to Large Load End-Users in the CAISO Balancing Authority Area, and a Large Load is an End-User located at a single site behind one or more Points of Withdrawal from the CAISO Controlled Grid with a peak load of 50 MW or greater. Section 10A.2 specifies the contents of an ECLL Interconnection Application, including the load ramp schedule, site plan, Site Control, permits and approvals, electrical configuration, related requests, technical data, and Co-Located Large Load information. Section 10A.3 directs that ECLL Interconnection Applications be processed, and ECLL Interconnection Agreements be tendered, executed, and filed, under the LLIP in Appendix VI. Section 10A.4 provides for Interim Firm Interconnection Service and Flexible Interim Load Interconnection (“FILI”) Service before the required facilities are completed. FILI Service is subject to curtailment, including during normal operating conditions, and is curtailed before Firm Interconnection Service. Section 10A.5 addresses Co-Located Large Loads, and Section 10A.6 requires a CRA for Large Load-Driven Upgrades.12

LS Power Grid’s proposed LLIP establishes a Commission-jurisdictional framework under which an Eligible Customer for Large Load may request Flexible Interim Load Interconnection Service (“FILI Service”), subject to study, deﬁned operating limitations, and curtailment by LS Power Grid or CAISO as necessary to maintain reliability. The implementing FILI Service Agreement will be entered into with LS Power Grid and, as applicable, CAISO, and will establish the applicable operating requirements, curtailment conditions, and curtailment implementation mechanism. LS Power Grid will work with CAISO and stakeholders to develop a standardized pro forma FILI Service Agreement for a subsequent ﬁling. Pending that ﬁling, each FILI Service Agreement will be consistent with the LLIP, the applicable Large Load Interconnection Agreement, and CAISO Tariﬀ section 25.7.2, and will be ﬁled with the Commission as required under the LLIP and applicable law.

A U S T I N   D A L L A S   D U B A I   H O U S T O N   L O N D O N   N E W  Y O R K   P A R I S   S A N  A N T O N I O   S E A T T L E   W A S H I N G T O N ,  D C The LLIP applies to new Large Loads, capacity increases to Large Loads previously processed under the LLIP, and increases of 50 MW or greater to existing loads not previously processed under the LLIP, for completed Interconnection Requests submitted after Appendix VI becomes effective. It provides for a scoping meeting, a Large Load Study Process Agreement, and a Large Load Interconnection Study subject to CAISO review and concurrence, with a ninety-Calendar- Day reasonable-efforts study period. The study must consider alternative transmission technologies, including dynamic line ratings, advanced power flow control devices, advanced conductors, and transmission switching, and, if traditional network upgrades are selected, the PTO must demonstrate why alternative transmission technologies are not feasible or would not result in lower costs or a faster timeline.13 The LLIP also establishes financial readiness requirements, including a $250,000 Interconnection Study Deposit, Commercial Readiness Deposits that bring the aggregate deposit to twenty percent (20%) of the estimated construction cost of the Large Load Network Upgrades attributable to the request, and withdrawal penalties, and coordinates those deposits with the CRA so that the same costs are not secured twice.14 The existing Section 10 is retitled the Transmission System Wholesale Load (“TSWL”) Interconnection Process and continues to govern TSWL Interconnections. Section 10.1 directs ECLL requests to Section 10A, and revised Section 10.9 provides that, where a TSWL Interconnection will serve one or more Large Loads, the Eligible Customer taking or requesting transmission service on behalf of the Large Load must execute and maintain a CRA, or request that it be filed unexecuted, as a condition to LS Power Grid’s design, procurement, construction, or operation of any associated Large Load-Driven Upgrades. Section 3 adds definitions for, among other terms, Co-Located Large Load, Cost Recovery Agreement, ECLL, ECLL Interconnection, Firm Interconnection Service, FILI Service, Interim Firm Interconnection Service, Large Load, Point of Withdrawal, Site Control, and TSWL Interconnection, and renumbers the existing definitions.

B. Pro Forma CRA (Appendix V)
The pro forma CRA is a contract between LS Power Grid, as Transmission Owner, and the Eligible Customer taking wholesale transmission service under the CAISO Tariff on behalf of a Large Load Customer. Assigning the payment obligation to that Eligible Customer preserves the wholesale obligor structure and does not establish a FERC-jurisdictional retail rate or payment obligation for the Large Load Customer. The Large Load Customer executes a Limited Acknowledgment for information, authorization, notice, and cooperation purposes, but is not a party to the wholesale payment obligations. CAISO is not a party to the CRA, and the CRA creates no obligation of CAISO. Covered Upgrades include Large Load-Driven Upgrades and any LS Power Grid-owned share of a CAISO-Assigned Regional Project identified in the applicable Schedules for which an Annual MTRC is established; Affected System Upgrades and regional project shares owned by another entity are addressed through separate arrangements. Where LS Power Grid requires a CRA, Section 10.9

A U S T I N   D A L L A S   D U B A I   H O U S T O N   L O N D O N   N E W  Y O R K   P A R I S   S A N  A N T O N I O   S E A T T L E   W A S H I N G T O N ,  D C (for a TSWL Interconnection) and LLIP Section 5.3, incorporated through Section 10A.6 (for an ECLL Interconnection), require the applicable Eligible Customer to execute it, or to request that it be filed unexecuted, within the applicable interconnection-agreement execution period.15 For each Covered Upgrade, the CRA establishes an Annual MTRC calculated solely under the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5, separately from the Formula Rate. The Estimated Annual MTRC uses then-current estimated capital cost. Within one year after the Covered Upgrade enters service, when actual capital cost is known, LS Power Grid establishes the Final Annual MTRC as the final incurred capital cost multiplied by the Project-Specific Levelized Fixed Charge Rate calculated under Schedule 5 using the applicable capital structure, cost of debt, return on equity, income-tax inputs, a fixed five percent (5%) annual levelized-depreciation schedule, and the fixed expense allocator. The MTRC Term begins on the applicable in-service date and ends on the day immediately preceding its twentieth anniversary, with partial Rate Years prorated. The Final Annual MTRC is not recalculated through the Formula Rate and may change only as permitted by Section 6.3.1, including to correct error or fraud, to implement an effective prospective reassignment or a final Commission order, or to reflect prospectively a Commission-approved change in LS Power Grid’s return on equity or subsequently enacted tax rates or tax law.16

The twenty-year MTRC Term is a defined contractual revenue-support period, not the depreciable life of a Covered Upgrade. Each Covered Upgrade placed under CAISO’s Operational Control remains in LS Power Grid’s rate base and Base TRR and is subject to ordinary Formula Rate treatment. Where an upgrade is driven by more than one Large Load, Schedule 1 identifies an Initial Allocation Percentage for each Large Load Customer using the applicable final study and allocation methodology, accounting for loads studied individually, jointly, or in a cluster and for any portion of the facility that serves other transmission system needs or customers. The aggregate Annual MTRC allocated to all Large Load Customers may not exceed one hundred percent (100%) of the Annual MTRC for that Covered Upgrade; any unallocated portion remains subject to otherwise applicable CAISO cost-allocation and rate-recovery requirements. Each allocation percentage is applied only once in calculating the Annual MTRC.17 The Eligible Customer pays a Supplemental MTRC equal to the greater of zero and the applicable Annual MTRC less Qualifying Transmission Revenues (“QTR”). QTR consists of wholesale transmission revenues attributable to service to the Large Load Facility that reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected from CAISO customers or directly credit LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement. As applicable, QTR includes the portion of TAC revenues attributable to the Large Load Facility and allocable to LS Power Grid under the reproducible methodology in Schedule 2, qualifying Wheeling Access Charges, and other wholesale transmission-access,

A U S T I N   D A L L A S   D U B A I   H O U S T O N   L O N D O N   N E W  Y O R K   P A R I S   S A N  A N T O N I O   S E A T T L E   W A S H I N G T O N ,  D C wheeling, or transmission-service charges, including charges paid through a non-PTO serving entity, without duplicate recognition. The Annual MTRC therefore operates as a revenue floor, not an additive charge or separate capacity-reservation commitment. LS Power Grid invoices the forecast Supplemental MTRC, including any prior-period true-up adjustment, in monthly installments following each Annual Projection, and serves a Reconciliation Statement with each Annual Update using actual QTR. Under-billed or over-billed amounts are carried into billing following the next Annual Projection, without interest between LS Power Grid and the Eligible Customer.18

Section 6.7 of the CRA establishes for each Covered Upgrade a Total Required Revenue Commitment equal to the Final Annual MTRC multiplied by twenty, which is reduced by QTR, Supplemental MTRC Receipts, Applied Security Proceeds, and other permitted amounts, without double counting, and tracked in an upgrade-specific Cumulative Revenue Ledger. Cancellation, downsizing, curtailment, or cessation after an applicable in-service date does not itself eliminate the Eligible Customer’s obligations. Upon effective termination of the CRA or permanent cessation of service after that date, the Eligible Customer owes a lump-sum Termination Fee equal to the Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, less any Supplemental MTRC billed and unpaid as of the effective termination or cessation date, which remains separately payable and secured. The Termination Fee is an accelerated contractual termination charge, not a penalty. LS Power Grid holds each Termination Fee in a separately identified, interest-bearing Termination Fee Account and credits it to transmission customers through the TRBAA as Annual Termination Fee Credits equal to the Annual MTRC for each remaining Rate Year of the MTRC Term. The uncredited balance is accounted for as a deferred liability and is not included in rate base.19

Article 7 of the CRA requires Construction Security for incurred and irrevocably committed costs and Pre-In-Service Termination Costs, including applicable cancellation, demobilization, and restoration costs and properly accrued allowance for funds used during construction. Operational Security must be delivered no later than thirty Calendar Days before the earliest expected in-service date and equals one hundred percent (100%) of the nominal Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility. Consistent with the Commission’s decision to reduce unnecessary redundancy by allowing an Eligible Customer to rely on credit support posted by a large load under a retail agreement, direct Operational Security is reduced dollar-for-dollar by Qualifying Retail Security Support identified in a currently effective Retail Security Certification. That reduction does not reduce the underlying wholesale obligation and does not apply to Construction Security or Pre-In-Service Termination Costs. Security does not cap the Eligible Customer’s liability. Construction Security provided under an interconnection or engineering and procurement agreement for the same costs counts toward, and is not duplicated by, the CRA

A U S T I N   D A L L A S   D U B A I   H O U S T O N   L O N D O N   N E W  Y O R K   P A R I S   S A N  A N T O N I O   S E A T T L E   W A S H I N G T O N ,  D C requirement.20 A Commercial Readiness Deposit may be credited dollar-for-dollar toward required CRA Construction Security only if it is in a form permitted under Schedule 3 and LS Power Grid elects that treatment in writing; no amount may be applied or credited more than once.21 This ﬁling adopts the conservative requirement that direct Operational Security equal one hundred percent (100%) of the nominal Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, subject only to Qualifying Retail Security Support. LS Power Grid expects to work with CAISO and interested stakeholders to evaluate whether, and under what objectively deﬁned circumstances, a lower requirement or another credit-support structure could provide comparable protection against cost shifting. Any future change would be submitted to the Commission for review under FPA section 205.

C. Crediting CRA Amounts Through the TRBAA (Sections 3 and 5.3)
CRA amounts are credited to transmission customers through LS Power Grid’s existing TRBAA, not as Formula Rate revenue credits. Because the TO Tariﬀ deﬁnes Transmission Revenue Credits as amounts received from, or charges imposed by, CAISO, LS Power Grid proposes to revise the deﬁnitions of the Transmission Revenue Balancing Account (“TRBA”) (Section 3.97) and the TRBAA (Section 3.98) and Section 5.3 so that the TRBAA also ﬂows through to transmission customers all amounts that a CRA requires to be credited through it. The revisions clarify that Section 5.3 does not require the payment of interest between LS Power Grid and a party to a CRA, which is governed solely by the CRA, and the deﬁnition of Interest (Section 3.54) is revised to the same eﬀect. The deﬁnition of Transmission Revenue Credits is unchanged.22

Each October, LS Power Grid will include a Forecast TRBAA Credit equal to the projected Supplemental MTRC for the following year, increased or reduced by any prior-period true-up adjustment, plus any Annual Termination Fee Credit scheduled for that year. The Forecast TRBAA Credit lowers the TRR eﬀective January 1 and, through the TAC, the transmission charges paid by all customers, while the Eligible Customer pays the Supplemental MTRC directly to LS Power Grid. Supplemental MTRC cash receipts, Applied Security Proceeds applied to Supplemental MTRC obligations, Annual Termination Fee Credits, and any duplicate-recovery credits are recorded in the TRBA. A billed but unpaid Supplemental MTRC is not a TRBAA Credit. If LS Power Grid later receives payment or applies Security proceeds to that obligation, the amount is recorded only in the Rate Year in which it is received or applied. Any diﬀerence between the Forecast TRBAA Credit and the actual amounts recorded in the TRBA is reﬂected in the TRBA principal balance and ﬂowed through in subsequent TRBAA. QTR is not credited again, and posting, drawing, or holding Security does not itself produce a credit. The Eligible Customer remains liable for unpaid Large Load Charges, and the CRA’s Security and collection provisions mitigate, but do not eliminate,

A U S T I N   D A L L A S   D U B A I   H O U S T O N   L O N D O N   N E W  Y O R K   P A R I S   S A N  A N T O N I O   S E A T T L E   W A S H I N G T O N ,  D C nonpayment risk. Because the TRBA ensures that LS Power Grid collects no more and no less than its Base TRR, LS Power Grid retains none of the TRBAA Credits.23

LS Power Grid’s annual TRBAA ﬁling is made under FPA section 205 and is noticed by the Commission, open to intervention, comment, and protest, and subject to Commission review and acceptance. LS Power Grid will include in each ﬁling the calculations, workpapers, and testimony supporting the TRBAA Credits, substantially in the form of Exhibit No. LSC-202, with customer- speciﬁc information presented on an aggregated basis or submitted with a request for privileged treatment under 18 C.F.R. § 388.112. No change to Appendix I is required because TRBAA Credits are included in the TRBAA amount stated in Appendix I each year.24

Balances in the TRBA attributable to CRA amounts accrue interest in the same manner as other TRBA balances, because a balance created by a late payment, a non-payment before Security is applied, or the timing of monthly installments reﬂects an actual over- or under-recovery by LS Power Grid. By contrast, when further Supplemental MTRC will be invoiced, a prior-period true- up adjustment is added both to the Supplemental MTRC invoiced for a later Rate Year and to the Forecast TRBAA Credit for that same year, so the matched adjustment does not itself create an over- or under-recovery. Consistent with Section 6.6 of the CRA and Section 5.3 of the TO Tariﬀ, no interest accrues on a prior-period true-up adjustment between LS Power Grid and the Eligible Customer.25

D. Formula Rate Template and Rate Treatment of Covered Upgrades
No changes to LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate Template or Protocols are required. LS Power Grid’s existing Attachment 1a – Project Plant Detail Worksheet already reports plant separately by project, and Attachment 1 – Project Revenue Requirement Worksheet calculates each project’s revenue requirement using the applicable allocation factors, so each Covered Upgrade can be reported as a separate project on those worksheets. Attachment 9 – Revenue Credit Detail, Line 18, “Less: Revenue Credits included in the TRBAA,” removes any TRBAA Credits otherwise recorded in the revenue accounts included on Attachment 9, so those amounts are credited to customers once, through the TRBAA, and not also through the Formula Rate. The full eligible cost of each Covered Upgrade therefore remains in the Base TRR. The existing Protocols govern the supporting documentation, information exchange, review, challenge, and true-up of Formula Rate inputs, including Covered Upgrade plant and the amounts removed on Attachment 9, Line 18.26

Covered Upgrades placed under CAISO’s Operational Control are included in LS Power Grid’s rate base and Base TRR in the same manner as other LS Power Grid transmission plant and are subject to LS Power Grid’s Commission-accepted Formula Rate, including any generally applicable Commission-approved return incentive. Covered Upgrades are not subject to the commitments

Covered Upgrade costs will be separately identiﬁed on Attachment 1a and excluded from the cost containment worksheets provided under Sections 1.c and 3.d(vi) of the Protocols (Attachments 11 and 12 of the Formula Rate Template). LS Power Grid is not requesting a diﬀerent return on equity, a diﬀerent capital structure, or a new incentive ROE adder in this proceeding.27

E. Supporting Testimony and Illustrative Exhibits
Mr. Patrick Hayes’ testimony (Exhibit No. LSC-100) describes the Show Cause Order, the CAISO stakeholder process, the pro forma CRA, and the associated TO Tariff revisions, and explains how those measures assign cost responsibility for Large Load-Driven Upgrades, mitigate cost shifting and stranded-asset risk, avoid double recovery, and promote transparency. His supporting exhibits provide an Illustrative MTRC Calculation (Exhibit No. LSC-101), an Example Reconciliation Statement (Exhibit No. LSC-102), and an Example Invoice Statement (Exhibit No. LSC-103). Mr. D. Eric Arzola’s testimony (Exhibit No. LSC-200) explains the crediting of CRA amounts through the TRBAA and the related TO Tariff revisions, and explains why no changes are needed to the Formula Rate Template, the Protocols, or Appendix I. His supporting exhibits include an Illustrative Formula Rate Template populated with hypothetical data for the 2030 Rate Year (Exhibit No. LSC-201) and an Illustrative TRBAA Calculation for the TRBA years ending September 30, 2030 and 2031 (Exhibit No. LSC-202). Using a hypothetical $60 million Covered Upgrade with an Annual MTRC of $10,278,360, projected QTR of $3,363,840, and a Supplemental MTRC of $6,914,520, those exhibits demonstrate that the TRBAA Credits are removed on Attachment 9, Line 18 and returned to customers through the TRBAA, so that they are credited only once.

IV. THE PROPOSED REVISIONS ARE JUST AND REASONABLE
LS Power Grid’s approach materially mitigates the cost-shifting concern identified in the Show Cause Order. Before in-service, LLIP deposits, Construction Security, and the Pre-In-Service Termination Cost provisions protect against cancellation and related exposure. After an upgrade enters service, the Annual MTRC, Supplemental MTRC, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Required Operational Security, and Termination Fee establish and secure a defined twenty-year revenue responsibility tied to the allocated capital cost of the identified Covered Upgrades. That design responds to the concern that bilateral revenue contributions “untethered from any

27  See Ex. Nos. LSC-100 (Hayes Testimony) and LSC-200 (Arzola Testimony).

PDF page 11

Hon. Debbie-Anne A. Reese
November 16, 2026
Page 11

A U S T I N   D A L L A S   D U B A I   H O U S T O N   L O N D O N   N E W  Y O R K   P A R I S   S A N  A N T O N I O   S E A T T L E   W A S H I N G T O N ,  D C assessment of the actual cost of providing transmission service induced by individual large loads” may be insufficient to protect other customers against unjustified cost shifts.28 The proposal also supplies the transparency and crediting mechanisms the Commission identified. The CRA Schedules, Annual Projection workpapers, Reconciliation Statements, and Cumulative Revenue Ledger make the upgrade-specific calculations available to the Eligible Customer, which has information rights under CRA Section 6.12 and dispute rights under Article

11. Attachment 1a separately identifies Covered Upgrade plant, and each annual TRBAA filing is an FPA section 205 proceeding in which interested parties may review and challenge the TRBAA Credits. The LLIP further provides for study reports, a Report Meeting addressing study results and assigned cost responsibility, and CAISO posting of FILI Service Agreements. Customer-specific information is protected under Section 15.3 of the TO Tariff, the CAISO Tariff, and, in TRBAA filings, 18 C.F.R. § 388.112.

Crediting CRA amounts through the TRBAA ensures that Large Load payments are credited toward LS Power Grid’s TRR. The full cost of each Covered Upgrade remains in the Base TRR, consistent with CAISO’s requirement that facilities under its Operational Control be included in the TRR, while the TRBAA reduces the TRR paid by all customers through the TAC by the amounts received under the CRA. The TRBA ensures that LS Power Grid collects no more and no less than its Base TRR, and Attachment 9, Line 18 ensures that each dollar is credited only once. The CRA does not alter CAISO cost allocation or the retail arrangements between an Eligible Customer and a Large Load Customer.

Finally, the proposal is appropriately tailored to LS Power Grid as a non-load-serving PTO. It gives LS Power Grid a meaningful interconnection and cost-recovery role when a Large Load affects its facilities, even though LS Power Grid has no retail relationship with the load, and requires evaluation of alternative transmission technologies before traditional network upgrades are selected, consistent with the Commission’s focus on keeping costs at a responsible and accountable level. It does not disturb the Gates and Round Mountain projects, LS Power Grid’s existing revenue requirement, CAISO cost allocation, or CAISO’s study and planning authority.

V. REQUESTED EFFECTIVE DATE AND WAIVERS
LS Power Grid requests an effective date of January 16, 2027, sixty-one (61) days after the date of this filing, satisfying the Commission’s sixty-day notice requirement.29 LS Power Grid requests waiver of the cost-of-service-statement requirements of 18 C.F.R. § 35.13, and any other Part 35 requirements that otherwise may apply, to the extent necessary. The requested waivers are appropriate because the filing establishes a prospective interconnection and contractual framework, does not change the cost-of-service inputs or methods of LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate, and requires no changes to LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate Template or Protocols.

LS Power Grid TO Tariff

1. Preamble. The Participating TO’s revenue requirements and applicable rates and charges for transmission access over the CAISO Controlled Grid and the terms and conditions for transmission expansion and interconnection are set forth in this TO Tariff and the CAISO Tariff.  For purposes of this TO Tariff and the CAISO Tariff, LS Power Grid California, LLC (“LS Power Grid”) is a Non-Load-Serving Participating TO and has no End-Use Customers.

1.1
Transmission Access for Wheeling Customers.  Wheeling allows Scheduling Coordinators to deliver Energy through or out of the CAISO Controlled Grid to serve a load located outside the transmission or Distribution System of a Participating TO. Wheeling Access Charges shall be paid by Scheduling Coordinators to the CAISO pursuant to the CAISO Tariff.

1.2
Transmission Access for End-Users.  End-Users receive transmission service over the CAISO Controlled Grid through the Scheduling Coordinator designated by the End-User.  Charges to UDCs or MSSs of each Participating TO on behalf of the End-Users for access to the CAISO Controlled Grid shall be paid to the applicable Participating TO consistent with the distribution of the Regional Access Charge in Section 26.1.3 of the CAISO Tariff.

2. Effective Date.  This TO Tariff is effective on the date accepted by the FERC and shall continue to be effective, as amended from time to time, so long as LSPGC is a party to the Transmission Control Agreement.

2.1
Termination. This TO Tariff may be terminated by LSPGC upon such advance notice and with such authorization as FERC may require.

3. TO Definitions. Certain capitalized terms used in this TO Tariff shall have the meanings set out below unless otherwise stated or the context otherwise requires. Capitalized terms used in this TO Tariff and not defined below shall have the meanings set out in the CAISO Tariff as it may be amended from time to time.

3.1
Access Charge.   A charge paid by all UDCs, MSSs, and, in certain cases, Scheduling Coordinators delivering Energy to Gross Load, as set forth in Section 26.1 of the CAISO Tariff.

3.2
AGC. Generation equipment that automatically responds to signals from the CAISO’s EMS control in real time to control the power output of electric generators within a prescribed area in response to a change in system frequency, tie-line loading, or the relation of these to each other, so as to maintain the target system frequency and/or the established interchange with other areas within the predetermined limits.

3.3
Ancillary Services.  Regulation, Spinning Reserve, Non-Spinning Reserve, Voltage Support and Black Start together with such other interconnected

PDF page 19

5
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

operation services as the CAISO may develop in cooperation with Market Participants to support the transmission of Energy from generation resources to Loads while maintaining reliable operation of the CAISO Controlled Grid in accordance with Good Utility Practice.

3.4

3.5
Available Transfer Capacity. The available capacity of a given transmission path, in MW after allocation of rights associated with Existing Contracts and Transmission Ownership Rights, to that path’s Operating Transfer Capability established consistent with CAISO and WECC transmission capacity rating guidelines, as further described in Appendix L to the CAISO Tariff.

3.6
Base Transmission Revenue Requirement. The Transmission Revenue Requirement which does not reflect amounts for the TRBAA.

3.7
Black Start. The procedure by which a Generating Unit self-starts without an external source of electricity, thereby restoring power to the CAISO Controlled Grid following system or local area blackouts.

3.8
Business Day.  Monday through Friday, excluding federal holidays and the day after Thanksgiving Day.

3.9

CAISO ADR Procedures. The procedures for resolution of disputes or differences set out in Section 13 of the CAISO Tariff, as amended from time to time.

3.11
CAISO Controlled Grid. The system of transmission lines and associated facilities of the Participating TOs that have been placed under the CAISO’s Operational Control.

3.12
CAISO Protocols.  The rules, protocols, procedures and standards promulgated by the CAISO (as amended from time to time) to be complied with by the CAISO Scheduling Coordinators, Participating TOs and all other Market Participants in relation to the operation of the CAISO Controlled Grid and the participation in the markets for Energy and Ancillary Services in accordance with the CAISO Tariff.

Congestion. A characteristic of the transmission system produced by a binding Constraint to the optimum economic dispatch to meet Demand such that the LMP, exclusive of Marginal Cost of Losses, at different Locations of the transmission system is not equal.

3.19
Congestion Management. The alleviation of Congestion in accordance with applicable CAISO Protocols and Good Utility Practice.

3.22
CPUC. The California Public Utilities Commission or its successor.

3.23
Demand. The rates at which Energy is delivered to Load and Scheduling Points by Generation, transmission or distribution facilities. It is the product of voltage and the in-phase component of alternating current measured in units of watts or standard multiples therefore, e.g. 1000 W = 1 kW, 1000 kW= 1 MW, etc.

3.24
Direct Assignment Facilities. Facilities or portions of facilities that are owned by the Participating TO necessary to physically and electrically interconnect a

3.25
Dispatch. The operating control of an integrated electric system to: i) assign specific Generation Units and other sources of supply to effect the supply to meet the relevant area Demand taken as Load rises or falls; ii) control operations and maintenance of high voltage lines, substations, and equipment, including administration of safety procedures; iii) operate Interconnections; iv) manage Energy transactions with other interconnected Control Areas; and v) curtail Demand.

3.26
Distribution System. The distribution assets of a TO, UDC, or MSS.

3.27
Eligible Customer. (i) Any utility (including any Participating TO, Market Participant or power marketer), Federal power marketing agency, or any person generating Energy for sale or resale; Energy sold or produced by such entity may be Energy produced in the United States, Canada or Mexico; however, such entity is not eligible for transmission service that would be prohibited by FPA Section 212(h)(2); and (ii) any retail customer taking unbundled transmission service pursuant to a state retail access program or pursuant to a voluntary offer of unbundled retail transmissions service by the Participating TO.

3.28
Eligible Customer for Large Load (“ECLL”). A UDC or MSS that provides retail transmission and interconnection service to Large Load End-Users in the CAISO’s Balancing Authority Area.

3.29

3.34
End-Use Customer or End-User. A purchaser of electric power that purchases such power to satisfy a Load directly connected to the CAISO Controlled Grid or to a Distribution System and who does not resell the power.

3.35

3.36

3.37

3.39
Expedited Interconnection Agreement. A contract between a party which has submitted a Request for Expedited Interconnection Procedures and the Participating TO under which the Participating TO agrees to process, on an expedited basis, the Completed TSWL Interconnection Application of such party and which sets forth the terms, conditions, and cost responsibilities for such TSWL Interconnection.

3.41
Facility or Facilities Study. An engineering study conducted to determine required modifications to the Participating TO’s transmission system, including the estimated cost and scheduled completion date for such modifications, that will be required to provide needed services.

3.51
Good Utility Practice. Any of the practices, methods, and acts engaged in or approved by a significant portion of the electric utility industry during the relevant time period, or any of the practices, methods, and acts which, in the exercise of reasonable judgment in light of the facts known at the time the decision was made, could have been expected to accomplish the desired result at a reasonable cost consistent with good business practices, reliability, safety, and

LS Power Grid TO Tariff

expedition.

3.52
Gross Load. For purposes of calculating the transmission Access Charge, Gross Load is all Energy (adjusted for distribution losses) delivered for the supply of End-Use Customer Loads directly connected to the transmission facilities or directly connected to the Distribution System of a Utility Distribution Company or MSS Operator located in a PTO Service Territory. Gross Load shall exclude

(1) Load with respect to which the Wheeling Access Charge is payable, (2) Load that is exempt from the Access Charge pursuant to Section 4.1, Appendix I of the CAISO Tariff, and the portion of the load of an individual retail customer of a Utility Distribution Company, Small Utility Distribution Company or MSS Operator that is served by a Generating Unit that: (a) is located on the customer’s site or provides service to the customer’s site through over-the-fence arrangements as authorized by Section 218 of the California Public Utilities Code; (b) is a qualifying small power production facility or qualifying cogeneration facility, as those terms are defined in the FERC’s regulations implementing Section 201 of the Public Utility Regulatory Policies Act of 1978; and (c) secures Standby Service from the Participating TO under terms approved by a Local Regulatory Authority or FERC, as applicable, or can be curtailed concurrently with an Outage of the Generating Unit serving the Load.  Gross Load forecasts consistent with filed Transmission Revenue Requirements will be provided by each Participating TO to the CAISO.

3.53
Interconnection Agreement. A contract between a party requesting a TSWL Interconnection and a Participating TO, as it may be amended from time to time, and, for an ECLL Interconnection, the ECLL Interconnection Agreement.

3.54
Interest. Interest shall be calculated in accordance with the methodology specified for interest on refunds in the regulations of FERC at 18 C.F.R. § 35.19a(a)(2)(iii). Interest on delinquent amounts shall be calculated from the due date of the bill to the date of payment. When payments are made by mail, bills shall be considered as having been paid on the date of receipt. Notwithstanding the foregoing, interest, if any, on amounts payable or refundable between the Participating TO and a party to a Cost Recovery Agreement shall be governed solely by that Cost Recovery Agreement.

3.55
Interim Firm Interconnection Service. Interconnection service that can be provided, if any, on a firm basis prior to the completion of the ECLL Interconnection Facilities and an authorization for the Large Load to withdraw Energy.

3.56
Large Load. An End-User located at a single site behind one or more Point(s) of Withdrawal from the CAISO Controlled Grid and that has a peak load of 50 MW or greater.

3.57
Load. An end-use device of an End-Use Customer that consumes power.  Load

should not be confused with Demand, which is the measure of power that a Load receives or requires.

3.58
Local Publicly Owned Electric Utility. A municipality or municipal corporation operating as a public utility furnishing electric service, a municipal district furnishing electric services, or a joint powers authority that includes one or more of these agencies and that owns Generation or transmission facilities, or furnishes electric services over its own or its members’ electric Distribution System.

3.59
Local Regulatory Authority. The state or local governmental authority responsible for the regulation or oversight of a utility.

3.63
Metered Subsystem (“MSS”).  A geographically contiguous system, located within a single zone which has been operating as an electric utility for a number of years prior to the CAISO Operations Date as a municipal utility, water district, irrigation district, state agency or federal power marketing authority subsumed within the CAISO Balancing Authority Area and encompassed by CAISO certified revenue quality meters at each interface point with the CAISO Controlled Grid and CAISO-certified revenue quality meters on all Generating Units or, if aggregated, each individual resource and Participating Load, Reliability Demand Response Resource, and Proxy Demand Resource internal to the system, which is operated in accordance with a MSS agreement described in Section 4.9.1 of the CAISO Tariff.

3.64
NERC.  The North American Electric Reliability Corporation or its successor.

3.65
New Participating TO. A Participating TO that is not an Original Participating TO.

3.66
Non-Load-Serving Participating TO. A Participating TO that does not serve Load.

Non-Participating TO. A TO that is not a party to the TCA or, for the purpose of Section 16.1 of the CAISO Tariff, the holder of transmission service rights

LS Power Grid TO Tariff

under an Existing Contract that is not a Participating TO.

3.68
Non-Spinning Reserve. The portion of off-line generating capacity that is capable of being synchronized and ramping to a specified load in ten minutes (or load that is capable of being interrupted in ten minutes) and that is capable of running (or being interrupted) for at least two hours.

3.69
Operational Control. The rights of the CAISO under the Transmission Control Agreement and the CAISO Tariff to direct Participating TOs how to operate their transmission lines and facilities and other electric plant affecting the reliability of those lines and facilities for the purpose of affording comparable nondiscriminatory transmission access and meeting Applicable Reliability Criteria.

3.70
Original Participating TO. A Participating TO that was a Participating TO as of January 1, 2000. The Original Participating TOs are Pacific Gas and Electric Company, Southern California Edison Company, and San Diego Gas & Electric Company.

3.71
Participating TO (“PTO”). A party to the TCA whose application under Section 2.2 of the TCA has been accepted and who has placed, or expects to place, its transmission assets and/or Entitlements under the CAISO’s Operational Control in accordance with the TCA. A PTO may be an Original Participating TO or a New Participating TO. For the purposes of this Tariff, the Participating TO is LS Power Grid.

3.72
Physical Scheduling Plant. A group of two or more related Generating Units each of which is individually capable of producing Energy, but which either by physical necessity or operational design must be operated as if they were a single Generating Unit and any Generating Unit or Units containing related multiple generating components which meet one or more of the following criteria: (i) multiple generating components are related by a common flow of fuel which cannot be interrupted without substantial loss of efficiency of the combined output of all components; (ii) the Energy production from one component necessarily causes Energy production from other components; (iii) the operational arrangement of related multiple generating components determines the overall physical efficiency of the combined output of all components; (iv) the level of coordination required to schedule individual generating components would cause the CAISO to incur scheduling costs far in excess of the benefits of having scheduled such individual components separately; or (v) metered output is available only for the combined output of related multiple generation components and separate generating component metering is either impractical or economically inefficient.

3.73
Point of Withdrawal. Point(s) within the CAISO Balancing Authority Area, or for purposes of scheduling and operating the Day Ahead Market only, the EDAM Area where Energy and Ancillary Services are made available to a receiving party

under the CAISO Tariff, for purposes of scheduling and operating the Real-Time Market only, the EIM Area where Energy and Ancillary Services are made available to a receiving party under the CAISO Tariff, or for purposes of the definition of Large Load only, the point(s) where a non-transmission facility that is used to withdraw Energy from the CAISO Controlled Grid to serve an End- User connects to the CAISO Controlled Grid.

3.74
Projects.  LS Power Grid’s interest in the approximately       850 megavar (“MVAR”) static synchronous condenser (“STATCOM”) project to be located in Fresno County, California interconnected to the PG&E Gates Substation, and LS Power Grid’s interest in the approximately       530 MVAR STATCOM facility and associated 500 kilovolt (“kV”) switchyard to be located in Shasta County, California, interconnected to the PG&E Round Mountain to Table Mountain 500 kV transmission lines.

3.75
Project Proponent. A Market Participant or group of Market Participants that (i) advocates a transmission addition or upgrade; (ii) is unwilling to pay the full cost of the proposed transmission addition or upgrade, and thus is not a Project Sponsor; and (iii) initiates proceedings under the CAISO ADR Procedures to determine the need for the proposed transmission addition or upgrade.

3.76
Project Sponsor. A Market Participant or group of Market Participants or a Participating TO that proposes the construction of a transmission addition or upgrade in accordance with Section 24 of the CAISO Tariff.

3.77
Regional Access Charge.  A component of the Access Charge determined by the CAISO under Section 26.1 of the CAISO Tariff.

3.78
Regional Transmission Facility. A transmission facility under the Operational Control of the CAISO that is owned by the Participating TO or to which the Participating TO has an Entitlement that may be associated with a Converted Right, which operates at a voltage at or above 200 kilovolts, and supporting facilities, and the costs of which are not directly assigned to one or more specific customers.

3.79
Regional Transmission Group (“RTG”). A voluntary organization approved by FERC and composed of transmission owners, transmission users, and other entities, organized to efficiently coordinate the planning, expansion, and use of transmission on a regional and inter-regional basis.

3.80
Regional Transmission Revenue Requirement. The portion of the Participating TO’s TRR associated with and allocable to the Participating TO’s Regional Transmission Facilities and Rights associated with Regional Transmission Facilities.

3.81
Regional Utility-Specific Rate. The Participating TO’s Regional Transmission

PDF page 28

14
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Revenue Requirement divided by the Participating TO’s forecast of its Gross Load.

3.82
Regional Wheeling Access Charge. The Wheeling Access Charge assessed by the CAISO associated with the recovery of the Participating TO’s Regional Transmission Revenue Requirement in accordance with Section 26.1 of the CAISO Tariff.

3.83
Regulation. The service provided either by Generating Units certified by the CAISO as equipped and capable of responding to the CAISO’s direct digital control (AGC) signals, or by System Resources that have been certified by the CAISO as capable of delivering such service to the CAISO Balancing Authority Area, in an upward and downward direction to match, on a Real Time basis, Demand and resources, consistent with established NERC and WECC reliability standards, including any requirements of the Nuclear Regulatory Commission. Regulation is used to control the Power output of electric generators within a prescribed area in response to a change in system frequency, tie-line loading, or the relation of these to each other so as to maintain the target system frequency and/or the established interchange with other Balancing Authority Areas within the predetermined Regulation Limits. Regulation includes both the increase of output by a Generating unit or System Resource (Regulation Up) and the decrease in output by a Generating unit or System Resource (Regulation Down). Regulation Up and Regulation Down are distinct capacity products, with separately stated requirements and ASMPs in each Settlement Period.

3.84
Regulatory Authority. The Federal Energy Regulatory Commission.

3.85
Reliability Criteria. Pre-established criteria that are to be followed in order to maintain desired performance of the CAISO Controlled Grid under contingency or steady state conditions.

3.86
Reliability Upgrade. The transmission facilities, other than Direct Assignment Facilities, beyond the first point of Interconnection necessary to accomplish a TSWL Interconnection safely and reliably to the CAISO Controlled Grid, which would not have been necessary but for the TSWL Interconnection, including network upgrades necessary to remedy short circuit or stability problems resulting from the TSWL Interconnection. Reliability Upgrades also include, consistent with WECC practice, the facilities necessary to mitigate any adverse impact a TSWL Interconnection may have on a path’s WECC path rating. Reliability Upgrades shall be specified in the Interconnection Agreement that governs a TSWL Interconnection and shall be subject to FERC approval.

3.87
Request for Expedited Interconnection Procedures.  A written request by which an applicant for TSWL Interconnection can request expedited processing of its TSWL Interconnection Application.

PDF page 29

15
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

3.88
Scheduling Coordinator. An entity certified by the CAISO for the purpose of undertaking the functions specified in Section 4.5 of the CAISO Tariff.

3.89
Scheduling Point. A location at which the CAISO Controlled Grid or a transmission facility owned by a Transmission Ownership Right holder is connected, by a group of transmission paths for which a physical, non- simultaneous transmission capacity rating has been established for Congestion Management, to transmission facilities that are outside the CAISO’s Operational Control.

3.90
Site Control. An ECLL may demonstrate that its Large Load Customer has Site Control by:

(a) an option for a lease or an executed lease for real property of sufficient size to house the Large Load; or

(b) an option to purchase or title to real property of sufficient size to house the Large Load.

3.91
Spinning Reserve. The portion of unloaded synchronized generating capacity that is immediately responsive to system frequency and that is capable of being loaded in ten minutes, and that is capable of running for at least two hours.

3.92
System Impact Study. An engineering study conducted to determine whether a request for Interconnection to the CAISO Controlled Grid would require new transmission additions, upgrades, or other mitigation measures.

3.93
System Impact Study Agreement. An agreement between a Participating TO and an entity that has requested Interconnection to the Participating TO’s transmission system pursuant to which the entity requesting Interconnection agrees to reimburse the Participating TO for the cost of performing or reviewing a System Impact Study.

3.94
TO Tariff. This Transmission Owner Tariff, as it may be amended or superseded.

3.95
Transmission Control Agreement (“TCA”). The agreement between the CAISO and Participating TOs establishing the terms and conditions under which TOs will become Participating TOs and how the CAISO and each Participating TO will discharge its respective duties and responsibilities, as may be modified from time to time.

3.96
Transmission Owner (“TO”).  An entity owning transmission facilities or having firm contractual rights to use transmission facilities.

3.97
Transmission Revenue Balancing Account (“TRBA”).  A mechanism

16
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

established by the Participating TO which will ensure that all Transmission Revenue Credits and other credits specified in the CAISO Tariff, and all amounts that a Cost Recovery Agreement requires to be credited through the TRBAA, flow through to CAISO Tariff and TO Tariff transmission customers.

3.98
Transmission Revenue Balancing Account Adjustment (“TRBAA”).  A
mechanism established by the Participating TO which will ensure that all Transmission Revenue Credits and other credits specified in Sections 6 and 8 of Appendix F, Schedule 3 of the CAISO Tariff, and all amounts that a Cost Recovery Agreement requires to be credited through the TRBAA, flow through to CAISO Tariff and TO Tariff transmission customers.

3.99
Transmission Revenue Credit. The proceeds received from the CAISO (other than for the recovery of the Participating TO’s Regional Transmission Revenue Requirement through the Regional Transmission Access Charges).

3.100 Transmission Revenue Requirement (“TRR’). The total annual authorized revenue requirement associated with 1) transmission facilities and Entitlements turned over to the Operational Control of the CAISO by the Participating TO or

2) the transmission facilities that are not yet in operation, but have been approved under Section 24 and assigned to an Approved Project Sponsor. The costs of any transmission facility turned over to the Operational Control of the CAISO shall be fully included in the Participating TO’s TRR.  The TRR includes the costs of transmission facilities and Entitlements and deducts Transmission Revenue Credits and is shown in Appendix I.

3.101 Transmission System Wholesale Load (“TSWL”) Interconnection. The interconnection of the transmission facilities of the Participating TO to the transmission facilities of a Transmission Owner, electric utility, or other entity eligible to request interconnection. A TSWL Interconnection does not include a Generator Interconnection or an ECLL Interconnection.

3.102 TSWL Interconnection Application. An application that requests a TSWL Interconnection to the CAISO Controlled Grid, including any modifications to existing points of interconnection.

3.103 Uncontrollable Force. Any act of God, labor disturbance, act of the public enemy, war, insurrection, riot, fire, storm, flood, earthquake, explosion, any curtailment, order, regulation or restriction imposed by governmental, military or lawfully established civilian authorities or any other cause beyond the reasonable control of the CAISO, a Market participant, or the Participating TO, as the case may be, which could not be avoided through the exercise of Good Utility Practice.

3.104 Utility Distribution Company (“UDC”).  An entity that owns a Distribution System for the delivery of Energy to and from the CAISO Controlled Grid, and/or that provides regulated retail electric service to End-Users.

17
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

3.105 Voltage Support. Services provided by Generating Units or other equipment such as shunt capacitors, static var compensators, or synchronous condensers that are required to maintain established grid voltage criteria. This service is required under normal or system emergency conditions.

3.106 Western Electricity Coordinating Council (“WECC”).  The Western Electricity Coordinating Council or its successor.

3.107 Wheeling Access Charge. The charge assessed by the CAISO that is paid by a Scheduling Coordinator for Wheeling in accordance with Section 26.1 of the CAISO Tariff. Wheeling Access Charges shall not apply for Wheeling under a bundled non-economy Energy coordination agreement of a Participating TO executed prior to July 9, 1996. The Wheeling Access Charge consists of a Regional Wheeling Access Charge and, if applicable, a Local Wheeling Access Charge.

3.108 Wheeling Out. Except for Existing Rights exercised under an Existing Contract in accordance with Section 16.1 of the CAISO Tariff, the use of the CAISO Controlled Grid for the transmission of Energy from a Generating Unit located within the CAISO Controlled Grid to serve a Load located outside the transmission and Distribution System of a Participating TO.

3.109 Wheeling Through. Except for Existing Rights exercised under an Existing Contract in accordance with Section 16.1 of the CAISO Tariff, the use of the CAISO Controlled Grid for the transmission of Energy from a resource located outside the CAISO Controlled Grid to serve a Load located outside the transmission and Distribution System of a Participating TO.

3.110 Wheeling. Wheeling Out or Wheeling Through.

3.111
Wholesale Customer. A person wishing to purchase Energy and
Ancillary Services at a Bulk Supply Point or a Scheduling Point for resale.

4. Eligibility for Service. Transmission service over a Participating TO’s system shall be provided only to Eligible Customers for transmission service under the CAISO Tariff.  Any dispute as to whether an entity that resells power is eligible for an interconnection under this TO Tariff shall be resolved by the FERC. Any dispute as to whether an End-User is eligible for an interconnection under this TO Tariff will be resolved by the Local Regulatory Authority.

5. Access Charges and Transmission Rates. The applicable Access Charges are provided in the CAISO Tariff.

5.1
Wheeling Access Charge. The Wheeling Access Charge shall be determined in accordance with the CAISO Tariff. The Wheeling Access Charge assessed by the CAISO consists of a Regional Wheeling Access Charge and, if applicable, a Local Wheeling Access Charge.  Wheeling revenues are disbursed by the CAISO to Participating TOs pursuant to Section 26.1.4.3 of the CAISO Tariff.

PDF page 32

18
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

5.2
Transmission Revenue Requirement.  Appendix F, Schedule 3, Section 6 of the CAISO Tariff provides the Transmission Revenue Requirement for each Participating TO to be used to develop the Access Charges. LS Power Grid’s TRR is set forth in Appendix I.

5.3
Transmission Revenue Balancing Account Adjustment (“TRBAA”). The Participating TO shall maintain a Transmission Revenue Balancing Account with an annual TRBAA that will ensure that all Transmission Revenue Credits and adjustments for any over-or under-recovery of its annual Transmission Revenue Requirement, if any, specified in Sections 6, 8 and 13 of Appendix F, Schedule 3 of the CAISO Tariff, flow through to transmission customers. The TRBAA used to calculate the Regional Transmission Revenue Requirement shall include other adjustments specified in Appendix F, Schedule 3, Sections 6, 8 and 13 of the CAISO Tariff. The TRBAA shall also ensure that all amounts that a Cost Recovery Agreement requires to be credited to transmission customers through the TRBAA flow through to transmission customers.

The TRBAA shall be equal to:
TRBAA = Cr + Cf + I
Where:

Cr =  The principal balance in the Transmission Revenue Balancing Account (“TRBA”) recorded in FERC Account No. 254 as of September 30 of the year prior to commencement of the January billing cycle. This balance represents the unamortized balance in the TRBA from the previous period and the difference in the amount of revenues or expenditures from Transmission Revenue Credits, amounts recorded in the TRBA under any Cost Recovery Agreement, and any over- or under-recovery of its annual Transmission Revenue Requirement and the amount of such revenues or expenditures that has been refunded to or collected from customers through operation of the TRBAA;

Cf =  The forecast of Transmission Revenue Credits, if any, for the following calendar year, plus any forecast credit that a Cost Recovery Agreement requires to be included in the TRBAA for the following calendar year;

I =
The interest balance for the TRBA, which shall be calculated using the interest rate pursuant to Section 35.19a of FERC’s regulations under the Federal Power Act (18 CFR Section 35.19a). Interest shall be calculated based on the average TRBA principal balance each month, compounded quarterly. Amounts recorded in the TRBA under any Cost Recovery Agreement shall be included in the TRBA principal balance for purposes of this calculation. Nothing in this Section 5.3 requires the payment of

PDF page 33

19
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

interest between the Participating TO and a party to a Cost Recovery Agreement on amounts payable or refundable under that agreement, which shall be governed solely by the terms of the Cost Recovery Agreement; and

The LS Power Grid TRBAA, calculated in accordance with the CAISO Tariff and approved by the FERC in accordance with Appendix F, Schedule 3, Section 9, is stated in Appendix I.  To ensure the TRBA is effective each January 1, LS Power Grid shall file the TRBAA no later than October 15th of each calendar year.

6. Ancillary Services - Applicability and Charges. Ancillary Services are needed to maintain reliability within the CAISO Controlled Grid. If any Ancillary Services are required, LS Power Grid will not provide such services directly to the transmission customer and the transmission customer will be required to meet any such requirement in accordance with the CAISO Tariff.

7. Billing and Payment.

7.1
The CAISO, in accordance with the CAISO Tariff, shall pay the Participating TO, among other things, all applicable Access Charge revenues and Wheeling revenues in connection with the Project and any other transmission facility owned by the Participating TO and placed under the operational control of the CAISO.

7.2
Users of LS Power Grid’s Regional Transmission Facilities and Entitlements placed under the CAISO’s Operational Control shall pay to the CAISO all applicable charges in accordance with the CAISO Tariff.

8. Obligation to Interconnect or Construct Transmission Expansions and Facility Upgrades.  Unless LS Power Grid is the lead Participating TO with respect to an Interconnection, the provisions of Section 8 of this TO Tariff, and its subparts, shall have no application to the Project or any other Regional Transmission Facilities, or Entitlements turned over to the CAISO’s operational control by LS Power Grid, and the corresponding provisions of the lead Participating TO’s TO Tariff shall govern.

8.1
Participating TO Obligation to Interconnect. The Participating TO shall, at the request of a third party, interconnect its system to the wholesale Generating Facility or Load of such third party, or modify an existing wholesale Interconnection. Interconnections under this TO Tariff shall be available to entities eligible to request Interconnection consistent with the provisions of Section 210(a) of the FPA. The procedures for the Interconnections of Load to the CAISO Controlled Grid shall be governed by this TO Tariff and the CAISO Tariff. The procedures for Interconnection of wholesale Generating Facilities to the CAISO Controlled Grid shall be governed by the CAISO Tariff.

8.1.1 Interconnection to Transmission System. Any interconnection must

PDF page 34

20
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

be consistent with Good Utility Practice, in conformance with all Applicable Reliability Criteria, all applicable statutes, regulations, and CAISO reliability criteria for the CAISO Controlled Grid. The Participating TO will not accommodate the interconnection if doing so would impair systems reliability, or would otherwise impair the ability of the Participating TO to honor its Encumbrances existing as of the time an entity submits its interconnection application. The Participating TO shall identify any such adverse effect on its Encumbrances in the interconnection studies. To the extent the Participating TO determines that the interconnection will have an adverse effect on Encumbrances, the party requesting interconnection shall mitigate such adverse effect.

8.1.1.1 Letter Agreement for Generator Interconnections. Pursuant to Section 12 of Appendix DD or KK of the CAISO Tariff, prior to executing a Generator Interconnection Agreement, a party seeking a Generator Interconnection may, in order to advance the implementation of its Generator Interconnection, request, and the Participating TO shall offer the party, a Letter Agreement that authorizes the Participating TO to begin engineering, design, and procurement of long lead-time items, or construction necessary for the establishment of the Generator Interconnection. However, the Participating TO shall not be obligated to offer a Letter Agreement if the party seeking Generator Interconnection is in Dispute Resolution as a result of an allegation that the party seeking Generator Interconnection has failed to meet any milestones or comply with any prerequisites specified in the CAISO Tariff. The Letter Agreement is an optional procedure. The Letter Agreement shall provide for the party seeking Generator

Interconnection to pay the cost of all activities authorized by the party and to make advance payments or provide other satisfactory security for such costs.

Following a party seeking Generator Interconnection’s request for a Letter Agreement, the Participating TO shall prepare and tender to the party seeking Generator Interconnection a draft Letter Agreement, including draft exhibits that include the proposed scope of work, estimated costs, payments, financial security and milestones, as applicable. The party seeking Generator Interconnection shall provide written comments, or notification of no comments, to the draft exhibits within thirty (30) Calendar Days. The Participating TO and the party seeking Generator Interconnection shall negotiate concerning any disputed provisions of the exhibits to the draft Letter Agreement for not more than ninety (90) Calendar Days after the Participating TO tenders the draft Letter Agreement to the party seeking Generator Interconnection. If the party seeking Generator Interconnection determines that negotiations are at an impasse, it may request termination of the negotiations at any time after tender of the draft

PDF page 35

21
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Letter Agreement and request submission of the unexecuted Letter Agreement with FERC or initiate dispute resolution procedures pursuant to Section 14 of this TO Tariff. If the party seeking Generator Interconnection requests termination of the negotiations, but within ninety (90) Calendar Days after the Participating TO tenders the draft Letter Agreement to the party seeking Generator Interconnection, fails to request either the filing of the unexecuted Letter Agreement or initiate dispute resolution procedures, it shall be deemed to have withdrawn its request for a Letter Agreement and the Participating TO shall have no further obligation to enter into a Letter Agreement, unless an extension is mutually agreed to by the parties. The Participating TO shall provide to the party seeking Generator Interconnection a final Letter Agreement within fifteen (15) Business Days after the completion of the negotiation process.

Following submission of the final Letter Agreement to the party seeking Generator Interconnection, the party shall either: (i) execute two originals of the tendered Letter Agreement and return them to the Participating TO; or (ii) request in writing that the Participating TO file with FERC a Letter Agreement in unexecuted form. As soon as practicable, but not later than ten (10) Business Days after receiving either the two executed originals of the tendered Letter Agreement or the request to file an unexecuted Letter Agreement, the Participating TO shall file the Letter Agreement with FERC, together with its explanation of any matters as to which the party seeking Generator Interconnection and the Participating TO disagree and support for the costs that the Participating TO proposes to charge to the party seeking Generator Interconnection under the Letter Agreement. An unexecuted Letter Agreement should contain terms and conditions deemed appropriate by the Participating TO for the Generator Interconnection request.

If the party seeking Generator Interconnection executes the final Letter Agreement, the Participating TO and the party seeking Generator Interconnection shall perform their respective obligations in accordance with the terms of the Letter Agreement, subject to modification by FERC. Upon submission of an unexecuted Letter Agreement, the party seeking Generator Interconnection and the Participating TO shall promptly comply with the unexecuted Letter Agreement, subject to modification by FERC.

8.1.2 Costs Associated with Interconnection. Each party requesting Interconnection shall pay the costs of planning, installing, owning, operating, and maintaining any Direct Assignment Facilities and, if applicable, any Reliability Upgrades required to provide the requested Interconnection, except as provided in Appendix DD of the CAISO

PDF page 36

22
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Tariff. In addition, such party shall implement all existing operating procedures necessary to safely and reliably interconnect such party’s Generating Facility or wholesale Load to the facilities of the Participating TO and to ensure the CAISO Controlled Grid’s conformance with the CAISO Grid Planning Criteria, and shall bear all costs of implementing such operating procedures, except as provided in Appendix DD of the CAISO Tariff. Any additional costs associated with accommodating the Interconnection shall be allocated in accordance with the cost responsibility methodology set forth in the CAISO Tariff for transmission expansions or upgrades.

8.1.3 Interconnection Agreement. Interconnection Agreements shall be tendered, executed, and filed as provided in Sections 10.9 and 10A.3.

8.1.4 Due Diligence to Construct. The Participating TO shall use due diligence to construct, within a reasonable time, any Direct Assignment Facilities and any Reliability Upgrades that it is obligated to construct pursuant to this TO Tariff and Section 24 and Appendix DD of the CAISO Tariff. The Participating TO’s obligation to build will be subject to: (1) its ability, after making a good faith effort, to obtain any necessary approvals and property rights under applicable federal, state, and local laws; (2) the presence of a cost recovery mechanism with cost responsibility assigned in accordance with the CAISO Tariff or applicable FERC precedent; and (3) a signed Interconnection Agreement, Engineering and Procurement Agreement, or, by mutual agreement of the parties, FERC acceptance for filing of an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement.

8.1.5 Energization. The Participating TO shall not be obligated to energize, nor shall the applicant or wholesale load be entitled to have its interconnection to the CAISO Controlled Grid energized, unless and until an Interconnection Agreement has been executed, or filed at FERC pursuant to Section 8.1.3, and become effective and such applicant or wholesale load has demonstrated to the CAISO’s reasonable satisfaction that it has complied with all of the requirements of the CAISO Tariff and the requirements of this TO Tariff.

8.1.6 Coordination with CAISO on Interconnection Requests. The Participating TO shall coordinate with the CAISO, pursuant to the provisions of the TCA and CAISO Tariff.

8.2
Participating TO Obligation to Construct Transmission Expansions or Facility Upgrades. The Participating TO shall be obligated to build transmission additions and facility upgrades where the Participating TO is obligated to construct or expand facilities in accordance with and subject to the limitations under Section 24 of the CAISO Tariff and this TO Tariff.

It is intended that FERC grant substantial deference to the factual determinations of the CAISO, (including the CAISO’s ADR Procedures), the CPUC, WECC, or RTG coordinated planning processes as to the need for or construction of a facility, the need for full cost recovery, and the allocation of costs.

Applicability and

Obligation to Build.

9.2.2 Delay in Construction or Expansion. The Participating TO also shall make available to such Project Sponsor and the CAISO, as the case may be, studies related to the cause and extent of the delay and the Participating TO’s ability to complete the new facilities, and evaluate any options available. The Participating TO also shall make available to such Project Sponsor and the CAISO, as the case may be, studies reasonably needed to evaluate the alternatives.
