9.3
Provisions Relating To Transmission Construction On the System Of Other TOs.  A Participating TO shall not be responsible for making arrangements for any engineering, permitting, and construction of transmission or distribution facilities on the system(s) of any other entity or for obtaining any regulatory approval for such facilities.  The Participating TO will undertake reasonable efforts through the coordinated planning process to assist in making such arrangements, including, without limitation, providing any information or data required by such other electric system pursuant to Good Utility Practice.

10. TSWL Interconnection Process.  All requests for TSWL Interconnection with the Projects or any other Regional Transmission Facilities, or Entitlements turned over to the CAISO’s operational control by LS Power Grid shall be directed to the Participating TO.

10.1
Applicability. All requests for TSWL Interconnection directly to the CAISO Controlled Grid from parties eligible to request such Interconnection consistent with Section 210(a) of the FPA shall be processed pursuant to the provisions of this Section 10. All requests for Interconnection of wholesale generation directly to the CAISO Controlled Grid shall be processed pursuant to the provisions of the CAISO Tariff. Requests for interconnection submitted by an ECLL on behalf of Large Load Customers shall be processed pursuant to Section 10A of this TO Tariff.

10.2
TSWL Interconnection Applications. An applicant requesting TSWL
Interconnection shall submit a written TSWL Interconnection Application which provides the information detailed below to the Participating TO. The Participating TO shall timestamp the application to establish study priority.

(i) the identity, address, telephone number, and email of the applicant requesting TSWL Interconnection;

(ii) the Interconnection point(s) to the CAISO Controlled Grid contemplated by the applicant;

(iii) the resultant (or new) maximum amount of TSWL Interconnection capacity contemplated by the applicant;

(iv) the proposed date for energizing the TSWL Interconnection and the term of the interconnection service; and

(v) such other information as the Participating TO reasonably requires to process the application.

In addition to the information specified above, the following information may also be provided in order to properly evaluate system conditions:

(vi) If the applicant is a wholesale load, the electrical location of the source of the power (if known) to be transmitted pursuant to applicant’s request for Interconnection. If the source of the power is not known, a system purchase will be assumed. If the location of the load is not known, a system sale will be assumed; and, in addition, if an applicant proposes to perform or cause a third party to perform any required System Impact

PDF page 39

25
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Study or any required Facilities Study, it shall so indicate in its TSWL Interconnection Application. The results of any study or studies performed by an applicant must be approved by both the CAISO and the Participating TO.

Within ten (10) Business Days after receipt of a TSWL Interconnection Application, the Participating TO and the CAISO if applicable shall determine whether the application is complete (“Completed TSWL Interconnection Application”). Whenever possible, the Participating TO will attempt to remedy deficiencies in the TSWL Interconnection Application through informal communications with the applicant. If such efforts are unsuccessful, the Participating TO shall return the TSWL Interconnection Application to the applicant.

The Participating TO will treat the information in the TSWL Interconnection Application, including the applicant’s identity, as confidential at the request of the applicant except to the extent that disclosure of the information is required by this TO Tariff, by regulatory or judicial order, for reliability purposes pursuant to Good Utility Practice, or pursuant to RTG or CAISO transmission information sharing agreements. The Participating TO shall treat this information consistent with the standards of conduct contained in Part 37 of FERC’s regulations.

10.2.1 Amendment to Completed TSWL Interconnection Application. An applicant shall only be limited to amending its Completed TSWL Interconnection Application once. Such amendment shall occur on or before ten (10) Business Days following the date the Participating TO tenders any Facilities Study Agreement. Specifically, an applicant may submit an amendment to its Completed TSWL Interconnection

Application to reflect a revised configuration for its New Facility. The amended Completed TSWL Interconnection Application shall be treated in accordance with Section 25 of the CAISO Tariff and Section 10.4 of this TO Tariff; the applicant’s Completed TSWL Interconnection Application shall be deemed withdrawn; and the applicant shall maintain its existing queue position, if (a) the amended Completed TSWL Interconnection Application is received by the Participating TO within ten (10) Business Days of the Participating TO’s tender of a Facilities Study Agreement; and

(b) the applicant has not submitted a previous amendment to the Completed TSWL Interconnection Application. In the event an applicant amends its Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, it will be responsible for any additional study costs that result from that amendment, including costs associated with revisions to studies for other applicants holding later queue positions.

10.3
Review of Completed TSWL Interconnection Application. After receiving a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, the Participating TO and the CAISO, if applicable, will determine on a non-discriminatory basis whether a

PDF page 40

26
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

System Impact Study is required. Whenever the Participating TO, and the CAISO, if applicable, determines that a System Impact Study is not required and that neither Reliability Upgrades nor changes in existing operating procedures are required, the Participating TO shall notify the applicant within fifteen (15) Business Days of the Completed Application Date. If the TSWL Interconnection can be accommodated without any Direct Assignment Facilities, then within thirty (30) Business Days of such notice from the Participating TO, the applicant shall request the Participating TO to tender to the applicant an Interconnection Agreement within thirty (30) Business Days of such request. The Participating TO shall tender to the applicant an Interconnection Agreement as provided in Section 10.9. If the Participating TO determines upon review of the Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, that Direct Assignment Facilities are required, the Participating TO shall tender to the applicant a Facilities Study Agreement within twenty (20) Business Days of the Completed Application Date and continue the Interconnection process pursuant to Section 10.7.

10.4
Notice of Need for System Impact Study. If the Participating TO, and the CAISO, if applicable, determines that a System Impact Study is necessary to accommodate the requested Interconnection, the Participating TO shall so inform the applicant as soon as practicable. In such cases, the Participating TO shall within twenty (20) Business Days of receipt of a Completed TSWL

Interconnection Application, tender a System Impact Study Agreement that defines the scope, content, assumptions and terms of reference for such study to be completed by the Participating TO, the estimated time required to complete it, and such other provisions as the parties may reasonably require, and pursuant to which the applicant shall agree to reimburse the Participating TO for the reasonable actual costs of performing the required System Impact Study. A description of the Participating TO’s transmission assessment practices for completing a System Impact Study shall be provided in the Participating TO’s FERC Form 715.

Alternatively, if the applicant will perform the System Impact Study, the Participating TO shall within twenty (20) Business Days of receipt of a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, tender a System Impact Study Agreement that defines the scope, content, assumptions and terms of reference for such study to be reviewed by the Participating TO; the estimated time required to complete it; and such other provisions as the parties may reasonably require, and pursuant to which the applicant shall agree to reimburse the Participating TO for the reasonable actual costs of reviewing the required System Impact Study.

For a TSWL Interconnection request to remain a Completed TSWL
Interconnection Application, the applicant shall execute the System Impact Study Agreement and return it to the Participating TO within ten (10) Business Days together with payment for the reasonable estimated cost of performing the System Impact Study or reviewing the applicant’s System Impact Study. Alternatively, if the applicant requests the Participating TO to proceed with the System Impact

PDF page 41

27
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Study or review thereof and commits to abide by the terms, conditions, and cost assignments ultimately determined under the CAISO ADR Procedures, including any determination by FERC or appeal of a FERC determination in accordance with that process, the Participating TO shall promptly proceed with the System Impact Study provided that such request is accompanied by payment of the reasonable estimated cost of the System Impact Study, and the parties shall submit the disputed terms for resolution under the CAISO ADR Procedures. If the applicant elects not to execute a System Impact Study Agreement, and does not request that the Participating TO proceed with the System Impact Study or review thereof, its application shall be deemed withdrawn, and the applicant shall reimburse to the Participating TO all costs reasonably incurred in processing the application.

10.5
System Impact Study Cost Reimbursement and Agreement.

10.5.1 Cost Reimbursement. The System Impact Study Agreement shall clearly specify the charge, based on the Participating TO’s estimate of the cost and time for completion of the System Impact Study. The charge shall not exceed the reasonable actual cost of the study. In performing the System Impact Study, the Participating TO shall rely, to the extent reasonably practicable, on existing transmission planning studies. The applicant will not be assessed a charge for such existing studies; however, the applicant will be responsible for the reasonable charges associated with any modifications to existing planning studies that are reasonably necessary to evaluate the impact on the applicant’s request.

10.6
System Impact Study Procedures. Upon receipt of an executed System Impact Study Agreement or initiation of the CAISO ADR Procedures and receipt of payment for estimated study costs, the Participating TO will use due diligence to either (a) complete the required System Impact Study within a sixty (60) calendar day period or (b) complete its review of an applicant’s System Impact Study within thirty (30) calendar days of its receipt of the completed study. The System Impact Study will identify whether any Direct Assignment Facilities or Reliability Upgrades are necessary to deliver a New Facility’s full output over the CAISO Controlled Grid, or any transmission additions or upgrades are necessary to serve a wholesale load. The System Impact Study will also identify any adverse impact on Encumbrances existing as of the applicant’s Completed Application Date. In the event that the Participating TO is unable to complete the required System Impact Study within such time period, it shall so notify the applicant, in writing, and provide an estimated completion date along with an explanation of the reasons why additional time is required to complete the required studies. A copy of the completed System Impact Study and related work papers shall be made available to the applicant and the CAISO. The Participating TO shall notify the applicant and the CAISO immediately upon completion of the System Impact Study.

PDF page 42

28
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

10.6.1 Procedures Upon Completion of System Impact Study. Within fifteen
(15) Business Days of completion of the System Impact Study or review and approval of an applicant’s System Impact Study, the Participating TO shall notify the applicant whether the transmission system will be adequate to accommodate all of a request for Interconnection. If no costs are likely to be incurred for any Direct Assignment Facilities, any Reliability Upgrades, or implementing any operating procedures, then within thirty

(30) Business Days of receipt of written approval of the applicant’s System Impact Study from the Participating TO and the CAISO the

applicant shall request the Participating TO to tender an Interconnection Agreement within thirty (30) Business Days of such request. The

Participating TO shall tender to the applicant an Interconnection Agreement as provided in Section 10.9. If costs are likely to be incurred to accommodate a request for Interconnection, the Participating TO shall tender to the applicant a Facilities Study Agreement pursuant to Section 10.7.

10.7
Notice of Need for Facilities Study. If a System Impact Study indicates that additions or upgrades to the CAISO Controlled Grid are needed to satisfy an applicant’s request for interconnection, the Participating TO shall, within fifteen

(15) Business Days of the date of the System Impact Study or the completion of review and approval of the applicant’s System Impact Study by the Participating TO, tender to the applicant a Facilities Study Agreement that defines the scope, content, assumptions and terms of reference for such study to be completed by the Participating TO; the estimated time required to complete the required study; and such other provisions as the parties may reasonably require, and pursuant to which the applicant agrees to reimburse the Participating TO for the reasonable actual costs of performing the required Facilities Study. Alternatively, if the applicant will perform the Facilities Study, the Participating TO shall within fifteen (15) Business Days of the completion date of the System Impact Study or the completion of review and approval of the applicant’s System Impact Study, tender a Facilities Study Agreement that defines the scope, content, assumptions and terms of reference for such study to be reviewed by the Participating TO; the estimated time required to complete the required review; and such other provisions as the parties may reasonably require, and pursuant to which the applicant agrees to reimburse the Participating TO for the reasonable actual costs of reviewing the required Facilities Study. For a TSWL Interconnection request to remain a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, the applicant shall execute the Facilities Study Agreement and return it to the Participating TO within ten (10) Business Days together with payment for the reasonable estimated costs of performing the Facilities Study or reviewing the applicant’s Facilities Study. Alternatively, if the applicant requests the Participating TO to proceed with the Facilities Study or review thereof and commits to abide by terms, conditions, and cost assignments ultimately determined under the CAISO ADR Procedures, including any determination by FERC or appeal of a FERC determination in accordance with that process, the Participating TO shall

PDF page 43

29
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

promptly proceed with the Facilities Study provided that such request is accompanied by payment for the reasonable estimated cost of the Facilities Study, and the parties shall submit the disputed terms for resolution under the CAISO ADR Procedures. If the applicant elects not to execute a Facilities Study Agreement and does not request that the Participating TO proceed with the Facilities Study or review thereof, its application shall be deemed withdrawn and the applicant shall reimburse to the Participating TO all costs reasonably incurred in processing the application not covered by the System Impact Study Agreement.

10.8
Facilities Study Procedures. Upon receipt of an executed Facilities Study Agreement or initiation of the CAISO ADR Procedures and receipt of payment for the estimated study costs, the Participating TO will use due diligence to either

(a) complete the required Facilities Study within a sixty (60) calendar day period or (b) complete its review of an applicant’s Facilities Study within thirty (30) calendar days of its receipt of the Completed Study. In the event that Participating TO is unable to complete the required Facilities Study within such time period, it shall so notify the applicant, in writing, and provide an estimated completion date along with an explanation of the reasons why additional time is required to complete the required studies. A copy of the completed Facilities Study shall be made available to the applicant.

10.8.1 Tender of Interconnection Agreement. Within thirty (30) Business Days of receipt of the completed Facilities Study performed by the Participating TO or receipt of written approval of the applicant’s Facilities Study from the Participating TO, the applicant shall request the Participating TO to tender an Interconnection Agreement within thirty (30) Business Days of such request. The Participating TO shall tender to the applicant an Interconnection Agreement as provided in Section 10.9.

10.9
Interconnection Agreement. Pursuant to Section 10.3, 10.6.1, or 10.8.1, a party requesting TSWL Interconnection shall request in writing that the Participating TO tender to such party an Interconnection Agreement that will be filed with FERC, or the Local Regulatory Authority, in the case of a Local Publicly Owned Electric Utility. The Interconnection Agreement shall include, without limitation, cost responsibilities and payment provisions for any engineering, equipment, construction, ownership, operation and maintenance costs for any Direct Assignment Facilities, any Reliability Upgrades, and for any other mitigation measures. The Interconnection Agreement will set forth a payment schedule that enables the Participating TO to recover its costs.

For a TSWL Interconnection request to remain a Completed TSWL
Interconnection Application, the applicant requesting the TSWL Interconnection or the entity with whom the Participating TO will interconnect for the Interconnection shall execute the Interconnection Agreement and return it to the Participating TO within thirty (30) Business Days of receipt. Alternatively, if an applicant requesting the TSWL Interconnection or the entity with whom the

PDF page 44

30
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Participating TO will interconnect for the Interconnection requests the Participating TO to file an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement and commits to abide by the terms, conditions, and cost assignments determined to be just and reasonable under the CAISO ADR Procedures, including any determination by FERC or on appeal of a FERC determination in accordance with that process, the Participating TO shall promptly file an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement. Provided, however, that if the CAISO ADR Procedures concerns whether the requesting entity is eligible for a TSWL Interconnection, the Participating TO shall not be obligated to file an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement or commence construction of any facilities or incur other costs under the Interconnection Agreement until a final order determining the just and reasonable rates, terms, and conditions for such Interconnection Agreement has been issued by the applicable court or regulatory authority. If the applicant or the entity with whom the Participating TO will interconnect for the Interconnection elects not to execute the Interconnection Agreement and does not request the Participating TO to file an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement, its Completed TSWL Interconnection Application shall be deemed withdrawn, and the applicant shall reimburse to the Participating TO all costs reasonably incurred in processing the application not covered by any System Impact Study Agreement or Facilities Study Agreement.

Where a TSWL Interconnection will serve one or more Large Loads, as a condition to the Participating TO’s design, procurement, construction, or operation of any Large Load-Driven Upgrades (as defined in the pro forma Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement set forth in Appendix V) associated with the TSWL Interconnection, the applicant or Eligible Customer shall also, within the same period and subject to the same consequences as the Interconnection Agreement, execute and maintain a Cost Recovery Agreement substantially in the form set forth in Appendix V to this TO Tariff, or, if the applicant or Eligible Customer requests that the Cost Recovery Agreement be filed unexecuted with FERC, comply with that agreement as made effective by FERC, in each case as applicable to the requested TSWL Interconnection. Disputes arising under a Cost Recovery Agreement shall be resolved under the dispute resolution provisions of that agreement.

10.10 Partial Interim Service. If the Participating TO determines that there will not be adequate transmission capability to satisfy the full amount requested in a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, the Participating TO nonetheless shall be obligated to offer and provide the portion of the requested TSWL Interconnection that can be accommodated without any additional Direct Assignment Facilities or Reliability Upgrades. However, the Participating TO shall not be obligated to provide the incremental amount of requested TSWL Interconnection that requires such additional facilities or upgrades until such facilities or upgrades have been placed in service.

10.11 Expedited Interconnection Procedures. In lieu of the procedures set forth

PDF page 45

31
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

above, the applicant shall have the option to expedite the processing of its Completed TSWL Interconnection Application. In order to exercise this option, the applicant shall submit in writing a Request for Expedited Interconnection Procedures to the Participating TO within ten (10) Business Days after receiving a copy of the System Impact Study for the proposed TSWL Interconnection. Within ten (10) Business Days after receiving a Request for Expedited Interconnection Procedures, the Participating TO shall tender an Expedited Interconnection Agreement that requires the applicant to compensate the Participating TO for all costs reasonably incurred pursuant to the terms of this TO Tariff for processing the Completed TSWL Interconnection Application and providing the requested TSWL Interconnection. While the Participating TO agrees to provide the applicant with its best estimate of the costs of any needed Direct Assignment Facilities and, if applicable, Reliability Upgrades, and such other charges that may be incurred, unless otherwise agreed by the parties, such estimate shall not be binding and the applicant must agree in writing to compensate the Participating TO for all actual TSWL Interconnection costs reasonably incurred pursuant to the provisions of this TO Tariff. The applicant shall execute and return such Expedited Interconnection Agreement within ten (10) Business Days of its receipt or the applicant’s request for TSWL Interconnection will cease to be a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application and will be deemed terminated and withdrawn. In that event, the applicant shall reimburse the Participating TO for all costs reasonably incurred in processing the application not covered by the terms of the System Impact Study Agreement.

10A. Interconnection Process for ECLL Interconnections. All requests for ECLL Interconnection shall be directed to the Participating TO.

10A.1 Applicability. An ECLL Interconnection must be requested in order to connect the facilities of the Participating TO to the facilities of an ECLL that has received a request to connect and provide retail transmission service to a Large Load Customer.

10A.2 ECLL Application and Application Fee

10A.2.1
Application Contents. An ECLL requesting an ECLL
Interconnection shall submit an ECLL Interconnection Application in the form of Attachment 1 to Appendix VI, which provides the following required information, and shall send a copy of the application to the CAISO. The ECLL Interconnection Application and all supporting

documentation shall be submitted electronically to the Participating TO in the manner posted on the Participating TO’s public website.

(a) the identity, address, telephone number, and email of the ECLL;
(b) the Point of Withdrawal;
(c) a proposed Large Load Customer load ramp schedule identifying anticipated load additions over time and the Large Load

PDF page 46

32
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Customer’s maximum load;
(d) a site plan;
(e) documentation demonstrating that the Large Load Customer has Site Control;

(f) documentation demonstrating that the Large Load Customer has local permits and regulatory approvals for development of its Large Load;

(g) a single-line electrical diagram;
(h) the electrical location of the source of the power (if known) to be transmitted pursuant to the ECLL’s request for ECLL

Interconnection. If the source of the power is not known, a system purchase will be assumed;

(i) disclosure of ECLL interconnection requests in the ECLL’s footprint that are for the same Large Load Customer or any of its affiliate(s);

(j) a completed technical data form in the form of Appendix A to Attachment 1 to Appendix VI;

(k) if the Large Load Customer will be a Co-Located Large Load, information concerning the relevant Generating Facility must be provided by the ECLL. Such information may be either: (1) if the Generating Facility has not signed a generator interconnection agreement, the identifying queue number of the Generating Facility; or (2) if the Generating Facility has signed a generator interconnection agreement, information that will allow the Participating TO to identify such agreement; and

(l) the proposed date for energizing the ECLL Interconnection and the proposed term of the ECLL Interconnection service.

10A.2.2
Participating TO Review of ECLL Interconnection
Application. Within ten (10) Business Days after receipt of an ECLL Interconnection Application, the Participating TO shall determine whether the application is complete (“Completed ECLL Interconnection Application”). Wherever possible, the Participating TO will attempt to remedy deficiencies in the ECLL Interconnection Application through informal communications with the ECLL. If such efforts are unsuccessful, the Participating TO shall return the ECLL Interconnection Application to the ECLL.

The Participating TO will treat the information provided in the ECLL Interconnection Application, including the ECLL’s and Large Load Customer’s identity, as confidential at the request of the ECLL except to the extent that disclosure of this information is required by this TO Tariff or the CAISO Tariff, by regulatory or judicial order, for reliability purposes pursuant to Good Utility Practice, or pursuant to RTG or CAISO transmission information sharing agreements. The Participating TO shall treat this information consistent with the standards of conduct contained in

PDF page 47

33
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Part 37 of FERC’s regulations.

10A.3 Study Process and ECLL Interconnection Agreement. ECLL Interconnection Applications shall be processed, and ECLL Interconnection Agreements shall be tendered, executed, and filed, in accordance with the Large Load Interconnection Procedures set forth in Appendix VI. Disputes arising under a Cost Recovery Agreement shall be resolved under the dispute resolution provisions of that agreement.

10A.4 Interim Service

10A.4.1
Interim Firm Interconnection Service and Flexible Interim
Load Interconnection (FILI) Service. If the ECLL has requested Interim Firm Interconnection Service and/or FILI Service, the Participating TO shall perform an analysis and report the extent to which the requested interim services can be provided, prior to such time that all ECLL Interconnection Facilities are in service and the Large Load Customer is authorized to withdraw Energy. However, the Participating TO shall not be obligated to provide any amount of Firm Interconnection Service requested until necessary facilities have been placed in service. FILI Service will be available for all remaining Load that cannot receive Interim Firm Interconnection Service, if the Large Load Customer has the ability to reduce the load at a specified point of interconnection according to the timelines and protocols set forth in an executed and effective FILI Service Agreement, until the ECLL Interconnection Facilities are in service. As ECLL Interconnection Facilities are placed into service and transmission interconnection capability is increased, available quantities of Firm Interconnection Service and FILI Service will be updated and reflected in the applicable FILI Service Agreement.

If the Large Load Customer seeks FILI Service, the ECLL will ensure that the Large Load Customer signs a FILI Service Agreement developed by the Participating TO, or asks for the agreement to be filed unexecuted.

10A.4.2
Study Process for Interim Service and Determination of
Availability of Interim Firm Interconnection Service and FILI Service. The Participating TO shall perform a FILI Study in accordance with Section 4.6.2 of Appendix VI to determine the maximum amount of any Interim Firm Interconnection Service, if any, and FILI Service, if any, that may be provided based on the anticipated availability of transmission capacity once the Large Load Customer is permitted to withdraw energy by the ECLL. The Participating TO shall update this analysis under the terms of the FILI Service Agreement, at least annually.

The actual amount of any FILI Service to be provided during any operating period shall be determined by the Participating TO through

PDF page 48

34
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

operational assessments and analysis conducted closer to real-time operating conditions. Accordingly, the maximum FILI Service established in the annual analysis does not guarantee the availability of such service under actual system conditions.

10A.4.3
Conditions Related to FILI Service

10A.4.3.1
FILI Service Reliability Requirements. The FILI Service Agreement will identify what ECLL Interconnection Facilities must be installed, the necessary protection and control systems and any other equipment necessary to be installed, and operating limitations to allow the Large Load Customer to withdraw energy (“FILI Service Reliability Requirements”). FILI Service shall not commence until the Participating TO and ECLL have completed installation and commissioning of all equipment specified under the FILI Service Agreement.

10A.4.3.2
Curtailment of FILI Service. FILI Service is subject to curtailment at all times, unless the Participating TO’s right to curtail is expressly conditioned or otherwise limited in an executed and effective FILI Service Agreement. A Large Load Customer receiving FILI Service shall be subject to curtailment prior to any curtailment of customers receiving Firm Interconnection Service. The Participating TO and CAISO may curtail FILI Service as necessary to maintain the reliable operation of the CAISO Controlled Grid, including during normal operating conditions.

10A.4.3.3
Exceedance of Authorized FILI Service. An ECLL shall
ensure that the operating instructions issued by the Participating TO pursuant to a FILI Service Agreement are not exceeded at the relevant Point of Withdrawal. The Participating TO shall have the right to disconnect service to the Large Load Customer in the event that energy withdrawals violate the terms of the FILI Service Agreement. The Participating TO shall follow Good Utility Practice in carrying out any such disconnection and subsequent reconnection when the conditions for such reconnection set forth in the FILI Service Agreement have been met. Nothing in this TO Tariff prevents the CAISO from applying any financial penalties applicable to Large Load Customers.

10A.5 Co-Located Large Load. The interconnection processes applicable to a Co- Located Large Load shall be determined based on whether the Generating Facility is a (1) non-exporting Generating Facility interconnected in accordance with the generator interconnection rules of the applicable ECLL, as approved by its Local Regulatory Authority (including, for a UDC subject to the jurisdiction of the CPUC, CPUC Electric Rule 21); or (2) an exporting Generating Facility subject to

PDF page 49

35
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

the applicable CAISO generator interconnection procedures and requirements. The ECLL for a Co-Located Large Load connecting to facilities of an exporting Generating Facility will be subject to this Section 10A.

For a Co-Located Large Load interconnected to the facilities of an exporting Generating Facility, the CAISO will be a party to a pro forma agreement that bridges the pro forma Large Generator Interconnection Agreement (LGIA) with the ECLL Interconnection Agreement, or will negotiate a non-conforming LGIA to account for certain details of the Co-Located Large Load interconnection on an individual basis. The Generating Facility developer will also execute a CAISO pro forma Participating Generator Agreement for the Generating Facility, but not a Participating Load Agreement for the Co-Located Large Load.

10A.6 Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement. As a condition to the Transmission Owner’s design, procurement, construction, or operation of a Large Load-Driven Upgrade, the Eligible Customer shall execute and maintain a Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement substantially in the form set forth in Appendix V to this TO Tariff, as applicable to the requested service and associated Large Load Facility.

11. Uncontrollable Forces and Indemnification

11.1
Procedures to Follow If Uncontrollable Force Occurs. In the event of the occurrence of an Uncontrollable Force which prevents the Participating TO or a Market Participant from performing any of its obligations under this TO Tariff, the affected entity shall (i) if it is the Participating TO, immediately give notice to the Market Participants in writing of the occurrence of such Uncontrollable Force and, if it is a Market Participant, immediately give notice to the Participating TO of the occurrence of such Uncontrollable Force; (ii) not be entitled to suspend performance in any greater or longer duration than is required by the Uncontrollable Force, (iii) use its best efforts to mitigate the effects of such Uncontrollable Force, remedy its inability to perform, and resume full performance hereunder, (iv) in the case of the Participating TO, keep the Market Participants apprised of such efforts and, in the case of the Market Participants, keep the Participating TO apprised of mitigation efforts, in each case on a continual basis; and (v) provide written notice of the resumption of performance hereunder. Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, the settlement of any strike, lockout, or labor dispute constituting an Uncontrollable Force shall be within the sole discretion of the entity affected thereby, and the requirement that a Participating TO or Market Participant must use its best efforts to remedy the cause of the Uncontrollable Force and mitigate its effects and resume full performance hereunder shall not apply to strikes, lockouts, or labor disputes.  No Market Participant or Participating TO will be considered in default as to any obligation under this TO Tariff if prevented from fulfilling the obligation due to the occurrence of an Uncontrollable Force.

11.2
Indemnification.  A Market Participant shall at all times indemnify, defend, and

PDF page 50

36
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

save the Participating TO harmless from any and all damages, losses, claims, (including claims and actions relating to injury or to death of any person or damage to property), demands, suits, recoveries, costs and expenses, court costs, attorney fees, and all other obligations by or to third parties, arising out of or resulting from the Participating TO’s performance of its obligations under this TO Tariff on behalf of a Market Participant, except in cases of negligence or intentional wrongdoing by the Participating TO.

12. Regulatory Filings. Nothing contained herein shall be construed as affecting, in any way, the right of any electric utility (as defined by the Federal Power Act) Participating TO furnishing services in accordance with this TO Tariff, CAISO Tariff or any tariff and rate schedule which results from or incorporates this TO Tariff, unilaterally to make application to FERC as it deems necessary and appropriate to recover its Transmission Revenue Requirements, or for a change in its rates, including changes in rate methodology, or for a change in designation of transmission facilities to be placed under the CAISO’s control, in each case under Section 205 of the FPA and pursuant to the FERC’s Rules and Regulations promulgated thereunder. Nothing contained herein shall be construed as affecting in any way the ability of any Eligible Customer receiving services in accordance with this TO Tariff to exercise its rights under the Federal Power Act and pursuant to the FERC’s rules and regulations promulgated thereunder.

12.1
Open Access. For purposes of the Stranded Cost Recovery available under Order Nos. 888 and 888-A, this Tariff, combined with the CAISO Tariff and wholesale distribution access tariff, if any, shall be considered an open access tariff under FERC Order Nos. 888 and 888-A.

13. Creditworthiness
13.1
UDCs, MSSs, and Scheduling Coordinators Using the Participating TO’s Low Voltage Transmission Facilities. For the purpose of determining the ability of a UDC, MSS, and Scheduling Coordinator to meet its obligations related to using the Participating TO’s Low Voltage Transmission Facilities hereunder, where the Participating TO is collecting the Low Voltage Access Charge directly from each UDC, MSS and Scheduling Coordinator, the Participating TO may require reasonable credit review procedures for the UDC, MSS, or Scheduling Coordinator. This review shall be made in accordance with standard commercial practices. In addition, the Participating TO may require the UDC, MSS, or Scheduling Coordinator to provide and maintain in effect during the term of the service, an unconditional and irrevocable letter of credit as security to meet its responsibilities and obligations under this TO Tariff, or an alternative form of security proposed by the UDC, MSS, or Scheduling Coordinator and acceptable to the Participating TO, and consistent with commercial practices established by the Uniform Commercial Code, that protect the Participating TO against the risk of non-payment.

13.2
End-Users. Creditworthiness and security rules applicable to End-Users shall be pursuant to the then-current rules of the applicable Local

PDF page 51

37
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

Regulatory Authority and any agreement under this TO Tariff to which the End-User is a party.

14.    Disputes.  Except as limited below, as provided in Sections 10.9 and 10A.3, or as otherwise limited by law, the CAISO ADR Procedures shall apply to all disputes between parties which arise under this TO Tariff or under or in respect of the proposed terms and conditions of a Facilities Study Agreement, System Impact Study Agreement or Expedited Interconnection Agreement. The CAISO ADR Procedures set forth in Section 13 of the CAISO Tariff shall not apply to disputes as to whether rates and charges set forth in this TO Tariff (other than charges for studies) are just and reasonable under the FPA.

15. Miscellaneous.

15.1
Notices. Any notice, demand, or request in accordance with this TO Tariff, unless otherwise provided in this TO Tariff, shall be in writing and shall be deemed properly served, given, or made: (i) upon delivery if delivered in person or electronically via email, (ii) five days after deposit in the mail if sent by first class United States mail, postage prepaid, (iii) upon receipt of confirmation by return electronic facsimile if sent by facsimile, or (iv) upon Party at the address set forth in Appendix II.  Any Party may at any time, by notice to the other Parties, change the designation or address of the person specified in Appendix II to receive notice on its behalf.  Any notice of a routine character in connection with service under this TO Tariff or in connection with operation of facilities shall be given in such a manner as the Parties may determine from time to time, unless otherwise provided in this TO Tariff.

15.2
Waiver. Any waiver at any time by any Party of its rights with respect to any default under this TO Tariff, or with respect to any other matter arising in connection with this TO Tariff, shall not constitute or be deemed a waiver with respect to any subsequent default or other matter arising in connection with this TO Tariff. Any delay short of the statutory period of limitations in asserting or enforcing any right shall not constitute or be deemed a waiver.

15.3
Confidentiality.

15.3.1  Maintaining Confidentiality If Not for Public Disclosure. The Participating TO shall maintain the confidentiality of all of the documents, data, and information provided to it by any other Market Participant that such Market Participant may designate as confidential, provided, however, that the information will not be held confidential by the Participating TO if (1) the Participating TO or Market Participant is required to provide such information for public disclosure pursuant to this TO Tariff or applicable regulatory requirements, or (2) the

information becomes available to the public on a non-confidential basis (other than from the Participating TO).

PDF page 52

38
LS Power Grid TO Tariff

15.3.2  Disclosure of Confidential Information. Notwithstanding anything in this Section 15.3 to the contrary, if the Participating TO or any Market Participant is required by applicable laws or regulations, or in the course of administrative or judicial proceedings, to disclose information that is otherwise required to be maintained in confidence pursuant to this Section 15.3, the Participating TO or Market Participant may disclose such information; provided, however, that as soon as such Participating TO or Market Participant learns of the disclosure requirement and prior to making such disclosure, such Participating TO or Market Participant shall notify the affected Participating TO or Market Participants of the requirement and the terms thereof. The affected Participating TO or Market Participants may, at their sole discretion and own costs, direct any challenge to or defense against the disclosure requirement and the disclosing Participating TO or Market Participant shall cooperate with such affected Participating TO or Market Participants to the maximum extent practicable to minimize the disclosure of the information consistent with applicable law. The disclosing Participating TO or Market Participant shall cooperate with the affected Participating TO or Market Participants to obtain proprietary or confidential treatment of confidential information by the person to whom such information is disclosed prior to any such disclosure.

15.4
TO Tariff Supersedes Existing Tariffs. This TO Tariff, together with the CAISO Tariff and wholesale distribution access tariff if any, supersedes any pre- existing open access transmission tariff of the Participating TO.

15.5
Titles. The captions and headings in this TO Tariff are inserted solely to facilitate reference and shall have no bearing upon the interpretation of any of the rates, terms, and conditions of this TO Tariff.

15.6
Severability. If any term, covenant, or condition of this TO Tariff or the application or effect of any such term, covenant, or condition is held invalid as to any person, entity, or circumstance, or is determined to be unjust, unreasonable, unlawful, imprudent, or otherwise not in the public interest, by any court or government agency of competent jurisdiction, then such term, covenant, or condition shall remain in force and effect to the maximum extent permitted by law, and all other terms, covenants, and conditions of this TO Tariff and their application shall not be affected thereby but shall remain in force and effect. The Participating TO and Market Participants shall be relieved of their obligations only to the extent necessary to eliminate such regulatory or other determination, unless a court or governmental agency of competent jurisdiction holds that such provisions are not severable from all other provisions of this TO Tariff.

15.7
Preservation of Obligations. Upon termination of this TO Tariff, all unsatisfied obligations of any entity shall be preserved until satisfied.

15.8
Governing Law. This TO Tariff shall be interpreted, governed by, and construed under the laws of the State of California, without regard to the principles of conflict of laws thereof, or the laws of the United States, as applicable, as if executed and to be performed wholly within the State of California.

15.9
Appendices Incorporated. The several appendices to this TO Tariff, as may be revised from time to time, are attached to this TO Tariff and are incorporated by reference as if fully set forth herein.

15.10 Conflict With CAISO Tariff.  If a Market Participant identifies a conflict between the TO Tariff and the CAISO Tariff, the Participating TO and the Market Participant shall make good-faith efforts to resolve the conflict.  If the Participating TO and Market Participant are unable to informally resolve that conflict, the Participating TO and Market Participant may use the CAISO ADR Procedures to resolve it as set forth in Section 14 of this Tariff.

15.11 Conflicting Operating Instructions. In the event a Market Participant receives conflicting operating instructions from the CAISO and one or more Participating TO(s), if human safety would not knowingly be neither jeopardized nor electric facilities subject to damage while the Market Participant seeks to reconcile the conflict with the appropriate CAISO and Participating TO employees before acting, the Market Participant should attempt a reconciliation. Otherwise, the Market Participant shall adhere to CAISO Tariff Section 4.2 and follow the CAISO’s instructions. In no event shall a Market Participant be required to follow operating instructions from the CAISO if following those instructions would knowingly jeopardize human safety.

RECITALS
WHEREAS, LS Power Grid is a transmission-only company established to develop, own, and operate transmission facilities (“Transmission Facilities”) pursuant to the provisions of its Transmission Owner Tariff (“TO Tariff”) and the California Independent System Operator Corporation (“CAISO”) Open Access Transmission Tariff (“CAISO Tariff”);

WHEREAS, Eligible Customer is eligible to take transmission service under the CAISO Tariff and does or will take such service on behalf of the Large Load Customer, whether or not Eligible Customer owns or operates an electric distribution system;

WHEREAS, Eligible Customer and Transmission Owner are parties to, or are entering into, an Eligible Customer for Large Load (“ECLL”) Interconnection Agreement or Transmission System Wholesale Load (“TSWL”) Interconnection Agreement (each, an “Interconnection Agreement”) concerning the Large Load Facility;

WHEREAS, Large Load Customer owns, operates, or proposes to develop the Large Load Facility identified in Schedule 1;

WHEREAS, the Eligible Customer has submitted an ECLL Interconnection Application or TSWL Interconnection Application for the interconnection of the Large Load Facility as a new or incremental load;

WHEREAS, Eligible Customer intends to procure transmission service pursuant to the CAISO Tariff and its associated schedules on behalf of Large Load Customer; WHEREAS, in connection with the Large Load Facility, Transmission Owner must make additions and/or modifications to its transmission system, including the Large Load-Driven Upgrades identified in Schedule 1;

WHEREAS, CAISO may, under the CAISO Tariff or a Commission-accepted
methodology, formally assign to the Large Load Facility a specific share of the costs or revenue requirement of a regional transmission project, and the Parties intend this Agreement to address

PDF page 55

2

that assigned share without changing any allocation otherwise established under the CAISO Tariff;

WHEREAS, the applicable studies may identify impacts on transmission systems other than LS Power Grid’s transmission system and may identify Affected System Upgrades necessary to accommodate or mitigate such impacts;

WHEREAS, Eligible Customer is responsible for entering into and maintaining the arrangements, payment obligations, and financial security or credit support required by each applicable Affected System Owner to ensure that costs and financial risks associated with Affected System Upgrades are not shifted to customers that did not cause those costs; WHEREAS, Transmission Owner, Eligible Customer, and Distribution Utility have executed or expect to execute an engineering and procurement agreement and/or an Interconnection Agreement governing the design, construction, and interconnection of the Transmission Owner’s facilities to the Distribution Utility’s facilities, including the Network Upgrades, required to support service of the Large Load Facility;

WHEREAS, the Parties enter into this Agreement to establish the Eligible Customer’s wholesale revenue-support and security obligations associated with the Large Load Facility and the Large Load-Driven Upgrades, without establishing or prescribing the retail rates, charges, or recovery arrangements between Eligible Customer and Large Load Customer;

2. Definitions
Capitalized terms not defined herein have the meanings given in the CAISO Tariff, the LS Power Grid TO Tariff Formula Rate Implementation Protocols in Appendix IV of the TO Tariff (“Protocols”), or the relevant Interconnection Agreement.

2.1.
“Affected System” means an electric transmission system, other than LS Power Grid’s transmission system, that an applicable study determines may be materially affected by the interconnection or service of the Large Load Facility, including the transmission system of another Participating TO or a transmission system outside the CAISO Controlled Grid.

2.2.
“Affected System Arrangement” means an executed agreement or other binding arrangement entered into by Eligible Customer with an Affected System Owner that establishes the applicable study, engineering, design, procurement, construction, ownership, cost responsibility, cancellation-cost, revenue-support, payment, financial security or credit support, true-up, and other obligations associated with an Affected System Upgrade.

2.3.
“Affected System Owner” means the owner or operator responsible for an Affected System.

2.4.
“Affected System Upgrade” means an addition, modification, or upgrade to an Affected System that an applicable study determines is necessary to accommodate or mitigate an impact caused, in whole or in part, by the interconnection or service of the Large Load Facility.

2.5.

Before the Final MTRC Determination Date for a Covered Upgrade, the Annual MTRC is the Estimated Annual MTRC. On and after that date, it is the Final Annual MTRC fixed under Section 6.3. Each Annual MTRC is levelized over a twenty (20)-year MTRC Term beginning on the applicable Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date, as applicable. The Annual MTRC is not an input to, or calculation made under, LS Power Grid’s annual Formula Rate update. 2.6.

3. Effective Date, Term and Termination
3.1.
Effective Date. This Agreement shall become effective upon execution by the Parties, subject to acceptance by the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission (“FERC” or “Commission”) (if applicable), or, if filed unexecuted, upon the date specified by FERC (the “Effective Date”).

3.2.
Term. This Agreement shall remain in effect from the Effective Date until LS Power Grid issues a final Reconciliation Statement determining that the Total Required Revenue Commitment for every Covered Upgrade has been satisfied, transferred to a successor, prospectively reassigned, or otherwise discharged. The MTRC Term for each Covered Upgrade begins on its applicable Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date and ends on its applicable MTRC Term End Date. Earlier Break-Even remains available if the Total Required Revenue Commitment is satisfied before the applicable MTRC Term End Date. If LS Power Grid holds a Termination Fee Balance when the Total Required Revenue Commitment is otherwise discharged, this Agreement shall remain in effect for purposes of the Termination Fee Account

PDF page 63

10

provisions in Section 3.3.4 until the Termination Fee Balance has been fully credited or released.

3.3.
Termination. This Agreement may be terminated as follows:
3.3.1.
Termination Before the First Applicable In-Service Date. Before any Large Load-Driven Upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date and before any LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project reaches its Regional Project In-Service Date, this Agreement may be terminated upon thirty (30) Calendar Days’ written notice if: (a) the Large Load Service Commencement Date has not occurred by the Outside Date; (b) Eligible Customer or Large Load Customer cancels or abandons the Large Load Facility or associated request for service; (c) the applicable interconnection, access to the CAISO Controlled Grid, or other arrangement necessary for the Large Load Facility is terminated; (d) an Event of Default has occurred and is continuing, or LS Power Grid exercises a termination right arising from default under this Agreement or an applicable interconnection or transmission service agreement; or (e) Eligible Customer fails to timely execute, maintain, or provide the documentation, financial security, or credit support required under Sections 4.2, 6.3.6, or 7.1, and such failure continues for ten (10) Business Days after written notice from LS Power Grid or cannot reasonably be cured by the date on which LS Power Grid must commence or continue physical construction or incur a material noncancelable procurement or construction commitment. Any such termination shall be subject to Applicable Laws and Regulations and any required Commission authorization or acceptance and shall not release or limit the obligations described in Sections 3.3.2 and 3.3.3.

If any Large Load-Driven Upgrade has reached its Upgrade In-Service Date or any LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project has reached its Regional Project In-Service Date identified in Schedule 4, cancellation, abandonment, termination, or failure of the Large Load Facility to commence or continue service shall not terminate this Agreement. Section 3.3.4 and Articles 6 and 7 shall apply to each Large Load-Driven Upgrade and LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project for which the applicable in-service date has occurred. Section 6.3.6 shall govern any assigned share of a project owned by another entity.

3.3.2.
Pre-In-Service Termination Costs and Construction Security. If the Large Load Facility, its associated request for service, or any associated Large Load- Driven Upgrade is cancelled, abandoned, or otherwise terminated before an applicable Large Load-Driven Upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date, Eligible Customer shall be responsible for all actual and reasonable costs prudently incurred or irrevocably committed by LS Power Grid with respect to that Large Load-Driven Upgrade that have not otherwise been recovered by LS Power Grid (“Pre-In-Service Termination Costs”). Pre-In-Service Termination Costs include costs incurred before or after the applicable cancellation,

PDF page 64

11

abandonment, or termination to wind down, secure, remove, or otherwise address the unfinished Large Load-Driven Upgrade.

Pre-In-Service Termination Costs include, as applicable:
(a) engineering, design, study, permitting, environmental, siting, land and land- right, procurement, construction, testing, project management, legal, regulatory, and administrative costs;

(b) vendor and contractor cancellation charges, termination charges, change- order costs, restocking charges, demobilization costs, contract closeout costs, and third-party claims;

(c) the costs of securing, preserving, mothballing, relocating, redeploying, dismantling, removing, transporting, storing, selling, or disposing of equipment, materials, and partially completed facilities;

(d) site restoration, environmental remediation, and the restoration or modification of LS Power Grid’s existing facilities made necessary by the cancellation or abandonment;

(e) Allowance for Funds Used During Construction (“AFUDC”) properly accrued in accordance with the Commission’s Uniform System of Accounts through the date on which the applicable construction activities cease or the applicable project is determined to have been abandoned, excluding AFUDC associated with amounts of Construction Work in Progress included in rate base or otherwise recovered through rates; and

(f) any other costs reasonably incurred by LS Power Grid as a direct result of the cancellation, abandonment, suspension, or termination.

LS Power Grid may draw upon and apply Construction Security to Pre-In- Service Termination Costs as such costs are incurred or the associated obligations become fixed and payable. LS Power Grid may retain Construction Security until all Pre-In-Service Termination Costs are reasonably determinable and LS Power Grid has served on Eligible Customer a final statement reconciling Pre-In-Service Termination Costs, Construction Security applied, and the recoveries described in this Section, together with supporting information (the “Termination Cost Reconciliation”). Construction Security does not limit Eligible Customer’s underlying liability. Eligible Customer shall pay any deficiency within ten (10) Business Days after receipt of the Termination Cost Reconciliation. The Termination Cost Reconciliation shall become final in the same manner and within the same period as a Reconciliation Statement under Section 6.6, and LS Power Grid shall release any excess Construction Security after the Termination Cost Reconciliation becomes final, including through resolution of any dispute under Article 11. Construction Security applied under this Section does not constitute Applied Security Proceeds and does not produce a TRBAA Credit except to the extent provided in Section 3.3.3(d). Nothing in this Agreement limits LS Power Grid’s right to seek recovery of unpaid Pre-In- Service Termination Costs through its Formula Rate or otherwise as the Commission may authorize.

PDF page 65

12

LS Power Grid shall use commercially reasonable efforts to mitigate Pre-In- Service Termination Costs, provided that LS Power Grid shall not be required to take any action inconsistent with prudent utility practice, safety requirements, Applicable Laws and Regulations, existing contractual obligations, or the reliable operation of its transmission facilities. The Termination Cost Reconciliation shall reflect, when realized, applicable vendor refunds, insurance recoveries, net salvage proceeds, reuse or redeployment credits, and other recoveries attributable to the cancelled or abandoned scope, but shall not credit amounts representing LS Power Grid’s lawfully earned return or carrying costs except to the extent necessary to prevent duplicate recovery.

This Section applies separately to each Large Load-Driven Upgrade. If one or more Large Load-Driven Upgrades have reached their Upgrade In-Service Dates when the Large Load Facility or associated request for service is cancelled, this Section shall apply only to the Large Load-Driven Upgrades and portions of the project that have not reached their Upgrade In-Service Dates. The treatment of each Large Load-Driven Upgrade that has reached its Upgrade In-Service Date shall be governed by Section 3.3.4 and Articles 6 and 7.

3.3.3.
Rate Base and Formula Rate Treatment Before an Upgrade In-Service Date. To the extent costs associated with a cancelled or abandoned Large Load- Driven Upgrade that has not reached its Upgrade In-Service Date have been included in Construction Work in Progress or any other component of LS Power Grid’s rate base, LS Power Grid shall, consistent with the Commission’s Uniform System of Accounts, its Formula Rate and Protocols, and other Applicable Laws and Regulations:

(a) remove, reclassify, or otherwise exclude such costs from rate base to the extent required by applicable Commission accounting and ratemaking requirements;

(b) reflect that treatment in the next practicable Annual Update and corresponding true-up;

(c) cease accruing AFUDC to the extent and at the time required by the Commission’s accounting requirements; and

(d) make any adjustment or credit necessary to prevent LS Power Grid from recovering the same cost both through Construction Security or another payment by Eligible Customer and through its Formula Rate; provided that any such credit to transmission customers shall be recorded in the TRBA and flowed through to transmission customers through the TRBAA as provided in Section 6.8, and not through a revenue credit or other input to the Formula Rate.

Any depreciation, amortization, or other amount recovered through LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate that represents recovery of the same underlying capital or expense included in Pre-In-Service Termination Costs shall be reflected, without duplication, in the Termination Cost Reconciliation under Section 3.3.2. Application of Construction Security shall not require LS Power Grid to refund

PDF page 66

13

or surrender any return, carrying cost, tax allowance, or other amount lawfully recovered for a period during which LS Power Grid’s investment was properly included in rate base, except to the extent necessary to prevent duplicate recovery.

This Section 3.3.3 applies only to a Large Load-Driven Upgrade, or portion thereof, that has not reached its Upgrade In-Service Date.

3.3.4.
Effect of Cancellation After an Upgrade or Regional Project In-Service Date. After any Large Load-Driven Upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date or any LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project reaches its Regional Project In-Service Date specified in Schedule 4, Eligible Customer shall promptly notify LS Power Grid if Eligible Customer or Large Load Customer anticipates or becomes aware of the cancellation, abandonment, downsizing, curtailment, permanent cessation, or material reduction of service at the Large Load Facility.

Cancellation, abandonment, suspension, downsizing, termination, or failure of the Large Load Facility to commence or continue service shall not, by itself:

(a) terminate this Agreement;
(b) require the removal from rate base of a Large Load-Driven Upgrade or a LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project that has reached its in-service date;

(c) reduce or cap any Annual MTRC, Regional Project Allocated ARR, Total Required Revenue Commitment, or remaining Cumulative Revenue

Responsibility; or
(d) terminate, reduce, or excuse Eligible Customer’s obligation to pay the Supplemental MTRC and prior-period true-up adjustments under Section 6.6, maintain and replenish Operational Security, or satisfy the remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility.

Upon any effective termination of this Agreement, or upon the permanent cessation of service by the Large Load Facility following an applicable Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date, Eligible Customer shall pay LS Power Grid a one-time lump-sum termination fee equal to the Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, less any Supplemental MTRC that has been billed and remains unpaid as of the effective termination or cessation date (the “Termination Fee”). LS Power Grid shall calculate the Termination Fee separately for each Covered Upgrade that has reached its applicable in-service date from the most recently served Cumulative Revenue Ledger, updated through the effective termination or cessation date for then-known Qualifying Transmission Revenues, Supplemental MTRC Receipts, Applied Security Proceeds, billed and unpaid Supplemental MTRC, and any effective prospective reassignment. Billed and unpaid Supplemental MTRC excluded from the Termination Fee remains separately payable and secured; its later payment, satisfaction through Applied Security Proceeds, or direct recovery shall reduce

PDF page 67

14

the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility. The Termination Fee is due thirty (30) Calendar Days after LS Power Grid’s invoice. It is an accelerated contractual termination charge, not a penalty or consideration for future transmission service. Its receipt in full discharges only the remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility included in the fee. The Termination Fee is not a Supplemental MTRC Receipt, Applied Security Proceeds, prepayment for future transmission service, customer advance for construction, contribution in aid of construction, or reduction to rate base. LS Power Grid shall administer the Termination Fee only as provided in Section 3.3.4 and Article 6.

Each Large Load-Driven Upgrade that has reached its Upgrade In-Service Date and each LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project for which an Annual MTRC has been established shall remain subject to the applicable Formula Rate and Protocols for so long as it is properly includable in rate base under applicable Commission requirements. The associated payment, Security, reconciliation, and TRBAA Credit obligations shall continue in accordance with Articles 6 and 7, subject to any prospective reassignment or adjustment authorized under the CAISO Tariff or a Commission-accepted methodology, or transfer to a Successor Eligible Customer under Section 6.10.

LS Power Grid shall hold each Termination Fee, and any proceeds of Operational Security applied to its payment, in a separately identified, interest bearing Termination Fee Account for the applicable Covered Upgrade. The amounts in that account, together with actual earnings and less amounts credited or released, constitute the “Termination Fee Balance.” For each Covered Upgrade, the Annual Termination Fee Credit for each Rate Year remaining in the MTRC Term shall equal the applicable Annual MTRC, prorated for any partial Rate Year, but shall not exceed the then-available Termination Fee Balance. In the final Rate Year of the MTRC Term, the Annual Termination Fee Credit shall include the entire remaining Termination Fee Balance, including actual earnings, so that the Termination Fee Account is reduced to zero, subject to any amount required to be released under this Section 3.3.4. LS Power Grid shall include each Annual Termination Fee Credit in the TRBAA only for the Rate Year for which it is scheduled. The Termination Fee Balance shall be separately accounted for as a deferred liability pending its scheduled annual TRBAA treatment, shall not be included in rate base, and shall not itself be a TRBAA Credit or Formula Rate input. If a prospective reassignment becomes effective after receipt of a Termination Fee, LS Power Grid shall release the portion of the Termination Fee Balance attributable to the reassigned responsibility, together with actual earnings, and prospectively adjust the remaining Annual Termination Fee Credits.

If the Commission subsequently requires LS Power Grid to retire, remove, reclassify, or exclude all or a portion of an in-service Large Load-Driven Upgrade or LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project from rate base, LS Power Grid shall make corresponding prospective adjustments to the applicable Annual MTRC, Regional Project Allocated ARR, Total Required

PDF page 68

15

Revenue Commitment, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Supplemental MTRC, and Operational Security. Such adjustments shall prevent Eligible Customer from remaining responsible under this Agreement for costs that are no longer included in LS Power Grid’s transmission revenue requirement, without requiring a refund or reopening of amounts properly determined for a prior Rate Year.

If, at the time of cancellation or abandonment, one or more Large Load-Driven Upgrades have reached their Upgrade In-Service Dates or a LS Power Grid- owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project has reached its project in-service date, and one or more other upgrades or project shares have not, Sections 3.3.2 and 3.3.3 shall apply to Large Load-Driven Upgrades and project scope that have not reached their applicable in-service dates; this Section 3.3.4 and Articles 6 and 7 shall apply to the upgrades and LS Power Grid-owned project shares that have reached their applicable in-service dates; and Section 6.3.6 shall continue to govern any assigned share of a project owned by another entity.

3.3.5.
Mutual Termination. The Parties may terminate this Agreement by mutual written consent only after addressing all incurred construction costs, the continuing Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Supplemental MTRC amounts, prior-period true-up adjustments, and Security. No termination is effective to extinguish such obligations unless expressly accepted or approved by FERC. 3.4.

Regulatory Effect of Termination. No termination shall become effective until the Parties comply with all Applicable Laws and Regulations, including any required notice to or acceptance by FERC. A regulatory rejection, cancellation, downsizing, curtailment, cessation, Force Majeure event, or termination shall not excuse incurred construction costs or the obligations arising on or after an applicable Upgrade In- Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date unless FERC expressly determines otherwise.

3.5.
Effect of Termination. Termination does not release Eligible Customer from obligations arising under Articles 4, 6, or 7, including future annual Supplemental MTRC payments, prior-period true-up adjustments, and Security maintenance and replenishment, until the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility is finally satisfied or transferred in accordance with this Agreement. Upon LS Power Grid’s receipt of the Termination Fee in full, no further Supplemental MTRC shall accrue for any period after the effective termination or cessation date, subject to final true-ups and reconciliation for periods before that date. Security does not limit the underlying liability. The Large Load Customer’s information, notice, authorization, and cooperation obligations stated in its Limited Acknowledgment also survive.

4. Transmission Project
4.1.
Design, Procurement, and Construction. Subject to Eligible Customer’s execution of this Agreement and the relevant Interconnection Agreement, delivery of Construction Security under Section 7.1, and satisfaction of Section 4.2, LS Power

PDF page 69

16

Grid shall design, procure, construct, install, and own the Large Load-Driven Upgrades described in the relevant Interconnection Agreement and Schedule 1, using reasonable efforts to meet the applicable construction schedule and expected Upgrade In-Service Dates.

Eligible Customer shall maintain Construction Security under Section 7.1 sufficient to secure LS Power Grid’s incurred and committed construction costs and the Pre-In- Service Termination Costs described in Section 3.3.2. Construction Security shall apply separately to each Large Load-Driven Upgrade until that upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date and the conditions for release or conversion under Section 7.2 have been satisfied.

If different Large Load-Driven Upgrades reach their Upgrade In-Service Dates on different dates, Construction Security shall continue to apply to each Large Load- Driven Upgrade and portion of the project that has not reached its Upgrade In-Service Date, notwithstanding the release or conversion of Construction Security attributable to an upgrade that has entered service.

Upon cancellation, abandonment, or termination, before an applicable Large Load- Driven Upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date, LS Power Grid may draw, retain, and apply Construction Security in accordance with Sections 3.3.2 and 7.1 and the relevant Interconnection Agreement. LS Power Grid shall not be required to take action inconsistent with its safety practices, design criteria, construction procedures, Applicable Laws and Regulations, or the reliable operation of its transmission facilities.

4.2.
Affected Systems
4.2.1. Identification and Coordination. Eligible Customer shall cooperate with LS Power Grid, CAISO, each affected Participating TO, and each other Affected System Owner in identifying and studying potential impacts of the interconnection or service of the Large Load Facility on any Affected System.

4.2.2. Required Affected System Arrangements. For each Affected System Upgrade identified through an applicable study, Eligible Customer shall timely enter into and maintain an Affected System Arrangement with the applicable Affected System Owner. Each Affected System Arrangement shall address, as applicable:

(a) study, engineering, design, procurement, construction, testing, and energization responsibilities;

(b) responsibility for the costs of the Affected System Upgrade;
(c) responsibility for cancellation, abandonment, demobilization, equipment removal, disposition, remediation, restoration, AFUDC, and other costs resulting from cancellation or failure of the Large Load Facility to proceed;

(d) construction-period and post-in-service payment obligations and financial security or credit support sufficient to protect the Affected System Owner and its other customers against cost shifting;

PDF page 70

17

(e) applicable invoicing, reconciliation, true-up, refund, and release procedures; and

(f) any Commission filing, acceptance, or other regulatory authorization required for the Affected System Arrangement.

4.2.3. Evidence Before LS Power Grid Construction. At least thirty (30) Calendar Days before LS Power Grid’s scheduled commencement of physical construction, and in all events before any notice to proceed for physical construction may occur or LS Power Grid incurs a material noncancelable procurement or construction commitment, Eligible Customer shall provide LS Power Grid:

(a) a complete copy of each executed Affected System Arrangement;
(b) evidence that all financial security or credit support required to be effective before construction under each Affected System Arrangement has been provided;

(c) evidence of any required Commission filing, acceptance, or other regulatory authorization; and

(d) a certification that each Affected System Arrangement remains in full force and effect and that Eligible Customer is not in default thereunder.

LS Power Grid may permit specifically identified preliminary engineering, procurement, or other limited activities to proceed before satisfaction of this Section by written notice. Such permission shall not waive any requirement of this Section or obligate LS Power Grid to commence or continue physical construction. 4.2.4. Continuing Obligation. Eligible Customer shall promptly provide LS Power Grid a copy of any material amendment, replacement, termination notice, default notice, demand upon financial security or credit support, or other material notice concerning an Affected System Arrangement. Eligible Customer shall maintain each required Affected System Arrangement and all financial security or credit support required thereunder until the obligations secured by that arrangement have been finally satisfied, transferred, or released.

4.2.5. Later-Identified Affected Systems. If an Affected System or Affected System Upgrade is identified after LS Power Grid has commenced work, Eligible Customer shall promptly enter into the required Affected System Arrangement and provide the documentation required by this Section. Subject to the applicable tariffs and agreements, LS Power Grid may suspend further work, procurement, energization, or service to the extent reasonably necessary to avoid additional exposure until the requirement is satisfied.

4.2.6. Failure to Satisfy Requirements. Eligible Customer’s failure to timely execute, maintain, or provide evidence of an Affected System Arrangement or required financial security or credit support constitutes a material default under this Agreement. Subject to Applicable Laws and Regulations and the applicable tariffs and agreements, LS Power Grid may: (a) decline to commence or continue construction or material procurement commitments; (b) suspend work, energization, or service; (c) exercise the termination right in Section 3.3.1; and (d) recover all

PDF page 71

18

resulting Pre-In-Service Termination Costs and other amounts payable under this Agreement.

4.2.7. Confidentiality. Documents provided under this Section shall be subject to the confidentiality protections in Section 6.12. Eligible Customer may redact commercially sensitive provisions unrelated to the requirements of this Section, but may not redact information reasonably necessary for LS Power Grid to verify the scope, effectiveness, cost responsibility, financial security or credit support, termination protection, or continuing enforceability of the Affected System Arrangement.

4.2.8. Separate Administration. Costs, revenues, payments, and financial security or credit support arising under an Affected System Arrangement shall be administered under that arrangement and the applicable Affected System Owner’s tariff. Such amounts shall not be included in any Annual MTRC, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Supplemental MTRC, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, or TRBAA Credit, except to the extent expressly authorized by the Commission and necessary to prevent duplicate recovery.

5. Regulatory Approval(s)
5.1.
FERC Filing. This Agreement is the form of agreement required under Sections 10.9 and 10A.6 of the TO Tariff as a condition of LS Power Grid’s design, procurement, and construction of Large Load-Driven Upgrades. Consistent with 18 C.F.R. § 35.1(g), a conforming, executed Agreement entered into under the Commission-accepted pro forma with populated Schedules generally shall not require an individual filing under Section 205 of the Federal Power Act. LS Power Grid shall file an unexecuted Agreement or an Agreement containing a material deviation, and any other agreement required to be filed by Commission order or rule. If FERC does not accept a required filing in its entirety, the Parties shall use reasonable efforts to amend and refile it; regulatory rejection does not excuse incurred construction costs or payment, Security, and true-up obligations for facilities placed in service, except to the extent FERC expressly determines otherwise.

6. Cost Recovery
6.1.
Cost Recovery and Minimum Contribution. LS Power Grid shall include the Large Load-Driven Upgrades in its Formula Rate to the extent the project costs are properly includable under the Formula Rate, the Protocols, and applicable law. This Agreement does not remove any Covered Upgrade from rate base or require a capital-cost reimbursement.
