The fixed twenty (20)-year Annual MTRC for each Covered Upgrade is instead calculated under the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5, initially using estimated cost and then using final incurred cost on the applicable Final MTRC Determination Date. LS Power Grid shall recover its formula-rate revenue requirement through TAC and shall flow TRBAA Credits through to transmission customers through the TRBAA as provided in Section 6.8. Amounts received under this Agreement are not inputs to the Formula Rate, and this Agreement does not require

PDF page 72

19

any modification of the Formula Rate. Eligible Customer shall pay a Supplemental MTRC only to the extent forecast or actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues are less than the applicable Annual MTRC. The Annual MTRC is a revenue floor, not an additive charge, and does not alter CAISO cost allocation or LS Power Grid plant accounting.

Eligible Customer acknowledges that LS Power Grid will incur costs and undertake planning, engineering, procurement, and construction in reliance on the Large Load Facility’s development and operation. Eligible Customer therefore accepts the wholesale payment and Security obligations in this Agreement. Any retail recovery from the Large Load Customer remains governed exclusively by applicable state law and Eligible Customer’s retail arrangements.

Affected System Upgrades and the associated costs, revenues, payments, and financial security or credit support are governed by Section 4.2 and the applicable Affected System Arrangement. Unless expressly authorized by the Commission, no Affected System Upgrade or associated revenue requirement shall be included in LS Power Grid’s Annual MTRC, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Supplemental MTRC, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, or TRBAA Credit.

6.2.
Qualifying Transmission Revenues. For each Rate Year or partial Rate Year, “Qualifying Transmission Revenues” means wholesale transmission revenues paid by or on behalf of Eligible Customer under the CAISO Tariff or another Commission- accepted arrangement that are attributable to service to the Large Load Facility and reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected from CAISO customers or directly credit LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement. Qualifying Transmission Revenues include: (a) the portion of TAC revenue attributable to the Large Load Facility and allocable to LS Power Grid under the reproducible methodology in Schedule 2; and (b) Wheeling Access Charges and other wholesale transmission-access, wheeling, or transmission-service charges paid to CAISO, a Participating TO, or a non-PTO transmission or load-serving entity for deliveries serving the Large Load Facility, but only to the extent they reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected through TAC or directly credit LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement and are not otherwise reflected as a credit in calculating the Annual MTRC, the Supplemental MTRC, or a TRBAA Credit. Qualifying Transmission Revenues do not include Supplemental MTRC Receipts, Applied Security Proceeds, retail charges, or amounts that do not reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected from CAISO customers or directly credit LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement.

6.3.
Annual MTRC and Allocation.
6.3.1. Upgrade-Specific MTRC Calculation. LS Power Grid shall calculate a separate Annual MTRC for each Covered Upgrade solely under the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5. Before the Final MTRC Determination Date, LS Power Grid shall calculate and update the Estimated Annual MTRC from the then-current estimated capital cost. Within one (1) year after the applicable Covered Upgrade enters service, when the actual capital cost of that Covered Upgrade is known and

PDF page 73

20

all costs associated with its estimated capital cost have been accounted for, LS Power Grid shall establish the Final Annual MTRC using final incurred capital cost and the Project-Specific Levelized Fixed Charge Rate calculated under Schedule 5. Schedule 5 shall calculate the Project-Specific Levelized Fixed Charge Rate by: (i) determining the present-worth revenue requirement using the applicable capital structure, cost of debt, return on equity, federal and state income-tax rates, income- tax conversion factor, and levelized-depreciation schedule; (ii) applying a depreciation rate of five percent (5%) of the applicable capital cost input in each year of the twenty (20)-year MTRC Term to calculate present-worth federal and state income-tax depreciation benefits; (iii) annualizing the resulting present-worth revenue requirement over the MTRC Term using the applicable after-tax discount rate and annualization factor; and (iv) adding the fixed expense allocator specified in Schedule 5. The Final Annual MTRC shall equal the applicable final incurred capital cost multiplied by the resulting Project-Specific Levelized Fixed Charge Rate. No component reflected in the Project-Specific Levelized Fixed Charge Rate or the fixed expense allocator shall be added again as a separate component of the Annual MTRC. The Final Annual MTRC and the Schedule 5 inputs used to calculate it are fixed as of the Final MTRC Determination Date and shall not be recalculated for later changes in actual cost, Formula Rate inputs, or other cost-of- service components, except to correct an error or fraud, to implement an effective prospective reassignment, to implement a final FERC order applicable to the calculation, or to reflect, prospectively, a change in (i) LS Power Grid’s return on equity component of its Formula Rate, resulting from a final FERC order accepting or approving such change, or (ii) applicable federal or state income tax rates or income tax law enacted after the Final MTRC Determination Date; provided that, the five percent (5%) annual levelized-depreciation schedule in Schedule 5 shall remain fixed for the MTRC Term unless a final FERC order requires otherwise. If LS Power Grid adjusts the Final Annual MTRC after the Final MTRC

Determination Date pursuant to the foregoing provision, LS Power Grid shall update the MTRC Calculation Template and serve it upon Eligible Customer in accordance with the notice provisions contained in this Agreement. Upon such service to Eligible Customer, the updated MTRC Calculation Template shall replace the existing MTRC Calculation Template in its entirety and become effective on the date of such service. The foregoing effectiveness of an updated MTRC Calculation Template does not preclude Eligible Customer from disputing any change to the MTRC Calculation Template pursuant to Article 11 of this Agreement. Any resulting adjustment to the Total Required Revenue Commitment shall apply only to the Rate Years remaining in the MTRC Term as provided in Section 6.7. Each Annual MTRC shall apply for its separate twenty (20)-year MTRC Term, with the initial and final partial Rate Years prorated based on the number of monthly Billing Periods in the applicable Rate Year during which the Covered Upgrade is in service. Schedule 1 shall identify the initial Estimated Annual MTRC and initial projected Total Required Revenue Commitment for each Large Load-Driven Upgrade, and Schedule 5 shall record the Final MTRC Determination Date, Final Annual MTRC, Total Required Revenue Commitment, and the completed

PDF page 74

21

calculation for each Covered Upgrade. Schedule 4 shall provide the corresponding project-specific information for a LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project, if applicable.

6.3.2. Initial Allocation. Schedule 1 shall identify, separately for each Large Load- Driven Upgrade, the percentage of the upgrade initially allocated to the Large Load Customer under this Agreement (the “Initial Allocation Percentage”). The Initial Allocation Percentage shall be determined using the applicable final study and allocation methodology and shall account for:

(a) Large Load Customers studied individually, jointly, or in a cluster;
(b) the extent to which two or more Large Load Customers caused the need for or share a Large Load-Driven Upgrade;

(c) differing requested service levels, development phases, and facility configurations; and

(d) any portion of the facility determined at the outset to serve other transmission system needs or customers.

The aggregate percentages allocated to all Large Load Customers for a Large Load- Driven Upgrade shall not exceed one hundred percent (100%). Any portion not allocated to a Large Load Customer shall be excluded from the Annual MTRC allocable to any Large Load Customer and remain subject to the otherwise- applicable CAISO cost allocation and rate recovery requirements. 6.3.3. Applicable Allocation Percentage. For each Large Load-Driven Upgrade, the “Applicable Allocation Percentage” shall equal the Initial Allocation Percentage identified in Schedule 1, as adjusted prospectively by any reassignment effective under Section 6.9 or as otherwise required by a final Commission order or applicable tariff provision. A cancellation, abandonment, downsizing, curtailment, permanent cessation, or material reduction of service by the Large Load Customer shall not, by itself, reduce the Applicable Allocation Percentage. 6.3.4. Annual MTRC Allocation. The Annual MTRC applicable to Eligible Customer shall equal the Annual MTRC for the Covered Upgrade as a whole multiplied by the Applicable Allocation Percentage; provided that, where Schedule 5 applies that percentage to capital cost or otherwise calculates the Annual MTRC for an already allocated project share, the resulting Annual MTRC is already allocated and the percentage shall not be applied again. LS Power Grid shall maintain the calculation separately by Large Load Customer, Large Load Facility, and Covered Upgrade. The aggregate Annual MTRCs allocated to all large load customers shall not exceed the Annual MTRC for the Covered Upgrade as a whole.

6.3.5. CAISO-Assigned Regional Projects Owned by LS Power Grid. For each CAISO-Assigned Regional Project owned by LS Power Grid and included in its Formula Rate, LS Power Grid shall calculate separately the Regional Project Allocated ARR attributable to the specific share formally assigned to the Large Load Facility. The Regional Project Allocated ARR shall be calculated solely under

PDF page 75

22

the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5, using LS Power Grid’s allocated share of the project’s estimated capital cost before the Final MTRC Determination Date and its allocated share of final incurred capital cost on that date, and shall be established, updated, and fixed in the same manner and on the same schedule that Section 6.3.1 provides for other Covered Upgrades. The resulting Annual MTRC shall be included in the Supplemental MTRC calculation and the Total Required Revenue Commitment beginning on the Regional Project In-Service Date. The allocated share shall be determined under the applicable allocation under the CAISO Tariff or Commission-accepted methodology and the project-specific information in Schedule 4. No portion assigned to other customers, other facilities, or broader regional needs shall be included. Before the Regional Project In-Service Date, any costs of a cancelled or abandoned project shall be treated under the applicable CAISO Tariff, Commission order, and any abandoned-plant incentive specifically authorized for that project. This Agreement does not create a separate pre-service payment or Construction Security obligation solely because CAISO has assigned a regional project share to Large Load Facility. Any pre-service payment, contribution, or security required by the CAISO Tariff, an applicable Interconnection Agreement, or another Commission-accepted arrangement remains due under that authority and shall be identified in Schedule 4. Any abandoned-plant recovery, customer payment, or security proceeds shall receive the accounting and rate treatment required by the Commission, and any credit necessary to prevent duplicate recovery of the same costs shall be recorded in the TRBA and flowed through to transmission customers through the TRBAA as provided in Section 6.8. Any prospective adjustment or reassignment of the share assigned to the Large Load Facility shall occur only as provided by the CAISO Tariff or a Commission- accepted methodology. Rate base inclusion, depreciation, and other accounting for the underlying project share remain subject to the Formula Rate, the Protocols, and the Commission's Uniform System of Accounts; this Section 6.3.5 governs only the calculation of the Annual MTRC applicable to the Large Load Facility under this Agreement.

6.3.6. CAISO-Assigned Regional Projects Owned by Another Entity. If CAISO formally assigns a specific share of a CAISO-Assigned Regional Project to the Large Load Facility and that project is not owned by LS Power Grid or is not included in LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate, Eligible Customer shall satisfy the assigned share under the CAISO Tariff or enter into and maintain the required arrangement with the project owner or other responsible entity. The arrangement shall address, as applicable, payment of assigned costs and revenue responsibility, construction and cancellation obligations, security or credit support, invoicing, reconciliation, true-up, refund, release, and any required regulatory filing or acceptance. Eligible Customer shall provide LS Power Grid a copy of the executed arrangement or other evidence reasonably sufficient to demonstrate compliance, including the amount and basis of the assigned share, the billing party, payment terms, and required security.

PDF page 76

23

Eligible Customer shall provide the required arrangement or evidence by the deadline specified in Schedule 4, which shall be no later than the earliest applicable deadline under the CAISO Tariff or project-owner arrangement and, where the assignment is known before construction, before the project owner commences construction. If CAISO makes the assignment after construction has commenced or after the project enters service, Eligible Customer shall comply within the period specified by CAISO or the project owner and provide evidence to LS Power Grid promptly. Failure to comply is an Event of Default if not cured as provided in Section 7.11(c).

Amounts owed to another project owner are not included in any Annual MTRC, Regional Project Allocated ARR, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Supplemental MTRC, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, or TRBAA Credit unless the amount is included in LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate and Schedule 4 expressly identifies it as LS Power Grid’s assigned share. The applicable tariff or project-owner arrangement shall identify and apply any credits required to prevent duplicate recovery of the same assigned share through both a direct charge and a transmission rate. Payments to another owner satisfy only the corresponding obligation under that tariff or arrangement and do not reduce the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility owed to LS Power Grid unless they satisfy the same underlying obligation included in LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate. The Eligible Customer’s obligations under this Section survive termination of this Agreement and cessation of service until the assigned share has been paid or assumed by a successor and the obligation has been discharged, transferred, or released under the applicable CAISO Tariff or project-owner arrangement by the entity entitled to enforce it.

6.4.
Supplemental MTRC. Beginning on the in-service date of each Covered Upgrade, Eligible Customer shall pay the positive shortfall, if any, between the applicable Annual MTRC, prorated for a partial Rate Year based on the number of monthly Billing Periods in that Rate Year during which the Covered Upgrade is in service, and Qualifying Transmission Revenues attributable to the Large Load Facility during the corresponding Rate Year or partial Rate Year. Until the Final MTRC Determination Date, billing shall use the Estimated Annual MTRC. Upon initial finalization, the Final Annual MTRC shall apply back to the applicable in-service date, and estimated charges shall be reconciled in the next Reconciliation Statement under Section 6.6. Thereafter, the calculation shall use the Final Annual MTRC, subject to adjustments permitted by Section 6.3.1. Qualifying Transmission Revenues attributable to a specific Covered Upgrade shall be applied to that upgrade. Qualifying Transmission Revenues not attributable to a specific Covered Upgrade shall be allocated among the Covered Upgrades then in their MTRC Terms pro rata based on their respective prorated Annual MTRCs for the applicable period.

The calculation may be expressed as:
Supplemental MTRC = max (applicable Annual MTRC − Qualifying Transmission Revenues, $0).

PDF page 77

24

The Annual MTRC is not an annual true-up of project cost or Formula Rate inputs. Annual reconciliation is limited to Qualifying Transmission Revenues, Supplemental MTRC invoices and receipts, Applied Security Proceeds, TRBAA Credits, partial-year proration, effective prospective reassignment, and correction of error, fraud, a final FERC order applicable to the calculation, and any prospective recalculation of the Final Annual MTRC permitted by Section 6.3.1.

6.5.
Invoice. With each Annual Projection, LS Power Grid shall forecast Qualifying Transmission Revenues and calculate the projected Supplemental MTRC using the applicable fixed or estimated Annual MTRC from Schedule 5, increased or reduced by any prior-period true-up adjustment under Section 6.6. LS Power Grid shall invoice the forecasted Supplemental MTRC for the applicable Rate Year or partial Rate Year in equal monthly installments for each Billing Period in that Rate Year during which the Covered Upgrade is in service; for a full Rate Year, the projected Supplemental MTRC shall be invoiced in twelve (12) equal monthly installments. Each installment is payable within thirty (30) Calendar Days after receipt of the invoice. If a Covered Upgrade reaches its applicable in-service date during a Rate Year for which the Annual Projection did not include a projected Supplemental MTRC for that Covered Upgrade, LS Power Grid shall promptly forecast Qualifying Transmission Revenues and invoice the prorated Supplemental MTRC for the remaining Billing Periods of that Rate Year. LS Power Grid shall include the projected Supplemental MTRC, including any prior-period true-up adjustment, as a Forecast TRBAA Credit in the next annual TRBAA under Section 6.8. Only cash actually received by LS Power Grid, or Applied Security Proceeds actually applied by LS Power Grid, constitutes an actual receipt for purposes of Sections 6.6 through 6.8. Concurrently with each Annual Projection, LS Power Grid shall provide Eligible Customer upgrade-specific workpapers showing the applicable Annual MTRC, forecast Qualifying Transmission Revenues, projected Supplemental MTRC, any prior-period true-up adjustment, Forecast TRBAA Credit, Total Required Revenue Commitment, and Required Operational Security. 6.6.

Annual Reconciliation Statement. In connection with each Annual Update, LS Power Grid shall determine the actual Supplemental MTRC obligation using actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues and the applicable fixed or estimated Annual MTRC from Schedule 5. LS Power Grid shall identify, by Eligible Customer and Covered Upgrade, the opening unpaid billed Supplemental MTRC balance, Supplemental MTRC invoiced during the Rate Year, cash received by LS Power Grid, Applied Security Proceeds applied to identified obligations, direct recoveries, closing unpaid billed balance, and each Annual Termination Fee Credit. Concurrently with each Annual Update, LS Power Grid shall serve on Eligible Customer a Reconciliation Statement containing those determinations, the updated Cumulative Revenue Ledger, Required Operational Security recalculated under Section 7.4, a reconciliation of the Forecast TRBAA Credit to actual amounts recorded in the TRBA, and supporting workpapers. An unpaid Supplemental MTRC remains due from Eligible Customer and, when later received or satisfied through Applied Security Proceeds, is recorded in the TRBA only in the Rate Year of receipt or application. The Reconciliation

PDF page 78

25

Statement shall not reopen the Final Annual MTRC or Total Required Revenue Commitment except as expressly allowed by Section 6.3.1.

The difference between the Supplemental MTRC billed for a Rate Year, excluding any prior-period true-up adjustment included in that billing, and the actual Supplemental MTRC determined in the Reconciliation Statement for that Rate Year shall constitute a “prior-period true-up adjustment,” which shall increase (if under-billed) or reduce (if over-billed) the projected Supplemental MTRC invoiced under Section 6.5 following the next Annual Projection. If no further Supplemental MTRC will be invoiced under this Agreement, LS Power Grid shall invoice any under-billed amount, payable within thirty (30) Calendar Days after receipt of the invoice, and shall refund any over-billed amount to Eligible Customer within thirty (30) Calendar Days after service of the Reconciliation Statement. No interest shall accrue on the Supplemental MTRC or on any prior-period true-up adjustment, invoice, refund, or other adjustment of the Supplemental MTRC between LS Power Grid and Eligible Customer. Any refund to Eligible Customer of an amount previously recorded as a TRBAA Credit shall be reversed in the TRBA when paid, and any under-billed amount later received shall be recorded in the TRBA when received, in each case as provided in Section 6.8. Any amount returned by LS Power Grid to transmission customers through the TRBAA shall include interest as provided in Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff.

A Reconciliation Statement shall become final on the earlier of (a) Eligible Customer’s written acceptance of the Reconciliation Statement or (b) ninety (90) Calendar Days after service, unless before that date Eligible Customer serves a Notice of Dispute under Section 11.1 identifying with reasonable specificity the items disputed. If a Notice of Dispute is timely served, the undisputed portions of the Reconciliation Statement shall become final on the date that would otherwise apply, and the disputed items shall become final upon the applicable Dispute Determination under Section 11.5. A final Reconciliation Statement may be revised only to implement a Dispute Determination, to reflect CAISO resettlements or other corrected data as provided in Schedule 2, or to correct errors or fraud. Finality of a Reconciliation Statement under this Agreement does not affect the Commission’s review and approval of any TRBAA.

For purposes of this Agreement, Supplemental MTRC Receipts exclude Applied Security Proceeds. Applied Security Proceeds shall be counted only when LS Power Grid applies such proceeds to satisfy an identified payment obligation and shall reduce that obligation dollar-for-dollar. The posting, maintenance, replenishment, drawing, or holding of Security shall not, by itself, constitute a Supplemental MTRC Receipt, an Applied Security Proceed, satisfaction of the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, or a TRBAA Credit.

No amount shall be counted both as a Supplemental MTRC Receipt and as an Applied Security Proceed. If LS Power Grid receives payment of an obligation previously satisfied through Applied Security Proceeds, LS Power Grid shall not count the subsequent payment toward the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility or a TRBAA Credit and shall instead return the duplicative amount, release an equivalent amount of

PDF page 79

26

Security, or, with supporting notice to Eligible Customer, apply the amount to another then-outstanding obligation under this Agreement.

6.7.
Cumulative Revenue Responsibility and Break-Even. LS Power Grid shall maintain a Cumulative Revenue Ledger for each Covered Upgrade and shall update it at least annually in connection with the Annual Update. LS Power Grid shall include an updated Cumulative Revenue Ledger statement, with supporting workpapers, in each Reconciliation Statement served on Eligible Customer under Section 6.6. For each Covered Upgrade, the Total Required Revenue Commitment equals the Final Annual MTRC multiplied by twenty (20), adjusted only for partial-year proration and any prospective recalculation of the Final Annual MTRC permitted by Section 6.3.1, which shall apply only to the Rate Years remaining in the MTRC Term after the recalculation takes effect. Until the Final MTRC Determination Date, the projected Total Required Revenue Commitment shall equal the Estimated Annual MTRC multiplied by twenty (20).

Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility equals the applicable Total Required Revenue Commitment less:

(a) cumulative Qualifying Transmission Revenues;
(b) cumulative Supplemental MTRC Receipts;
(c) cumulative Applied Security Proceeds;
(d) effective prospective reassignment credits; and
(e) cash amounts received and applied toward the Termination Fee, excluding any portion satisfied through Applied Security Proceeds already counted under clause (c).

Each dollar shall be entered in the Cumulative Revenue Ledger only once. Supplemental MTRC Receipts shall exclude Applied Security Proceeds. Applied Security Proceeds shall be entered only when applied to an identified Supplemental MTRC, prior-period true-up adjustment, Termination Fee, or other payment obligation under Article 6 or Section 3.3.4. Security that is posted, drawn but not applied, held as a deferred liability, replenished, replaced, or returned shall not reduce the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility. Annual Termination Fee Credits and the corresponding Termination Fee Balance shall be separately identified in the Reconciliation Statement and in the workpapers required under Section 6.8 and shall not again reduce the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility.

If an amount previously credited in the Cumulative Revenue Ledger is subsequently refunded, reversed, disallowed, or otherwise ceases to satisfy the applicable obligation, LS Power Grid shall make a corresponding adjustment to the ledger, subject to the finality and no-revival provisions of this Section.

Cancellation, downsizing, curtailment, or departure does not extinguish the remaining responsibility. When a final Reconciliation Statement determines that the responsibility has reached zero (“Break-Even”), LS Power Grid shall terminate the operational payment and Security obligations and release any excess Security. Once

PDF page 80

27

finally satisfied, the responsibility shall not revive because of a later change in ROE, expense, load, forecast, or other Formula Rate input, subject only to final true-ups and correction of errors or fraud for periods before satisfaction.

6.8.
TRBAA Credit and No Double Recovery. Supplemental MTRC Receipts, Applied Security Proceeds other than Applied Security Proceeds applied to a Termination Fee, Annual Termination Fee Credits, and any duplicate-recovery credit under Section 3.3.3(d) or Section 6.3.5 shall be credited to transmission customers solely through the TRBAA and shall not be recorded as revenue credits in, or otherwise included as inputs to, the Formula Rate. In each annual TRBAA, LS Power Grid shall include as the Forecast TRBAA Credit: (a) the projected Supplemental MTRC for the following Rate Year, increased or reduced by any prior-period true-up adjustment invoiced under Section 6.5; and (b) for any Covered Upgrade with a Termination Fee Balance, the Annual Termination Fee Credit scheduled under Section 3.3.4 for the following Rate Year, prorated as applicable and limited to the available Termination Fee Balance. LS Power Grid shall record in the TRBA only Supplemental MTRC cash receipts, Applied Security Proceeds other than Applied Security Proceeds applied to a Termination Fee, and Annual Termination Fee Credits actually received, applied, or scheduled for the applicable Rate Year, respectively. Applied Security Proceeds applied to a Termination Fee shall be deposited into and held in the Termination Fee Account under Section 3.3.4 and shall be credited to transmission customers solely through the applicable Annual Termination Fee Credits. The difference between the Forecast TRBAA Credit and those actual amounts shall be reflected in the principal balance of the TRBA and flowed through to transmission customers in a subsequent TRBAA. A billed but unpaid Supplemental MTRC is not a TRBAA Credit. A later Supplemental MTRC Receipt or Applied Security Proceeds, other than Applied Security Proceeds applied to a Termination Fee, shall be recorded only in the Rate Year in which it is received or applied, respectively. Any amount previously recorded as a TRBAA Credit that is later refunded, reversed, or otherwise ceases to satisfy the applicable obligation shall be reversed in the TRBA when refunded or reversed. Interest on the TRBA balance shall be calculated as provided in Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff. Qualifying Transmission Revenues shall not be included again as a TRBAA Credit because they are already reflected in calculating the Supplemental MTRC. Each dollar shall be included in a TRBAA Credit only once. Applied Security Proceeds shall not also be treated as Supplemental MTRC Receipts, and the posting, drawing, holding, replenishment, replacement, or return of Security shall not independently produce a TRBAA Credit. A Forecast TRBAA Credit applies only to the forecast Rate Year and shall be reconciled through the TRBA to actual Supplemental MTRC Receipts, Applied Security Proceeds other than those applied to a Termination Fee, and Annual Termination Fee Credits, as applicable. LS Power Grid shall maintain workpapers reconciling each Forecast TRBAA Credit and each amount recorded in the TRBA under this Section 6.8 to the applicable Annual MTRC calculation in Schedule 5, forecast and actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues, opening unpaid billed balance, Supplemental MTRC invoiced, prior-period true-up adjustment, Supplemental MTRC cash receipt, Applied Security Proceed,

PDF page 81

28

direct recovery, closing unpaid billed balance, Termination Fee Balance, Annual Termination Fee Credit, resulting forecast-to-actual TRBA difference, Rate Year, Large Load Customer, and Covered Upgrade. LS Power Grid shall include with each TRBAA filing workpapers sufficient to permit transmission customers to verify the TRBAA Credits reflected in that TRBAA, which may present customer-specific information on an aggregated basis or be submitted subject to a request for privileged treatment under 18 C.F.R. § 388.112 or other appropriate confidentiality protections. 6.9.

Prospective Reassignment of Revenue Responsibility.
6.9.1. Eligibility for Reassignment of Revenue Responsibility. Whether or not the Large Load Customer remains in service, a portion of the prospective revenue responsibility associated with a Large Load-Driven Upgrade may be reassigned to a subsequent load customer if an objective transmission study conducted under the applicable tariff or study process determines that:

(a) the subsequent large load customer relies upon identifiable incremental capability created by the Large Load-Driven Upgrade; and

(b) that reliance avoids or reduces identifiable transmission upgrades that otherwise would have been required to provide the subsequent large load customer’s requested service.

Incidental power flows, generalized system benefits, or use of the transmission system that does not satisfy both requirements shall not support a reassignment. This Section 6.9 applies only to a load customer other than the Large Load Customer, at a facility other than the Large Load Facility. It does not apply to a transfer of ownership or operation of the Large Load Facility, a change in Eligible Customer, or an assignment of this Agreement, which are governed by Sections 6.10 and 10.1 and do not alter the Applicable Allocation Percentage or the Large Load-Driven Upgrades attributable to the Large Load Facility.

6.9.2. Reassignment Calculation. LS Power Grid shall determine the prospective reassignment separately for each Large Load-Driven Upgrade satisfying Section 6.9.1 (each, a “Shared Upgrade”). For each Shared Upgrade:

(a) the Demonstrated Avoided Upgrade Cost shall equal the reasonable estimated cost of the transmission upgrades that would have been required for the subsequent large load customer absent the Shared Upgrade, less the reasonable estimated cost of any additional transmission upgrades required when the capability of the Shared Upgrade is considered;

(b) the Remaining Allocated Net Plant shall equal the portion of the Shared Upgrade’s net plant balance included in rate base immediately before the reassignment that remains assigned to the Large Load Customer under this Agreement;

(c) the Capability-Supported Amount shall equal the portion of the Remaining Allocated Net Plant reasonably attributable to the incremental capability of

PDF page 82

29

the Shared Upgrade actually relied upon by the subsequent large load customer; and

(d) the Reassigned Investment Amount shall equal the least of:
(i) the Demonstrated Avoided Upgrade Cost;
(ii) the Capability-Supported Amount; and
(iii) the Remaining Allocated Net Plant.
The Demonstrated Avoided Upgrade Cost, Capability-Supported Amount, and Remaining Allocated Net Plant shall be determined using a consistent cost basis and price year. If Remaining Allocated Net Plant is greater than zero, the Reassignment Percentage shall equal the Reassigned Investment Amount divided by the Remaining Allocated Net Plant, bounded between zero and one hundred percent (100%). If Remaining Allocated Net Plant is zero or negative, no Reassignment Percentage shall be calculated under this methodology; any reassignment shall require an applicable tariff methodology or Commission determination addressing that circumstance.

6.9.3. Prospective Adjustment of Revenue Responsibility. Beginning on the Reassignment Effective Date, the applicable Annual MTRC assigned to the Large Load Customer shall be prospectively reduced by the Reassignment Percentage applied to the Shared Upgrade. LS Power Grid shall record a separate effective prospective reassignment credit in the Cumulative Revenue Ledger equal to the Reassignment Percentage multiplied by the Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility associated with the Shared Upgrade that remains assigned to the Large Load Customer immediately before the Reassignment Effective Date. That credit shall be the sole Cumulative Revenue Ledger adjustment for the reassigned responsibility. It shall not adjust the Total Required Revenue Commitment or result in a second reduction to the Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility or Required Operational Security for the same reassigned responsibility. LS Power Grid shall recalculate the Supplemental MTRC and Operational Security under this Agreement to reflect the reassignment. The Reassignment Effective Date shall be the latest of:

(a) the date the subsequent large load customer commences the service for which the Shared Upgrade provides the identified incremental capability;

(b) the effective date of a cost recovery agreement establishing the subsequent large load customer’s corresponding revenue responsibility;

(c) the date on which all Security required from the eligible customer serving the subsequent large load customer becomes effective; and

(d) the date on which the study and any other approvals required under the applicable tariff become final.

No reduction in the Large Load Customer’s Operational Security shall occur until the corresponding responsibility and required Security have become effective for the subsequent large load customer.

PDF page 83

30

6.9.4. Prospective Treatment and Prevention of Duplicate Reassignment. A reassignment under this Section shall:

(a) operate prospectively only and shall not produce a refund, credit, or reopening of amounts properly determined for a prior Rate Year;

(b) transfer prospective revenue responsibility only and shall not constitute a sale or transfer of transmission facilities, a transfer of plant ownership, a change in plant accounting, or a modification of any CAISO cost allocation;

(c) not exceed the prospective responsibility remaining under this Agreement for the Shared Upgrade; and

(d) not assign the same transmission capability, avoided upgrade cost, Annual MTRC, or Cumulative Revenue Responsibility to more than one large load customer.

LS Power Grid shall maintain an upgrade-specific reassignment ledger identifying the capability and responsibility assigned to each large load customer. Once responsibility has been effectively reassigned, that responsibility shall not revert to the Large Load Customer if the subsequent large load customer later terminates, reduces, or fails to commence service. The reassigned responsibility shall instead remain with the subsequent large load customer and the Eligible Customer responsible for its payment, subject to any further prospective reassignment permitted under the applicable agreement and tariff.

Any prospective reassignment, adjustment, or change in the cost share of a CAISO- Assigned Regional Project shall be recognized under this Agreement only to the extent authorized or required by the CAISO Tariff or a Commission-accepted methodology. LS Power Grid shall apply an effective change prospectively to the Regional Project Allocated ARR, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Supplemental MTRC, TRBAA Credit, and Operational Security, and shall update Schedule 4 and the applicable workpapers. The change shall not assign the same project share to more than one customer or alter costs allocated to other customers under the CAISO Tariff.

6.10. Change in Eligible Customer. Cost responsibility is assigned to the Large Load Customer and Large Load Facility, as paid by the Eligible Customer then taking wholesale transmission service on behalf of the Large Load Customer and Large Load Facility. If the Eligible Customer serving a Large Load Customer or Large Load Facility changes, the Successor Eligible Customer must execute an assumption agreement covering the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility and any outstanding obligations under Section 6.3.6, provide required Security, and become responsible on a date coordinated with CAISO billing before the prior Eligible Customer is released. Following the complete departure of the Large Load Customer, the last responsible Eligible Customer remains obligated unless responsibility is transferred under an applicable tariff or state-law mechanism, assumed by a successor, or prospectively reassigned as permitted under this Agreement and the CAISO Requirements. If ownership or operation of the Large Load Facility is transferred, Eligible Customer shall notify LS Power Grid within thirty (30) Calendar Days and shall cause the

PDF page 84

31

transferee to execute a Limited Acknowledgment in the form attached to this Agreement. The transfer shall not alter the Applicable Allocation Percentage, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, or Security obligations under this Agreement. A sale, lease, or other transfer of the Large Load Facility, or of the Large Load Customer’s interest in such Large Load Facility, is not a reassignment under Section 6.9 and does not require a study under Section 6.9.1. Cost responsibility shall continue to follow the Large Load Facility, the transferee shall execute a Limited Acknowledgment substantially in the form included in this Agreement, and Eligible Customer shall remain responsible for its obligations under this Agreement unless they are transferred to a Successor Eligible Customer under this Section 6.10. 6.11. CAISO Data and Settlement Coordination. Eligible Customer shall provide LS Power Grid, or authorize and direct its Scheduling Coordinator, Utility Distribution Company, Metering Coordinator, and other applicable entities to provide LS Power Grid, with the metering, settlement, billing, resettlement, allocation, and other data reasonably necessary to calculate and verify Qualifying Transmission Revenues and administer this Agreement. Eligible Customer shall execute any authorization or data- release documentation reasonably necessary for LS Power Grid to obtain such information.

To the extent authorized or required by the CAISO Tariff, CAISO may provide or make available relevant settlement, metering, billing, resettlement, and allocation data as provided under the CAISO Requirements. Nothing in this Agreement, however, imposes an obligation on CAISO or makes CAISO a party to this Agreement. LS Power Grid and Eligible Customer shall cooperate with CAISO and other applicable entities to establish and maintain the billing identifiers, metering points, data exchanges, and authorizations identified in Schedule 2.

LS Power Grid shall calculate Qualifying Transmission Revenues, the Annual MTRC, and the Supplemental MTRC; maintain the Cumulative Revenue Ledger; and serve on Eligible Customer the Reconciliation Statement, including the Cumulative Revenue Ledger statement, required by Sections 6.6 and 6.7.

If final data are not available when a calculation is required, LS Power Grid may use reasonable estimates based on the best information then available, subject to reconciliation under Section 6.6 when final or corrected data become available. 6.12. Information Rights and Confidentiality. Upon reasonable request, LS Power Grid shall provide Eligible Customer the workpapers, data, and other information reasonably necessary to verify the identification of Large Load-Driven Upgrades and CAISO-Assigned Regional Projects and all calculations of each Annual MTRC, Regional Project Allocated ARR, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Qualifying Transmission Revenues, Supplemental MTRC, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, TRBAA Credits, reassignment, and Security under this Agreement. Any dispute concerning those matters shall be resolved under Article 11. Large Load Customer and any Successor Eligible Customer may participate in a dispute under Article 11 to the

PDF page 85

32

extent the dispute affects its obligations under this Agreement or its Limited Acknowledgment. Customer-specific information shall be protected under Section 16.3 of the TO Tariff, the CAISO Tariff, and any applicable confidentiality agreement.

7. Security and Payment Obligations
7.1.
Construction Security. Before an applicable Large Load-Driven Upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date, Eligible Customer shall maintain Construction Security under the relevant agreement sufficient to secure:

(a) LS Power Grid’s incurred and irrevocably committed engineering, design, procurement, permitting, construction, testing, and related costs attributable to that upgrade; and

(b) the Pre-In-Service Termination Costs described in Section 3.3.2, including properly accrued AFUDC and applicable cancellation, demobilization, equipment removal, disposition, remediation, and restoration costs

(“Construction Security”).
Construction Security secures, but does not limit, Eligible Customer’s liability for those amounts. Construction Security provided under the relevant Interconnection Agreement or an engineering and procurement agreement for the same costs shall count toward, and shall not be duplicated by, the Construction Security required under this Agreement. Construction Security shall remain in effect for each Large Load- Driven Upgrade until that upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date and the applicable release or conversion conditions in Section 7.2 are satisfied. A Large Load-Driven Upgrade reaching its Upgrade In-Service Date shall not result in the release of Construction Security attributable to another Large Load-Driven Upgrade that has not reached its Upgrade In-Service Date. Eligible Customer shall replenish Construction Security within ten (10) Business Days after notice of a permitted draw and supporting information if additional secured obligations remain and the remaining Construction Security is less than the amount required under this Section.

For a CAISO-Assigned Regional Project owned by LS Power Grid, any pre-service payment, contribution, or security required from Eligible Customer for its formally assigned share shall be provided only as required by the CAISO Tariff, an applicable Interconnection Agreement, or another Commission-accepted arrangement, and shall be identified in Schedule 4. This Agreement does not independently require pre- service Construction Security solely because CAISO has assigned a share of a regional project to the Large Load Facility. The treatment of costs for a cancelled or abandoned regional project shall be governed by Section 6.3.5 and the applicable Commission order, CAISO Tariff, and any project-specific abandoned-plant incentive authorized by the Commission. For a project owned by another entity, construction and operational security shall be provided under the CAISO Tariff or the arrangement required by Section 6.3.6 and Schedule 4; it shall not be treated as LS Power Grid Operational

PDF page 86

33

Security unless included in LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate and expressly identified as LS Power Grid’s share.

7.2.
Delivery of Operational Security. No later than thirty (30) Calendar Days before the earliest expected Upgrade In-Service Date identified in Schedule 1 or Regional Project In-Service Date identified in Schedule 4 for a LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project, Eligible Customer shall deliver Operational Security calculated under this Article for the Large Load-Driven Upgrades and LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project expected to enter service. Operational Security must be fully effective before any applicable Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date.

If different Large Load-Driven Upgrades or LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Projects are expected to reach their applicable in-service dates on different dates, LS Power Grid shall recalculate the Required Operational Security before each subsequent in-service date to include the additional Cumulative Revenue Responsibility attributable to the upgrade or project share entering service. Eligible Customer shall provide any resulting increase no later than fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of a Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier. Inclusion of pre-service Construction Work in Progress in rate base shall not constitute an Upgrade In-Service Date or independently trigger the Operational Security requirement. Construction Security shall remain applicable to each Large Load-Driven Upgrade that has not reached its Upgrade In-Service Date.

No Construction Security attributable to a Large Load-Driven Upgrade shall be released or converted unless:

(a) the Required Operational Security attributable to that upgrade is effective;
(b) that upgrade has reached its Upgrade In-Service Date; and
(c) LS Power Grid has determined that all Pre-In-Service Termination Costs and other construction-related obligations attributable to that upgrade have been paid or adequately reserved for.

Any portion of Construction Security retained for unresolved construction costs shall be released after LS Power Grid determines that those costs have been paid or adequately reserved for or, if Section 3.3.2 applies, after the Termination Cost Reconciliation becomes final. Failure to timely deliver Required Operational Security is a default permitting LS Power Grid, to the extent authorized by the applicable tariffs and agreements, to suspend energization or service, terminate applicable interconnection arrangements, and draw Construction Security for secured obligations. 7.3.

Required Operational Security. Required Operational Security shall equal one hundred percent (100%) of the nominal Remaining Cumulative Revenue

Responsibility. LS Power Grid shall reduce that amount only by the portion of Qualifying Retail Security Support identified in a currently effective Retail Security Certification. A Retail Security Certification is accepted in reliance on Eligible

PDF page 87

34

Customer’s representations and does not require LS Power Grid to review the underlying retail-security documents in the ordinary course. If LS Power Grid has a reasonable basis to question the certification, or after a payment default, Credit Review Event, or Material Adverse Credit Event, LS Power Grid may request supporting information reasonably necessary to verify continued compliance. Any resulting deficiency shall be delivered or restored within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of a Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier. 7.4.

Annual Resizing and Security Notices. LS Power Grid shall recalculate Required Operational Security with each Annual Projection, Final MTRC Determination Date, recalculation of a Final Annual MTRC under Section 6.3.1, Reconciliation Statement, effective prospective reassignment, replacement or expiration of Qualifying Retail Security Support, or Credit Review Event. If LS Power Grid reasonably determines that the Required Security Amount exceeds available Security, it may issue a Security Notice identifying the required increase or restoration and reasonable supporting detail then available. Eligible Customer shall provide the required increase or restoration within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of the Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier. A reduction shall be released only after the relevant calculation and reconciliation are final.

7.5.
Form of Operational Security. Except to the extent of currently effective Qualifying Retail Security Support, Eligible Customer shall provide direct Operational Security in the form of cash, an irrevocable standby Letter of Credit meeting Schedule 3, or any combination of cash and Letter of Credit. LS Power Grid may accept another form of Security that it determines, in its sole discretion, is acceptable.

Each Letter of Credit shall have an initial stated term of at least twelve (12) months and shall remain continuously effective until released under this Agreement; provided, however, that a Letter of Credit may have a stated expiration date before its release date if Eligible Customer timely extends or replaces it so that Security in an amount not less than the Required Security Amount remains continuously available to LS Power Grid.

If an issuing bank has the right not to renew a Letter of Credit, the Letter of Credit shall require the issuing bank to provide LS Power Grid and Eligible Customer at least ninety (90) Calendar Days’ prior written notice of nonrenewal. Eligible Customer shall provide a conforming extension or replacement Letter of Credit, or additional cash collateral or other Security accepted by LS Power Grid, no later than sixty (60) Calendar Days before the applicable expiration date.

If Eligible Customer fails to provide the required extension, replacement, or other Security by that deadline, LS Power Grid may, before the Letter of Credit expires, draw all or any portion of the then-available amount under the Letter of Credit and retain the proceeds as cash collateral, apply the proceeds to secured obligations, or both. Any proceeds retained but not applied shall continue to constitute Security until released under this Agreement.

PDF page 88

35

7.6.
Draw and Application. LS Power Grid may draw against all or any portion of a Letter of Credit, apply cash collateral, or apply another form of Security accepted by LS Power Grid to secured obligations upon: (a) an unpaid Supplemental MTRC, true- up, Termination Fee, or other amount due after the applicable payment period; (b) Eligible Customer’s failure to timely deliver, maintain, replace, restore, or increase Security; (c) cancellation, termination, withdrawal, abandonment, or permanent cessation of the Large Load Facility or a material breach of this Agreement; (d) a material inaccuracy in a Retail Security Certification or Security instrument; (e) Eligible Customer: (i) files a voluntary petition in bankruptcy or seeks reorganization, arrangement, or similar relief under any bankruptcy, insolvency, or debtor-relief law;

(ii) becomes subject to an involuntary bankruptcy or insolvency proceeding that is not dismissed within sixty (60) Calendar Days; (iii) has a receiver, trustee, liquidator, or custodian appointed for it or a substantial portion of its assets, which appointment is not vacated within sixty (60) Calendar Days; (iv) makes a general assignment for the benefit of creditors; or (v) admits in writing its inability to pay debts as they become due; or (f) Eligible Customer fails to extend, replace, or otherwise maintain Security in the amount and within the time required by Section 7.5, including following a notice of nonrenewal, issuer rating reduction, or other event affecting the availability of a Letter of Credit.

A draw shall not automatically require replenishment to the amount maintained immediately before the draw. Following any application of drawn proceeds to an amount due, LS Power Grid shall recalculate the remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility and the Operational Security then required under this Article. Eligible Customer shall replenish only the amount, if any, by which the remaining Operational Security is less than the recalculated Required Security Amount, within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of a Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier.

Proceeds drawn solely to prevent the expiration or nonrenewal of a Letter of Credit, and cash collateral not yet applied to an identified payment obligation, shall remain refundable Security and shall continue to constitute Operational Security until applied to an amount then due or released under Section 7.9. Such proceeds and cash collateral shall be included in determining the amount of remaining Operational Security and shall not create an additional replenishment obligation merely because they are held by LS Power Grid.

The posting, drawing, holding, replacement, or replenishment of Operational Security shall not, by itself, satisfy the secured obligation, reduce Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, or produce a TRBAA Credit. Drawn proceeds of Operational Security shall become Applied Security Proceeds only when LS Power Grid applies them to an identified Supplemental MTRC, prior-period true-up adjustment, Termination Fee, or other secured payment obligation arising under Article 6 or Section 3.3.4. Upon application, the proceeds shall reduce that obligation dollar-for-dollar and shall receive the TRBAA treatment required by Section 6.8; provided, however, that Applied Security Proceeds applied to a Termination Fee shall be administered under Section 3.3.4 and credited to transmission customer only through the Annual Termination Fee

PDF page 89

36

Credits provided under Section 6.8. Proceeds of Construction Security applied to Pre- In-Service Termination Costs or other construction-related obligations shall be treated as provided in Sections 3.3.2 and 3.3.3.

7.7.
Security Maintenance. Because Required Operational Security is initially one hundred percent (100%) of nominal Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, no separate acceleration concept applies. An incorrect, expired, withdrawn, unavailable, or materially misleading Retail Security Certification, or the loss of enforceable retail-security rights described in it, is a Material Adverse Credit Event. Eligible Customer shall provide replacement direct Security for any resulting uncovered amount within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of a Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier.

7.8.
Post-Termination Administration and Cash Collateral. LS Power Grid shall continue the annual calculations, invoices, and true-ups required under this Agreement following termination of service to, or the departure, permanent cessation, or material reduction of service by, the Large Load Customer until the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility and all other obligations under this Agreement have been finally satisfied, transferred, reassigned, refunded, or otherwise resolved. The Parties intend that cash collateral and proceeds of Security be treated as a deposit or deferred liability rather than as a Contribution in Aid of Construction, customer financing of transmission plant, or a reduction to LS Power Grid’s rate base. To the extent permitted by applicable Commission accounting and ratemaking requirements and tax law, LS Power Grid shall record and administer such amounts as the corresponding annual costs and payment obligations arise. The treatment described in this Section is subject to the Commission’s Uniform System of Accounts, the Formula Rate, the Protocols, applicable Commission orders, and other Applicable Laws and Regulations. If the Commission requires a different accounting or ratemaking treatment, LS Power Grid shall implement the required treatment and make corresponding adjustments necessary to prevent duplicate recovery and preserve, to the extent permitted, the economic allocation established by this Agreement. The Parties shall cooperate in seeking any necessary Commission authorization or acceptance.

Nothing in this Agreement guarantees a particular accounting, ratemaking, or tax result. Cash collateral and unapplied Security proceeds shall remain subject to release under Section 7.9, including release to reflect an effective prospective reassignment under Section 6.9.

7.9.
Release of Security. LS Power Grid shall release excess Operational Security only after the applicable Reconciliation Statement becomes final. LS Power Grid shall release Security attributable to an effective prospective reassignment and shall release all remaining Security within thirty (30) Calendar Days after final satisfaction of the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility and all other secured obligations. Termination alone does not require release.

7.10. Security Does Not Limit Liability. The Required Security Amount is based on LS Power Grid’s then-current estimates and calculations and does not cap, replace, or

PDF page 90

37

discharge Eligible Customer’s underlying obligations. A shortfall between Security and the amount due remains immediately payable to LS Power Grid.

7.11. Events of Default; Remedies. Each of the following is an “Event of Default” by Eligible Customer: (a) failure to pay any undisputed amount when due that continues for ten (10) Business Days after written notice from LS Power Grid; (b) failure to deliver, maintain, replace, restore, or increase Security when required under this Article 7 or Schedule 3 that continues after the applicable cure period; (c) any other material breach of this Agreement, including under Section 4.2 or 6.3.6, that continues for thirty (30) Calendar Days after written notice from LS Power Grid or, if the breach cannot reasonably be cured within that period, for such longer period, not to exceed ninety (90) Calendar Days, as Eligible Customer diligently pursues a cure; and (d) any event described in Section 7.6(e). Upon an Event of Default, LS Power Grid may, subject to Applicable Laws and Regulations and the applicable tariffs and agreements:

(i) draw and apply Security under Section 7.6; (ii) exercise the rights described in Sections 4.2.6 and 7.2; (iii) before the first Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date, terminate this Agreement under Section 3.3.1; and (iv) pursue any other remedy available at law or in equity or before the Commission. No termination after an Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date releases the obligations described in Section 3.3.4.

8. Force Majeure.
8.1.
If a Party is rendered unable, wholly or in part, by Force Majeure to carry out a nonmonetary obligation, that obligation shall be suspended only to the extent and for the period performance is prevented. Force Majeure does not suspend or excuse payment of incurred construction costs, Pre-In-Service Termination Costs, Supplemental MTRC, the Termination Fee, prior-period true-up adjustments, or the maintenance, replacement, replenishment, or increase of Security. If a Force Majeure event continues for more than twenty (20) Calendar Days in a sixty (60)-Calendar Day period, the Parties shall meet to address performance; any resulting termination remains subject to Sections 3.4 and 3.5.

9. Indemnification
9.1.
Indemnity. Each Party (the “Indemnifying Party”) shall at all times indemnify, defend, and hold the other Party and its Affiliates and their respective directors, officers, employees, and agents (each, an “Indemnified Person”) harmless from any and all damages, losses, claims, including claims and actions relating to injury to or death of any person or damage to property, demand, suits, recoveries, costs and expenses, court costs, attorney fees, and all other obligations by or to third parties, arising out of or resulting from the Indemnifying Party’s actions or omissions in performing its obligations under this Agreement, except to the extent caused by the gross negligence or willful misconduct of the Indemnified Person.

9.1.1.
Indemnified Person. If an Indemnified Person is entitled to indemnification as a result of a claim by a third party, and the Indemnifying Party fails, after notice

PDF page 91

38

and reasonable opportunity to proceed, to assume the defense of such claim, such Indemnified Person may at the expense of the Indemnifying Party contest, settle or consent to the entry of any judgment with respect to, or pay in full, such claim.

9.1.2.
Indemnifying Party. If an Indemnifying Party is obligated to indemnify and hold any Indemnified Person harmless, the amount owing to the Indemnified Person shall be the amount of such Indemnified Person’s actual loss, net of any insurance or other recovery.

9.2.
Indemnity Procedures. Promptly after receipt by an Indemnified Person of any claim or notice of the commencement of any action or administrative or legal proceeding or investigation as to which the indemnity provided for in Section 9.1 may apply, the Indemnified Person shall notify the Indemnifying Party of such fact. Any failure of or delay in such notification shall not affect a Party’s indemnification obligation unless such failure or delay is materially prejudicial to the Indemnifying Party. The Indemnifying Party shall have the right to assume the defense thereof with counsel designated by such Indemnifying Party and reasonably satisfactory to the Indemnified Person. If the defendants in any such action include one or more Indemnified Persons and the Indemnifying Party and if the Indemnified Person reasonably concludes that there may be legal defenses available to it and/or other Indemnified Persons which are different from or additional to those available to the Indemnifying Party, the Indemnified Person shall have the right to select separate counsel to assert such legal defenses and to otherwise participate in the defense of such action on its own behalf. In such instances, the Indemnifying Party shall only be required to pay the fees and expenses of one additional attorney to represent an Indemnified Person or Indemnified Persons having such differing or additional legal defenses.

The Indemnified Person shall be entitled, at its expense, to participate in any such action, suit or proceeding, the defense of which has been assumed by the Indemnifying Party. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the Indemnifying Party (i) shall not be entitled to assume and control the defense of any such action, suit or proceedings if and to the extent that, in the opinion of the Indemnified Person and its counsel, such action, suit or proceeding involves the potential imposition of criminal liability on the Indemnified Person, or there exists a conflict or adversity of interest between the Indemnified Person and the Indemnifying Party, in which event the Indemnifying Party shall pay the reasonable expenses of the Indemnified Person, and (ii) shall not settle or consent to the entry of any judgment in any action, suit or proceeding without the consent of the Indemnified Person, which shall not be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed.

9.3.
Consequential Damages. Except for amounts expressly payable under Articles 3, 4, 6, and 7, no Party shall be liable under this Agreement for special, indirect, incidental, consequential, or punitive damages, including lost profit or revenue, loss of use, cost of capital, or temporary equipment or services, under any theory of liability. Amounts

PDF page 92

39

required to satisfy the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Supplemental MTRC, construction costs, prior-period true-up adjustments, and Security obligations are direct contractual payment obligations and are not excluded damages.

10. Assignment
10.1. Except for a change in Eligible Customer governed by Section 6.10, a Party may assign this Agreement only with the other Party’s written consent; provided that a Party may assign to an Affiliate with equal or greater creditworthiness and legal and operational capacity to perform. No assignment or change in retail service provider releases the existing Eligible Customer until the assignee or Successor Eligible Customer executes the required assumption, provides required Security, and the transfer becomes effective under the applicable tariffs. Any prohibited assignment is void, and Security provided by or on behalf of Large Load Customer does not make Large Load Customer the wholesale obligor.

11. Disputes
All disputes, claims, or controversies arising out of or relating to this Agreement, including any dispute concerning the identification or allocation of Large Load-Driven Upgrades or Covered Upgrades; any calculation, update, or recalculation of an Estimated Annual MTRC, Final Annual MTRC, Regional Project Allocated ARR, or the MTRC Calculation Template; Qualifying Transmission Revenues; Supplemental MTRC; Total Required Revenue Commitment; Cumulative Revenue Responsibility; TRBAA Credits; Pre-In-Service Termination Costs; the Termination Fee; prospective reassignment; or Security, shall be resolved under this Article 11, except that a dispute concerning CAISO’s designation of a regional project or assignment of a project cost share shall be resolved under the applicable CAISO Tariff procedures. Any payment, refund, credit, or other adjustment resulting from the resolution of a dispute under this Article 11 shall be implemented as provided in Section 11.5. 11.1. Submission and Informal Resolution. In the event of a dispute or claim arising out of or relating to this Agreement, the disputing Party shall provide the other Party with a written notice describing the dispute (“Notice of Dispute”). The dispute shall be referred promptly to designated senior representatives for informal resolution. The representatives shall meet or confer within ten (10) Business Days and shall use reasonable efforts to resolve the matter within thirty (30) Calendar Days after receipt of the Notice of Dispute. This informal process is not a condition to seeking emergency relief, filing a FERC complaint, or invoking any ADR processes. 11.2. Alternative Dispute Resolution. If a dispute is not resolved informally, any Party may request Alternative Dispute Resolution (“ADR”). The Parties shall use the ADR procedures, neutral-selection process, confidentiality protections, and timing requirements specified in the applicable FERC rules. ADR shall be conducted by persons knowledgeable in electric utility, transmission, and FERC matters and shall not alter a jurisdictional rate, term, or condition unless accepted or approved by the FERC.

PDF page 93

40

11.3. FERC Complaints and Other Rights. Any Party may instead file a complaint, petition, or other pleading with the FERC under Section 206 or Section 306 of the Federal Power Act, or seek other relief available under the Federal Power Act or Applicable Laws and Regulations, and LS Power Grid may make any filing under Section 205 of the Federal Power Act permitted by Section 12.12. No Party’s participation in informal negotiations or ADR waives or limits its right to seek FERC action. A final ADR determination affecting jurisdictional rates, terms, conditions, charges, or Network Upgrades shall be filed with the FERC if required. 11.4. Costs and Interim Performance. Unless otherwise determined through ADR, the Parties shall bear their own costs and share neutral costs as specified by the applicable ADR procedure. During a dispute, each Party shall continue performing undisputed payment and operational obligations, and shall pay undisputed amounts when due. 11.5. Implementation of Dispute Determinations; TRBAA. Within thirty (30) Calendar Days after a dispute under this Article 11 is finally resolved, whether by written agreement of the Parties, a final ADR determination (subject to any required Commission filing or acceptance), or a final Commission order (each, a “Dispute Determination”), LS Power Grid shall: (a) update the affected MTRC Calculation Template, Schedules, Cumulative Revenue Ledger, Reconciliation Statement, and Required Operational Security, as applicable, and serve the updated documents on Eligible Customer; and (b) invoice or refund, as applicable, any amount owed between the Parties as a result of the Dispute Determination, without interest. To the extent a Dispute Determination changes a TRBAA Credit or any other amount previously credited to, or recovered from, transmission customers, including any change in Supplemental MTRC Receipts or Applied Security Proceeds for a prior Rate Year, LS Power Grid shall record the resulting difference in the TRBA under Section 6.8 and flow it through to transmission customers through the TRBAA, and any amount so returned by LS Power Grid to transmission customers shall include interest as provided in Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff. If a final Commission order in a proceeding concerning LS Power Grid’s TRBAA requires a change to any TRBAA Credit attributable to this Agreement, LS Power Grid shall implement that order in the TRBA and shall make any corresponding updates and adjustments between the Parties under this Section 11.5 as if the order were a Dispute Determination. Nothing in this Section reopens the Final Annual MTRC or Total Required Revenue Commitment except as required to implement a Dispute Determination or as permitted by Section 6.3.1.

12. Miscellaneous.
12.1. Amendment. Except for updates to the MTRC Calculation Template expressly authorized by Section 6.3.1, updates implementing a Dispute Determination under Section 11.5, and administrative updates expressly permitted elsewhere in this Agreement, this Agreement may be amended only by a written instrument executed by the Parties. An authorized template update shall not change the substantive methodology or other terms of this Agreement. Any material deviation from the Commission-accepted pro forma and any unexecuted amendment shall be filed with FERC as required by the applicable tariffs and Commission rules.

PDF page 94

41

12.2. Entire Agreement. This Agreement, its Schedules, the relevant Interconnection Agreement, and other agreements expressly incorporated herein state the Parties’ complete understanding concerning the wholesale cost recovery mechanism. Nothing in this Agreement establishes the retail rates, charges, or security arrangements between Eligible Customer and Large Load Customer.

12.3. Governing Tariffs and Order of Precedence. This Agreement shall be interpreted and administered consistently with the Federal Power Act, applicable Commission orders, the CAISO Tariff, and the TO Tariff. The CAISO Tariff, the TO Tariff, and any other tariff or agreement within the CAISO Requirements that is on file with or accepted by the Commission control over this Agreement and its Schedules to the extent of an irreconcilable conflict, with each controlling the matters within its respective scope. Nothing in this Agreement shall require a Party or CAISO to take action inconsistent with an applicable Commission-accepted tariff, agreement, or order.

Subject to the preceding paragraph:
(a) the Formula Rate and Protocols shall control the calculation, information exchange, and true-up of LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement, including the rate base treatment of the Covered Upgrades; Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff shall control the flow-through of TRBAA Credits to transmission customers through the TRBAA; and any dispute concerning a TRBAA Credit, or any other matter arising under this Agreement, shall be resolved under Article 11, and any resulting adjustment shall be implemented as provided in Section 11.5. The MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5, and not the Formula Rate or Protocols, shall control calculation of the Estimated Annual MTRC and Final Annual MTRC;

(b) this Agreement shall control the determination and administration of the Supplemental MTRC, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Operational Security, prospective reassignment, and the other wholesale revenue-support obligations established herein;

(c) the applicable Interconnection Agreement shall control the physical interconnection, design, engineering, procurement, construction, testing, energization, and operating requirements, except to the extent this Agreement expressly establishes or supplements construction-cost, pre-service termination- cost, revenue-support, or Security obligations; provided that the applicable instruments shall be interpreted, to the extent reasonably possible, to give effect to both agreements and prevent duplicate recovery;

(d) each applicable Affected System Arrangement shall control the design, construction, ownership, operation, cost responsibility, financial security or credit support, and cost recovery applicable to the corresponding Affected System Upgrade as between the parties to that arrangement. Nothing in this Agreement modifies an Affected System Owner’s tariff, makes LS Power Grid responsible for an obligation of an Affected System Owner, or makes an Affected System Owner a Party to this Agreement;

PDF page 95

42

(e) the body of this Agreement shall control over a Schedule in the event of a conflict, except where the Schedule identifies a project-specific election, value, methodology, or term that this Agreement expressly authorizes the Parties to establish in the Schedule; and

(f) the Schedules shall control over other project-specific supporting documents or workpapers that have not been filed with or accepted by the Commission. The Parties shall attempt to interpret the applicable instruments so that each is given effect and no provision is rendered superfluous. If an irreconcilable conflict affects a Commission-jurisdictional rate, term, or condition, the Parties shall seek any required Commission clarification, acceptance, or authorization. Pending resolution, the Parties shall continue performing all undisputed obligations.

12.4. Governing Law. This Agreement shall be interpreted, governed by, and construed under the laws of the State of California, without regard to the principles of conflict of laws thereof, or the laws of the United States, as applicable, as if executed and to be performed wholly within the State of California.

12.5. Severability. If any term, covenant, or condition of this Agreement or the application or effect of any such term, covenant, or condition is held invalid as to any person, entity, or circumstance, or is determined to be unjust, unreasonable, unlawful, imprudent, or otherwise not in the public interest, by any court or government agency of competent jurisdiction, then such term, covenant, or condition shall remain in force and effect to the maximum extent permitted by law, and all other terms, covenants, and conditions of this Agreement and their application shall not be affected thereby but shall remain in force and effect. The Parties shall be relieved of their obligations only to the extent necessary to eliminate such regulatory or other determination, unless a court or governmental agency of competent jurisdiction holds that such provisions are not severable from all other provisions of this Agreement.

12.6. No Third Party Beneficiaries. Except for the rights of Large Load Customer and any Successor Eligible Customer under Section 6.12, this Agreement is not intended to and does not create rights, remedies, or benefits of any character whatsoever in favor of any persons, corporations, associations, or entities other than the Parties, and the obligations herein assumed are solely for the use and benefit of the Parties, their successors in interest and, where permitted, their assigns.

12.7. Survival. Expiration or termination of this Agreement shall not affect any right, liability, or obligation that accrued before the effective date of expiration or termination or that, by its express terms or nature, is intended to continue thereafter. Without limiting the foregoing, the following shall survive expiration or termination for the period necessary to give them effect:

(a) all outstanding payment, refund, reimbursement, reconciliation, true-up, Security, and release obligations arising under Articles 3, 4, 6, and 7;

PDF page 96

43

(b) the determination and final satisfaction, transfer, or prospective reassignment of the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility;

(c) the calculation and implementation of TRBAA Credits and other adjustments necessary to prevent duplicate recovery;

(d) the confidentiality and information-protection obligations applicable under Section 6.12, Section 16.3 of the TO Tariff, the CAISO Tariff, and any applicable confidentiality agreement;

(e) the Parties’ rights to obtain, inspect, retain, verify, and challenge books, records, metering data, settlement data, calculations, and supporting workpapers relating to periods before expiration or termination;

(f) the indemnification obligations in Article 9 with respect to acts, omissions, circumstances, or claims arising before expiration or termination;

(g) the dispute-resolution provisions in Article 11 with respect to any surviving right, liability, or obligation; and

(h) any provision that expressly states that it survives or that must survive to prevent duplicate recovery or give effect to the Parties’ rights and obligations. Survival of a provision shall not extend the underlying payment or Security obligation beyond the point at which that obligation has been finally satisfied, transferred, reassigned, refunded, or released in accordance with this Agreement.

12.8. No Waiver. A Party’s delay or failure to exercise any right or remedy under this Agreement shall not constitute a waiver of that or any other right or remedy. A waiver shall be effective only if stated in a written instrument executed by the Party granting the waiver and shall apply only to the specific matter and circumstances stated therein. A waiver on one occasion shall not constitute a continuing waiver or a waiver on any other occasion. No course of performance or dealing shall amend this Agreement or waive any Commission-jurisdictional rate, term, or condition. Nothing in this Section limits a Party’s rights under the Federal Power Act or Applicable Laws and Regulations.

12.9. Counterparts and Electronic Signatures. This Agreement, any amendment, and any assumption or acknowledgment executed pursuant to it may be executed in counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original and all of which together shall constitute one instrument.
