Signatures transmitted electronically, including by portable document format or an electronic-signature platform, shall have the same force and effect as original signatures to the extent permitted by Applicable Laws and Regulations. Each Party agrees that it shall not contest the validity or enforceability of an instrument solely because it was executed or transmitted electronically. 12.10. Authority. Each Party represents and warrants that:

(b) it has the requisite power and authority to execute, deliver, and perform this Agreement;

Schedule 1 - 9

7. Schedule Qualifications
7.1 Projected Amounts. The capital costs, Estimated Annual MTRC, projected Total Required Revenue Commitment, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, and Operational Security amounts stated in this Schedule are initial projections provided for planning and administration purposes. They do not constitute fixed amounts, caps, or guarantees.

7.2 Initial and Applicable Allocation Percentages. The Initial Allocation Percentage identified for each Large Load-Driven Upgrade shall remain the initial allocation of that upgrade to the Large Load Customer. The Applicable Allocation Percentage shall equal the Initial Allocation Percentage, as adjusted prospectively by any reassignment effective under Section 6.9 or as otherwise required by a final Commission order or applicable tariff provision. A change in capital costs, the Upgrade In-Service Date, Formula Rate inputs, or the annual revenue requirement shall not, by itself, change the Initial Allocation Percentage or Applicable Allocation Percentage. Cancellation, abandonment, downsizing, curtailment, permanent cessation, or material reduction of service by the Large Load Customer also shall not, by itself, reduce either percentage.

7.3 Allocation Limitation. The aggregate Annual MTRC assigned to all Large Load Customers for a Covered Upgrade shall not exceed one hundred percent (100%) of the Annual MTRC for that Covered Upgrade. LS Power Grid shall not allocate the same upgrade cost, transmission capability, or annual revenue requirement to more than one Large Load Customer. 7.4 Upgrade In-Service Dates and MTRC Term End Dates. Each Expected Upgrade In- Service Date stated in this Schedule is a planning estimate. LS Power Grid shall provide written notice to Eligible Customer of a material change to an Expected Upgrade In-Service Date. Within five (5) Business Days after a Covered Upgrade achieves its Upgrade In-Service Date, LS Power Grid shall provide written notice to Eligible Customer stating the actual Upgrade In- Service Date and the corresponding MTRC Term End Date. LS Power Grid shall update this Schedule and the applicable Cumulative Revenue Ledger to reflect those dates. The actual Upgrade In-Service Date shall be determined in accordance with the definition of that term in the Agreement. Inclusion of pre-service Construction Work in Progress in rate base does not constitute an Upgrade In-Service Date.

7.5 Annual Updates. Changes in the Estimated Annual MTRC, Final Annual MTRC, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Supplemental MTRC, Qualifying Transmission Revenues, or Operational Security resulting from the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5, the Annual Projection, the Annual Update and Reconciliation Statement, or an effective prospective reassignment under Section 6.9 do not require an amendment to this Schedule.

7.6 Prospective Reassignment. LS Power Grid shall maintain the customer-, project-, and upgrade-specific ledger required by Sections 6.7 and 6.9. Any prospective reassignment shall be reflected in that ledger and in the applicable Annual Projection and Reconciliation Statement. A prospective reassignment shall adjust the Applicable Allocation Percentage but shall not revise the Initial Allocation Percentage, produce a historical refund, or reopen a prior Rate Year.

Schedule 1 - 10

7.7 Updated Expected Dates. An update to an expected construction commencement date, Expected Upgrade In-Service Date, or expected Large Load Service Commencement Date may be made by written notice and does not require an amendment to this Schedule unless the update also changes a material rate, term, condition, facility scope, or allocation established by this Agreement.

Schedule 2 - 1

Schedule 2
Qualifying Transmission Revenues Methodology
1. Purpose and Controlling Principles
This Schedule establishes the reproducible methodology for calculating projected and actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues attributable to the Large Load Facility. It is intended to identify wholesale transmission revenues paid by or on behalf of Eligible Customer that are attributable to the Large Load Facility and reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected from CAISO customers or directly credit LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement. Such revenues may include applicable TAC revenues and qualifying Wheeling Access Charges or other wholesale transmission-access, wheeling, or transmission-service charges under the CAISO Tariff or another Commission-accepted arrangement, including charges paid through a non-PTO serving entity. The Supplemental MTRC therefore measures only the remaining shortfall below the applicable Annual MTRC.

Qualifying Transmission Revenues shall be counted once in calculating the Supplemental MTRC and the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility against the combined Annual MTRC for all Covered Upgrades. They shall not also be treated as a TRBAA Credit under Section 6.8 because they reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected from CAISO customers. This Schedule does not modify CAISO settlement rules, establish a retail rate, impose any obligation on CAISO, or alter any cost allocation established under the CAISO Requirements.

2. Project and Settlement Identifiers
The following information shall be completed for each Large Load Facility and updated administratively when a meter, settlement identifier, Eligible Customer, or other listed item changes. An administrative update that does not alter the Commission-accepted methodology does not require an amendment to the Agreement unless a filing is otherwise required by Applicable Laws and Regulations.

3. Data Sources and Responsibilities
(a) LS Power Grid shall calculate projected and actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues using the best available data from CAISO settlement statements, wheeling, transmission- access, and transmission service invoices, revenue-quality meter data, Eligible Customer and non-PTO serving entity billing records, Scheduling Coordinator records, and other data made available under the CAISO Tariff, another Commission-accepted arrangement, or authorized by Eligible Customer and Large Load Customer.

(b) Eligible Customer shall provide, or cause its Scheduling Coordinator, Utility Distribution Company, Metering Coordinator, or other authorized agent to provide, all information reasonably necessary to identify and verify withdrawals attributable to the Large Load Facility. Eligible Customer shall maintain all authorizations reasonably necessary for release of that information.

(c) The Large Load Customer shall provide the information and authorizations required by its Limited Acknowledgment, including information needed to distinguish the Large Load Facility from other load behind a common meter or within a common settlement identifier.

(d) LS Power Grid may use CAISO data made available under the CAISO Requirements or through the authorizations provided under this Agreement. Nothing in this Schedule makes CAISO a party to the Agreement or independently requires CAISO to calculate, invoice, collect, or administer the Supplemental MTRC.

(e) If final data are unavailable when a projection, invoice, Annual Update, or Reconciliation Statement is due, LS Power Grid may use a reasonable estimate based on the most recent available data, the service forecast, applicable TAC rates, and known settlement adjustments. Estimated amounts shall be replaced with actual data in the next practicable reconciliation.

PDF page 113

Schedule 2 - 3

4. Applicable Withdrawals
For each CAISO settlement interval or other reproducible billing interval, Applicable Withdrawals shall equal the metered energy withdrawals attributable to the Large Load Facility at the metering point identified in Section 2 of this Schedule, expressed in MWh and adjusted as follows:

(a) Imports, exports, station service, auxiliary load, behind-the-meter generation, storage charging and discharging, and other injections or withdrawals shall be treated consistently with the manner in which they are recognized for the applicable TAC charge under the CAISO Tariff.

(b) Distribution losses and transmission losses shall be included only to the extent they are included in the billing determinant for the applicable TAC component. The loss factor or method shall be identified in the project-specific table in Section 10 of this Schedule.

(c) If the Large Load Facility shares a meter or settlement identifier with other load, the withdrawals attributable to the Large Load Facility shall be determined from revenue- quality submeters or another objective allocation method identified in Section 10 of this Schedule. An allocation based solely on estimates may be used temporarily but shall be reconciled when reliable data become available.

(d) Withdrawals shall not be counted for a period before the corresponding Annual MTRC is included in the Supplemental MTRC calculation under Section 6.4 of the Agreement, except where necessary to correct a prior-period error or resettlement.

5. Applicable TAC Revenue
Applicable TAC Revenue for an interval shall equal Applicable Withdrawals multiplied by the CAISO transmission rate or rates actually applicable to those withdrawals for that interval. The calculation shall include only transmission charge components that contribute to recovery of the revenue requirement in which LS Power Grid’s costs are included. It shall exclude retail distribution charges, generation charges, non-transmission surcharges, taxes collected for another governmental entity, and any other charge that does not contribute to recovery of LS Power Grid’s transmission revenue requirement.

If CAISO uses a monthly, annual, or other non-interval billing determinant, LS Power Grid shall perform the calculation using that same billing period and determinant. If more than one TAC rate or component applies, each component shall be calculated separately before applying the LS Power Grid Allocation Factor.

6. LS Power Grid Allocation Factor
The LS Power Grid Allocation Factor shall identify the portion of Applicable TAC Revenue allocable to LS Power Grid for the relevant period. Unless a different Commission-accepted method is stated in Section 10 of this Schedule, the factor shall be calculated from the same revenue requirement, billing determinant, allocation percentage, and other inputs used by CAISO to include LS Power Grid’s net transmission revenue requirement in the applicable TAC rate. For each applicable rate component and period:

PDF page 114

Schedule 2 - 4

LS Power Grid Allocation Factor = LS Power Grid Net Revenue Requirement included in the applicable TAC component ÷ Total Net Revenue Requirement recovered through that TAC component.

If LS Power Grid’s revenue requirement is recovered through a project-specific, zonal, or other direct allocation rather than a systemwide factor, the corresponding Commission-accepted allocation percentage or rate component shall be used. The supporting workpapers shall identify the source and calculation of each factor.

7. Calculation of Qualifying Transmission Revenues
Projected and actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues shall be calculated as follows for each applicable interval, month, or billing period and then aggregated for the Rate Year or partial Rate Year:

Qualifying Transmission Revenues =  Qualifying TAC Revenue + Qualifying Wheeling and Other Wholesale Transmission Revenues ± Applicable Adjustments.

Qualifying TAC Revenue = Σ [(Applicable Withdrawals × Applicable TAC Rate) × LS Power Grid Allocation Factor]. Qualifying Wheeling and Other Wholesale Transmission Revenues equal Wheeling Access Charges and other wholesale transmission-access, wheeling, or transmission-service charges actually paid by or on behalf of Eligible Customer to CAISO, a Participating TO, or a non-PTO transmission or load-serving entity for deliveries serving the Large Load Facility, but only to the extent those revenues reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected through TAC or directly credit LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement. Applicable Adjustments shall include only amounts attributable to the Large Load Facility, including CAISO resettlements, billing corrections, refunds, charge reversals, balancing-account adjustments, and other adjustments that change the qualifying transmission revenue. Bad debt or uncollectible amounts shall reduce Qualifying Transmission Revenues only to the extent the corresponding revenue is not recovered from another source and is not otherwise included in LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate recovery.

8. Projections, Actuals, Resettlements, and Customer Changes
(a) The Annual Projection shall use forecast withdrawals, then-effective or forecast TAC rates, the most recent LS Power Grid Allocation Factor, and known adjustments. The workpapers shall separately identify each material assumption.

(b) The Reconciliation Statement shall replace projected values with actual withdrawals, rates, factors, and adjustments for the applicable period. CAISO resettlements or other corrections received after a Reconciliation Statement becomes final shall be reflected in the next practicable Reconciliation Statement and Annual Update, subject to Section 6.6 of the Agreement, and any dispute concerning them shall be resolved under Article 11 of the Agreement.

(c) A change in Eligible Customer, Scheduling Coordinator, or retail service provider shall not interrupt the calculation or cause the same withdrawals or revenues to be omitted or counted more than once. Qualifying Transmission Revenues shall remain associated with the Large Load Customer and Large Load Facility, regardless of which Eligible Customer is responsible for payment during the applicable period.

PDF page 115

Schedule 2 - 5

(d) If two or more Eligible Customers serve the Large Load Facility during a Rate Year, the Qualifying Transmission Revenues and corresponding Supplemental MTRC shall be assigned between them based on the periods during which each provided service, unless the applicable transition agreement provides another Commission-accepted allocation.

9. Workpapers, Verification, and Records
With each Annual Projection and Reconciliation Statement, LS Power Grid shall provide workpapers sufficient for Eligible Customer to reproduce the calculation, including applicable meter quantities, TAC rates, LS Power Grid Allocation Factors, adjustments, source references, and a reconciliation to the corresponding Supplemental MTRC and Cumulative Revenue Ledger. Customer-specific or commercially sensitive information may be provided subject to the confidentiality provisions of Section 16.3 of the TO Tariff, the CAISO Tariff, and any applicable confidentiality agreement.

LS Power Grid, Eligible Customer, and Large Load Customer shall retain supporting records for the period required by the CAISO Tariff, Commission regulations, and Applicable Laws and Regulations, and in all events until completion of any timely initiated review, audit, or dispute concerning the applicable Rate Year. Any dispute concerning the calculation shall be resolved under Article 11 of the Agreement.

Schedule 3 - 1

Schedule 3
Credit and Security Standards
1. Purpose and Application
This Schedule establishes the forms, delivery, maintenance, replacement, draw, application, and release requirements for all Construction Security and Operational Security required under Article 7. Except for the limited reduction of direct Operational Security provided in Section 3, all Security shall be provided directly by Eligible Customer.

2. Permitted Forms of Security
Eligible Customer may provide Security in the form of cash, a Letter of Credit, or any combination thereof. LS Power Grid may accept another form of Security if it determines, in its sole discretion, that the form is acceptable.

Cash collateral shall be delivered in immediately available funds to an account designated by LS Power Grid and shall count dollar-for-dollar toward the applicable Required Security Amount while held by LS Power Grid.

(a) Construction Security
Eligible Customer shall provide and maintain all Construction Security directly in one or more forms permitted under this Section 2. A Retail Security Certification and Qualifying Retail Security Support shall not constitute, reduce, replace, or satisfy Construction Security or any Pre- In-Service Termination Costs.

(b) Operational Security
Except to the extent reduced under Section 3, Eligible Customer shall provide and maintain direct Operational Security in one or more forms permitted under this Section 2 in an amount equal to one hundred percent (100%) of the nominal Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility.

3. Retail Security Certification
A current Retail Security Certification reduces the direct Operational Security requirement dollar-for-dollar by the amount of Qualifying Retail Security Support identified in that certification. It does not reduce Eligible Customer’s underlying obligations, constitute Security posted directly with LS Power Grid, or apply to Construction Security or Pre-In-Service Termination Costs.

Each Retail Security Certification must be substantially in the form of Exhibit A contained in Schedule 3 of this Agreement. Eligible Customer shall deliver a Retail Security Certification before first relying on Qualifying Retail Security Support to reduce the direct Operational

PDF page 118

Schedule 3 - 2

Security requirement. The Retail Security Certification shall remain effective unless and until superseded, withdrawn, or deemed ineffective under this Agreement. Eligible Customer shall deliver an updated Retail Security Certification within three (3) Business Days after any reduction, draw, expiration, nonrenewal, termination, amendment, loss of enforceable draw or application rights, or other event that could impair the certified support.

With each Reconciliation Statement, LS Power Grid may identify the amount of Qualifying Retail Security Support then reflected in calculating Required Operational Security. That identification does not require Eligible Customer to deliver a new Retail Security Certification unless an event requiring an update has occurred.

LS Power Grid may rely on the certification without reviewing the underlying retail security documents in the ordinary course, but may request supporting information after a payment default, Credit Review Event, Material Adverse Credit Event, or reasonable basis to question the certification.

4. Letter of Credit Requirements
If Eligible Customer provides a Letter of Credit as Security, the Letter of Credit must be irrevocable, unconditional, transferable, payable at sight, and permit partial and multiple draws. It must be issued by a major United States commercial bank or United States branch of a major foreign commercial bank rated at least A- by S&P, A3 by Moody’s, or A- by Fitch, in each case by at least two of those three agencies, and otherwise be in form and substance reasonably acceptable to LS Power Grid.

Each Letter of Credit must have an initial stated term of at least twelve (12) months and provide LS Power Grid at least ninety (90) Calendar Days’ prior written notice of nonrenewal. Eligible Customer shall provide a conforming extension, replacement Letter of Credit, cash collateral, or other Security accepted by LS Power Grid no later than sixty (60) Calendar Days before the applicable expiration date following a notice of nonrenewal, and within fifteen (15) Business Days after an issuer rating reduction.

If Eligible Customer fails to timely provide the required replacement or substitute Security, LS Power Grid may, before the applicable Letter of Credit expires, draw all or any portion of the then-available amount and retain the proceeds as cash collateral, apply the proceeds to secured obligations, or both.

5. Security Notices; Maintenance; and Replenishment
LS Power Grid may issue a Security Notice whenever it reasonably determines that the Required Security Amount exceeds available Security, including as a result of a draw, application of cash collateral, change in the Required Security Amount, loss of Qualifying Retail Security Support, or other event affecting Security availability.

Eligible Customer shall provide any required increase, restoration, replacement, or other cure within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of the Security Notice, or by the applicable in- service date if earlier.

PDF page 119

Schedule 3 - 3

6. Draws; Application; and Release
LS Power Grid may draw against a Letter of Credit, apply cash collateral, or apply another accepted form of Security as permitted under Article 7. A draw, the holding of proceeds or cash collateral, or replenishment does not by itself satisfy an obligation or reduce Cumulative Revenue Responsibility. Proceeds of Operational Security become Applied Security Proceeds only when applied to an identified secured obligation as provided in Section 7.6; proceeds of Construction Security are treated as provided in Sections 3.3.2 and 3.3.3.

LS Power Grid shall release excess Security after the applicable Reconciliation Statement becomes final. LS Power Grid shall release all remaining Security within thirty (30) Calendar Days after final satisfaction of all secured obligations, subject to any specifically identified unresolved dispute.

7. Required Security Amount Not a Limitation
The Required Security Amount is based on then-current estimates and calculations and does not limit Eligible Customer’s total liability or responsibility for its obligations under this Agreement.

Schedule 5 - 1

Schedule 5
MTRC Calculation Template and Cumulative Revenue Ledger
This Schedule contains the project-specific calculation of each Estimated Annual MTRC and Final Annual MTRC and the Cumulative Revenue Ledger. LS Power Grid shall calculate each Annual MTRC solely under this Schedule. The Final Annual MTRC shall equal the applicable final incurred capital cost multiplied by the Project-Specific Levelized Fixed Charge Rate calculated in this Schedule. The calculation shall identify the applicable capital-cost input, present-worth tax-depreciation benefits, Formula Rate inputs, fixed expense allocator, applicable allocation percentage, Annual MTRC, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, and supporting inputs.

For purposes of calculating present-worth federal and state income-tax depreciation benefits, the Schedule applies a levelized-depreciation rate of five percent (5%) of the applicable capital cost input in each year of the twenty (20)-year MTRC Term. This levelized-depreciation schedule is not a separate annual charge. The Schedule is fixed on the Final MTRC Determination Date except to correct an error or fraud, to implement an effective prospective reassignment, to implement a final FERC order applicable to the calculation, to implement a Dispute Determination under Section 11.5, or to reflect, prospectively, a change in (i) LS Power Grid’s return on equity component of its Formula Rate, resulting from a final FERC order accepting or approving such change, or (ii) applicable federal or state tax rates or tax law enacted after the Final MTRC Determination Date; provided that, the five percent (5%) annual levelized- depreciation schedule shall remain fixed for the MTRC Term unless a final FERC order requires otherwise.

The following project-specific calculation and ledger shall be completed for each Covered Upgrade. LS Power Grid shall include an updated ledger statement in each Reconciliation Statement served on Eligible Customer under Sections 6.6 and 6.7; this Schedule does not impose a separate FERC filing requirement.

Annual Ledger Service. LS Power Grid shall update the Cumulative Revenue Ledger at least annually in connection with its Annual Update and shall include the resulting statement and the supporting workpapers in each Reconciliation Statement served on Eligible Customer. The statement is an agreement-administration record and is not required to be filed with FERC unless FERC otherwise requires. Any dispute concerning the statement shall be resolved under Article 11 of the Agreement.

 LARGE LOAD INTERCONNECTION PROCEDURES (LLIP)

Section 1.
Objectives and Applicability
1.1 Objectives

The objective of this LLIP is to implement requirements for Large Load interconnections to the Participating TO’s Transmission System pursuant to the TO Tariff.  For purposes of this LLIP, the “Participating TO” is LS Power Grid California, LLC (“LS Power Grid”), and the “TO Tariff” is the LS Power Grid California, LLC Transmission Owner Tariff.  Because LS Power Grid is a Non-Load-Serving Participating TO and has no End-Use Customers, a Large Load interconnecting under this LLIP will not be a retail transmission customer of the Participating TO.

1.2 Applicability

The LLIP is applicable to a Large Load Interconnection Customer that is either (1) proposing to interconnect a Large Load to the Participating TO’s Transmission System; (2) seeking to increase the capacity of an existing Large Load processed under the LLIP; or (3) with respect to an existing load interconnected to the Participating TO’s Transmission System that was not processed under the LLIP, seeking an increase of 50 MW or greater.  This LLIP only applies to completed Interconnection Requests submitted after the effective date of this Appendix VI to the TO Tariff.  Consistent with Section 10A of the TO Tariff, all requests for ECLL Interconnection shall be directed to the Participating TO.  An Interconnection Request submitted under this LLIP shall constitute the ECLL Interconnection Application, and the Interconnection Studies and the Large Load Interconnection Agreement under this LLIP shall constitute the study process and the ECLL Interconnection Agreement, respectively, for purposes of Section 10A of the TO Tariff.

Section 2.
Definitions

Terms used in this LLIP with initial capitalization shall have the meanings set forth below.  The singular of any definition shall include the plural and the plural shall include the singular.  If a term with initial capitalization used herein is not defined, such term shall have the meanings ascribed to such term in Section 3 of the TO Tariff.

Affected System shall mean an electric system other than the Participating TO’s Transmission System that may be affected by the proposed interconnection.

Affected System Operator shall mean the entity that owns or operates an Affected System, and includes an Affected System Owner as defined in any Cost Recovery Agreement.

Applicable Laws and Regulations shall mean all duly promulgated applicable federal, state and local laws, regulations, rules, ordinances, codes, decrees, judgments, directives, or judicial or administrative orders, permits and other duly authorized actions of any Governmental Authority.

PDF page 133

2

Base Case shall mean data including, but not limited to, base power flow, short circuit and stability data bases, underlying load, generation, and transmission facility assumptions, contingency lists, including relevant remedial action schemes, and transmission diagrams used to perform the Interconnection Studies.  The Base Case may include Critical Energy Infrastructure Information (as that term is defined by FERC).  The Base Case may include transmission facilities as approved by the Participating TO or ISO, as applicable, Reliability Network Upgrades that are included in a signed interconnection agreement, Large Load Network Upgrades, Large Loads and any co-located generation, and load forecast. The Base Case may also include generation facilities that (i) are directly interconnected to a Distribution System or the ISO Controlled Grid; (ii) are interconnected to Affected Systems and may have an impact on the Interconnection Request; (iii) have a pending request to interconnect to a Distribution System or an Affected System; or (iv) are not interconnected to a Distribution System or the ISO Controlled Grid, but are subject to the terms and conditions of a fully executed interconnection agreement (or its equivalent predecessor agreement) or for which an unexecuted interconnection agreement (or its equivalent predecessor agreement) has been requested to be filed with FERC.

Business Day shall mean Monday through Friday, excluding Federal Holidays.

Calendar Day shall mean any day including Saturday, Sunday or a Federal Holiday.

Commercial Readiness Deposit shall mean a deposit paid as set forth in LLIP Sections 4.2.1 and 4.6.1.4.

Confidential Information shall mean any confidential, proprietary or trade secret information of a plan, specification, pattern, procedure, design, device, list, concept, policy or compilation relating to the present or planned business of a Party, which is designated as confidential by the Party supplying the information, whether conveyed orally, electronically, in writing, through inspection, or otherwise.

Cost Recovery Agreement shall have the meaning set forth in Section 3 of the TO Tariff.

Dispute Resolution shall mean the applicable procedure for resolution of a dispute between the Parties as described in Section 14 of the TO Tariff, including, as applicable, the ISO ADR Procedures.

Flexible Interim Load Interconnection (FILI) Service shall have the meaning set forth in Section 3 of the TO Tariff.

FILI Study shall mean the study performed by the Participating TO pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.2 to determine the availability of Interim Firm Interconnection Service and FILI Service.

Interconnection Financial Security shall mean any of the financial instruments listed in LLIP Section 4.7.1 provided by the Large Load Interconnection Customer to comply with its obligations under the LLIP or the Large Load Interconnection Agreement.

PDF page 134

3

Interconnection Request shall mean a request, in the form of Attachment 1 to Appendix VI to the TO Tariff, to interconnect a new Large Load, or to increase the capacity of, or make a Material Modification to the operating characteristics of, an existing Large Load that is interconnected with the Participating TO’s Transmission System.

Interconnection Study shall mean any of the following studies: the Large Load Interconnection Study, the FILI Study, restudies, and any additional studies performed pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.3.

Interconnection Study Deposit shall mean the cash deposit provided to the Participating TO under LLIP Section 4.2.1 as a requirement of a valid Interconnection Request to be used to offset the cost of the Interconnection Studies.

Large Load Capacity shall mean the maximum capacity of the Large Load at the Point of Interconnection.

Large Load Customer shall mean the End-User that owns, operates, or proposes to develop the Large Load and receives, or will receive, retail transmission service from the Large Load Interconnection Customer, and shall be the Large Load Customer for purposes of the TO Tariff and any Cost Recovery Agreement.

Large Load Interconnection Agreement shall mean the interconnection agreement between the Participating TO and the Large Load Interconnection Customer, developed by the Participating TO consistent with the TO Tariff and the results of the Interconnection Studies and negotiated between the Parties pursuant to LLIP Section 5, which sets forth the terms and conditions governing the interconnection of the Large Load to the Participating TO’s Transmission System, including, without limitation, cost responsibility, payment provisions, cost recovery, and financial security for the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, as it may be amended from time to time. The Large Load Interconnection Agreement shall constitute the ECLL Interconnection Agreement for purposes of the TO Tariff, as applicable, and shall be filed with FERC by the Participating TO to the extent required. For purposes of the TO Tariff, the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades identified in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement shall together constitute the ECLL Interconnection Facilities.

Large Load Interconnection Customer shall mean an Eligible Customer for Large Load (“ECLL”), as defined in Section 3 of the TO Tariff, that proposes to interconnect its facilities with the Participating TO’s Transmission System for the purpose of providing retail transmission service to a Large Load.

Large Load Interconnection Customer Interconnection Facilities shall mean all facilities and equipment owned, controlled, or operated by the Large Load Interconnection Customer or the Large Load Customer up to the Point of Change of Ownership as identified in the Large

PDF page 135

4

Load Interconnection Agreement, including any modifications, additions or upgrades to such facilities and equipment.

Large Load Interconnection Procedures (LLIP) shall mean the interconnection procedures applicable to an Interconnection Request set forth in this Appendix VI to the TO Tariff.

Large Load Interconnection Study shall mean an engineering study conducted by the Participating TO for a Large Load Interconnection Customer under the LLIP that evaluates the impact of the proposed interconnection on the safety and reliability of Participating TO’s Transmission System and, if applicable, an Affected System. The Large Load Interconnection Study will also determine a list of facilities (including Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades), the cost of those facilities, and the time required to interconnect the Large Load with the Participating TO’s Transmission System. The scope of the study is defined in LLIP Section 4.6.1.1.

Large Load Network Upgrades shall mean the additions, modifications, and upgrades to the ISO Controlled Grid at or beyond the Point of Interconnection necessary to serve one or more Large Loads safely and reliably that are identified through the Large Load Interconnection Study and submitted to the ISO for concurrence pursuant to Section 25.7 of the ISO Tariff. Large Load Network Upgrades do not include Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities. To the extent a Large Load Network Upgrade is owned or to be constructed by the Participating TO and is identified in Schedule 1 to a Cost Recovery Agreement, such Large Load Network Upgrades shall constitute Large Load-Driven Upgrades, as defined in that Cost Recovery Agreement.

Large Load Study Process Agreement shall mean the agreement, developed and tendered by the Participating TO pursuant to LLIP Section 4.5, entered into by the Large Load Interconnection Customer and the Participating TO which sets forth the Parties’ agreement to perform Interconnection Studies under the LLIP.

Material Modification shall mean those modifications that have a material impact on the cost or timing of any Interconnection Request, or any other valid interconnection request to the Participating TO or the ISO, with an equal or later Queue Position date.

Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities shall mean all facilities and equipment owned, controlled, or operated by the Participating TO from the Point of Change of Ownership to the Point of Interconnection as identified in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, including any modifications, additions or upgrades to such facilities and equipment. Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities are transmission facilities but are not controlled by the ISO.  Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities are sole use facilities and shall not include Large Load Network Upgrades.

Participating TO’s Transmission System shall mean the facilities owned and operated by the Participating TO and that have been placed under the ISO’s Operational Control, which facilities form part of the ISO Controlled Grid.

PDF page 136

5

Point of Change of Ownership shall mean the point, as set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, where the Large Load Interconnection Customer's interconnection facilities connect to the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities.

Point of Interconnection shall mean the point, as set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, where the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities connect to the Participating TO’s Transmission System.  For purposes of the TO Tariff, the Point of Interconnection shall be the Point of Withdrawal for the Large Load.

Queue Position shall mean the order of a valid Interconnection Request, relative to all other pending valid Interconnection Requests, established pursuant to LLIP Section 3.8.

Reasonable Efforts shall mean, with respect to an action required to be attempted or taken by a Party under the LLIP or the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, efforts that are timely and consistent with Good Utility Practice and are otherwise substantially equivalent to those a Party would use to protect its own interests.

Report Meeting shall mean the meeting among the Participating TO, the Large Load Interconnection Customer, and if applicable, the ISO and other Affected System Operators to discuss the results of the Interconnection Studies as set forth in the LLIP.

Scoping Meeting shall mean the meeting between representatives of the Large Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO, and if applicable, the ISO, conducted for the purpose of discussing alternative interconnection options, to exchange information including any transmission data and earlier study evaluations that would be reasonably expected to impact such interconnection options, to analyze such information, and to determine the potential feasible Points of Interconnection.

Service Commencement Date shall mean the date the Large Load is initially operated in parallel with the Participating TO’s Transmission System and transmission interconnection service is provided to the Large Load Interconnection Customer pursuant to the terms of an executed Large Load Interconnection Agreement. For purposes of any Cost Recovery Agreement, the Service Commencement Date shall be the Large Load Service Commencement Date specified in Schedule 1 to that agreement.

Site Control shall have the meaning set forth in Section 3 of the TO Tariff.

System Protection Facilities shall mean the equipment, including necessary protection signal communications equipment, that protects (1) the Participating TO’s Transmission System, Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, ISO Controlled Grid, and Affected Systems from faults or other electrical disturbances occurring at the Large Load and (2) the Large Load from faults or other electrical system disturbances occurring on the ISO Controlled Grid, Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, and Affected Systems or on other delivery systems or other generating systems to which the ISO Controlled Grid is directly connected.

Transmission Interconnection Service shall mean the service provided by the Participating TO associated with interconnecting the Large Load to the Participating TO’s Transmission

PDF page 137

6

System and enabling the Large Load Interconnection Customer to receive electric energy at the Point of Interconnection, pursuant to the terms of the Large Load Interconnection Agreement and the TO Tariff.

Withdrawal Penalty shall mean the penalty assessed by Participating TO to a Large Load Interconnection Customer that chooses to withdraw or is deemed withdrawn from Participating TO’s interconnection queue or whose Large Load does not otherwise reach its Service Commencement Date. The calculation of the Withdrawal Penalty is set forth in LLIP Section 3.10.1.

Section 3.
General Provisions Applicable to All Interconnection Requests

3.1
Interconnection Requests
A Large Load Interconnection Customer shall submit to Participating TO an Interconnection Request in the form of Attachment 1 to this LLIP for processing under the LLIP.  Consistent with Section 10A.2.1 of the TO Tariff, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall send a copy of the Interconnection Request to the ISO, and the Interconnection Request and all supporting documentation shall be submitted electronically to the Participating TO in the manner posted on the Participating TO’s public website.

An Interconnection Request for the expansion of capacity of an existing Large Load shall be treated the same as an Interconnection Request for a new Large Load pursuant to this LLIP.

Co-located generation serving a Large Load that will not export to the ISO Controlled Grid shall be interconnected in accordance with the generator interconnection rules of the applicable ECLL, as approved by its Local Regulatory Authority (including, for a UDC subject to the jurisdiction of the CPUC, CPUC Electric Rule 21), consistent with Section 10A.5 of the TO Tariff; provided that, where such co-located generation is included in the Interconnection Request, the Participating TO will evaluate its incremental impacts on the Participating TO’s Transmission System as part of the LLIP study process. Co- located generation that will export to the ISO Controlled Grid is required to submit a generator interconnection request to the ISO in accordance with Section 25.7.6 of the ISO Tariff for evaluation of the proposed generator interconnection and the Large Load will be evaluated pursuant to the LLIP.  For a Large Load co- located with an exporting generating facility, the ISO will be a party to a pro forma agreement that bridges the generator’s Large Generator Interconnection Agreement with the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, or will negotiate a non-conforming Large Generator Interconnection Agreement, consistent with Section 10A.5 of the TO Tariff.

3.2
Transmission Interconnection Service

3.2.1 The Product.  Transmission Interconnection Service allows Large Load Interconnection Customer to connect the Large Load to the Participating

PDF page 138

7

TO’s Transmission System and be eligible to receive electric energy and capacity from the ISO Controlled Grid.

3.2.2 No Applicability to Transmission Service.  Nothing in this LLIP shall constitute a request for transmission service or confer upon a Large Load Interconnection Customer any right to receive transmission service.

3.2.3 Roles and Responsibilities.

3.2.3.1 Each Interconnection Request will be subject to the direction and oversight of the Participating TO.  The Participating TO will conduct or cause to be performed the required Interconnection Studies and any additional studies the Participating TO determines to be reasonably necessary.  The analysis of impacts on, and upgrades required to, the Participating TO’s Transmission System will be performed by the Participating TO and will be subject to review and concurrence by the ISO.  The Participating TO will perform all required studies related to the Participating TO’s Transmission System and will coordinate with Affected System Operators in accordance with LLIP Section 3.5.

3.2.3.2 The Participating TO will complete or cause to be completed all studies as required within the timelines provided in this LLIP.

3.2.3.3 Each Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the actual costs of all Interconnection Studies, and any additional studies the Participating TO determines to be reasonably necessary in response to the Interconnection Request.  The Participating TO shall reimburse the ISO for the actual cost of any portion of the Interconnection Studies that the ISO performs related to the ISO Controlled Grid, as applicable.

3.2.3.3.1 The Participating TO shall issue invoices for Interconnection Studies that shall include a detailed and itemized accounting of the cost of each Interconnection Study.  Whenever the actual cost of performing the

Interconnection Studies exceeds the Interconnection
Study Deposit, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the undisputed difference in accordance with the Participating TO issued invoice within thirty (30)

Calendar Days.  The Participating TO shall not be
obligated to continue to have any studies conducted
unless the Large Load Interconnection Customer has paid all undisputed amounts in compliance herewith.

PDF page 139

8

3.3
Comparability
Participating TO shall receive, process and analyze all Interconnection Requests in a timely manner as set forth in this LLIP.  Participating TO will use the same Reasonable Efforts in processing and analyzing Interconnection Requests from all Large Load Interconnection Customers, whether the Large Load is owned by Participating TO and its Affiliates or by a third party.

3.4
Base Case Data
Participating TO shall provide Base Case data to a Large Load Interconnection Customer upon request subject to confidentiality provisions in LLIP Section 7.1. Participating TO shall provide base power flow, short circuit databases, including all underlying assumptions, and contingency list used in the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Studies.  Participating TO is permitted to require that the Large Load Interconnection Customer sign a confidentiality agreement before the release of commercially sensitive information or Critical Energy Infrastructure Information in the Base Case data. Such Base Cases may include all (i) Large Loads, (ii) generation projects, and (iii) transmission projects, including merchant transmission projects that are proposed for the Participating TO’s Transmission System for which a transmission expansion plan has been submitted and approved by the applicable authority.

3.5
Coordination with Affected Systems
The Participating TO will notify the Affected System Operators that are potentially affected by a Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Request.  The Participating TO will coordinate the conduct of any studies required to determine the impact of the Interconnection Request on Affected Systems with Affected System Operators and, if possible, include those results (if available) in its applicable Interconnection Study within the time frame specified in this LLIP.  Participating TO will include such Affected System Operators in all meetings held with Large Load Interconnection Customer as required by this LLIP.  Large Load Interconnection Customer will cooperate with Participating TO in all matters related to the conduct of studies and the determination of modifications to Affected Systems.  If an Affected System Operator, that has been informed its system is an Affected System refuses to cooperate with the Participating TO with whom interconnection has been requested in all matters related to the conduct of studies and the determination of whether modifications to its Affected System are necessary to avoid adverse impacts, it waives any right in any fora that the Participating TO’s provision of service has adversely impacted such Affected System Operator.

3.6
Capacity of the Large Load

The Interconnection Request shall be evaluated using the maximum capacity requested by the Large Load Interconnection Customer.  However, if the maximum capacity that the Large Load is capable of receiving is greater than the

PDF page 140

9

maximum capacity requested by the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Large Load Interconnection Customer will be required to limit the Large Load capacity to no greater than the amount requested (e.g., through use of a control system, power relay(s), or other similar device settings or adjustments), and the Large Load Interconnection Customer must obtain the Participating TO’s agreement, with such agreement not to be unreasonably withheld, that the manner in which the Large Load Interconnection Customer proposes to implement such a limit will not adversely affect the safety and reliability of the Participating TO’s Transmission System.  If the Participating TO does not so agree, then the Interconnection Request must be withdrawn or revised to specify the maximum capacity that the Large Load is capable of receiving.    Furthermore, nothing in this section shall prevent a Participating TO from considering a capacity higher than the limited capacity, if appropriate, when evaluating system protection impacts.

3.7
Proposed Service Commencement Date
The  proposed Service Commencement Date of the new Large Load or increase in capacity of the existing Large Load shall not exceed seven years from the date the Interconnection Request is received by Participating TO for those Interconnection Requests studied in accordance with the Large Load Interconnection Procedures, unless Large Load Interconnection Customer demonstrates and the Participating TO agrees, such agreement not to be unreasonably withheld, that engineering, permitting and construction of the new Large Load or increase in capacity of the existing Large Load will take longer than the seven-year period.

3.7.1 Interconnection Request.  If the proposed Service Commencement Date in the Interconnection Request is more than seven years from the date the Interconnection Request is received by the Participating TO, the Large Load Interconnection Customer must demonstrate, subject to the Participating TO’s agreement, such agreement not to be unreasonably withheld, that the required engineering, permitting and construction of the Large Load will take longer than the seven-year period.

3.7.2 After Start of the Large Load Interconnection Study.  Any request to extend the Service Commencement Date after commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study under the LLIP, or following execution of a Large Load Interconnection Agreement, shall be subject to the following:

(i) Requested extension does not exceed seven years from Service Commencement Date in the original Interconnection Request.  A request to extend the Service Commencement Date that does not exceed seven years from the Service Commencement Date in the original

Interconnection Request is subject to the modification assessment process specified in LLIP Section 4.6.1.6.1.

(ii) Requested extension exceeds seven years from Service Commencement Date in the original Interconnection Request.  A request to extend the

PDF page 141

10

Service Commencement Date that exceeds seven years from the Service Commencement Date in the original Interconnection Request shall (a) demonstrate, subject to the Participating TO’s agreement, such agreement not to be unreasonably withheld, that engineering, permitting and construction of the new Large Load or increase in capacity of the existing Large Load will take longer than the seven-year period, and (b) be subject to the modification assessment process specified in LLIP Section 4.6.1.6.1.

Any extension of the Service Commencement Date under this LLIP Section 3.7.2 shall not extend the Outside Date under any applicable Cost Recovery Agreement unless the parties to that agreement agree in writing to such extension.

3.8   Queue Position

           3.8.1 Assignment of Queue Position.
Participating TO shall assign a Queue Position as follows: the Queue Position shall be assigned based upon the date and time of receipt of all items required pursuant to the provisions of LLIP Section 4.2.1.

3.8.2 Higher Queue Position.
A higher Queue Position assigned to an Interconnection Request is one that has been placed “earlier” in the queue in relation to another Interconnection Request that is assigned a lower Queue Position.

3.9
Withdrawal
Large Load Interconnection Customer may withdraw its Interconnection Request at any time by written notice of such withdrawal to Participating TO, and the Participating TO will notify the ISO and Affected System Operators, if any, within three (3) Business Days of receipt of such a notice.  In addition, after confirmation by the Participating TO of a valid Interconnection Request, if the Large Load Interconnection Customer fails to adhere to all requirements of this LLIP, except as provided in LLIP Section 7.2 (Disputes), Participating TO shall deem the Interconnection Request to be withdrawn and shall provide written notice to Large Load Interconnection Customer of the deemed withdrawal within five (5) Business Days and an explanation of the reasons for such deemed withdrawal.  Upon receipt of such written notice, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall have five (5) Business Days in which to either (i) respond with information or action that cures the deficiency, or (ii) support its position that the deemed withdrawal was erroneous and notify the Participating TO of its intent to pursue Dispute Resolution.

For an Interconnection Request under the LLIP, withdrawal shall result in the removal of the Interconnection Request from the Interconnection Studies.  If a Large Load Interconnection Customer disputes the withdrawal and removal from the Interconnection Studies and has elected to pursue Dispute Resolution, Large

PDF page 142

11

Load Interconnection Customer's Interconnection Request will not be considered in any ongoing Interconnection Study during the Dispute Resolution process.

In the event of such withdrawal, Participating TO, subject to the provisions in LLIP Section 7.1, as applicable, shall provide, at Large Load Interconnection Customer's request, all information that Participating TO developed for any completed study conducted up to the date of withdrawal of the Interconnection Request.

3.10
Withdrawal Penalty

A Large Load Interconnection Customer with an Interconnection Request being processed under the LLIP will be subject to a Withdrawal Penalty if it withdraws its Interconnection Request or is deemed withdrawn, or the Large Load does not otherwise achieve its Service Commencement Date, which date may be modified if the necessary Participating TO-owned facilities cannot be constructed on a timeline that meets such date, unless: (1) the withdrawal does not have a material impact on the cost or timing of any equal or later queued interconnection request;

(2) Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws after receiving Large Load Interconnection Customer’s most recent Interconnection Study report and the Large Load Network Upgrade costs assigned to the Interconnection Request identified in that report have increased by more than twenty-five percent (25%) compared to costs identified in Large Load Interconnection Customer’s preceding Interconnection Study report; or (3) Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws after receiving an Interconnection Study report issued after completion of the initial Large Load Interconnection Study and the Large Load Network Upgrade costs assigned to the Interconnection Request identified in such Interconnection Study report have increased by more than one hundred percent (100%) compared to costs identified in the initial Large Load Interconnection Study report.

3.10.1 Calculation of the Withdrawal Penalty

If the Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws its Interconnection Request or is deemed withdrawn prior to the commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, Large Load Interconnection Customer will not be subject to a Withdrawal Penalty.  If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws, is deemed withdrawn, or otherwise does not achieve its Service Commencement Date at any point after the commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, that Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Withdrawal Penalty will be the greater of: (1) the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Study Deposit required under LLIP Section 4.2.1; or (2) as follows in (a)–(c):

(a) If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws or is deemed

PDF page 143

12

withdrawn during the Large Load Interconnection Study or after receipt of a Large Load Interconnection Study report, Large Load Interconnection Customer will be charged two (2) times the actual cost of the Large Load Interconnection Study performed for the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

(b) If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws or is deemed withdrawn after receipt of the draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement but before Large Load Interconnection Customer has executed a Large Load Interconnection Agreement or has requested that its Large Load Interconnection Agreement be filed unexecuted, Large Load Interconnection Customer will be charged ten percent (10%) of its estimated Large Load Network Upgrade costs as reflected in the most recently completed Interconnection Study.

(c) If Large Load Interconnection Customer has executed a Large Load Interconnection Agreement or has requested that its Large Load Interconnection Agreement be filed unexecuted and subsequently withdraws its Interconnection Request, the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Withdrawal Penalty will be calculated as set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement.

The Commercial Readiness Deposit shall be retained as financial security for the Withdrawal Penalty (if applicable) and, other than as set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, any security not applied to the Withdrawal Penalty will be released to the Large Load Interconnection Customer following withdrawal or the Service Commencement Date. Where a Cost Recovery Agreement applies, (i) any Commercial Readiness Deposit or Interconnection Financial Security provided for the same costs shall count toward, and shall not be duplicated by, the Construction Security required under the Cost Recovery Agreement, and (ii) any Withdrawal Penalty paid with respect to a Large Load Network Upgrade shall be credited against the Pre-In-Service Termination Costs payable under the Cost Recovery Agreement for that upgrade, so that the Participating TO does not recover the same costs more than once.

3.11
Standards for Co-located Inverter Based Generating Facilities

Inverters used for the production, and/or later injection from storage, of electricity shall meet the inverter certification standards of UL-1741 and UL-1741 Supplement B.

Section 4.  Large Load Study Process

4.1
Timing For Submitting Interconnection Requests
A Large Load Interconnection Customer may submit an Interconnection Request for processing under the LLIP at any time during the year. The Participating TO,

PDF page 144

13

in coordination with the ISO, as applicable, will study the Interconnection Request.

4.2
Processing of Interconnection Request

4.2.1
Initiating an Interconnection Request.  To initiate an
Interconnection Request under the LLIP, Large Load Interconnection Customer must submit all of the following: (i) an Interconnection Study Deposit of $250,000; (ii) a completed Interconnection Request in the form of Attachment 1 to the LLIP, including preferred Point of Interconnection and voltage level, all information required by Section 10A.2.1 of the TO Tariff (other than any election of interim service, which shall be made in accordance with LLIP Section 4.6.2), and all other technical data; (iii) a Commercial Readiness Deposit equal to two times the Interconnection Study Deposit described in this LLIP Section 4.2.1 in the form of an irrevocable letter of credit, cash, a surety bond, or other form of security that is reasonably acceptable to Participating TO, in accordance with LLIP Section 4.7.1. This Commercial Readiness Deposit is refunded to Large Load Interconnection Customer according to LLIP Section 3.10.1; however, the Participating TO shall be entitled to hold the Commercial Readiness Deposit until the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Large Load achieves its Service Commencement Date; and (iv) demonstration of Site Control.

4.2.1.1 Use of Interconnection Study Deposit.  The Interconnection Study Deposit shall be applied to pay for prudent costs incurred by the Participating TO, the ISO, or third parties at the direction of the Participating TO or ISO, as applicable, to perform and administer the Interconnection Studies.

In the event a Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws its Interconnection Request prior to the commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, Large Load Interconnection Customer must pay Participating TO the actual costs of processing its Interconnection Request. In the event a Large Load

Interconnection Customer withdraws after commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, Participating TO shall charge and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the actual costs of the Interconnection Studies.

Any difference between the Interconnection Study Deposit and the actual cost of the Interconnection Studies shall be paid by or refunded to the Large Load Interconnection Customer. Any

invoices for Interconnection Studies shall include a detailed and itemized accounting of the cost of each Interconnection Study. Large Load Interconnection Customers shall pay any such

undisputed costs within thirty (30) Calendar Days of receipt of an

PDF page 145

14

invoice. If Large Load Interconnection Customer fails to pay such undisputed costs within the time allotted, its Interconnection Request shall be deemed withdrawn and will be subject to a Withdrawal Penalty pursuant to LLIP Section 3.10.1.

The Participating TO will reimburse the ISO or third parties, as applicable, for all work performed on behalf of the withdrawn Interconnection Request at the Participating TO’s direction. The Large Load Interconnection Customer must pay all monies due before it is allowed to obtain any Interconnection Study data or results.

4.3
Validation of Interconnection Request

4.3.1 Acknowledgment of Interconnection Request.  Participating TO shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer within ten (10) Business Days of receipt of the Interconnection Request, which notice shall state whether the Interconnection Request is deemed valid.

4.3.2 Deficiencies in Interconnection Request.  An Interconnection Request will not be considered to be a valid request until all items in LLIP Section 4.2.1 have been received by Participating TO and deemed valid by the Participating TO.  If an Interconnection Request fails to meet the requirements set forth in LLIP Section 4.2.1, Participating TO shall include in its notification to the Large Load Interconnection Customer under LLIP Section 4.3.1 the reasons for such failure and that the Interconnection Request does not constitute a valid request.  Consistent with Section 10A.2.3 of the TO Tariff, the Participating TO will attempt to remedy deficiencies in the Interconnection Request through informal communications with the Large Load Interconnection Customer, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide Participating TO the additional requested information needed to constitute a valid request. Whenever the additional requested information is provided by the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Participating TO shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer within five (5) Business Days of receipt of the additional requested information whether the Interconnection Request is valid.  If the Interconnection Request continues to fail to meet the requirements set forth in LLIP Section 4.2.1, the Participating TO shall include in its notification to the Large Load Interconnection Customer the reasons for such failure.  If an Interconnection Request has not been deemed valid, the Large Load Interconnection Customer must submit all information necessary to meet the requirements of LLIP Section 4.2.1 no later than twenty (20) Business Days after the date the original Interconnection Request was submitted, or ten (10) Business Days after the Participating TO first provided notice that the Interconnection Request was not valid, whichever is later.

PDF page 146

15

Interconnection Requests that have not met the requirements of LLIP Section 4.2.1 within twenty (20) Business Days after the date the original Interconnection Request was submitted or ten (10) Business Days after the Participating TO first provided notice that the Interconnection Request was not valid, whichever is later, will be deemed invalid, will be returned to the Large Load Interconnection Customer in accordance with Section 10A.2.3 of the TO Tariff, and will not be included in the LLIP.

Interconnection Requests deemed invalid under this LLIP Section 4.3.2 are not subject to LLIP Section 3.9.  Large Load Interconnection Customers with invalid Interconnection Requests under this LLIP Section 4.3.2 may seek relief under LLIP Section 7.2 by so notifying the Participating TO within two (2) Business Days of the notice of invalidity.

4.4     Potential Deferral of the Interconnection Studies

           The Participating TO may defer commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study when it determines, using Good Utility Practice and reasonable engineering judgment, that the Interconnection Request cannot be studied meaningfully until other pending study results for higher-queued projects are available.

           Within ten (10) Business Days after deeming an Interconnection Request valid under LLIP Section 4.3.1, the Participating TO will notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer in writing if it is deferring the start of the Interconnection Studies. The notice will include: (i) information regarding the pending studies; and (ii) the estimated start date of the Large Load Interconnection Study.  During the period of deferral, the Participating TO will provide a monthly status update to the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

4.5
Scoping Meeting
Within fifteen (15) Business Days after the Participating TO notifies the Large Load Interconnection Customer that the Interconnection Request has been deemed valid as set forth in LLIP Section 4.3.2, the Participating TO shall establish a date agreeable to the Large Load Interconnection Customer, and the ISO, if applicable, for the Scoping Meeting.

The purpose of the Scoping Meeting shall be to discuss reasonable Service Commencement Dates and alternative interconnection options, to exchange information including any transmission data that would reasonably be expected to impact such interconnection options, to analyze such information and to determine the potential feasible Points of Interconnection and eliminate alternatives given resources and available information. The Scoping Meeting shall also include discussion of a deferral of the Interconnection Studies in accordance with LLIP Section 4.4.

PDF page 147

16

The Participating TO will bring to the meeting, as reasonably necessary to accomplish its purpose, such already available technical data, including, but not limited to, (i) general facility loadings, (ii) general instability issues, (iii) general short circuit issues, (iv) general voltage issues, and (v) general reliability issues.

The Large Load Interconnection Customer will bring to the Scoping Meeting, in addition to the technical data form (Appendix A to Attachment 1) submitted with its Interconnection Request, any system studies previously performed.  The Participating TO, the ISO, if applicable, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer will also bring to the meeting personnel and other resources as may be reasonably required to accomplish the purpose of the meeting in the time allocated for the meeting.  On the basis of the meeting, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall designate its Point of Interconnection.  The duration of the meeting shall be sufficient to accomplish its purpose.

Within five (5) Business Days after the Scoping Meeting, the Participating TO shall tender to the Large Load Interconnection Customer a Large Load Study Process Agreement developed by the Participating TO consistent with the TO Tariff and this LLIP, which shall contain an outline of the scope of the Large Load Interconnection Study, contain a non-binding good faith estimate of the cost to perform such study, and shall specify that the Large Load Interconnection Customer is responsible for the actual cost of the Interconnection Studies, including reasonable administrative costs.  No later than thirty (30) Calendar Days after the Scoping Meeting, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall either execute and deliver to the Participating TO  the Large Load Study Process Agreement or request that the Participating TO file it unexecuted with FERC, or the Interconnection Request shall be deemed withdrawn.  If an unexecuted filing is requested, the Interconnection Studies will be paused until the Large Load Study Process Agreement is made effective by FERC.

4.6
Interconnection Studies
The Interconnection Studies shall consist of a Large Load Interconnection Study, any FILI Study requested pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.2, and any additional studies, including but not limited to those identified in LLIP Section 4.6.3 required for interconnection of the Large Load, and co-located generation not exporting to the ISO Controlled Grid, to the Participating TO’s Transmission System.    The analysis of impacts on, and upgrades required to, the Participating TO’s Transmission System will be performed by the Participating TO and will be subject to the review and concurrence of the ISO in accordance with Section 25.7 of the ISO Tariff. The Interconnection Studies will identify Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and required Large Load Network Upgrades necessary to mitigate thermal overloads and voltage violations, and address short circuit, stability, and reliability issues associated with the requested Transmission Interconnection Service.

PDF page 148

17

All cost estimates for Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, as applicable, contained in the Interconnection Studies will be set forth in the Interconnection Study report in present dollar costs as well as time-adjusted dollar costs, adjusted to the estimated year of construction of the components being constructed.

The Participating TO will prepare an Interconnection Study report to document the results of the Interconnection Studies.

4.6.1 Large Load Interconnection Study.

4.6.1.1  Scope of the Large Load Interconnection Study.  The Large Load Interconnection Study will consist of a  short circuit analysis, a power flow analysis, an electromagnetic transient (“EMT”) screening, a non-binding good faith estimate of the scope and cost of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, and any other studies that are deemed necessary. The Large Load Interconnection Study will also identify the need for any additional studies (e.g., stability analysis, EMT study, etc.) to be performed as part of the scope of work set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.3.  When there is co-located generation not exporting to the ISO Controlled Grid and not subject to electrical short circuit duty dependence, the Large Load Interconnection Study will identify the impacts associated with the Large Load and any incremental impacts of the co-located generation.

The Large Load Interconnection Study will consider the use of alternative transmission technologies including dynamic line ratings, advanced power flow control devices, advanced conductors, and transmission switching, when evaluating transmission solutions to accommodate new Large Loads and their associated transmission system impacts.  If traditional network upgrades are selected instead of alternative transmission technologies, the Participating TO will provide a demonstration of why alternative transmission technologies are not feasible or would not result in lower costs or a faster timeline for accommodating the interconnection of the Large Load.

The Large Load Interconnection Study shall state the assumptions upon which it is based, state the results of the analyses, and provide the requirement or potential impediments to providing the requested Transmission Interconnection Service, including an estimate of the cost and length of time that would be necessary to correct any problems identified in those analyses and implement the Interconnection.

PDF page 149

18

The Large Load Interconnection Study shall provide a list of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, as applicable, that are required as a result of the Interconnection Request.

4.6.1.2 Timing of the Large Load Interconnection Study Results.  The Participating TO shall use Reasonable Efforts to complete the Large Load Interconnection Study within ninety (90) Calendar Days after the execution of a Large Load Study Process Agreement or, if the Large Load Study Process Agreement is filed unexecuted, after it is made effective by FERC.  The Participating TO will issue a Large Load Interconnection Study report to the Large Load Interconnection Customer. The Participating TO will share study results with the ISO for review and concurrence.

At any time the Participating TO determines that it will not meet the required time frame for completing the Large Load Interconnection  Study, Participating TO shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer as to the schedule status of the Large Load Interconnection Study and provide an estimated completion date with an explanation of the reasons why additional time is required.

Upon request, the Participating TO shall provide the Large Load Interconnection Customer all supporting documentation, workpapers and relevant pre-Interconnection Request and post- Interconnection Request power flow and short circuit databases for the Large Load Interconnection Study, subject to confidentiality arrangements consistent with LLIP Section 7.1.

Should the Large Load Interconnection Customer provide written comments on the Large Load Interconnection Study report at least five (5) Business Days before the Report Meeting conducted to discuss the report, the Participating TO will address the written comments in the Large Load Interconnection Study Report Meeting.  Should the Large Load Interconnection Customer provide comments at any later time (up to the time of the Report Meeting), then such comments shall be considered informal inquiries to which the Participating TO will provide informal, informational responses at the Report Meeting, to the extent possible.

The Large Load Interconnection Customer may submit, in writing, additional comments on the final Large Load Interconnection Study report up to three (3) Business Days following the Report Meeting.  Based on any discussion at the Report Meeting and any comments received from the Large Load Interconnection Customer

PDF page 150

19

or the ISO, the Participating TO will determine whether it is necessary to follow the Large Load Interconnection Study report with a revised study report.  The Participating TO will issue any such revised report to the Large Load Interconnection Customer no later than fifteen (15) Business Days following the Report Meeting. If the ISO has not completed its review of the Large Load Interconnection Study report, the Participating TO shall delay its determination of whether a revised study report is required until that review is complete.

4.6.1.3 Large Load Interconnection Study Report Meeting.  A Report Meeting shall be held among the Participating TO, the ISO, if applicable, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer to discuss the results of the Large Load Interconnection Study, including assigned cost responsibility.  Any such Report Meeting will be held within ten (10) Business Days of the date the Large Load Interconnection Study report is provided to the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

4.6.1.4 Commercial Readiness Deposit.  Within thirty (30) Calendar Days after receiving the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide an additional Commercial Readiness Deposit sufficient to bring the aggregate Commercial Readiness Deposit to twenty percent (20%) of the Participating TO’s then-current estimated construction cost of the Large Load Network Upgrades identified in that report that are attributable to the Interconnection Request.  If the final Large Load Interconnection Study report is revised following ISO review, the required Commercial Readiness Deposit shall be adjusted to reflect twenty percent (20%) of the corresponding estimated construction cost identified in the revised final report, and any required increase shall be provided within thirty (30) Calendar Days after issuance of that report.

The Commercial Readiness Deposit secures the Withdrawal Penalty and other pre-in-service obligations under this LLIP and the Large Load Interconnection Agreement. It does not establish a direct assignment of the Large Load Network Upgrade costs, constitute a payment toward an Annual MTRC, reduce the Total Required Revenue Commitment, or satisfy Operational Security under a Cost Recovery Agreement.

PDF page 151

20

To the extent a Commercial Readiness Deposit is in a form permitted under Schedule 3 of an applicable Cost Recovery Agreement, LS Power Grid may elect in writing to credit it dollar- for-dollar toward required Construction Security under that agreement. No amount of Commercial Readiness Deposit may be applied or credited more than once. Any portion not so credited shall be administered and released under Sections 3.9 and 3.10 of this LLIP and the Large Load Interconnection Agreement.

4.6.1.5 Restudy. If restudy of the Large Load Interconnection Study is required due to a higher queued project dropping out of the queue, or a modification of a higher queued project subject to LLIP Section 4.6.1.6, Participating TO shall notify Large Load Interconnection Customer in writing. Such restudy shall take no longer than forty-five (45) Calendar Days from the date of notice. Any cost of restudy shall be borne by the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

4.6.1.6 Modifications.  At any time during the course of the Interconnection Studies, the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Participating TO, or the ISO, as applicable, may identify changes to the planned interconnection that may improve the costs and benefits (including reliability) of the interconnection, and the ability of the proposed change to accommodate the Interconnection Request.  To the extent the identified changes are acceptable to the Participating TO, the ISO, as applicable, and Large Load Interconnection Customer, such acceptance not to be unreasonably withheld, Participating TO shall modify the configuration in accordance with such changes without altering the Interconnection Request’s eligibility for participating in Interconnection Studies.

4.6.1.6.1 For any modifications other than those agreed upon under LLIP Section 4.6.1.6, the Large Load Interconnection Customer must first request that Participating TO evaluate whether such modification is a Material Modification.  In response to Large Load Interconnection Customer's

request, Participating TO, in coordination with the ISO, if applicable, shall evaluate the proposed modifications prior to making them and inform Large Load Interconnection Customer in writing of whether the modifications would constitute a Material Modification.  The Participating TO may, at its option, engage the services of the ISO to assist in the assessment of the modification.
