9.19.1 Applicability and
Determination of Facilities. A Participating TO shall
perform a study in accordance with the Section where the Participating TO is obligated to construct or expand facilities in accordance with Section 24 and Appendix DD of the CAISO Tariff and this TO Tariff (other than if the obligation to expand or construct is pursuant to Section 10 or 10A of this TO Tariff or the CAISO’s generator interconnection procedures).

9.2
Obligation to Build.

9.2.1 Due Diligence to Construct. Subject to Section 9.3.3 of this TO Tariff, the Participating TO shall use due diligence to construct, within a reasonable time, additions or upgrades to its transmission system that it is obligated to construct pursuant to the CAISO Tariff and this TO Tariff.

9.2.2 Delay in Construction or Expansion. If any event occurs that will materially affect the time for completion of new facilities, or the ability to complete them, the Participating TO shall promptly notify the CAISO with regard to facilities determined to be needed.  The Participating TO also shall make available to such Project Sponsor and the CAISO, as the case may be, studies related to the cause and extent of the delay and the Participating TO’s ability to complete the new facilities, and evaluate any options available. The Participating TO also shall make available to such Project Sponsor and the CAISO, as the case may be, studies reasonably needed to evaluate the alternatives.

9.3
Provisions Relating To Transmission Construction On the System Of Other

PDF page 193

25
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

TOs.  A Participating TO shall not be responsible for making arrangements for any engineering, permitting, and construction of transmission or distribution facilities on the system(s) of any other entity or for obtaining any regulatory approval for such facilities.  The Participating TO will undertake reasonable efforts through the coordinated planning process to assist in making such arrangements, including, without limitation, providing any information or data required by such other electric system pursuant to Good Utility Practice.

10. TSWL
Interconnection Process.  All requests for TSWL Interconnection
directly to with the CAISO Controlled Grid from parties eligible Projects or any other Regional Transmission Facilities, or Entitlements turned over to request such Interconnection the CAISO’s operational control by LS Power Grid shall be processed pursuant directed to the provisions of the CAISO TariffParticipating TO.

10.1
Applicability. All requests for TSWL Interconnection directly to the CAISO Controlled Grid from parties eligible to request such Interconnection consistent with Section 210(a) of the FPA shall be processed pursuant to the provisions of this Section 10. All requests for Interconnection of wholesale generation directly to the CAISO Controlled Grid shall be processed pursuant to the provisions of the CAISO Tariff. Requests for interconnection submitted by an ECLL on behalf of Large Load Customers shall be processed pursuant to Section 10A of this TO Tariff.

10.2
TSWL Interconnection Applications. An applicant requesting TSWL
Interconnection shall submit a written TSWL Interconnection Application which provides the information detailed below to the Participating TO. The Participating TO shall timestamp the application to establish study priority.

(i) the identity, address, telephone number, and email of the applicant requesting TSWL Interconnection;

(ii) the Interconnection point(s) to the CAISO Controlled Grid contemplated by the applicant;

(iii) the resultant (or new) maximum amount of TSWL Interconnection capacity contemplated by the applicant;

(iv) the proposed date for energizing the TSWL Interconnection and the term of the interconnection service; and

(v) such other information as the Participating TO reasonably requires to process the application.

In addition to the information specified above, the following information may also be provided in order to properly evaluate system conditions:

(vi) If the applicant is a wholesale load, the electrical location of the source of the power (if known) to be transmitted pursuant to applicant’s request for Interconnection. If the source of the power is not known, a system purchase will be assumed. If the location of the load is not known, a system sale will be assumed; and, in addition, if an applicant proposes to perform or cause a third party to perform any required System Impact

PDF page 194

26
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

Study or any required Facilities Study, it shall so indicate in its TSWL Interconnection Application. The results of any study or studies performed by an applicant must be approved by both the CAISO and the Participating TO.

Within ten (10) Business Days after receipt of a TSWL Interconnection Application, the Participating TO and the CAISO if applicable shall determine whether the application is complete (“Completed TSWL Interconnection Application”). Whenever possible, the Participating TO will attempt to remedy deficiencies in the TSWL Interconnection Application through informal communications with the applicant. If such efforts are unsuccessful, the Participating TO shall return the TSWL Interconnection Application to the applicant.

The Participating TO will treat the information in the TSWL Interconnection Application, including the applicant’s identity, as confidential at the request of the applicant except to the extent that disclosure of the information is required by this TO Tariff, by regulatory or judicial order, for reliability purposes pursuant to Good Utility Practice, or pursuant to RTG or CAISO transmission information sharing agreements. The Participating TO shall treat this information consistent with the standards of conduct contained in Part 37 of FERC’s regulations.

10.2.1 Amendment to Completed TSWL Interconnection Application. An applicant shall only be limited to amending its Completed TSWL Interconnection Application once. Such amendment shall occur on or before ten (10) Business Days following the date the Participating TO tenders any Facilities Study Agreement. Specifically, an applicant may submit an amendment to its Completed TSWL Interconnection

Application to reflect a revised configuration for its New Facility. The amended Completed TSWL Interconnection Application shall be treated in accordance with Section 25 of the CAISO Tariff and Section 10.4 of this TO Tariff; the applicant’s Completed TSWL Interconnection Application shall be deemed withdrawn; and the applicant shall maintain its existing queue position, if (a) the amended Completed TSWL Interconnection Application is received by the Participating TO within ten (10) Business Days of the Participating TO’s tender of a Facilities Study Agreement; and

(b) the applicant has not submitted a previous amendment to the Completed TSWL Interconnection Application. In the event an applicant amends its Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, it will be responsible for any additional study costs that result from that amendment, including costs associated with revisions to studies for other applicants holding later queue positions.

10.3
Review of Completed TSWL Interconnection Application. After receiving a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, the Participating TO and the CAISO, if applicable, will determine on a non-discriminatory basis whether a

PDF page 195

27
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

System Impact Study is required. Whenever the Participating TO, and the CAISO, if applicable, determines that a System Impact Study is not required and that neither Reliability Upgrades nor changes in existing operating procedures are required, the Participating TO shall notify the applicant within fifteen (15) Business Days of the Completed Application Date. If the TSWL Interconnection can be accommodated without any Direct Assignment Facilities, then within thirty (30) Business Days of such notice from the Participating TO, the applicant shall request the Participating TO to tender to the applicant an Interconnection Agreement within thirty (30) Business Days of such request. The Participating TO shall tender to the applicant an Interconnection Agreement as provided in Section 10.9. If the Participating TO determines upon review of the Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, that Direct Assignment Facilities are required, the Participating TO shall tender to the applicant a Facilities Study Agreement within twenty (20) Business Days of the Completed Application Date and continue the Interconnection process pursuant to Section 10.7.

10.4
Notice of Need for System Impact Study. If the Participating TO, and the CAISO, if applicable, determines that a System Impact Study is necessary to accommodate the requested Interconnection, the Participating TO shall so inform the applicant as soon as practicable. In such cases, the Participating TO shall within twenty (20) Business Days of receipt of a Completed TSWL

Interconnection Application, tender a System Impact Study Agreement that defines the scope, content, assumptions and terms of reference for such study to be completed by the Participating TO, the estimated time required to complete it, and such other provisions as the parties may reasonably require, and pursuant to which the applicant shall agree to reimburse the Participating TO for the reasonable actual costs of performing the required System Impact Study. A description of the Participating TO’s transmission assessment practices for completing a System Impact Study shall be provided in the Participating TO’s FERC Form 715.

Alternatively, if the applicant will perform the System Impact Study, the Participating TO shall within twenty (20) Business Days of receipt of a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, tender a System Impact Study Agreement that defines the scope, content, assumptions and terms of reference for such study to be reviewed by the Participating TO; the estimated time required to complete it; and such other provisions as the parties may reasonably require, and pursuant to which the applicant shall agree to reimburse the Participating TO for the reasonable actual costs of reviewing the required System Impact Study.

For a TSWL Interconnection request to remain a Completed TSWL
Interconnection Application, the applicant shall execute the System Impact Study Agreement and return it to the Participating TO within ten (10) Business Days together with payment for the reasonable estimated cost of performing the System Impact Study or reviewing the applicant’s System Impact Study. Alternatively, if the applicant requests the Participating TO to proceed with the System Impact

PDF page 196

28
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

Study or review thereof and commits to abide by the terms, conditions, and cost assignments ultimately determined under the CAISO ADR Procedures, including any determination by FERC or appeal of a FERC determination in accordance with that process, the Participating TO shall promptly proceed with the System Impact Study provided that such request is accompanied by payment of the reasonable estimated cost of the System Impact Study, and the parties shall submit the disputed terms for resolution under the CAISO ADR Procedures. If the applicant elects not to execute a System Impact Study Agreement, and does not request that the Participating TO proceed with the System Impact Study or review thereof, its application shall be deemed withdrawn, and the applicant shall reimburse to the Participating TO all costs reasonably incurred in processing the application.

10.5
System Impact Study Cost Reimbursement and Agreement.

10.5.1 Cost Reimbursement. The System Impact Study Agreement shall clearly specify the charge, based on the Participating TO’s estimate of the cost and time for completion of the System Impact Study. The charge shall not exceed the reasonable actual cost of the study. In performing the System Impact Study, the Participating TO shall rely, to the extent reasonably practicable, on existing transmission planning studies. The applicant will not be assessed a charge for such existing studies; however, the applicant will be responsible for the reasonable charges associated with any modifications to existing planning studies that are reasonably necessary to evaluate the impact on the applicant’s request.

10.6
System Impact Study Procedures. Upon receipt of an executed System Impact Study Agreement or initiation of the CAISO ADR Procedures and receipt of payment for estimated study costs, the Participating TO will use due diligence to either (a) complete the required System Impact Study within a sixty (60) calendar day period or (b) complete its review of an applicant’s System Impact Study within thirty (30) calendar days of its receipt of the completed study. The System Impact Study will identify whether any Direct Assignment Facilities or Reliability Upgrades are necessary to deliver a New Facility’s full output over the CAISO Controlled Grid, or any transmission additions or upgrades are necessary to serve a wholesale load. The System Impact Study will also identify any adverse impact on Encumbrances existing as of the applicant’s Completed Application Date. In the event that the Participating TO is unable to complete the required System Impact Study within such time period, it shall so notify the applicant, in writing, and provide an estimated completion date along with an explanation of the reasons why additional time is required to complete the required studies. A copy of the completed System Impact Study and related work papers shall be made available to the applicant and the CAISO. The Participating TO shall notify the applicant and the CAISO immediately upon completion of the System Impact Study.

PDF page 197

29
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

10.6.1 Procedures Upon Completion of System Impact Study. Within fifteen
(15) Business Days of completion of the System Impact Study or review and approval of an applicant’s System Impact Study, the Participating TO shall notify the applicant whether the transmission system will be adequate to accommodate all of a request for Interconnection. If no costs are likely to be incurred for any Direct Assignment Facilities, any Reliability Upgrades, or implementing any operating procedures, then within thirty

(30) Business Days of receipt of written approval of the applicant’s System Impact Study from the Participating TO and the CAISO the

applicant shall request the Participating TO to tender an Interconnection Agreement within thirty (30) Business Days of such request. The

Participating TO shall tender to the applicant an Interconnection Agreement as provided in Section 10.9. If costs are likely to be incurred to accommodate a request for Interconnection, the Participating TO shall tender to the applicant a Facilities Study Agreement pursuant to Section 10.7.

10.7
Notice of Need for Facilities Study. If a System Impact Study indicates that additions or upgrades to the CAISO Controlled Grid are needed to satisfy an applicant’s request for interconnection, the Participating TO shall, within fifteen

(15) Business Days of the date of the System Impact Study or the completion of review and approval of the applicant’s System Impact Study by the Participating TO, tender to the applicant a Facilities Study Agreement that defines the scope, content, assumptions and terms of reference for such study to be completed by the Participating TO; the estimated time required to complete the required study; and such other provisions as the parties may reasonably require, and pursuant to which the applicant agrees to reimburse the Participating TO for the reasonable actual costs of performing the required Facilities Study. Alternatively, if the applicant will perform the Facilities Study, the Participating TO shall within fifteen (15) Business Days of the completion date of the System Impact Study or the completion of review and approval of the applicant’s System Impact Study, tender a Facilities Study Agreement that defines the scope, content, assumptions and terms of reference for such study to be reviewed by the Participating TO; the estimated time required to complete the required review; and such other provisions as the parties may reasonably require, and pursuant to which the applicant agrees to reimburse the Participating TO for the reasonable actual costs of reviewing the required Facilities Study. For a TSWL Interconnection request to remain a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, the applicant shall execute the Facilities Study Agreement and return it to the Participating TO within ten (10) Business Days together with payment for the reasonable estimated costs of performing the Facilities Study or reviewing the applicant’s Facilities Study. Alternatively, if the applicant requests the Participating TO to proceed with the Facilities Study or review thereof and commits to abide by terms, conditions, and cost assignments ultimately determined under the CAISO ADR Procedures, including any determination by FERC or appeal of a FERC determination in accordance with that process, the Participating TO shall

PDF page 198

30
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

promptly proceed with the Facilities Study provided that such request is accompanied by payment for the reasonable estimated cost of the Facilities Study, and the parties shall submit the disputed terms for resolution under the CAISO ADR Procedures. If the applicant elects not to execute a Facilities Study Agreement and does not request that the Participating TO proceed with the Facilities Study or review thereof, its application shall be deemed withdrawn and the applicant shall reimburse to the Participating TO all costs reasonably incurred in processing the application not covered by the System Impact Study Agreement.

10.8
Facilities Study Procedures. Upon receipt of an executed Facilities Study Agreement or initiation of the CAISO ADR Procedures and receipt of payment for the estimated study costs, the Participating TO will use due diligence to either

(a) complete the required Facilities Study within a sixty (60) calendar day period or (b) complete its review of an applicant’s Facilities Study within thirty (30) calendar days of its receipt of the Completed Study. In the event that Participating TO is unable to complete the required Facilities Study within such time period, it shall so notify the applicant, in writing, and provide an estimated completion date along with an explanation of the reasons why additional time is required to complete the required studies. A copy of the completed Facilities Study shall be made available to the applicant.

10.8.1 Tender of Interconnection Agreement. Within thirty (30) Business Days of receipt of the completed Facilities Study performed by the Participating TO or receipt of written approval of the applicant’s Facilities Study from the Participating TO, the applicant shall request the Participating TO to tender an Interconnection Agreement within thirty (30) Business Days of such request. The Participating TO shall tender to the applicant an Interconnection Agreement as provided in Section 10.9.

10.9
Interconnection Agreement. Pursuant to Section 10.3, 10.6.1, or 10.8.1, a party requesting TSWL Interconnection shall request in writing that the Participating TO tender to such party an Interconnection Agreement that will be filed with FERC, or the Local Regulatory Authority, in the case of a Local Publicly Owned Electric Utility. The Interconnection Agreement shall include, without limitation, cost responsibilities and payment provisions for any engineering, equipment, construction, ownership, operation and maintenance costs for any Direct Assignment Facilities, any Reliability Upgrades, and for any other mitigation measures. The Interconnection Agreement will set forth a payment schedule that enables the Participating TO to recover its costs.

For a TSWL Interconnection request to remain a Completed TSWL
Interconnection Application, the applicant requesting the TSWL Interconnection or the entity with whom the Participating TO will interconnect for the Interconnection shall execute the Interconnection Agreement and return it to the Participating TO within thirty (30) Business Days of receipt. Alternatively, if an applicant requesting the TSWL Interconnection or the entity with whom the

PDF page 199

31
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

Participating TO will interconnect for the Interconnection requests the Participating TO to file an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement and commits to abide by the terms, conditions, and cost assignments determined to be just and reasonable under the CAISO ADR Procedures, including any determination by FERC or on appeal of a FERC determination in accordance with that process, the Participating TO shall promptly file an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement. Provided, however, that if the CAISO ADR Procedures concerns whether the requesting entity is eligible for a TSWL Interconnection, the Participating TO shall not be obligated to file an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement or commence construction of any facilities or incur other costs under the Interconnection Agreement until a final order determining the just and reasonable rates, terms, and conditions for such Interconnection Agreement has been issued by the applicable court or regulatory authority. If the applicant or the entity with whom the Participating TO will interconnect for the Interconnection elects not to execute the Interconnection Agreement and does not request the Participating TO to file an unexecuted Interconnection Agreement, its Completed TSWL Interconnection Application shall be deemed withdrawn, and the applicant shall reimburse to the Participating TO all costs reasonably incurred in processing the application not covered by any System Impact Study Agreement or Facilities Study Agreement.

Where a TSWL Interconnection will serve one or more Large Loads, as a condition to the Participating TO’s design, procurement, construction, or operation of any Large Load-Driven Upgrades (as defined in the pro forma Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement set forth in Appendix V) associated with the TSWL Interconnection, the applicant or Eligible Customer shall also, within the same period and subject to the same consequences as the Interconnection Agreement, execute and maintain a Cost Recovery Agreement substantially in the form set forth in Appendix V to this TO Tariff, or, if the applicant or Eligible Customer requests that the Cost Recovery Agreement be filed unexecuted with FERC, comply with that agreement as made effective by FERC, in each case as applicable to the requested TSWL Interconnection. Disputes arising under a Cost Recovery Agreement shall be resolved under the dispute resolution provisions of that agreement.

10.10 Partial Interim Service. If the Participating TO determines that there will not be adequate transmission capability to satisfy the full amount requested in a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application, the Participating TO nonetheless shall be obligated to offer and provide the portion of the requested TSWL Interconnection that can be accommodated without any additional Direct Assignment Facilities or Reliability Upgrades. However, the Participating TO shall not be obligated to provide the incremental amount of requested TSWL Interconnection that requires such additional facilities or upgrades until such facilities or upgrades have been placed in service.

10.11 Expedited Interconnection Procedures. In lieu of the procedures set forth

PDF page 200

32
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

above, the applicant shall have the option to expedite the processing of its Completed TSWL Interconnection Application. In order to exercise this option, the applicant shall submit in writing a Request for Expedited Interconnection Procedures to the Participating TO within ten (10) Business Days after receiving a copy of the System Impact Study for the proposed TSWL Interconnection. Within ten (10) Business Days after receiving a Request for Expedited Interconnection Procedures, the Participating TO shall tender an Expedited Interconnection Agreement that requires the applicant to compensate the Participating TO for all costs reasonably incurred pursuant to the terms of this TO Tariff for processing the Completed TSWL Interconnection Application and providing the requested TSWL Interconnection. While the Participating TO agrees to provide the applicant with its best estimate of the costs of any needed Direct Assignment Facilities and, if applicable, Reliability Upgrades, and such other charges that may be incurred, unless otherwise agreed by the parties, such estimate shall not be binding and the applicant must agree in writing to compensate the Participating TO for all actual TSWL Interconnection costs reasonably incurred pursuant to the provisions of this TO Tariff. The applicant shall execute and return such Expedited Interconnection Agreement within ten (10) Business Days of its receipt or the applicant’s request for TSWL Interconnection will cease to be a Completed TSWL Interconnection Application and will be deemed terminated and withdrawn. In that event, the applicant shall reimburse the Participating TO for all costs reasonably incurred in processing the application not covered by the terms of the System Impact Study Agreement.

10A. Interconnection Process for ECLL Interconnections. All requests for ECLL Interconnection shall be directed to the Participating TO.

10A.1 Applicability. An ECLL Interconnection must be requested in order to connect the facilities of the Participating TO to the facilities of an ECLL that has received a request to connect and provide retail transmission service to a Large Load Customer.

10A.2 ECLL Application and Application Fee

10A.2.1
Application Contents. An ECLL requesting an ECLL
Interconnection shall submit an ECLL Interconnection Application in the form of Attachment 1 to Appendix VI, which provides the following required information, and shall send a copy of the application to the CAISO. The ECLL Interconnection Application and all supporting

documentation shall be submitted electronically to the Participating TO in the manner posted on the Participating TO’s public website.

(a) the identity, address, telephone number, and email of the ECLL;
(b) the Point of Withdrawal;
(c) a proposed Large Load Customer load ramp schedule identifying anticipated load additions over time and the Large Load

PDF page 201

33
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

Customer’s maximum load;
(d) a site plan;
(e) documentation demonstrating that the Large Load Customer has Site Control;

(f) documentation demonstrating that the Large Load Customer has local permits and regulatory approvals for development of its Large Load;

(g) a single-line electrical diagram;
(h) the electrical location of the source of the power (if known) to be transmitted pursuant to the ECLL’s request for ECLL

Interconnection. If the source of the power is not known, a system purchase will be assumed;

(i) disclosure of ECLL interconnection requests in the ECLL’s footprint that are for the same Large Load Customer or any of its affiliate(s);

(j) a completed technical data form in the form of Appendix A to Attachment 1 to Appendix VI;

(k) if the Large Load Customer will be a Co-Located Large Load, information concerning the relevant Generating Facility must be provided by the ECLL. Such information may be either: (1) if the Generating Facility has not signed a generator interconnection agreement, the identifying queue number of the Generating Facility; or (2) if the Generating Facility has signed a generator interconnection agreement, information that will allow the Participating TO to identify such agreement; and

(l) the proposed date for energizing the ECLL Interconnection and the proposed term of the ECLL Interconnection service.

10A.2.2
Participating TO Review of ECLL Interconnection
Application. Within ten (10) Business Days after receipt of an ECLL Interconnection Application, the Participating TO shall determine whether the application is complete (“Completed ECLL Interconnection Application”). Wherever possible, the Participating TO will attempt to remedy deficiencies in the ECLL Interconnection Application through informal communications with the ECLL. If such efforts are unsuccessful, the Participating TO shall return the ECLL Interconnection Application to the ECLL.

The Participating TO will treat the information provided in the ECLL Interconnection Application, including the ECLL’s and Large Load Customer’s identity, as confidential at the request of the ECLL except to the extent that disclosure of this information is required by this TO Tariff or the CAISO Tariff, by regulatory or judicial order, for reliability purposes pursuant to Good Utility Practice, or pursuant to RTG or CAISO transmission information sharing agreements. The Participating TO shall treat this information consistent with the standards of conduct contained in

PDF page 202

34
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

Part 37 of FERC’s regulations.

10A.3 Study Process and ECLL Interconnection Agreement. ECLL Interconnection Applications shall be processed, and ECLL Interconnection Agreements shall be tendered, executed, and filed, in accordance with the Large Load Interconnection Procedures set forth in Appendix VI. Disputes arising under a Cost Recovery Agreement shall be resolved under the dispute resolution provisions of that agreement.

10A.4 Interim Service

10A.4.1
Interim Firm Interconnection Service and Flexible Interim
Load Interconnection (FILI) Service. If the ECLL has requested Interim Firm Interconnection Service and/or FILI Service, the Participating TO shall perform an analysis and report the extent to which the requested interim services can be provided, prior to such time that all ECLL Interconnection Facilities are in service and the Large Load Customer is authorized to withdraw Energy. However, the Participating TO shall not be obligated to provide any amount of Firm Interconnection Service requested until necessary facilities have been placed in service. FILI Service will be available for all remaining Load that cannot receive Interim Firm Interconnection Service, if the Large Load Customer has the ability to reduce the load at a specified point of interconnection according to the timelines and protocols set forth in an executed and effective FILI Service Agreement, until the ECLL Interconnection Facilities are in service. As ECLL Interconnection Facilities are placed into service and transmission interconnection capability is increased, available quantities of Firm Interconnection Service and FILI Service will be updated and reflected in the applicable FILI Service Agreement.

If the Large Load Customer seeks FILI Service, the ECLL will ensure that the Large Load Customer signs a FILI Service Agreement developed by the Participating TO, or asks for the agreement to be filed unexecuted.

10A.4.2
Study Process for Interim Service and Determination of
Availability of Interim Firm Interconnection Service and FILI Service. The Participating TO shall perform a FILI Study in accordance with Section 4.6.2 of Appendix VI to determine the maximum amount of any Interim Firm Interconnection Service, if any, and FILI Service, if any, that may be provided based on the anticipated availability of transmission capacity once the Large Load Customer is permitted to withdraw energy by the ECLL. The Participating TO shall update this analysis under the terms of the FILI Service Agreement, at least annually.

The actual amount of any FILI Service to be provided during any operating period shall be determined by the Participating TO through

PDF page 203

35
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

operational assessments and analysis conducted closer to real-time operating conditions. Accordingly, the maximum FILI Service established in the annual analysis does not guarantee the availability of such service under actual system conditions.

10A.4.3
Conditions Related to FILI Service

10A.4.3.1
FILI Service Reliability Requirements. The FILI Service Agreement will identify what ECLL Interconnection Facilities must be installed, the necessary protection and control systems and any other equipment necessary to be installed, and operating limitations to allow the Large Load Customer to withdraw energy (“FILI Service Reliability Requirements”). FILI Service shall not commence until the Participating TO and ECLL have completed installation and commissioning of all equipment specified under the FILI Service Agreement.

10A.4.3.2
Curtailment of FILI Service. FILI Service is subject to curtailment at all times, unless the Participating TO’s right to curtail is expressly conditioned or otherwise limited in an executed and effective FILI Service Agreement. A Large Load Customer receiving FILI Service shall be subject to curtailment prior to any curtailment of customers receiving Firm Interconnection Service. The Participating TO and CAISO may curtail FILI Service as necessary to maintain the reliable operation of the CAISO Controlled Grid, including during normal operating conditions.

10A.4.3.3
Exceedance of Authorized FILI Service. An ECLL shall
ensure that the operating instructions issued by the Participating TO pursuant to a FILI Service Agreement are not exceeded at the relevant Point of Withdrawal. The Participating TO shall have the right to disconnect service to the Large Load Customer in the event that energy withdrawals violate the terms of the FILI Service Agreement. The Participating TO shall follow Good Utility Practice in carrying out any such disconnection and subsequent reconnection when the conditions for such reconnection set forth in the FILI Service Agreement have been met. Nothing in this TO Tariff prevents the CAISO from applying any financial penalties applicable to Large Load Customers.

10A.5 Co-Located Large Load. The interconnection processes applicable to a Co- Located Large Load shall be determined based on whether the Generating Facility is a (1) non-exporting Generating Facility interconnected in accordance with the generator interconnection rules of the applicable ECLL, as approved by its Local Regulatory Authority (including, for a UDC subject to the jurisdiction of the CPUC, CPUC Electric Rule 21); or (2) an exporting Generating Facility subject to

PDF page 204

36
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

the applicable CAISO generator interconnection procedures and requirements. The ECLL for a Co-Located Large Load connecting to facilities of an exporting Generating Facility will be subject to this Section 10A.

For a Co-Located Large Load interconnected to the facilities of an exporting Generating Facility, the CAISO will be a party to a pro forma agreement that bridges the pro forma Large Generator Interconnection Agreement (LGIA) with the ECLL Interconnection Agreement, or will negotiate a non-conforming LGIA to account for certain details of the Co-Located Large Load interconnection on an individual basis. The Generating Facility developer will also execute a CAISO pro forma Participating Generator Agreement for the Generating Facility, but not a Participating Load Agreement for the Co-Located Large Load.

10A.6 Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement. As a condition to the Transmission Owner’s design, procurement, construction, or operation of a Large Load-Driven Upgrade, the Eligible Customer shall execute and maintain a Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement substantially in the form set forth in Appendix V to this TO Tariff, as applicable to the requested service and associated Large Load Facility.

11. Uncontrollable Forces and Indemnification

11.1
Procedures to Follow If Uncontrollable Force Occurs. In the event of the occurrence of an Uncontrollable Force which prevents the Participating TO or a Market Participant from performing any of its obligations under this TO Tariff, the affected entity shall (i) if it is the Participating TO, immediately give notice to the Market Participants in writing of the occurrence of such Uncontrollable Force and, if it is a Market Participant, immediately give notice to the Participating TO of the occurrence of such  uncontrollable  Uncontrollable Force; (ii) not be entitled to suspend performance in any greater or longer duration than is required by the Uncontrollable Force, (iii) use its best efforts to mitigate the effects of such Uncontrollable Force, remedy its inability to perform, and resume full performance hereunder, (iv) in the case of the Participating TO, keep the Market Participants apprised of such efforts and, in the case of the Market Participants, keep the Participating TO apprised of mitigation efforts, in each case on a continual basis; and (v) provide written notice of the resumption of performance hereunder. Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, the settlement of any strike, lockout, or labor dispute constituting an Uncontrollable Force shall be within the sole discretion of the entity affected thereby, and the requirement that a Participating TO or Market Participant must use its best efforts to remedy the cause of the Uncontrollable Force and mitigate its effects and resume full performance hereunder shall not apply to strikes, lockouts, or labor disputes.  No Market Participant or Participating TO will be considered in default as to any obligation under this TO Tariff if prevented from fulfilling the obligation due to the occurrence of an Uncontrollable Force.

11.2
Indemnification.  A Market Participant shall at all times indemnify, defend, and

PDF page 205

37
LS Power GridLSPGC TO Tariff

save the Participating TO harmless from any and all damages, losses, claims, (including claims and actions relating to injury or to death of any person or damage to property), demands, suits, recoveries, costs and expenses, court costs, attorney fees, and all other obligations by or to third parties, arising out of or resulting from the Participating TO’s performance of its obligations under this TO Tariff on behalf of a Market Participant, except in cases of negligence or intentional wrongdoing by the Participating TO.

12. Regulatory Filings. Nothing contained herein shall be construed as affecting, in any way, the right of any electric utility (as defined by the Federal Power Act) Participating TO furnishing services in accordance with this TO Tariff, CAISO Tariff or any tariff and rate schedule which results from or incorporates this TO Tariff, unilaterally to make application to FERC as it deems necessary and appropriate to recover its Transmission Revenue Requirements, or for a change in its rates, including changes in rate methodology, or for a change in designation of transmission facilities to be placed under the CAISO’s control, in each case under Section 205 of the FPA and pursuant to the FERC’s Rules and Regulations promulgated thereunder. Nothing contained herein shall be construed as affecting in any way the ability of any Eligible Customer receiving services in accordance with this TO Tariff to exercise its rights under the Federal Power Act and pursuant to the FERC’s rules and regulations promulgated thereunder.

This review shall be made in accordance with standard commercial practices. In addition, the Participating TO may require the UDC, MSS, or Scheduling Coordinator to provide and maintain in effect during the term of the service, an unconditional and irrevocable letter of credit as security to meet its responsibilities and obligations under this TO Tariff, or an alternative form of security proposed by the UDC, MSS, or Scheduling Coordinator and acceptable to the Participating TO, and consistent with commercial practices established by the Uniform Commercial Code, that protect the Participating TO against the risk of non-payment.

Conflicting Operating Instructions. In the event a Market
Participant receives conflicting operating instructions from the CAISO and one or more Participating TO(s), if human safety would not knowingly be neither jeopardized nor electric facilities subject to damage while the Market Participant seeks to reconcile the conflict with the appropriate CAISO and Participating TO employees before acting, the Market Participant should attempt a reconciliation. Otherwise, the Market Participant shall adhere to CAISO Tariff Section 4.2 and follow the CAISO’s instructions. In no event shall a Market Participant be required to follow operating instructions from the CAISO if following those instructions would knowingly jeopardize human safety.

[PRO FORMA] LARGE LOAD COST RECOVERY AGREEMENT

This Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement (this “Agreement”) is entered into as of this __ day of _______ 20  , by and between [________], a [●] and an Eligible Customer under the CAISO Tariff (“Eligible Customer”), and LS Power Grid California, LLC, a Delaware limited liability company (“LS Power Grid” or “Transmission Owner”). LS Power Grid and Eligible Customer are referred to collectively as the “Parties” and individually as a “Party.” [_______], a [●] (“Large Load Customer”), executes the Limited Acknowledgment set forth below solely for the purposes stated therein and is not a Party to the wholesale payment obligations established by this Agreement.

RECITALS

WHEREAS, LS Power Grid is a transmission-only company established to develop, own, and operate transmission facilities (“Transmission Facilities”) pursuant to the provisions of its Transmission Owner Tariff (“TO Tariff”) and the California Independent System Operator Corporation (“CAISO”) Open Access Transmission Tariff (“CAISO Tariff”);

WHEREAS, Eligible Customer is eligible to take transmission service under the CAISO Tariff and does or will take such service on behalf of the Large Load Customer, whether or not Eligible Customer owns or operates an electric distribution system;

WHEREAS, Eligible Customer and Transmission Owner are parties to, or are entering into, an Eligible Customer for Large Load (“ECLL”) Interconnection Agreement or Transmission System Wholesale Load (“TSWL”) Interconnection Agreement (each, an “Interconnection Agreement”) concerning the Large Load Facility;

WHEREAS, Large Load Customer owns, operates, or proposes to develop the Large Load Facility identified in Schedule 1;

WHEREAS, the Eligible Customer has submitted an ECLL Interconnection Application or TSWL Interconnection Application for the interconnection of the Large Load Facility as a new or incremental load;

WHEREAS, Eligible Customer intends to procure transmission service pursuant to the CAISO Tariff and its associated schedules on behalf of Large Load Customer; WHEREAS, in connection with the Large Load Facility, Transmission Owner must make additions and/or modifications to its transmission system, including the Large Load-Driven Upgrades identified in Schedule 1;

WHEREAS, CAISO may, under the CAISO Tariff or a Commission-accepted
methodology, formally assign to the Large Load Facility a specific share of the costs or revenue requirement of a regional transmission project, and the Parties intend this Agreement to address

PDF page 210

2

that assigned share without changing any allocation otherwise established under the CAISO Tariff;

WHEREAS, the applicable studies may identify impacts on transmission systems other than LS Power Grid’s transmission system and may identify Affected System Upgrades necessary to accommodate or mitigate such impacts;

WHEREAS, Eligible Customer is responsible for entering into and maintaining the arrangements, payment obligations, and financial security or credit support required by each applicable Affected System Owner to ensure that costs and financial risks associated with Affected System Upgrades are not shifted to customers that did not cause those costs; WHEREAS, Transmission Owner, Eligible Customer, and Distribution Utility have executed or expect to execute an engineering and procurement agreement and/or an Interconnection Agreement governing the design, construction, and interconnection of the Transmission Owner’s facilities to the Distribution Utility’s facilities, including the Network Upgrades, required to support service of the Large Load Facility;

WHEREAS, the Parties enter into this Agreement to establish the Eligible Customer’s wholesale revenue-support and security obligations associated with the Large Load Facility and the Large Load-Driven Upgrades, without establishing or prescribing the retail rates, charges, or recovery arrangements between Eligible Customer and Large Load Customer;

WHEREAS, the Commission has recognized that the framework under which CAISO provides transmission service is unique and that certain responsibilities fall to the Participating Transmission Owners, Cal. Indep. Sys. Operator Corp., 195 FERC ¶ 61,214, at P 37 (2026), and this Agreement is the form of agreement established under Sections 10.9 and 10A.6 of the TO Tariff to secure Eligible Customer’s minimum contribution toward Transmission Owner’s transmission revenue requirement;

WHEREAS, CAISO is not a party to this Agreement; this Agreement does not create any obligation of CAISO, including any obligation to provide settlement, metering, billing, resettlement, or allocation data, which arises, if at all, under the CAISO Requirements; and LS Power Grid shall recover the costs of the Covered Upgrades through its Formula Rate and shall flow TRBAA Credits, including any adjustment resulting from the resolution of a dispute under this Agreement, through to transmission customers through its TRBAA;

NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises and covenants set forth herein, and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged, the Parties intending to be legally bound, agree as follows:

2. Definitions
Capitalized terms not defined herein have the meanings given in the CAISO Tariff, the LS Power Grid TO Tariff Formula Rate Implementation Protocols in Appendix IV of the TO Tariff (“Protocols”), or the relevant Interconnection Agreement.

2.1.
“Affected System” means an electric transmission system, other than LS Power Grid’s transmission system, that an applicable study determines may be materially affected by the interconnection or service of the Large Load Facility, including the transmission system of another Participating TO or a transmission system outside the CAISO Controlled Grid.

2.2.
“Affected System Arrangement” means an executed agreement or other binding arrangement entered into by Eligible Customer with an Affected System Owner that establishes the applicable study, engineering, design, procurement, construction, ownership, cost responsibility, cancellation-cost, revenue-support, payment, financial security or credit support, true-up, and other obligations associated with an Affected System Upgrade.

2.3.
“Affected System Owner” means the owner or operator responsible for an Affected System.

2.4.
“Affected System Upgrade” means an addition, modification, or upgrade to an Affected System that an applicable study determines is necessary to accommodate or mitigate an impact caused, in whole or in part, by the interconnection or service of the Large Load Facility.

2.5.
“Annual MTRC” means the annual Minimum Transmission Revenue Contribution for a Covered Upgrade, calculated solely under the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5. Before the Final MTRC Determination Date for a Covered Upgrade, the Annual MTRC is the Estimated Annual MTRC. On and after that date, it is the Final Annual MTRC fixed under Section 6.3. Each Annual MTRC is levelized over a twenty (20)-year MTRC Term beginning on the applicable Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date, as applicable. The Annual MTRC is not an input to, or calculation made under, LS Power Grid’s annual Formula Rate update. 2.6.

“Annual Termination Fee Credit” means the annual amount of a Termination Fee Balance included in the TRBAA under Section 3.3.4.

2.7.
