extent the dispute affects its obligations under this Agreement or its Limited Acknowledgment. Customer-specific information shall be protected under Section 16.3 of the TO Tariff, the CAISO Tariff, and any applicable confidentiality agreement.

7. Security and Payment Obligations
7.1.
Construction Security. Before an applicable Large Load-Driven Upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date, Eligible Customer shall maintain Construction Security under the relevant agreement sufficient to secure:

(a) LS Power Grid’s incurred and irrevocably committed engineering, design, procurement, permitting, construction, testing, and related costs attributable to that upgrade; and

(b) the Pre-In-Service Termination Costs described in Section 3.3.2, including properly accrued AFUDC and applicable cancellation, demobilization, equipment removal, disposition, remediation, and restoration costs

(“Construction Security”).
Construction Security secures, but does not limit, Eligible Customer’s liability for those amounts. Construction Security provided under the relevant Interconnection Agreement or an engineering and procurement agreement for the same costs shall count toward, and shall not be duplicated by, the Construction Security required under this Agreement. Construction Security shall remain in effect for each Large Load- Driven Upgrade until that upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date and the applicable release or conversion conditions in Section 7.2 are satisfied. A Large Load-Driven Upgrade reaching its Upgrade In-Service Date shall not result in the release of Construction Security attributable to another Large Load-Driven Upgrade that has not reached its Upgrade In-Service Date. Eligible Customer shall replenish Construction Security within ten (10) Business Days after notice of a permitted draw and supporting information if additional secured obligations remain and the remaining Construction Security is less than the amount required under this Section.

For a CAISO-Assigned Regional Project owned by LS Power Grid, any pre-service payment, contribution, or security required from Eligible Customer for its formally assigned share shall be provided only as required by the CAISO Tariff, an applicable Interconnection Agreement, or another Commission-accepted arrangement, and shall be identified in Schedule 4. This Agreement does not independently require pre- service Construction Security solely because CAISO has assigned a share of a regional project to the Large Load Facility. The treatment of costs for a cancelled or abandoned regional project shall be governed by Section 6.3.5 and the applicable Commission order, CAISO Tariff, and any project-specific abandoned-plant incentive authorized by the Commission. For a project owned by another entity, construction and operational security shall be provided under the CAISO Tariff or the arrangement required by Section 6.3.6 and Schedule 4; it shall not be treated as LS Power Grid Operational

PDF page 241

33

Security unless included in LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate and expressly identified as LS Power Grid’s share.

7.2.
Delivery of Operational Security. No later than thirty (30) Calendar Days before the earliest expected Upgrade In-Service Date identified in Schedule 1 or Regional Project In-Service Date identified in Schedule 4 for a LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project, Eligible Customer shall deliver Operational Security calculated under this Article for the Large Load-Driven Upgrades and LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project expected to enter service. Operational Security must be fully effective before any applicable Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date.

If different Large Load-Driven Upgrades or LS Power Grid-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Projects are expected to reach their applicable in-service dates on different dates, LS Power Grid shall recalculate the Required Operational Security before each subsequent in-service date to include the additional Cumulative Revenue Responsibility attributable to the upgrade or project share entering service. Eligible Customer shall provide any resulting increase no later than fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of a Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier. Inclusion of pre-service Construction Work in Progress in rate base shall not constitute an Upgrade In-Service Date or independently trigger the Operational Security requirement. Construction Security shall remain applicable to each Large Load-Driven Upgrade that has not reached its Upgrade In-Service Date.

No Construction Security attributable to a Large Load-Driven Upgrade shall be released or converted unless:

(a) the Required Operational Security attributable to that upgrade is effective;
(b) that upgrade has reached its Upgrade In-Service Date; and
(c) LS Power Grid has determined that all Pre-In-Service Termination Costs and other construction-related obligations attributable to that upgrade have been paid or adequately reserved for.

Any portion of Construction Security retained for unresolved construction costs shall be released after LS Power Grid determines that those costs have been paid or adequately reserved for or, if Section 3.3.2 applies, after the Termination Cost Reconciliation becomes final. Failure to timely deliver Required Operational Security is a default permitting LS Power Grid, to the extent authorized by the applicable tariffs and agreements, to suspend energization or service, terminate applicable interconnection arrangements, and draw Construction Security for secured obligations. 7.3.

Required Operational Security. Required Operational Security shall equal one hundred percent (100%) of the nominal Remaining Cumulative Revenue

Responsibility. LS Power Grid shall reduce that amount only by the portion of Qualifying Retail Security Support identified in a currently effective Retail Security Certification. A Retail Security Certification is accepted in reliance on Eligible

PDF page 242

34

Customer’s representations and does not require LS Power Grid to review the underlying retail-security documents in the ordinary course. If LS Power Grid has a reasonable basis to question the certification, or after a payment default, Credit Review Event, or Material Adverse Credit Event, LS Power Grid may request supporting information reasonably necessary to verify continued compliance. Any resulting deficiency shall be delivered or restored within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of a Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier. 7.4.

Annual Resizing and Security Notices. LS Power Grid shall recalculate Required Operational Security with each Annual Projection, Final MTRC Determination Date, recalculation of a Final Annual MTRC under Section 6.3.1, Reconciliation Statement, effective prospective reassignment, replacement or expiration of Qualifying Retail Security Support, or Credit Review Event. If LS Power Grid reasonably determines that the Required Security Amount exceeds available Security, it may issue a Security Notice identifying the required increase or restoration and reasonable supporting detail then available. Eligible Customer shall provide the required increase or restoration within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of the Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier. A reduction shall be released only after the relevant calculation and reconciliation are final.

7.5.
Form of Operational Security. Except to the extent of currently effective Qualifying Retail Security Support, Eligible Customer shall provide direct Operational Security in the form of cash, an irrevocable standby Letter of Credit meeting Schedule 3, or any combination of cash and Letter of Credit. LS Power Grid may accept another form of Security that it determines, in its sole discretion, is acceptable.

Each Letter of Credit shall have an initial stated term of at least twelve (12) months and shall remain continuously effective until released under this Agreement; provided, however, that a Letter of Credit may have a stated expiration date before its release date if Eligible Customer timely extends or replaces it so that Security in an amount not less than the Required Security Amount remains continuously available to LS Power Grid.

If an issuing bank has the right not to renew a Letter of Credit, the Letter of Credit shall require the issuing bank to provide LS Power Grid and Eligible Customer at least ninety (90) Calendar Days’ prior written notice of nonrenewal. Eligible Customer shall provide a conforming extension or replacement Letter of Credit, or additional cash collateral or other Security accepted by LS Power Grid, no later than sixty (60) Calendar Days before the applicable expiration date.

If Eligible Customer fails to provide the required extension, replacement, or other Security by that deadline, LS Power Grid may, before the Letter of Credit expires, draw all or any portion of the then-available amount under the Letter of Credit and retain the proceeds as cash collateral, apply the proceeds to secured obligations, or both. Any proceeds retained but not applied shall continue to constitute Security until released under this Agreement.

PDF page 243

35

7.6.
Draw and Application. LS Power Grid may draw against all or any portion of a Letter of Credit, apply cash collateral, or apply another form of Security accepted by LS Power Grid to secured obligations upon: (a) an unpaid Supplemental MTRC, true- up, Termination Fee, or other amount due after the applicable payment period; (b) Eligible Customer’s failure to timely deliver, maintain, replace, restore, or increase Security; (c) cancellation, termination, withdrawal, abandonment, or permanent cessation of the Large Load Facility or a material breach of this Agreement; (d) a material inaccuracy in a Retail Security Certification or Security instrument; (e) Eligible Customer: (i) files a voluntary petition in bankruptcy or seeks reorganization, arrangement, or similar relief under any bankruptcy, insolvency, or debtor-relief law;

(ii) becomes subject to an involuntary bankruptcy or insolvency proceeding that is not dismissed within sixty (60) Calendar Days; (iii) has a receiver, trustee, liquidator, or custodian appointed for it or a substantial portion of its assets, which appointment is not vacated within sixty (60) Calendar Days; (iv) makes a general assignment for the benefit of creditors; or (v) admits in writing its inability to pay debts as they become due; or (f) Eligible Customer fails to extend, replace, or otherwise maintain Security in the amount and within the time required by Section 7.5, including following a notice of nonrenewal, issuer rating reduction, or other event affecting the availability of a Letter of Credit.

A draw shall not automatically require replenishment to the amount maintained immediately before the draw. Following any application of drawn proceeds to an amount due, LS Power Grid shall recalculate the remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility and the Operational Security then required under this Article. Eligible Customer shall replenish only the amount, if any, by which the remaining Operational Security is less than the recalculated Required Security Amount, within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of a Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier.

Proceeds drawn solely to prevent the expiration or nonrenewal of a Letter of Credit, and cash collateral not yet applied to an identified payment obligation, shall remain refundable Security and shall continue to constitute Operational Security until applied to an amount then due or released under Section 7.9. Such proceeds and cash collateral shall be included in determining the amount of remaining Operational Security and shall not create an additional replenishment obligation merely because they are held by LS Power Grid.

The posting, drawing, holding, replacement, or replenishment of Operational Security shall not, by itself, satisfy the secured obligation, reduce Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, or produce a TRBAA Credit. Drawn proceeds of Operational Security shall become Applied Security Proceeds only when LS Power Grid applies them to an identified Supplemental MTRC, prior-period true-up adjustment, Termination Fee, or other secured payment obligation arising under Article 6 or Section 3.3.4. Upon application, the proceeds shall reduce that obligation dollar-for-dollar and shall receive the TRBAA treatment required by Section 6.8; provided, however, that Applied Security Proceeds applied to a Termination Fee shall be administered under Section 3.3.4 and credited to transmission customer only through the Annual Termination Fee

PDF page 244

36

Credits provided under Section 6.8. Proceeds of Construction Security applied to Pre- In-Service Termination Costs or other construction-related obligations shall be treated as provided in Sections 3.3.2 and 3.3.3.

7.7.
Security Maintenance. Because Required Operational Security is initially one hundred percent (100%) of nominal Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, no separate acceleration concept applies. An incorrect, expired, withdrawn, unavailable, or materially misleading Retail Security Certification, or the loss of enforceable retail-security rights described in it, is a Material Adverse Credit Event. Eligible Customer shall provide replacement direct Security for any resulting uncovered amount within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of a Security Notice, or by the applicable in-service date if earlier.

7.8.
Post-Termination Administration and Cash Collateral. LS Power Grid shall continue the annual calculations, invoices, and true-ups required under this Agreement following termination of service to, or the departure, permanent cessation, or material reduction of service by, the Large Load Customer until the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility and all other obligations under this Agreement have been finally satisfied, transferred, reassigned, refunded, or otherwise resolved. The Parties intend that cash collateral and proceeds of Security be treated as a deposit or deferred liability rather than as a Contribution in Aid of Construction, customer financing of transmission plant, or a reduction to LS Power Grid’s rate base. To the extent permitted by applicable Commission accounting and ratemaking requirements and tax law, LS Power Grid shall record and administer such amounts as the corresponding annual costs and payment obligations arise. The treatment described in this Section is subject to the Commission’s Uniform System of Accounts, the Formula Rate, the Protocols, applicable Commission orders, and other Applicable Laws and Regulations. If the Commission requires a different accounting or ratemaking treatment, LS Power Grid shall implement the required treatment and make corresponding adjustments necessary to prevent duplicate recovery and preserve, to the extent permitted, the economic allocation established by this Agreement. The Parties shall cooperate in seeking any necessary Commission authorization or acceptance.

Nothing in this Agreement guarantees a particular accounting, ratemaking, or tax result. Cash collateral and unapplied Security proceeds shall remain subject to release under Section 7.9, including release to reflect an effective prospective reassignment under Section 6.9.

7.9.
Release of Security. LS Power Grid shall release excess Operational Security only after the applicable Reconciliation Statement becomes final. LS Power Grid shall release Security attributable to an effective prospective reassignment and shall release all remaining Security within thirty (30) Calendar Days after final satisfaction of the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility and all other secured obligations. Termination alone does not require release.

7.10. Security Does Not Limit Liability. The Required Security Amount is based on LS Power Grid’s then-current estimates and calculations and does not cap, replace, or

PDF page 245

37

discharge Eligible Customer’s underlying obligations. A shortfall between Security and the amount due remains immediately payable to LS Power Grid.

7.11. Events of Default; Remedies. Each of the following is an “Event of Default” by Eligible Customer: (a) failure to pay any undisputed amount when due that continues for ten (10) Business Days after written notice from LS Power Grid; (b) failure to deliver, maintain, replace, restore, or increase Security when required under this Article 7 or Schedule 3 that continues after the applicable cure period; (c) any other material breach of this Agreement, including under Section 4.2 or 6.3.6, that continues for thirty (30) Calendar Days after written notice from LS Power Grid or, if the breach cannot reasonably be cured within that period, for such longer period, not to exceed ninety (90) Calendar Days, as Eligible Customer diligently pursues a cure; and (d) any event described in Section 7.6(e). Upon an Event of Default, LS Power Grid may, subject to Applicable Laws and Regulations and the applicable tariffs and agreements:

(i) draw and apply Security under Section 7.6; (ii) exercise the rights described in Sections 4.2.6 and 7.2; (iii) before the first Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date, terminate this Agreement under Section 3.3.1; and (iv) pursue any other remedy available at law or in equity or before the Commission. No termination after an Upgrade In-Service Date or Regional Project In-Service Date releases the obligations described in Section 3.3.4.

8. Force Majeure.
8.1.
If a Party is rendered unable, wholly or in part, by Force Majeure to carry out a nonmonetary obligation, that obligation shall be suspended only to the extent and for the period performance is prevented. Force Majeure does not suspend or excuse payment of incurred construction costs, Pre-In-Service Termination Costs, Supplemental MTRC, the Termination Fee, prior-period true-up adjustments, or the maintenance, replacement, replenishment, or increase of Security. If a Force Majeure event continues for more than twenty (20) Calendar Days in a sixty (60)-Calendar Day period, the Parties shall meet to address performance; any resulting termination remains subject to Sections 3.4 and 3.5.

9. Indemnification
9.1.
Indemnity. Each Party (the “Indemnifying Party”) shall at all times indemnify, defend, and hold the other Party and its Affiliates and their respective directors, officers, employees, and agents (each, an “Indemnified Person”) harmless from any and all damages, losses, claims, including claims and actions relating to injury to or death of any person or damage to property, demand, suits, recoveries, costs and expenses, court costs, attorney fees, and all other obligations by or to third parties, arising out of or resulting from the Indemnifying Party’s actions or omissions in performing its obligations under this Agreement, except to the extent caused by the gross negligence or willful misconduct of the Indemnified Person.

9.1.1.
Indemnified Person. If an Indemnified Person is entitled to indemnification as a result of a claim by a third party, and the Indemnifying Party fails, after notice

PDF page 246

38

and reasonable opportunity to proceed, to assume the defense of such claim, such Indemnified Person may at the expense of the Indemnifying Party contest, settle or consent to the entry of any judgment with respect to, or pay in full, such claim.

9.1.2.
Indemnifying Party. If an Indemnifying Party is obligated to indemnify and hold any Indemnified Person harmless, the amount owing to the Indemnified Person shall be the amount of such Indemnified Person’s actual loss, net of any insurance or other recovery.

9.2.
Indemnity Procedures. Promptly after receipt by an Indemnified Person of any claim or notice of the commencement of any action or administrative or legal proceeding or investigation as to which the indemnity provided for in Section 9.1 may apply, the Indemnified Person shall notify the Indemnifying Party of such fact. Any failure of or delay in such notification shall not affect a Party’s indemnification obligation unless such failure or delay is materially prejudicial to the Indemnifying Party. The Indemnifying Party shall have the right to assume the defense thereof with counsel designated by such Indemnifying Party and reasonably satisfactory to the Indemnified Person. If the defendants in any such action include one or more Indemnified Persons and the Indemnifying Party and if the Indemnified Person reasonably concludes that there may be legal defenses available to it and/or other Indemnified Persons which are different from or additional to those available to the Indemnifying Party, the Indemnified Person shall have the right to select separate counsel to assert such legal defenses and to otherwise participate in the defense of such action on its own behalf. In such instances, the Indemnifying Party shall only be required to pay the fees and expenses of one additional attorney to represent an Indemnified Person or Indemnified Persons having such differing or additional legal defenses.

The Indemnified Person shall be entitled, at its expense, to participate in any such action, suit or proceeding, the defense of which has been assumed by the Indemnifying Party. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the Indemnifying Party (i) shall not be entitled to assume and control the defense of any such action, suit or proceedings if and to the extent that, in the opinion of the Indemnified Person and its counsel, such action, suit or proceeding involves the potential imposition of criminal liability on the Indemnified Person, or there exists a conflict or adversity of interest between the Indemnified Person and the Indemnifying Party, in which event the Indemnifying Party shall pay the reasonable expenses of the Indemnified Person, and (ii) shall not settle or consent to the entry of any judgment in any action, suit or proceeding without the consent of the Indemnified Person, which shall not be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed.

9.3.
Consequential Damages. Except for amounts expressly payable under Articles 3, 4, 6, and 7, no Party shall be liable under this Agreement for special, indirect, incidental, consequential, or punitive damages, including lost profit or revenue, loss of use, cost of capital, or temporary equipment or services, under any theory of liability. Amounts

PDF page 247

39

required to satisfy the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Supplemental MTRC, construction costs, prior-period true-up adjustments, and Security obligations are direct contractual payment obligations and are not excluded damages.

10. Assignment
10.1. Except for a change in Eligible Customer governed by Section 6.10, a Party may assign this Agreement only with the other Party’s written consent; provided that a Party may assign to an Affiliate with equal or greater creditworthiness and legal and operational capacity to perform. No assignment or change in retail service provider releases the existing Eligible Customer until the assignee or Successor Eligible Customer executes the required assumption, provides required Security, and the transfer becomes effective under the applicable tariffs. Any prohibited assignment is void, and Security provided by or on behalf of Large Load Customer does not make Large Load Customer the wholesale obligor.

11. Disputes
All disputes, claims, or controversies arising out of or relating to this Agreement, including any dispute concerning the identification or allocation of Large Load-Driven Upgrades or Covered Upgrades; any calculation, update, or recalculation of an Estimated Annual MTRC, Final Annual MTRC, Regional Project Allocated ARR, or the MTRC Calculation Template; Qualifying Transmission Revenues; Supplemental MTRC; Total Required Revenue Commitment; Cumulative Revenue Responsibility; TRBAA Credits; Pre-In-Service Termination Costs; the Termination Fee; prospective reassignment; or Security, shall be resolved under this Article 11, except that a dispute concerning CAISO’s designation of a regional project or assignment of a project cost share shall be resolved under the applicable CAISO Tariff procedures. Any payment, refund, credit, or other adjustment resulting from the resolution of a dispute under this Article 11 shall be implemented as provided in Section 11.5. 11.1. Submission and Informal Resolution. In the event of a dispute or claim arising out of or relating to this Agreement, the disputing Party shall provide the other Party with a written notice describing the dispute (“Notice of Dispute”). The dispute shall be referred promptly to designated senior representatives for informal resolution. The representatives shall meet or confer within ten (10) Business Days and shall use reasonable efforts to resolve the matter within thirty (30) Calendar Days after receipt of the Notice of Dispute. This informal process is not a condition to seeking emergency relief, filing a FERC complaint, or invoking any ADR processes. 11.2. Alternative Dispute Resolution. If a dispute is not resolved informally, any Party may request Alternative Dispute Resolution (“ADR”). The Parties shall use the ADR procedures, neutral-selection process, confidentiality protections, and timing requirements specified in the applicable FERC rules. ADR shall be conducted by persons knowledgeable in electric utility, transmission, and FERC matters and shall not alter a jurisdictional rate, term, or condition unless accepted or approved by the FERC.

PDF page 248

40

11.3. FERC Complaints and Other Rights. Any Party may instead file a complaint, petition, or other pleading with the FERC under Section 206 or Section 306 of the Federal Power Act, or seek other relief available under the Federal Power Act or Applicable Laws and Regulations, and LS Power Grid may make any filing under Section 205 of the Federal Power Act permitted by Section 12.12. No Party’s participation in informal negotiations or ADR waives or limits its right to seek FERC action. A final ADR determination affecting jurisdictional rates, terms, conditions, charges, or Network Upgrades shall be filed with the FERC if required. 11.4. Costs and Interim Performance. Unless otherwise determined through ADR, the Parties shall bear their own costs and share neutral costs as specified by the applicable ADR procedure. During a dispute, each Party shall continue performing undisputed payment and operational obligations, and shall pay undisputed amounts when due. 11.5. Implementation of Dispute Determinations; TRBAA. Within thirty (30) Calendar Days after a dispute under this Article 11 is finally resolved, whether by written agreement of the Parties, a final ADR determination (subject to any required Commission filing or acceptance), or a final Commission order (each, a “Dispute Determination”), LS Power Grid shall: (a) update the affected MTRC Calculation Template, Schedules, Cumulative Revenue Ledger, Reconciliation Statement, and Required Operational Security, as applicable, and serve the updated documents on Eligible Customer; and (b) invoice or refund, as applicable, any amount owed between the Parties as a result of the Dispute Determination, without interest. To the extent a Dispute Determination changes a TRBAA Credit or any other amount previously credited to, or recovered from, transmission customers, including any change in Supplemental MTRC Receipts or Applied Security Proceeds for a prior Rate Year, LS Power Grid shall record the resulting difference in the TRBA under Section 6.8 and flow it through to transmission customers through the TRBAA, and any amount so returned by LS Power Grid to transmission customers shall include interest as provided in Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff. If a final Commission order in a proceeding concerning LS Power Grid’s TRBAA requires a change to any TRBAA Credit attributable to this Agreement, LS Power Grid shall implement that order in the TRBA and shall make any corresponding updates and adjustments between the Parties under this Section 11.5 as if the order were a Dispute Determination. Nothing in this Section reopens the Final Annual MTRC or Total Required Revenue Commitment except as required to implement a Dispute Determination or as permitted by Section 6.3.1.

12. Miscellaneous.
12.1. Amendment. Except for updates to the MTRC Calculation Template expressly authorized by Section 6.3.1, updates implementing a Dispute Determination under Section 11.5, and administrative updates expressly permitted elsewhere in this Agreement, this Agreement may be amended only by a written instrument executed by the Parties. An authorized template update shall not change the substantive methodology or other terms of this Agreement. Any material deviation from the Commission-accepted pro forma and any unexecuted amendment shall be filed with FERC as required by the applicable tariffs and Commission rules.

PDF page 249

41

12.2. Entire Agreement. This Agreement, its Schedules, the relevant Interconnection Agreement, and other agreements expressly incorporated herein state the Parties’ complete understanding concerning the wholesale cost recovery mechanism. Nothing in this Agreement establishes the retail rates, charges, or security arrangements between Eligible Customer and Large Load Customer.

12.3. Governing Tariffs and Order of Precedence. This Agreement shall be interpreted and administered consistently with the Federal Power Act, applicable Commission orders, the CAISO Tariff, and the TO Tariff. The CAISO Tariff, the TO Tariff, and any other tariff or agreement within the CAISO Requirements that is on file with or accepted by the Commission control over this Agreement and its Schedules to the extent of an irreconcilable conflict, with each controlling the matters within its respective scope. Nothing in this Agreement shall require a Party or CAISO to take action inconsistent with an applicable Commission-accepted tariff, agreement, or order.

Subject to the preceding paragraph:
(a) the Formula Rate and Protocols shall control the calculation, information exchange, and true-up of LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement, including the rate base treatment of the Covered Upgrades; Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff shall control the flow-through of TRBAA Credits to transmission customers through the TRBAA; and any dispute concerning a TRBAA Credit, or any other matter arising under this Agreement, shall be resolved under Article 11, and any resulting adjustment shall be implemented as provided in Section 11.5. The MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5, and not the Formula Rate or Protocols, shall control calculation of the Estimated Annual MTRC and Final Annual MTRC;

(b) this Agreement shall control the determination and administration of the Supplemental MTRC, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Operational Security, prospective reassignment, and the other wholesale revenue-support obligations established herein;

(c) the applicable Interconnection Agreement shall control the physical interconnection, design, engineering, procurement, construction, testing, energization, and operating requirements, except to the extent this Agreement expressly establishes or supplements construction-cost, pre-service termination- cost, revenue-support, or Security obligations; provided that the applicable instruments shall be interpreted, to the extent reasonably possible, to give effect to both agreements and prevent duplicate recovery;

(d) each applicable Affected System Arrangement shall control the design, construction, ownership, operation, cost responsibility, financial security or credit support, and cost recovery applicable to the corresponding Affected System Upgrade as between the parties to that arrangement. Nothing in this Agreement modifies an Affected System Owner’s tariff, makes LS Power Grid responsible for an obligation of an Affected System Owner, or makes an Affected System Owner a Party to this Agreement;

PDF page 250

42

(e) the body of this Agreement shall control over a Schedule in the event of a conflict, except where the Schedule identifies a project-specific election, value, methodology, or term that this Agreement expressly authorizes the Parties to establish in the Schedule; and

(f) the Schedules shall control over other project-specific supporting documents or workpapers that have not been filed with or accepted by the Commission. The Parties shall attempt to interpret the applicable instruments so that each is given effect and no provision is rendered superfluous. If an irreconcilable conflict affects a Commission-jurisdictional rate, term, or condition, the Parties shall seek any required Commission clarification, acceptance, or authorization. Pending resolution, the Parties shall continue performing all undisputed obligations.

12.4. Governing Law. This Agreement shall be interpreted, governed by, and construed under the laws of the State of California, without regard to the principles of conflict of laws thereof, or the laws of the United States, as applicable, as if executed and to be performed wholly within the State of California.

12.5. Severability. If any term, covenant, or condition of this Agreement or the application or effect of any such term, covenant, or condition is held invalid as to any person, entity, or circumstance, or is determined to be unjust, unreasonable, unlawful, imprudent, or otherwise not in the public interest, by any court or government agency of competent jurisdiction, then such term, covenant, or condition shall remain in force and effect to the maximum extent permitted by law, and all other terms, covenants, and conditions of this Agreement and their application shall not be affected thereby but shall remain in force and effect. The Parties shall be relieved of their obligations only to the extent necessary to eliminate such regulatory or other determination, unless a court or governmental agency of competent jurisdiction holds that such provisions are not severable from all other provisions of this Agreement.

12.6. No Third Party Beneficiaries. Except for the rights of Large Load Customer and any Successor Eligible Customer under Section 6.12, this Agreement is not intended to and does not create rights, remedies, or benefits of any character whatsoever in favor of any persons, corporations, associations, or entities other than the Parties, and the obligations herein assumed are solely for the use and benefit of the Parties, their successors in interest and, where permitted, their assigns.

12.7. Survival. Expiration or termination of this Agreement shall not affect any right, liability, or obligation that accrued before the effective date of expiration or termination or that, by its express terms or nature, is intended to continue thereafter. Without limiting the foregoing, the following shall survive expiration or termination for the period necessary to give them effect:

(a) all outstanding payment, refund, reimbursement, reconciliation, true-up, Security, and release obligations arising under Articles 3, 4, 6, and 7;

PDF page 251

43

(b) the determination and final satisfaction, transfer, or prospective reassignment of the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility;

(c) the calculation and implementation of TRBAA Credits and other adjustments necessary to prevent duplicate recovery;

(d) the confidentiality and information-protection obligations applicable under Section 6.12, Section 16.3 of the TO Tariff, the CAISO Tariff, and any applicable confidentiality agreement;

(e) the Parties’ rights to obtain, inspect, retain, verify, and challenge books, records, metering data, settlement data, calculations, and supporting workpapers relating to periods before expiration or termination;

(f) the indemnification obligations in Article 9 with respect to acts, omissions, circumstances, or claims arising before expiration or termination;

(g) the dispute-resolution provisions in Article 11 with respect to any surviving right, liability, or obligation; and

(h) any provision that expressly states that it survives or that must survive to prevent duplicate recovery or give effect to the Parties’ rights and obligations. Survival of a provision shall not extend the underlying payment or Security obligation beyond the point at which that obligation has been finally satisfied, transferred, reassigned, refunded, or released in accordance with this Agreement.

12.8. No Waiver. A Party’s delay or failure to exercise any right or remedy under this Agreement shall not constitute a waiver of that or any other right or remedy. A waiver shall be effective only if stated in a written instrument executed by the Party granting the waiver and shall apply only to the specific matter and circumstances stated therein. A waiver on one occasion shall not constitute a continuing waiver or a waiver on any other occasion. No course of performance or dealing shall amend this Agreement or waive any Commission-jurisdictional rate, term, or condition. Nothing in this Section limits a Party’s rights under the Federal Power Act or Applicable Laws and Regulations.

12.9. Counterparts and Electronic Signatures. This Agreement, any amendment, and any assumption or acknowledgment executed pursuant to it may be executed in counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original and all of which together shall constitute one instrument. Signatures transmitted electronically, including by portable document format or an electronic-signature platform, shall have the same force and effect as original signatures to the extent permitted by Applicable Laws and Regulations. Each Party agrees that it shall not contest the validity or enforceability of an instrument solely because it was executed or transmitted electronically. 12.10. Authority. Each Party represents and warrants that:

(a) it is duly organized, validly existing, and in good standing under the laws of its jurisdiction of organization, to the extent such concepts apply;

(b) it has the requisite power and authority to execute, deliver, and perform this Agreement;

(d) the individual executing this Agreement on its behalf is authorized to do so; and
(e) subject to any required Commission acceptance or other regulatory approval expressly identified in this Agreement, this Agreement constitutes its valid and binding obligation, enforceable in accordance with its terms.

The Large Load Customer represents only that the individual executing the Limited Acknowledgment is authorized to execute that acknowledgment on its behalf. Execution of the Limited Acknowledgment does not make the Large Load Customer a Party or expand its obligations beyond those expressly stated in the Limited Acknowledgment or in a separate undertaking executed by the Large Load Customer. 12.11. The Initial Projected Cumulative Revenue Responsibility stated above represents the initial projection of the Total Required Revenue Commitment for the applicable Large Load-Driven Upgrade, calculated as the Estimated Annual MTRC multiplied by twenty (20) using the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5.

Construction Security shall remain applicable separately to each Large Load-Driven Upgrade until that upgrade reaches its Upgrade In-Service Date and the applicable conditions for release or conversion under Section 7.2 have been satisfied.

Operational Security must be fully effective before the applicable Upgrade In-Service Date. If different Large Load-Driven Upgrades are expected to reach their Upgrade In-Service Dates on different dates, LS Power Grid shall recalculate the required Operational Security before each subsequent Upgrade In-Service Date in accordance with Article 7.

7. Schedule Qualifications
7.1 Projected Amounts. The capital costs, Estimated Annual MTRC, projected Total Required Revenue Commitment, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, and Operational Security amounts stated in this Schedule are initial projections provided for planning and administration purposes. They do not constitute fixed amounts, caps, or guarantees.

7.2 Initial and Applicable Allocation Percentages. The Initial Allocation Percentage identified for each Large Load-Driven Upgrade shall remain the initial allocation of that upgrade to the Large Load Customer. The Applicable Allocation Percentage shall equal the Initial Allocation Percentage, as adjusted prospectively by any reassignment effective under Section 6.9 or as otherwise required by a final Commission order or applicable tariff provision. A change in capital costs, the Upgrade In-Service Date, Formula Rate inputs, or the annual revenue requirement shall not, by itself, change the Initial Allocation Percentage or Applicable Allocation Percentage. Cancellation, abandonment, downsizing, curtailment, permanent cessation, or material reduction of service by the Large Load Customer also shall not, by itself, reduce either percentage.

7.3 Allocation Limitation. The aggregate Annual MTRC assigned to all Large Load Customers for a Covered Upgrade shall not exceed one hundred percent (100%) of the Annual MTRC for that Covered Upgrade. LS Power Grid shall not allocate the same upgrade cost, transmission capability, or annual revenue requirement to more than one Large Load Customer. 7.4 Upgrade In-Service Dates and MTRC Term End Dates. Each Expected Upgrade In- Service Date stated in this Schedule is a planning estimate. LS Power Grid shall provide written notice to Eligible Customer of a material change to an Expected Upgrade In-Service Date. Within five (5) Business Days after a Covered Upgrade achieves its Upgrade In-Service Date, LS Power Grid shall provide written notice to Eligible Customer stating the actual Upgrade In- Service Date and the corresponding MTRC Term End Date. LS Power Grid shall update this Schedule and the applicable Cumulative Revenue Ledger to reflect those dates. The actual Upgrade In-Service Date shall be determined in accordance with the definition of that term in the Agreement. Inclusion of pre-service Construction Work in Progress in rate base does not constitute an Upgrade In-Service Date.

PDF page 263

Schedule 1 - 9

7.5 Annual Updates. Changes in the Estimated Annual MTRC, Final Annual MTRC, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Supplemental MTRC, Qualifying Transmission Revenues, or Operational Security resulting from the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5, the Annual Projection, the Annual Update and Reconciliation Statement, or an effective prospective reassignment under Section 6.9 do not require an amendment to this Schedule.

7.6 Prospective Reassignment. LS Power Grid shall maintain the customer-, project-, and upgrade-specific ledger required by Sections 6.7 and 6.9. Any prospective reassignment shall be reflected in that ledger and in the applicable Annual Projection and Reconciliation Statement. A prospective reassignment shall adjust the Applicable Allocation Percentage but shall not revise the Initial Allocation Percentage, produce a historical refund, or reopen a prior Rate Year. 7.7 Updated Expected Dates. An update to an expected construction commencement date, Expected Upgrade In-Service Date, or expected Large Load Service Commencement Date may be made by written notice and does not require an amendment to this Schedule unless the update also changes a material rate, term, condition, facility scope, or allocation established by this Agreement.

Schedule 2 - 1

Schedule 2
Qualifying Transmission Revenues Methodology
1. Purpose and Controlling Principles
This Schedule establishes the reproducible methodology for calculating projected and actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues attributable to the Large Load Facility. It is intended to identify wholesale transmission revenues paid by or on behalf of Eligible Customer that are attributable to the Large Load Facility and reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected from CAISO customers or directly credit LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement. Such revenues may include applicable TAC revenues and qualifying Wheeling Access Charges or other wholesale transmission-access, wheeling, or transmission-service charges under the CAISO Tariff or another Commission-accepted arrangement, including charges paid through a non-PTO serving entity. The Supplemental MTRC therefore measures only the remaining shortfall below the applicable Annual MTRC.

Qualifying Transmission Revenues shall be counted once in calculating the Supplemental MTRC and the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility against the combined Annual MTRC for all Covered Upgrades. They shall not also be treated as a TRBAA Credit under Section 6.8 because they reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected from CAISO customers. This Schedule does not modify CAISO settlement rules, establish a retail rate, impose any obligation on CAISO, or alter any cost allocation established under the CAISO Requirements.

2. Project and Settlement Identifiers
The following information shall be completed for each Large Load Facility and updated administratively when a meter, settlement identifier, Eligible Customer, or other listed item changes. An administrative update that does not alter the Commission-accepted methodology does not require an amendment to the Agreement unless a filing is otherwise required by Applicable Laws and Regulations.

3. Data Sources and Responsibilities
(a) LS Power Grid shall calculate projected and actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues using the best available data from CAISO settlement statements, wheeling, transmission- access, and transmission service invoices, revenue-quality meter data, Eligible Customer and non-PTO serving entity billing records, Scheduling Coordinator records, and other data made available under the CAISO Tariff, another Commission-accepted arrangement, or authorized by Eligible Customer and Large Load Customer.

(b) Eligible Customer shall provide, or cause its Scheduling Coordinator, Utility Distribution Company, Metering Coordinator, or other authorized agent to provide, all information reasonably necessary to identify and verify withdrawals attributable to the Large Load Facility. Eligible Customer shall maintain all authorizations reasonably necessary for release of that information.

(c) The Large Load Customer shall provide the information and authorizations required by its Limited Acknowledgment, including information needed to distinguish the Large Load Facility from other load behind a common meter or within a common settlement identifier.

(d) LS Power Grid may use CAISO data made available under the CAISO Requirements or through the authorizations provided under this Agreement. Nothing in this Schedule makes CAISO a party to the Agreement or independently requires CAISO to calculate, invoice, collect, or administer the Supplemental MTRC.

(e) If final data are unavailable when a projection, invoice, Annual Update, or Reconciliation Statement is due, LS Power Grid may use a reasonable estimate based on the most recent available data, the service forecast, applicable TAC rates, and known settlement adjustments. Estimated amounts shall be replaced with actual data in the next practicable reconciliation.

PDF page 267

Schedule 2 - 3

4. Applicable Withdrawals
For each CAISO settlement interval or other reproducible billing interval, Applicable Withdrawals shall equal the metered energy withdrawals attributable to the Large Load Facility at the metering point identified in Section 2 of this Schedule, expressed in MWh and adjusted as follows:

(a) Imports, exports, station service, auxiliary load, behind-the-meter generation, storage charging and discharging, and other injections or withdrawals shall be treated consistently with the manner in which they are recognized for the applicable TAC charge under the CAISO Tariff.

(b) Distribution losses and transmission losses shall be included only to the extent they are included in the billing determinant for the applicable TAC component. The loss factor or method shall be identified in the project-specific table in Section 10 of this Schedule.

(c) If the Large Load Facility shares a meter or settlement identifier with other load, the withdrawals attributable to the Large Load Facility shall be determined from revenue- quality submeters or another objective allocation method identified in Section 10 of this Schedule. An allocation based solely on estimates may be used temporarily but shall be reconciled when reliable data become available.

(d) Withdrawals shall not be counted for a period before the corresponding Annual MTRC is included in the Supplemental MTRC calculation under Section 6.4 of the Agreement, except where necessary to correct a prior-period error or resettlement.

5. Applicable TAC Revenue
Applicable TAC Revenue for an interval shall equal Applicable Withdrawals multiplied by the CAISO transmission rate or rates actually applicable to those withdrawals for that interval. The calculation shall include only transmission charge components that contribute to recovery of the revenue requirement in which LS Power Grid’s costs are included. It shall exclude retail distribution charges, generation charges, non-transmission surcharges, taxes collected for another governmental entity, and any other charge that does not contribute to recovery of LS Power Grid’s transmission revenue requirement.

If CAISO uses a monthly, annual, or other non-interval billing determinant, LS Power Grid shall perform the calculation using that same billing period and determinant. If more than one TAC rate or component applies, each component shall be calculated separately before applying the LS Power Grid Allocation Factor.

6. LS Power Grid Allocation Factor
The LS Power Grid Allocation Factor shall identify the portion of Applicable TAC Revenue allocable to LS Power Grid for the relevant period. Unless a different Commission-accepted method is stated in Section 10 of this Schedule, the factor shall be calculated from the same revenue requirement, billing determinant, allocation percentage, and other inputs used by CAISO to include LS Power Grid’s net transmission revenue requirement in the applicable TAC rate. For each applicable rate component and period:

PDF page 268

Schedule 2 - 4

LS Power Grid Allocation Factor = LS Power Grid Net Revenue Requirement included in the applicable TAC component ÷ Total Net Revenue Requirement recovered through that TAC component.

If LS Power Grid’s revenue requirement is recovered through a project-specific, zonal, or other direct allocation rather than a systemwide factor, the corresponding Commission-accepted allocation percentage or rate component shall be used. The supporting workpapers shall identify the source and calculation of each factor.

7. Calculation of Qualifying Transmission Revenues
Projected and actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues shall be calculated as follows for each applicable interval, month, or billing period and then aggregated for the Rate Year or partial Rate Year:

Qualifying Transmission Revenues =  Qualifying TAC Revenue + Qualifying Wheeling and Other Wholesale Transmission Revenues ± Applicable Adjustments.

Qualifying TAC Revenue = Σ [(Applicable Withdrawals × Applicable TAC Rate) × LS Power Grid Allocation Factor]. Qualifying Wheeling and Other Wholesale Transmission Revenues equal Wheeling Access Charges and other wholesale transmission-access, wheeling, or transmission-service charges actually paid by or on behalf of Eligible Customer to CAISO, a Participating TO, or a non-PTO transmission or load-serving entity for deliveries serving the Large Load Facility, but only to the extent those revenues reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected through TAC or directly credit LS Power Grid’s Net Revenue Requirement. Applicable Adjustments shall include only amounts attributable to the Large Load Facility, including CAISO resettlements, billing corrections, refunds, charge reversals, balancing-account adjustments, and other adjustments that change the qualifying transmission revenue. Bad debt or uncollectible amounts shall reduce Qualifying Transmission Revenues only to the extent the corresponding revenue is not recovered from another source and is not otherwise included in LS Power Grid’s Formula Rate recovery.

8. Projections, Actuals, Resettlements, and Customer Changes
(a) The Annual Projection shall use forecast withdrawals, then-effective or forecast TAC rates, the most recent LS Power Grid Allocation Factor, and known adjustments. The workpapers shall separately identify each material assumption.

(b) The Reconciliation Statement shall replace projected values with actual withdrawals, rates, factors, and adjustments for the applicable period. CAISO resettlements or other corrections received after a Reconciliation Statement becomes final shall be reflected in the next practicable Reconciliation Statement and Annual Update, subject to Section 6.6 of the Agreement, and any dispute concerning them shall be resolved under Article 11 of the Agreement.

(c) A change in Eligible Customer, Scheduling Coordinator, or retail service provider shall not interrupt the calculation or cause the same withdrawals or revenues to be omitted or counted more than once. Qualifying Transmission Revenues shall remain associated with the Large Load Customer and Large Load Facility, regardless of which Eligible Customer is responsible for payment during the applicable period.

PDF page 269

Schedule 2 - 5

(d) If two or more Eligible Customers serve the Large Load Facility during a Rate Year, the Qualifying Transmission Revenues and corresponding Supplemental MTRC shall be assigned between them based on the periods during which each provided service, unless the applicable transition agreement provides another Commission-accepted allocation.

9. Workpapers, Verification, and Records
With each Annual Projection and Reconciliation Statement, LS Power Grid shall provide workpapers sufficient for Eligible Customer to reproduce the calculation, including applicable meter quantities, TAC rates, LS Power Grid Allocation Factors, adjustments, source references, and a reconciliation to the corresponding Supplemental MTRC and Cumulative Revenue Ledger. Customer-specific or commercially sensitive information may be provided subject to the confidentiality provisions of Section 16.3 of the TO Tariff, the CAISO Tariff, and any applicable confidentiality agreement.

LS Power Grid, Eligible Customer, and Large Load Customer shall retain supporting records for the period required by the CAISO Tariff, Commission regulations, and Applicable Laws and Regulations, and in all events until completion of any timely initiated review, audit, or dispute concerning the applicable Rate Year. Any dispute concerning the calculation shall be resolved under Article 11 of the Agreement.

Schedule 3 - 1

Schedule 3
Credit and Security Standards
1. Purpose and Application
This Schedule establishes the forms, delivery, maintenance, replacement, draw, application, and release requirements for all Construction Security and Operational Security required under Article 7. Except for the limited reduction of direct Operational Security provided in Section 3, all Security shall be provided directly by Eligible Customer.

2. Permitted Forms of Security
Eligible Customer may provide Security in the form of cash, a Letter of Credit, or any combination thereof. LS Power Grid may accept another form of Security if it determines, in its sole discretion, that the form is acceptable.

Cash collateral shall be delivered in immediately available funds to an account designated by LS Power Grid and shall count dollar-for-dollar toward the applicable Required Security Amount while held by LS Power Grid.

(a) Construction Security
Eligible Customer shall provide and maintain all Construction Security directly in one or more forms permitted under this Section 2. A Retail Security Certification and Qualifying Retail Security Support shall not constitute, reduce, replace, or satisfy Construction Security or any Pre- In-Service Termination Costs.

(b) Operational Security
Except to the extent reduced under Section 3, Eligible Customer shall provide and maintain direct Operational Security in one or more forms permitted under this Section 2 in an amount equal to one hundred percent (100%) of the nominal Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility.

3. Retail Security Certification
A current Retail Security Certification reduces the direct Operational Security requirement dollar-for-dollar by the amount of Qualifying Retail Security Support identified in that certification. It does not reduce Eligible Customer’s underlying obligations, constitute Security posted directly with LS Power Grid, or apply to Construction Security or Pre-In-Service Termination Costs.

Each Retail Security Certification must be substantially in the form of Exhibit A contained in Schedule 3 of this Agreement. Eligible Customer shall deliver a Retail Security Certification before first relying on Qualifying Retail Security Support to reduce the direct Operational Security requirement. The Retail Security Certification shall remain effective unless and until

PDF page 272

Schedule 3 - 2

superseded, withdrawn, or deemed ineffective under this Agreement. Eligible Customer shall deliver an updated Retail Security Certification within three (3) Business Days after any reduction, draw, expiration, nonrenewal, termination, amendment, loss of enforceable draw or application rights, or other event that could impair the certified support.

With each Reconciliation Statement, LS Power Grid may identify the amount of Qualifying Retail Security Support then reflected in calculating Required Operational Security. That identification does not require Eligible Customer to deliver a new Retail Security Certification unless an event requiring an update has occurred.

LS Power Grid may rely on the certification without reviewing the underlying retail security documents in the ordinary course, but may request supporting information after a payment default, Credit Review Event, Material Adverse Credit Event, or reasonable basis to question the certification.

4. Letter of Credit Requirements
If Eligible Customer provides a Letter of Credit as Security, the Letter of Credit must be irrevocable, unconditional, transferable, payable at sight, and permit partial and multiple draws. It must be issued by a major United States commercial bank or United States branch of a major foreign commercial bank rated at least A- by S&P, A3 by Moody’s, or A- by Fitch, in each case by at least two of those three agencies, and otherwise be in form and substance reasonably acceptable to LS Power Grid.

Each Letter of Credit must have an initial stated term of at least twelve (12) months and provide LS Power Grid at least ninety (90) Calendar Days’ prior written notice of nonrenewal. Eligible Customer shall provide a conforming extension, replacement Letter of Credit, cash collateral, or other Security accepted by LS Power Grid no later than sixty (60) Calendar Days before the applicable expiration date following a notice of nonrenewal, and within fifteen (15) Business Days after an issuer rating reduction.

If Eligible Customer fails to timely provide the required replacement or substitute Security, LS Power Grid may, before the applicable Letter of Credit expires, draw all or any portion of the then-available amount and retain the proceeds as cash collateral, apply the proceeds to secured obligations, or both.

5. Security Notices; Maintenance; and Replenishment
LS Power Grid may issue a Security Notice whenever it reasonably determines that the Required Security Amount exceeds available Security, including as a result of a draw, application of cash collateral, change in the Required Security Amount, loss of Qualifying Retail Security Support, or other event affecting Security availability.

Eligible Customer shall provide any required increase, restoration, replacement, or other cure within fifteen (15) Business Days after receipt of the Security Notice, or by the applicable in- service date if earlier.

6. Draws; Application; and Release

PDF page 273

Schedule 3 - 3

LS Power Grid may draw against a Letter of Credit, apply cash collateral, or apply another accepted form of Security as permitted under Article 7. A draw, the holding of proceeds or cash collateral, or replenishment does not by itself satisfy an obligation or reduce Cumulative Revenue Responsibility. Proceeds of Operational Security become Applied Security Proceeds only when applied to an identified secured obligation as provided in Section 7.6; proceeds of Construction Security are treated as provided in Sections 3.3.2 and 3.3.3.

LS Power Grid shall release excess Security after the applicable Reconciliation Statement becomes final. LS Power Grid shall release all remaining Security within thirty (30) Calendar Days after final satisfaction of all secured obligations, subject to any specifically identified unresolved dispute.

7. Required Security Amount Not a Limitation
The Required Security Amount is based on then-current estimates and calculations and does not limit Eligible Customer’s total liability or responsibility for its obligations under this Agreement.

Required project information

Schedule 5 - 1

Schedule 5
MTRC Calculation Template and Cumulative Revenue Ledger
This Schedule contains the project-specific calculation of each Estimated Annual MTRC and Final Annual MTRC and the Cumulative Revenue Ledger. LS Power Grid shall calculate each Annual MTRC solely under this Schedule. The Final Annual MTRC shall equal the applicable final incurred capital cost multiplied by the Project-Specific Levelized Fixed Charge Rate calculated in this Schedule. The calculation shall identify the applicable capital-cost input, present-worth tax-depreciation benefits, Formula Rate inputs, fixed expense allocator, applicable allocation percentage, Annual MTRC, Total Required Revenue Commitment, Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, and supporting inputs.

For purposes of calculating present-worth federal and state income-tax depreciation benefits, the Schedule applies a levelized-depreciation rate of five percent (5%) of the applicable capital cost input in each year of the twenty (20)-year MTRC Term. This levelized-depreciation schedule is not a separate annual charge. The Schedule is fixed on the Final MTRC Determination Date except to correct an error or fraud, to implement an effective prospective reassignment, to implement a final FERC order applicable to the calculation, to implement a Dispute Determination under Section 11.5, or to reflect, prospectively, a change in (i) LS Power Grid’s return on equity component of its Formula Rate, resulting from a final FERC order accepting or approving such change, or (ii) applicable federal or state tax rates or tax law enacted after the Final MTRC Determination Date; provided that, the five percent (5%) annual levelized- depreciation schedule shall remain fixed for the MTRC Term unless a final FERC order requires otherwise.

The following project-specific calculation and ledger shall be completed for each Covered Upgrade. LS Power Grid shall include an updated ledger statement in each Reconciliation Statement served on Eligible Customer under Sections 6.6 and 6.7; this Schedule does not impose a separate FERC filing requirement.

Annual Ledger Service. LS Power Grid shall update the Cumulative Revenue Ledger at least annually in connection with its Annual Update and shall include the resulting statement and the supporting workpapers in each Reconciliation Statement served on Eligible Customer. The statement is an agreement-administration record and is not required to be filed with FERC unless FERC otherwise requires. Any dispute concerning the statement shall be resolved under Article 11 of the Agreement.

Attachment 1 – Interconnection Request Form

1

 LARGE LOAD INTERCONNECTION PROCEDURES (LLIP)

Section 1.
Objectives and Applicability
1.1 Objectives

The objective of this LLIP is to implement requirements for Large Load interconnections to the Participating TO’s Transmission System pursuant to the TO Tariff.  For purposes of this LLIP, the “Participating TO” is LS Power Grid California, LLC (“LS Power Grid”), and the “TO Tariff” is the LS Power Grid California, LLC Transmission Owner Tariff.  Because LS Power Grid is a Non-Load-Serving Participating TO and has no End-Use Customers, a Large Load interconnecting under this LLIP will not be a retail transmission customer of the Participating TO.

1.2 Applicability

The LLIP is applicable to a Large Load Interconnection Customer that is either (1) proposing to interconnect a Large Load to the Participating TO’s Transmission System; (2) seeking to increase the capacity of an existing Large Load processed under the LLIP; or (3) with respect to an existing load interconnected to the Participating TO’s Transmission System that was not processed under the LLIP, seeking an increase of 50 MW or greater.  This LLIP only applies to completed Interconnection Requests submitted after the effective date of this Appendix VI to the TO Tariff.  Consistent with Section 10A of the TO Tariff, all requests for ECLL Interconnection shall be directed to the Participating TO.  An Interconnection Request submitted under this LLIP shall constitute the ECLL Interconnection Application, and the Interconnection Studies and the Large Load Interconnection Agreement under this LLIP shall constitute the study process and the ECLL Interconnection Agreement, respectively, for purposes of Section 10A of the TO Tariff.

Section 2.
Definitions

Terms used in this LLIP with initial capitalization shall have the meanings set forth below.  The singular of any definition shall include the plural and the plural shall include the singular.  If a term with initial capitalization used herein is not defined, such term shall have the meanings ascribed to such term in Section 3 of the TO Tariff.

Affected System shall mean an electric system other than the Participating TO’s Transmission System that may be affected by the proposed interconnection.

Affected System Operator shall mean the entity that owns or operates an Affected System, and includes an Affected System Owner as defined in any Cost Recovery Agreement.

Applicable Laws and Regulations shall mean all duly promulgated applicable federal, state and local laws, regulations, rules, ordinances, codes, decrees, judgments, directives, or judicial or administrative orders, permits and other duly authorized actions of any Governmental Authority.

PDF page 291

2

Base Case shall mean data including, but not limited to, base power flow, short circuit and stability data bases, underlying load, generation, and transmission facility assumptions, contingency lists, including relevant remedial action schemes, and transmission diagrams used to perform the Interconnection Studies.  The Base Case may include Critical Energy Infrastructure Information (as that term is defined by FERC).  The Base Case may include transmission facilities as approved by the Participating TO or ISO, as applicable, Reliability Network Upgrades that are included in a signed interconnection agreement, Large Load Network Upgrades, Large Loads and any co-located generation, and load forecast. The Base Case may also include generation facilities that (i) are directly interconnected to a Distribution System or the ISO Controlled Grid; (ii) are interconnected to Affected Systems and may have an impact on the Interconnection Request; (iii) have a pending request to interconnect to a Distribution System or an Affected System; or (iv) are not interconnected to a Distribution System or the ISO Controlled Grid, but are subject to the terms and conditions of a fully executed interconnection agreement (or its equivalent predecessor agreement) or for which an unexecuted interconnection agreement (or its equivalent predecessor agreement) has been requested to be filed with FERC.

Business Day shall mean Monday through Friday, excluding Federal Holidays.

Calendar Day shall mean any day including Saturday, Sunday or a Federal Holiday.

Commercial Readiness Deposit shall mean a deposit paid as set forth in LLIP Sections 4.2.1 and 4.6.1.4.

Confidential Information shall mean any confidential, proprietary or trade secret information of a plan, specification, pattern, procedure, design, device, list, concept, policy or compilation relating to the present or planned business of a Party, which is designated as confidential by the Party supplying the information, whether conveyed orally, electronically, in writing, through inspection, or otherwise.

Cost Recovery Agreement shall have the meaning set forth in Section 3 of the TO Tariff.

Dispute Resolution shall mean the applicable procedure for resolution of a dispute between the Parties as described in Section 14 of the TO Tariff, including, as applicable, the ISO ADR Procedures.

Flexible Interim Load Interconnection (FILI) Service shall have the meaning set forth in Section 3 of the TO Tariff.

FILI Study shall mean the study performed by the Participating TO pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.2 to determine the availability of Interim Firm Interconnection Service and FILI Service.

Interconnection Financial Security shall mean any of the financial instruments listed in LLIP Section 4.7.1 provided by the Large Load Interconnection Customer to comply with its obligations under the LLIP or the Large Load Interconnection Agreement.

PDF page 292

3

Interconnection Request shall mean a request, in the form of Attachment 1 to Appendix VI to the TO Tariff, to interconnect a new Large Load, or to increase the capacity of, or make a Material Modification to the operating characteristics of, an existing Large Load that is interconnected with the Participating TO’s Transmission System.

Interconnection Study shall mean any of the following studies: the Large Load Interconnection Study, the FILI Study, restudies, and any additional studies performed pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.3.

Interconnection Study Deposit shall mean the cash deposit provided to the Participating TO under LLIP Section 4.2.1 as a requirement of a valid Interconnection Request to be used to offset the cost of the Interconnection Studies.

Large Load Capacity shall mean the maximum capacity of the Large Load at the Point of Interconnection.

Large Load Customer shall mean the End-User that owns, operates, or proposes to develop the Large Load and receives, or will receive, retail transmission service from the Large Load Interconnection Customer, and shall be the Large Load Customer for purposes of the TO Tariff and any Cost Recovery Agreement.

Large Load Interconnection Agreement shall mean the interconnection agreement between the Participating TO and the Large Load Interconnection Customer, developed by the Participating TO consistent with the TO Tariff and the results of the Interconnection Studies and negotiated between the Parties pursuant to LLIP Section 5, which sets forth the terms and conditions governing the interconnection of the Large Load to the Participating TO’s Transmission System, including, without limitation, cost responsibility, payment provisions, cost recovery, and financial security for the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, as it may be amended from time to time. The Large Load Interconnection Agreement shall constitute the ECLL Interconnection Agreement for purposes of the TO Tariff, as applicable, and shall be filed with FERC by the Participating TO to the extent required. For purposes of the TO Tariff, the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades identified in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement shall together constitute the ECLL Interconnection Facilities.

Large Load Interconnection Customer shall mean an Eligible Customer for Large Load (“ECLL”), as defined in Section 3 of the TO Tariff, that proposes to interconnect its facilities with the Participating TO’s Transmission System for the purpose of providing retail transmission service to a Large Load.

Large Load Interconnection Customer Interconnection Facilities shall mean all facilities and equipment owned, controlled, or operated by the Large Load Interconnection Customer or the Large Load Customer up to the Point of Change of Ownership as identified in the Large

PDF page 293

4

Load Interconnection Agreement, including any modifications, additions or upgrades to such facilities and equipment.

Large Load Interconnection Procedures (LLIP) shall mean the interconnection procedures applicable to an Interconnection Request set forth in this Appendix VI to the TO Tariff.

Large Load Interconnection Study shall mean an engineering study conducted by the Participating TO for a Large Load Interconnection Customer under the LLIP that evaluates the impact of the proposed interconnection on the safety and reliability of Participating TO’s Transmission System and, if applicable, an Affected System. The Large Load Interconnection Study will also determine a list of facilities (including Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades), the cost of those facilities, and the time required to interconnect the Large Load with the Participating TO’s Transmission System. The scope of the study is defined in LLIP Section 4.6.1.1.

Large Load Network Upgrades shall mean the additions, modifications, and upgrades to the ISO Controlled Grid at or beyond the Point of Interconnection necessary to serve one or more Large Loads safely and reliably that are identified through the Large Load Interconnection Study and submitted to the ISO for concurrence pursuant to Section 25.7 of the ISO Tariff. Large Load Network Upgrades do not include Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities. To the extent a Large Load Network Upgrade is owned or to be constructed by the Participating TO and is identified in Schedule 1 to a Cost Recovery Agreement, such Large Load Network Upgrades shall constitute Large Load-Driven Upgrades, as defined in that Cost Recovery Agreement.

Large Load Study Process Agreement shall mean the agreement, developed and tendered by the Participating TO pursuant to LLIP Section 4.5, entered into by the Large Load Interconnection Customer and the Participating TO which sets forth the Parties’ agreement to perform Interconnection Studies under the LLIP.

Material Modification shall mean those modifications that have a material impact on the cost or timing of any Interconnection Request, or any other valid interconnection request to the Participating TO or the ISO, with an equal or later Queue Position date.

Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities shall mean all facilities and equipment owned, controlled, or operated by the Participating TO from the Point of Change of Ownership to the Point of Interconnection as identified in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, including any modifications, additions or upgrades to such facilities and equipment. Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities are transmission facilities but are not controlled by the ISO.  Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities are sole use facilities and shall not include Large Load Network Upgrades.

Participating TO’s Transmission System shall mean the facilities owned and operated by the Participating TO and that have been placed under the ISO’s Operational Control, which facilities form part of the ISO Controlled Grid.

PDF page 294

5

Point of Change of Ownership shall mean the point, as set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, where the Large Load Interconnection Customer's interconnection facilities connect to the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities.

Point of Interconnection shall mean the point, as set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, where the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities connect to the Participating TO’s Transmission System.  For purposes of the TO Tariff, the Point of Interconnection shall be the Point of Withdrawal for the Large Load.

Queue Position shall mean the order of a valid Interconnection Request, relative to all other pending valid Interconnection Requests, established pursuant to LLIP Section 3.8.

Reasonable Efforts shall mean, with respect to an action required to be attempted or taken by a Party under the LLIP or the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, efforts that are timely and consistent with Good Utility Practice and are otherwise substantially equivalent to those a Party would use to protect its own interests.

Report Meeting shall mean the meeting among the Participating TO, the Large Load Interconnection Customer, and if applicable, the ISO and other Affected System Operators to discuss the results of the Interconnection Studies as set forth in the LLIP.

Scoping Meeting shall mean the meeting between representatives of the Large Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO, and if applicable, the ISO, conducted for the purpose of discussing alternative interconnection options, to exchange information including any transmission data and earlier study evaluations that would be reasonably expected to impact such interconnection options, to analyze such information, and to determine the potential feasible Points of Interconnection.

Service Commencement Date shall mean the date the Large Load is initially operated in parallel with the Participating TO’s Transmission System and transmission interconnection service is provided to the Large Load Interconnection Customer pursuant to the terms of an executed Large Load Interconnection Agreement. For purposes of any Cost Recovery Agreement, the Service Commencement Date shall be the Large Load Service Commencement Date specified in Schedule 1 to that agreement.

Site Control shall have the meaning set forth in Section 3 of the TO Tariff.

System Protection Facilities shall mean the equipment, including necessary protection signal communications equipment, that protects (1) the Participating TO’s Transmission System, Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, ISO Controlled Grid, and Affected Systems from faults or other electrical disturbances occurring at the Large Load and (2) the Large Load from faults or other electrical system disturbances occurring on the ISO Controlled Grid, Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, and Affected Systems or on other delivery systems or other generating systems to which the ISO Controlled Grid is directly connected.

Transmission Interconnection Service shall mean the service provided by the Participating TO associated with interconnecting the Large Load to the Participating TO’s Transmission

PDF page 295

6

System and enabling the Large Load Interconnection Customer to receive electric energy at the Point of Interconnection, pursuant to the terms of the Large Load Interconnection Agreement and the TO Tariff.

Withdrawal Penalty shall mean the penalty assessed by Participating TO to a Large Load Interconnection Customer that chooses to withdraw or is deemed withdrawn from Participating TO’s interconnection queue or whose Large Load does not otherwise reach its Service Commencement Date. The calculation of the Withdrawal Penalty is set forth in LLIP Section 3.10.1.

Section 3.
General Provisions Applicable to All Interconnection Requests

3.1
Interconnection Requests
