A Large Load Interconnection Customer shall submit to Participating TO an Interconnection Request in the form of Attachment 1 to this LLIP for processing under the LLIP.  Consistent with Section 10A.2.1 of the TO Tariff, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall send a copy of the Interconnection Request to the ISO, and the Interconnection Request and all supporting documentation shall be submitted electronically to the Participating TO in the manner posted on the Participating TO’s public website.

An Interconnection Request for the expansion of capacity of an existing Large Load shall be treated the same as an Interconnection Request for a new Large Load pursuant to this LLIP.

Co-located generation serving a Large Load that will not export to the ISO Controlled Grid shall be interconnected in accordance with the generator interconnection rules of the applicable ECLL, as approved by its Local Regulatory Authority (including, for a UDC subject to the jurisdiction of the CPUC, CPUC Electric Rule 21), consistent with Section 10A.5 of the TO Tariff; provided that, where such co-located generation is included in the Interconnection Request, the Participating TO will evaluate its incremental impacts on the Participating TO’s Transmission System as part of the LLIP study process. Co- located generation that will export to the ISO Controlled Grid is required to submit a generator interconnection request to the ISO in accordance with Section 25.7.6 of the ISO Tariff for evaluation of the proposed generator interconnection and the Large Load will be evaluated pursuant to the LLIP.  For a Large Load co- located with an exporting generating facility, the ISO will be a party to a pro forma agreement that bridges the generator’s Large Generator Interconnection Agreement with the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, or will negotiate a non-conforming Large Generator Interconnection Agreement, consistent with Section 10A.5 of the TO Tariff.

3.2
Transmission Interconnection Service

3.2.1 The Product.  Transmission Interconnection Service allows Large Load Interconnection Customer to connect the Large Load to the Participating

PDF page 296

7

TO’s Transmission System and be eligible to receive electric energy and capacity from the ISO Controlled Grid.

3.2.2 No Applicability to Transmission Service.  Nothing in this LLIP shall constitute a request for transmission service or confer upon a Large Load Interconnection Customer any right to receive transmission service.

3.2.3 Roles and Responsibilities.

3.2.3.1 Each Interconnection Request will be subject to the direction and oversight of the Participating TO.  The Participating TO will conduct or cause to be performed the required Interconnection Studies and any additional studies the Participating TO determines to be reasonably necessary.  The analysis of impacts on, and upgrades required to, the Participating TO’s Transmission System will be performed by the Participating TO and will be subject to review and concurrence by the ISO.  The Participating TO will perform all required studies related to the Participating TO’s Transmission System and will coordinate with Affected System Operators in accordance with LLIP Section 3.5.

3.2.3.2 The Participating TO will complete or cause to be completed all studies as required within the timelines provided in this LLIP.

3.2.3.3 Each Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the actual costs of all Interconnection Studies, and any additional studies the Participating TO determines to be reasonably necessary in response to the Interconnection Request.  The Participating TO shall reimburse the ISO for the actual cost of any portion of the Interconnection Studies that the ISO performs related to the ISO Controlled Grid, as applicable.

3.2.3.3.1 The Participating TO shall issue invoices for Interconnection Studies that shall include a detailed and itemized accounting of the cost of each Interconnection Study.  Whenever the actual cost of performing the

Interconnection Studies exceeds the Interconnection
Study Deposit, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the undisputed difference in accordance with the Participating TO issued invoice within thirty (30)

Calendar Days.  The Participating TO shall not be
obligated to continue to have any studies conducted
unless the Large Load Interconnection Customer has paid all undisputed amounts in compliance herewith.

PDF page 297

8

3.3
Comparability
Participating TO shall receive, process and analyze all Interconnection Requests in a timely manner as set forth in this LLIP.  Participating TO will use the same Reasonable Efforts in processing and analyzing Interconnection Requests from all Large Load Interconnection Customers, whether the Large Load is owned by Participating TO and its Affiliates or by a third party.

3.4
Base Case Data
Participating TO shall provide Base Case data to a Large Load Interconnection Customer upon request subject to confidentiality provisions in LLIP Section 7.1. Participating TO shall provide base power flow, short circuit databases, including all underlying assumptions, and contingency list used in the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Studies.  Participating TO is permitted to require that the Large Load Interconnection Customer sign a confidentiality agreement before the release of commercially sensitive information or Critical Energy Infrastructure Information in the Base Case data. Such Base Cases may include all (i) Large Loads, (ii) generation projects, and (iii) transmission projects, including merchant transmission projects that are proposed for the Participating TO’s Transmission System for which a transmission expansion plan has been submitted and approved by the applicable authority.

3.5
Coordination with Affected Systems
The Participating TO will notify the Affected System Operators that are potentially affected by a Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Request.  The Participating TO will coordinate the conduct of any studies required to determine the impact of the Interconnection Request on Affected Systems with Affected System Operators and, if possible, include those results (if available) in its applicable Interconnection Study within the time frame specified in this LLIP.  Participating TO will include such Affected System Operators in all meetings held with Large Load Interconnection Customer as required by this LLIP.  Large Load Interconnection Customer will cooperate with Participating TO in all matters related to the conduct of studies and the determination of modifications to Affected Systems.  If an Affected System Operator, that has been informed its system is an Affected System refuses to cooperate with the Participating TO with whom interconnection has been requested in all matters related to the conduct of studies and the determination of whether modifications to its Affected System are necessary to avoid adverse impacts, it waives any right in any fora that the Participating TO’s provision of service has adversely impacted such Affected System Operator.

3.6
Capacity of the Large Load

The Interconnection Request shall be evaluated using the maximum capacity requested by the Large Load Interconnection Customer.  However, if the maximum capacity that the Large Load is capable of receiving is greater than the

PDF page 298

9

maximum capacity requested by the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Large Load Interconnection Customer will be required to limit the Large Load capacity to no greater than the amount requested (e.g., through use of a control system, power relay(s), or other similar device settings or adjustments), and the Large Load Interconnection Customer must obtain the Participating TO’s agreement, with such agreement not to be unreasonably withheld, that the manner in which the Large Load Interconnection Customer proposes to implement such a limit will not adversely affect the safety and reliability of the Participating TO’s Transmission System.  If the Participating TO does not so agree, then the Interconnection Request must be withdrawn or revised to specify the maximum capacity that the Large Load is capable of receiving.    Furthermore, nothing in this section shall prevent a Participating TO from considering a capacity higher than the limited capacity, if appropriate, when evaluating system protection impacts.

3.7
Proposed Service Commencement Date
The  proposed Service Commencement Date of the new Large Load or increase in capacity of the existing Large Load shall not exceed seven years from the date the Interconnection Request is received by Participating TO for those Interconnection Requests studied in accordance with the Large Load Interconnection Procedures, unless Large Load Interconnection Customer demonstrates and the Participating TO agrees, such agreement not to be unreasonably withheld, that engineering, permitting and construction of the new Large Load or increase in capacity of the existing Large Load will take longer than the seven-year period.

3.7.1 Interconnection Request.  If the proposed Service Commencement Date in the Interconnection Request is more than seven years from the date the Interconnection Request is received by the Participating TO, the Large Load Interconnection Customer must demonstrate, subject to the Participating TO’s agreement, such agreement not to be unreasonably withheld, that the required engineering, permitting and construction of the Large Load will take longer than the seven-year period.

3.7.2 After Start of the Large Load Interconnection Study.  Any request to extend the Service Commencement Date after commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study under the LLIP, or following execution of a Large Load Interconnection Agreement, shall be subject to the following:

(i) Requested extension does not exceed seven years from Service Commencement Date in the original Interconnection Request.  A request to extend the Service Commencement Date that does not exceed seven years from the Service Commencement Date in the original

Interconnection Request is subject to the modification assessment process specified in LLIP Section 4.6.1.6.1.

(ii) Requested extension exceeds seven years from Service Commencement Date in the original Interconnection Request.  A request to extend the

PDF page 299

10

Service Commencement Date that exceeds seven years from the Service Commencement Date in the original Interconnection Request shall (a) demonstrate, subject to the Participating TO’s agreement, such agreement not to be unreasonably withheld, that engineering, permitting and construction of the new Large Load or increase in capacity of the existing Large Load will take longer than the seven-year period, and (b) be subject to the modification assessment process specified in LLIP Section 4.6.1.6.1.

Any extension of the Service Commencement Date under this LLIP Section 3.7.2 shall not extend the Outside Date under any applicable Cost Recovery Agreement unless the parties to that agreement agree in writing to such extension.

3.8   Queue Position

           3.8.1 Assignment of Queue Position.
Participating TO shall assign a Queue Position as follows: the Queue Position shall be assigned based upon the date and time of receipt of all items required pursuant to the provisions of LLIP Section 4.2.1.

3.8.2 Higher Queue Position.
A higher Queue Position assigned to an Interconnection Request is one that has been placed “earlier” in the queue in relation to another Interconnection Request that is assigned a lower Queue Position.

3.9
Withdrawal
Large Load Interconnection Customer may withdraw its Interconnection Request at any time by written notice of such withdrawal to Participating TO, and the Participating TO will notify the ISO and Affected System Operators, if any, within three (3) Business Days of receipt of such a notice.  In addition, after confirmation by the Participating TO of a valid Interconnection Request, if the Large Load Interconnection Customer fails to adhere to all requirements of this LLIP, except as provided in LLIP Section 7.2 (Disputes), Participating TO shall deem the Interconnection Request to be withdrawn and shall provide written notice to Large Load Interconnection Customer of the deemed withdrawal within five (5) Business Days and an explanation of the reasons for such deemed withdrawal.  Upon receipt of such written notice, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall have five (5) Business Days in which to either (i) respond with information or action that cures the deficiency, or (ii) support its position that the deemed withdrawal was erroneous and notify the Participating TO of its intent to pursue Dispute Resolution.

For an Interconnection Request under the LLIP, withdrawal shall result in the removal of the Interconnection Request from the Interconnection Studies.  If a Large Load Interconnection Customer disputes the withdrawal and removal from the Interconnection Studies and has elected to pursue Dispute Resolution, Large

PDF page 300

11

Load Interconnection Customer's Interconnection Request will not be considered in any ongoing Interconnection Study during the Dispute Resolution process.

In the event of such withdrawal, Participating TO, subject to the provisions in LLIP Section 7.1, as applicable, shall provide, at Large Load Interconnection Customer's request, all information that Participating TO developed for any completed study conducted up to the date of withdrawal of the Interconnection Request.

3.10
Withdrawal Penalty

A Large Load Interconnection Customer with an Interconnection Request being processed under the LLIP will be subject to a Withdrawal Penalty if it withdraws its Interconnection Request or is deemed withdrawn, or the Large Load does not otherwise achieve its Service Commencement Date, which date may be modified if the necessary Participating TO-owned facilities cannot be constructed on a timeline that meets such date, unless: (1) the withdrawal does not have a material impact on the cost or timing of any equal or later queued interconnection request;

(2) Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws after receiving Large Load Interconnection Customer’s most recent Interconnection Study report and the Large Load Network Upgrade costs assigned to the Interconnection Request identified in that report have increased by more than twenty-five percent (25%) compared to costs identified in Large Load Interconnection Customer’s preceding Interconnection Study report; or (3) Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws after receiving an Interconnection Study report issued after completion of the initial Large Load Interconnection Study and the Large Load Network Upgrade costs assigned to the Interconnection Request identified in such Interconnection Study report have increased by more than one hundred percent (100%) compared to costs identified in the initial Large Load Interconnection Study report.

3.10.1 Calculation of the Withdrawal Penalty

If the Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws its Interconnection Request or is deemed withdrawn prior to the commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, Large Load Interconnection Customer will not be subject to a Withdrawal Penalty.  If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws, is deemed withdrawn, or otherwise does not achieve its Service Commencement Date at any point after the commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, that Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Withdrawal Penalty will be the greater of: (1) the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Study Deposit required under LLIP Section 4.2.1; or (2) as follows in (a)–(c):

(a) If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws or is deemed

PDF page 301

12

withdrawn during the Large Load Interconnection Study or after receipt of a Large Load Interconnection Study report, Large Load Interconnection Customer will be charged two (2) times the actual cost of the Large Load Interconnection Study performed for the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

(b) If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws or is deemed withdrawn after receipt of the draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement but before Large Load Interconnection Customer has executed a Large Load Interconnection Agreement or has requested that its Large Load Interconnection Agreement be filed unexecuted, Large Load Interconnection Customer will be charged ten percent (10%) of its estimated Large Load Network Upgrade costs as reflected in the most recently completed Interconnection Study.

(c) If Large Load Interconnection Customer has executed a Large Load Interconnection Agreement or has requested that its Large Load Interconnection Agreement be filed unexecuted and subsequently withdraws its Interconnection Request, the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Withdrawal Penalty will be calculated as set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement.

The Commercial Readiness Deposit shall be retained as financial security for the Withdrawal Penalty (if applicable) and, other than as set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, any security not applied to the Withdrawal Penalty will be released to the Large Load Interconnection Customer following withdrawal or the Service Commencement Date. Where a Cost Recovery Agreement applies, (i) any Commercial Readiness Deposit or Interconnection Financial Security provided for the same costs shall count toward, and shall not be duplicated by, the Construction Security required under the Cost Recovery Agreement, and (ii) any Withdrawal Penalty paid with respect to a Large Load Network Upgrade shall be credited against the Pre-In-Service Termination Costs payable under the Cost Recovery Agreement for that upgrade, so that the Participating TO does not recover the same costs more than once.

3.11
Standards for Co-located Inverter Based Generating Facilities

Inverters used for the production, and/or later injection from storage, of electricity shall meet the inverter certification standards of UL-1741 and UL-1741 Supplement B.

Section 4.  Large Load Study Process

4.1
Timing For Submitting Interconnection Requests
A Large Load Interconnection Customer may submit an Interconnection Request for processing under the LLIP at any time during the year. The Participating TO,

PDF page 302

13

in coordination with the ISO, as applicable, will study the Interconnection Request.

4.2
Processing of Interconnection Request

4.2.1
Initiating an Interconnection Request.  To initiate an
Interconnection Request under the LLIP, Large Load Interconnection Customer must submit all of the following: (i) an Interconnection Study Deposit of $250,000; (ii) a completed Interconnection Request in the form of Attachment 1 to the LLIP, including preferred Point of Interconnection and voltage level, all information required by Section 10A.2.1 of the TO Tariff (other than any election of interim service, which shall be made in accordance with LLIP Section 4.6.2), and all other technical data; (iii) a Commercial Readiness Deposit equal to two times the Interconnection Study Deposit described in this LLIP Section 4.2.1 in the form of an irrevocable letter of credit, cash, a surety bond, or other form of security that is reasonably acceptable to Participating TO, in accordance with LLIP Section 4.7.1. This Commercial Readiness Deposit is refunded to Large Load Interconnection Customer according to LLIP Section 3.10.1; however, the Participating TO shall be entitled to hold the Commercial Readiness Deposit until the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Large Load achieves its Service Commencement Date; and (iv) demonstration of Site Control.

4.2.1.1 Use of Interconnection Study Deposit.  The Interconnection Study Deposit shall be applied to pay for prudent costs incurred by the Participating TO, the ISO, or third parties at the direction of the Participating TO or ISO, as applicable, to perform and administer the Interconnection Studies.

In the event a Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws its Interconnection Request prior to the commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, Large Load Interconnection Customer must pay Participating TO the actual costs of processing its Interconnection Request. In the event a Large Load

Interconnection Customer withdraws after commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, Participating TO shall charge and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the actual costs of the Interconnection Studies.

Any difference between the Interconnection Study Deposit and the actual cost of the Interconnection Studies shall be paid by or refunded to the Large Load Interconnection Customer. Any

invoices for Interconnection Studies shall include a detailed and itemized accounting of the cost of each Interconnection Study. Large Load Interconnection Customers shall pay any such

undisputed costs within thirty (30) Calendar Days of receipt of an

PDF page 303

14

invoice. If Large Load Interconnection Customer fails to pay such undisputed costs within the time allotted, its Interconnection Request shall be deemed withdrawn and will be subject to a Withdrawal Penalty pursuant to LLIP Section 3.10.1.

The Participating TO will reimburse the ISO or third parties, as applicable, for all work performed on behalf of the withdrawn Interconnection Request at the Participating TO’s direction. The Large Load Interconnection Customer must pay all monies due before it is allowed to obtain any Interconnection Study data or results.

4.3
Validation of Interconnection Request

4.3.1 Acknowledgment of Interconnection Request.  Participating TO shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer within ten (10) Business Days of receipt of the Interconnection Request, which notice shall state whether the Interconnection Request is deemed valid.

4.3.2 Deficiencies in Interconnection Request.  An Interconnection Request will not be considered to be a valid request until all items in LLIP Section 4.2.1 have been received by Participating TO and deemed valid by the Participating TO.  If an Interconnection Request fails to meet the requirements set forth in LLIP Section 4.2.1, Participating TO shall include in its notification to the Large Load Interconnection Customer under LLIP Section 4.3.1 the reasons for such failure and that the Interconnection Request does not constitute a valid request.  Consistent with Section 10A.2.3 of the TO Tariff, the Participating TO will attempt to remedy deficiencies in the Interconnection Request through informal communications with the Large Load Interconnection Customer, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide Participating TO the additional requested information needed to constitute a valid request. Whenever the additional requested information is provided by the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Participating TO shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer within five (5) Business Days of receipt of the additional requested information whether the Interconnection Request is valid.  If the Interconnection Request continues to fail to meet the requirements set forth in LLIP Section 4.2.1, the Participating TO shall include in its notification to the Large Load Interconnection Customer the reasons for such failure.  If an Interconnection Request has not been deemed valid, the Large Load Interconnection Customer must submit all information necessary to meet the requirements of LLIP Section 4.2.1 no later than twenty (20) Business Days after the date the original Interconnection Request was submitted, or ten (10) Business Days after the Participating TO first provided notice that the Interconnection Request was not valid, whichever is later.

PDF page 304

15

Interconnection Requests that have not met the requirements of LLIP Section 4.2.1 within twenty (20) Business Days after the date the original Interconnection Request was submitted or ten (10) Business Days after the Participating TO first provided notice that the Interconnection Request was not valid, whichever is later, will be deemed invalid, will be returned to the Large Load Interconnection Customer in accordance with Section 10A.2.3 of the TO Tariff, and will not be included in the LLIP.

Interconnection Requests deemed invalid under this LLIP Section 4.3.2 are not subject to LLIP Section 3.9.  Large Load Interconnection Customers with invalid Interconnection Requests under this LLIP Section 4.3.2 may seek relief under LLIP Section 7.2 by so notifying the Participating TO within two (2) Business Days of the notice of invalidity.

4.4     Potential Deferral of the Interconnection Studies

           The Participating TO may defer commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study when it determines, using Good Utility Practice and reasonable engineering judgment, that the Interconnection Request cannot be studied meaningfully until other pending study results for higher-queued projects are available.

           Within ten (10) Business Days after deeming an Interconnection Request valid under LLIP Section 4.3.1, the Participating TO will notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer in writing if it is deferring the start of the Interconnection Studies. The notice will include: (i) information regarding the pending studies; and (ii) the estimated start date of the Large Load Interconnection Study.  During the period of deferral, the Participating TO will provide a monthly status update to the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

4.5
Scoping Meeting
Within fifteen (15) Business Days after the Participating TO notifies the Large Load Interconnection Customer that the Interconnection Request has been deemed valid as set forth in LLIP Section 4.3.2, the Participating TO shall establish a date agreeable to the Large Load Interconnection Customer, and the ISO, if applicable, for the Scoping Meeting.

The purpose of the Scoping Meeting shall be to discuss reasonable Service Commencement Dates and alternative interconnection options, to exchange information including any transmission data that would reasonably be expected to impact such interconnection options, to analyze such information and to determine the potential feasible Points of Interconnection and eliminate alternatives given resources and available information. The Scoping Meeting shall also include discussion of a deferral of the Interconnection Studies in accordance with LLIP Section 4.4.

PDF page 305

16

The Participating TO will bring to the meeting, as reasonably necessary to accomplish its purpose, such already available technical data, including, but not limited to, (i) general facility loadings, (ii) general instability issues, (iii) general short circuit issues, (iv) general voltage issues, and (v) general reliability issues.

The Large Load Interconnection Customer will bring to the Scoping Meeting, in addition to the technical data form (Appendix A to Attachment 1) submitted with its Interconnection Request, any system studies previously performed.  The Participating TO, the ISO, if applicable, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer will also bring to the meeting personnel and other resources as may be reasonably required to accomplish the purpose of the meeting in the time allocated for the meeting.  On the basis of the meeting, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall designate its Point of Interconnection.  The duration of the meeting shall be sufficient to accomplish its purpose.

Within five (5) Business Days after the Scoping Meeting, the Participating TO shall tender to the Large Load Interconnection Customer a Large Load Study Process Agreement developed by the Participating TO consistent with the TO Tariff and this LLIP, which shall contain an outline of the scope of the Large Load Interconnection Study, contain a non-binding good faith estimate of the cost to perform such study, and shall specify that the Large Load Interconnection Customer is responsible for the actual cost of the Interconnection Studies, including reasonable administrative costs.  No later than thirty (30) Calendar Days after the Scoping Meeting, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall either execute and deliver to the Participating TO  the Large Load Study Process Agreement or request that the Participating TO file it unexecuted with FERC, or the Interconnection Request shall be deemed withdrawn.  If an unexecuted filing is requested, the Interconnection Studies will be paused until the Large Load Study Process Agreement is made effective by FERC.

4.6
Interconnection Studies
The Interconnection Studies shall consist of a Large Load Interconnection Study, any FILI Study requested pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.2, and any additional studies, including but not limited to those identified in LLIP Section 4.6.3 required for interconnection of the Large Load, and co-located generation not exporting to the ISO Controlled Grid, to the Participating TO’s Transmission System.    The analysis of impacts on, and upgrades required to, the Participating TO’s Transmission System will be performed by the Participating TO and will be subject to the review and concurrence of the ISO in accordance with Section 25.7 of the ISO Tariff. The Interconnection Studies will identify Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and required Large Load Network Upgrades necessary to mitigate thermal overloads and voltage violations, and address short circuit, stability, and reliability issues associated with the requested Transmission Interconnection Service.

PDF page 306

17

All cost estimates for Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, as applicable, contained in the Interconnection Studies will be set forth in the Interconnection Study report in present dollar costs as well as time-adjusted dollar costs, adjusted to the estimated year of construction of the components being constructed.

The Participating TO will prepare an Interconnection Study report to document the results of the Interconnection Studies.

4.6.1 Large Load Interconnection Study.

4.6.1.1  Scope of the Large Load Interconnection Study.  The Large Load Interconnection Study will consist of a  short circuit analysis, a power flow analysis, an electromagnetic transient (“EMT”) screening, a non-binding good faith estimate of the scope and cost of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, and any other studies that are deemed necessary. The Large Load Interconnection Study will also identify the need for any additional studies (e.g., stability analysis, EMT study, etc.) to be performed as part of the scope of work set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.3.  When there is co-located generation not exporting to the ISO Controlled Grid and not subject to electrical short circuit duty dependence, the Large Load Interconnection Study will identify the impacts associated with the Large Load and any incremental impacts of the co-located generation.

The Large Load Interconnection Study will consider the use of alternative transmission technologies including dynamic line ratings, advanced power flow control devices, advanced conductors, and transmission switching, when evaluating transmission solutions to accommodate new Large Loads and their associated transmission system impacts.  If traditional network upgrades are selected instead of alternative transmission technologies, the Participating TO will provide a demonstration of why alternative transmission technologies are not feasible or would not result in lower costs or a faster timeline for accommodating the interconnection of the Large Load.

The Large Load Interconnection Study shall state the assumptions upon which it is based, state the results of the analyses, and provide the requirement or potential impediments to providing the requested Transmission Interconnection Service, including an estimate of the cost and length of time that would be necessary to correct any problems identified in those analyses and implement the Interconnection.

PDF page 307

18

The Large Load Interconnection Study shall provide a list of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, as applicable, that are required as a result of the Interconnection Request.

4.6.1.2 Timing of the Large Load Interconnection Study Results.  The Participating TO shall use Reasonable Efforts to complete the Large Load Interconnection Study within ninety (90) Calendar Days after the execution of a Large Load Study Process Agreement or, if the Large Load Study Process Agreement is filed unexecuted, after it is made effective by FERC.  The Participating TO will issue a Large Load Interconnection Study report to the Large Load Interconnection Customer. The Participating TO will share study results with the ISO for review and concurrence.

At any time the Participating TO determines that it will not meet the required time frame for completing the Large Load Interconnection  Study, Participating TO shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer as to the schedule status of the Large Load Interconnection Study and provide an estimated completion date with an explanation of the reasons why additional time is required.

Upon request, the Participating TO shall provide the Large Load Interconnection Customer all supporting documentation, workpapers and relevant pre-Interconnection Request and post- Interconnection Request power flow and short circuit databases for the Large Load Interconnection Study, subject to confidentiality arrangements consistent with LLIP Section 7.1.

Should the Large Load Interconnection Customer provide written comments on the Large Load Interconnection Study report at least five (5) Business Days before the Report Meeting conducted to discuss the report, the Participating TO will address the written comments in the Large Load Interconnection Study Report Meeting.  Should the Large Load Interconnection Customer provide comments at any later time (up to the time of the Report Meeting), then such comments shall be considered informal inquiries to which the Participating TO will provide informal, informational responses at the Report Meeting, to the extent possible.

The Large Load Interconnection Customer may submit, in writing, additional comments on the final Large Load Interconnection Study report up to three (3) Business Days following the Report Meeting.  Based on any discussion at the Report Meeting and any comments received from the Large Load Interconnection Customer

PDF page 308

19

or the ISO, the Participating TO will determine whether it is necessary to follow the Large Load Interconnection Study report with a revised study report.  The Participating TO will issue any such revised report to the Large Load Interconnection Customer no later than fifteen (15) Business Days following the Report Meeting. If the ISO has not completed its review of the Large Load Interconnection Study report, the Participating TO shall delay its determination of whether a revised study report is required until that review is complete.

4.6.1.3 Large Load Interconnection Study Report Meeting.  A Report Meeting shall be held among the Participating TO, the ISO, if applicable, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer to discuss the results of the Large Load Interconnection Study, including assigned cost responsibility.  Any such Report Meeting will be held within ten (10) Business Days of the date the Large Load Interconnection Study report is provided to the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

4.6.1.4 Commercial Readiness Deposit.  Within thirty (30) Calendar Days after receiving the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide an additional Commercial Readiness Deposit sufficient to bring the aggregate Commercial Readiness Deposit to twenty percent (20%) of the Participating TO’s then-current estimated construction cost of the Large Load Network Upgrades identified in that report that are attributable to the Interconnection Request.  If the final Large Load Interconnection Study report is revised following ISO review, the required Commercial Readiness Deposit shall be adjusted to reflect twenty percent (20%) of the corresponding estimated construction cost identified in the revised final report, and any required increase shall be provided within thirty (30) Calendar Days after issuance of that report.

The Commercial Readiness Deposit secures the Withdrawal Penalty and other pre-in-service obligations under this LLIP and the Large Load Interconnection Agreement. It does not establish a direct assignment of the Large Load Network Upgrade costs, constitute a payment toward an Annual MTRC, reduce the Total Required Revenue Commitment, or satisfy Operational Security under a Cost Recovery Agreement.

PDF page 309

20

To the extent a Commercial Readiness Deposit is in a form permitted under Schedule 3 of an applicable Cost Recovery Agreement, LS Power Grid may elect in writing to credit it dollar- for-dollar toward required Construction Security under that agreement. No amount of Commercial Readiness Deposit may be applied or credited more than once. Any portion not so credited shall be administered and released under Sections 3.9 and 3.10 of this LLIP and the Large Load Interconnection Agreement.

4.6.1.5 Restudy. If restudy of the Large Load Interconnection Study is required due to a higher queued project dropping out of the queue, or a modification of a higher queued project subject to LLIP Section 4.6.1.6, Participating TO shall notify Large Load Interconnection Customer in writing. Such restudy shall take no longer than forty-five (45) Calendar Days from the date of notice. Any cost of restudy shall be borne by the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

4.6.1.6 Modifications.  At any time during the course of the Interconnection Studies, the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Participating TO, or the ISO, as applicable, may identify changes to the planned interconnection that may improve the costs and benefits (including reliability) of the interconnection, and the ability of the proposed change to accommodate the Interconnection Request.  To the extent the identified changes are acceptable to the Participating TO, the ISO, as applicable, and Large Load Interconnection Customer, such acceptance not to be unreasonably withheld, Participating TO shall modify the configuration in accordance with such changes without altering the Interconnection Request’s eligibility for participating in Interconnection Studies.

4.6.1.6.1 For any modifications other than those agreed upon under LLIP Section 4.6.1.6, the Large Load Interconnection Customer must first request that Participating TO evaluate whether such modification is a Material Modification.  In response to Large Load Interconnection Customer's

request, Participating TO, in coordination with the ISO, if applicable, shall evaluate the proposed modifications prior to making them and inform Large Load Interconnection Customer in writing of whether the modifications would constitute a Material Modification.  The Participating TO may, at its option, engage the services of the ISO to assist in the assessment of the modification.  Any change to the Point of Interconnection shall constitute a Material Modification.

PDF page 310

21

The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide the Participating TO a $30,000 deposit for the modification assessment at the time the request is submitted.  Except as provided below, any modification assessment will be concluded, and a response provided to the Large Load Interconnection Customer in writing, within forty-five (45) Calendar Days from the date the Participating TO receives all of the following: 1) the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s written notice to modify the project; 2) a narrative description of the requested modification; 3) technical data required to assess the request, including an updated technical data form (Appendix A to Attachment 1 to this LLIP), if requested by the Participating TO; 4) any additional information requested by Participating TO; and

5) payment of the $30,000 deposit.  If applicable, the Participating TO shall coordinate the modification request with the ISO.  If the modification assessment cannot be completed within that time period, the Participating TO shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer and provide an estimated completion date with an explanation of the reasons why additional time is required.  The Large Load Interconnection Customer will be responsible for the actual costs incurred by the Participating TO and, if applicable, the ISO in conducting the modification assessment.  If the actual costs of the modification assessment are less than the deposit provided by the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Large Load

Interconnection Customer will be refunded the balance within thirty (30) Calendar Days of being invoiced.  If the actual costs of the modification assessment are greater than the deposit provided by the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the balance within thirty (30) Calendar Days of being invoiced.

If the proposed modification is determined to be a Material Modification by the Participating TO pursuant to the modification assessment described above, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall then either:

(i) withdraw the proposed modification, or

(ii) withdraw the proposed modification and submit a new Interconnection Request reflecting such

modification.

PDF page 311

22

4.6.2   Optional Flexible Interim Load Interconnection (FILI) Study and Agreement. If the Large Load Interconnection Study finds that the full amount of the Interconnection Request cannot be accommodated prior to the time that all ECLL Interconnection Facilities are in service, the Large Load Interconnection Customer may request a FILI Study, by written notice to the Participating TO within thirty (30) Calendar Days after receipt of the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, to determine, consistent with Section 10A.4 of the TO Tariff, whether Interim Firm Interconnection Service and/or FILI Service may be provided.  A Large Load Interconnection Customer may elect to be studied only for Interim Firm Interconnection Service and not FILI Service.  If the Large Load Interconnection Customer elects to proceed with a FILI Study, the Participating TO shall use Reasonable Efforts to complete the FILI Study within sixty (60) Calendar Days after receipt of such request. Large Load Interconnection Customer will be responsible for the actual costs of the FILI Study. The Participating TO will perform the FILI Study. At any time the Participating TO determines that it will not meet the required time frame for completing the FILI Study, Participating TO shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer as to the schedule status of the FILI Study and provide an estimated completion date with an explanation of the reasons why additional time is required.

 The FILI Study will determine (i) the maximum amount of Interim Firm Interconnection Service, if any, that can be provided prior to completion of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades; (ii) whether the Large Load Interconnection Customer is eligible to receive FILI Service and the maximum amount of such service; (iii) the conditions and limitations of such service; (iv) the upgrades and the associated cost; and (v) the estimated duration required to provide the interim service. The Participating TO will share study results with the ISO for review and concurrence.  The Participating TO shall update its analysis of the availability of FILI Service at least annually under the terms of the FILI Service Agreement, and the actual amount of FILI Service available during any operating period shall be determined by the Participating TO through operational assessments conducted closer to real-time operating conditions, consistent with Section 10A.4 of the TO Tariff.  If requested by the Large Load Interconnection Customer within ten (10) Business Days of the date of the issuance of the FILI Study report, a Report Meeting shall be scheduled among the Participating TO, the ISO, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

The Large Load Interconnection Customer should note the following considerations:

a) Temporary limits may be adjusted if unforeseen conditions arise.
b) Advance notice of non-firm load curtailment may not be possible.

PDF page 312

23

c) Non-firm load may be curtailed during planned or repair outages, which may last longer than 6 months.

d) ISO may have additional operational requirements.
e) Participating TO is not obligated to provide flexibility beyond what it can, in its sole judgment, manage operationally.

f) Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities costs for interim interconnection service will be directly assigned to the Large Load Interconnection Customer.

g) Large Load Interconnection Customers not adhering to the contractual agreement and conditions may be curtailed indefinitely.

4.6.2.1 FILI Service Agreement. If the Large Load Interconnection Customer has elected FILI Service, as reflected in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall ensure that, prior to the Service Commencement Date, the Large Load Customer executes, or requests to be filed unexecuted with FERC, a FILI Service Agreement with the

Participating TO and, as applicable, the ISO, developed by the Participating TO consistent with Section 10A.4 of the TO Tariff and in accordance with ISO Tariff Section 25.7.2. The FILI Service Agreement shall describe the circumstances in which the ISO or Participating TO may temporarily curtail the Large Load receiving FILI Service, and the mechanism the ISO or

Participating TO will use to perform the curtailment. The ISO will post such FILI Service Agreements on the ISO Website.

4.6.3   Additional Studies. The Participating TO may identify the need for additional studies to comply with Applicable Reliability Standards. These additional studies may include, but are not limited to, the following: stability analysis, variable load impact analysis, and EMT analysis.  The associated new scope will be identified in the Large Load Interconnection Study pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.1.1. The additional studies will be performed by the Participating TO as part of the scope of work set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement. The additional studies may result in the identification of additional facilities scope and cost, and may require a revision to the schedule set forth in the Large Load Interconnection Agreement. The Large Load Interconnection Customer will be responsible for providing the necessary technical information and/or models requested by the Participating TO to conduct the additional studies. The Large Load Interconnection Customer will be responsible for the costs of such studies.

If an additional study results in the need to revise the scope of work of the Large Load Network Upgrades identified in the Large Load

Interconnection Study and the updated assigned costs have increased by more than twenty-five percent (25%) compared to costs identified in the Large Load Interconnection Study report, the Large Load Interconnection

PDF page 313

24

Customer shall not be subject to a Withdrawal Penalty under LLIP Section 3.10.1 if the Interconnection Request is withdrawn within thirty (30) Calendar Days of receipt of the additional study report.  If the Large Load Interconnection Customer elects to continue with its Interconnection Request, the Large Load Interconnection Agreement will be amended to reflect the results of the additional study.

4.6.4   Financing of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades.  The responsibility to finance Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities shall be assigned solely to the Large Load Interconnection Customer. The Participating TO will be responsible for financing Large Load Network Upgrades in accordance with the Large Load Interconnection Agreement and, as to any Large Load Network Upgrades that constitute Large Load-Driven Upgrades, the Cost Recovery Agreement required under LLIP Section 5.3.

4.7
Interconnection Financial Security

4.7.1 Types of Interconnection Financial Security.  The Interconnection Financial Security posted by a Large Load Interconnection Customer may be any combination of the following types of Interconnection Financial Security provided in favor of the Participating TO:

(a) an irrevocable and unconditional letter of credit issued by a bank or financial institution that has a credit rating of A or better by Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better by Moody’s;

(b) an irrevocable and unconditional surety bond issued by an insurance company that has a credit rating of A or better by Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better by Moody’s;

(c) an unconditional and irrevocable guaranty issued by a company that has a credit rating of A or better by Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better by Moody’s;

(d) a cash deposit standing to the credit of the Participating TO and in an interest-bearing escrow account maintained at a bank or financial institution that is reasonably acceptable to the Participating TO;

(e) a certificate of deposit in the name of the Participating TO issued by a bank or financial institution that has a credit rating of A or better by Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better by Moody’s; or

(f) a payment bond certificate in the name of the Participating TO issued by a bank or financial institution that has a credit rating of A or better by Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better by Moody’s.

PDF page 314

25

Interconnection Financial Security instruments as listed above shall be in such form as the Participating TO may reasonably require from time to time by notice to Large Load Interconnection Customers, or in such other form as has been evaluated and approved as reasonably acceptable by the Participating TO.

The Participating TO shall require the use of standardized forms of Interconnection Financial Security to the greatest extent possible.  If at any time the guarantor of the Interconnection Financial Security fails to maintain the credit rating required by this LLIP Section 4.7.1, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide to the Participating TO replacement Interconnection Financial Security meeting the requirements of this LLIP Section 4.7.1 within five (5) Business Days of the change in credit rating.

Interest on a cash deposit standing to the credit of the Participating TO in an interest-bearing escrow account under subpart (d) of this LLIP Section 4.7.1 will accrue to the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s benefit.

Execution and filing of the Large Load Interconnection Agreement at FERC are addressed in LLIP Section 5.

Section 5.
Large Load Interconnection Agreement

5.1
Tender.  Within thirty (30) Calendar Days after the Report Meeting for the final Large Load Interconnection Study report or, if the Large Load Interconnection Customer has timely requested a FILI Study pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.2, within thirty (30) Calendar Days after issuance of the final FILI Study report (or, if a Report Meeting is requested for the FILI Study, after such Report Meeting), the Participating TO shall tender to the Large Load Interconnection Customer a draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement developed by the Participating TO consistent with the TO Tariff, this LLIP, and the results of the Interconnection Studies.  The draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement shall address, without limitation, the scope, schedule, cost responsibility, payment provisions, cost recovery, and financial security for the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades, and any applicable operating requirements.  The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide written comments, or notification of no comments, on the draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement within thirty (30) Calendar Days.

5.2
Negotiation.  Notwithstanding LLIP Section 5.1, at the request of Large Load Interconnection Customer, Participating TO shall begin negotiations with Large Load Interconnection Customer concerning the terms and conditions of the Large Load Interconnection Agreement at any time after the Participating TO provides the Large Load Interconnection Customer with the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, or the final FILI Study report, if applicable.

PDF page 315

26

Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall negotiate in good faith concerning any disputed provisions of the draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement for not more than sixty (60) Calendar Days after the Participating TO provides the Large Load Interconnection Customer with the final Large Load Interconnection Study report or the final FILI Study report if applicable.  If Large Load Interconnection Customer determines that negotiations are at an impasse, it may request termination of the negotiations at any time after tender of the draft Large Load Interconnection Agreement pursuant to LLIP Section 5.1 and request submission of the unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement with FERC or initiate Dispute Resolution procedures pursuant to LLIP Section 7.2.  If Large Load Interconnection Customer requests termination of the negotiations, but within sixty (60) Calendar Days after issuance of the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, or the final FILI Study report if applicable, fails to request either the filing of the unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement or initiate Dispute Resolution, it shall be deemed to have withdrawn its Interconnection Request.  Unless otherwise agreed by the Parties, if Large Load Interconnection Customer has not executed the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, requested filing of an unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement, or initiated Dispute Resolution procedures pursuant to LLIP Section 7.2 within the time period set forth in LLIP Section 5.3, it shall be deemed to have withdrawn its Interconnection Request.  Participating TO shall provide to Large Load Interconnection Customer a final Large Load Interconnection Agreement within fifteen (15) Business Days after the completion of the negotiation process.

The Participating TO may declare an impasse upon sixty (60) Calendar Days after issuance of the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, or the final FILI Study report if applicable, or at any time following sixty (60) Calendar Days after issuance of the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, or the final FILI Study report if applicable, if the Parties have agreed to extend negotiation of the Large Load Interconnection Agreement.  If the Participating TO declares an impasse, the Participating TO will file the Large Load Interconnection Agreement unexecuted with FERC within twenty-one (21) Calendar Days.

5.3
Execution and Filing
Within thirty (30) Business Days after receipt of the final Large Load Interconnection Agreement, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall either: (i) execute the final Large Load Interconnection Agreement and return it to Participating TO; or (ii) request in writing that Participating TO file with FERC the Large Load Interconnection Agreement in unexecuted form, or its Interconnection Request shall be deemed withdrawn and subject to LLIP Section 3.10.  To the extent FERC filing is required, as soon as practicable, but not later than ten (10) Business Days after receiving either the executed Large Load Interconnection Agreement or the request to file an unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement, Participating TO shall file the Large Load Interconnection Agreement with FERC, together with, in the case of an unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement, its explanation of any matters as to which Large Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO disagree

PDF page 316

27

and support for the costs that Participating TO proposes to charge to Large Load Interconnection Customer under the Large Load Interconnection Agreement.  An unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement should contain terms and conditions deemed appropriate by Participating TO for the Interconnection Request.  If the Parties agree to proceed with design, procurement, and construction of facilities and upgrades under the agreed-upon terms of the unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement, they may proceed pending FERC action.  As a condition to the Participating TO’s design, procurement, construction, or operation of any Large Load-Driven Upgrades (as defined in the pro forma Large Load Cost Recovery Agreement set forth in Appendix V to the TO Tariff) associated with the Interconnection Request, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall also, within the same period and subject to the same consequences as the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, execute and maintain a Cost Recovery Agreement substantially in the form set forth in Appendix V to the TO Tariff, or, if the Large Load Interconnection Customer requests that the Cost Recovery Agreement be filed unexecuted with FERC, comply with that agreement as made effective by FERC.  A Cost Recovery Agreement required by this LLIP Section 5.3 is entered into pursuant to the TO Tariff.

5.4
Commencement of Interconnection Activities
If Large Load Interconnection Customer executes the final Large Load Interconnection Agreement, Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall perform their respective obligations in accordance with the terms of the Large Load Interconnection Agreement, subject to modification by FERC. Upon submission of an unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement, Large Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO shall promptly comply with the unexecuted Large Load Interconnection Agreement, if agreed to by the Parties pursuant to LLIP Section 5.3, subject to modification by FERC.

Section 6.
Construction of Participating TO's Interconnection Facilities and Large Load Network Upgrades

6.1
Schedule
Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall negotiate in good faith concerning a schedule for the Participating TO’s construction of Participating TO's Interconnection Facilities and the Large Load Network Upgrades.

6.2
Construction Sequencing

6.2.1 General.  In general, the Service Commencement Date will determine the sequence of construction of Large Load Network Upgrades.

6.2.2 Advancing Construction of Large Load Network Upgrades that are Part of an Expansion Plan of the Participating TO.  A Large Load Interconnection Customer with a Large Load Interconnection Agreement,

PDF page 317

28

in order to maintain its Service Commencement Date, may request that Participating TO advance to the extent necessary the completion of Large Load Network Upgrades that:  (i) are necessary to support such Service Commencement Date and (ii) would otherwise not be completed, pursuant to an expansion plan of Participating TO or approved ISO Transmission Plan covering the Participating TO’s facilities, in time to support such Service Commencement Date.  Upon such request, Participating TO will use Reasonable Efforts to advance the construction of such Large Load Network Upgrades to accommodate such request; provided that Large Load Interconnection Customer commits to pay Participating TO any associated expediting costs.

6.3
Special Provisions for Affected Systems
The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall timely enter into and maintain an agreement with each applicable Affected System Operator for each addition, modification, or upgrade to an Affected System identified through the Interconnection Studies or an applicable Affected System study.  The agreement shall specify the terms governing payments to be made by the Large Load Interconnection Customer to the Affected System Operator, including financial security or credit support sufficient to protect the Affected System Operator and its other customers against cost shifting.  Where a Cost Recovery Agreement applies, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide the Participating TO the evidence of such agreements required by, and within the time specified in, the Cost Recovery Agreement, and the Participating TO may decline to commence, or may suspend, construction as provided in the Cost Recovery Agreement.

Section 7.
Miscellaneous

7.1
Confidentiality

For the purposes of this LLIP Section 7.1, “Party” or “Parties” shall mean the Participating TO, Large Load Interconnection Customer, ISO, or any combination of the Participating TO, Large Load Interconnection Customer, or ISO.

Confidential Information shall include, without limitation, all information relating to a Party's technology, research and development, business affairs, and pricing.

Information is Confidential Information only if it is clearly designated or marked in writing as confidential on the face of the document, or, if the information is conveyed orally or by inspection, if the Party providing the information orally informs the Party receiving the information that the information is confidential.

If requested by either Party, the other Party shall provide in writing, the basis for asserting that the information referred to in this LLIP Section 7.1 warrants confidential treatment, and the requesting Party may disclose such writing to the

PDF page 318

29

appropriate Governmental Authority.  Each Party shall be responsible for the costs associated with affording confidential treatment to its information.

7.1.1 Scope.  Confidential Information shall not include information that the receiving Party can demonstrate: (1) is generally available to the public other than as a result of a disclosure by the receiving Party; (2) was in the lawful possession of the receiving Party on a non-confidential basis before receiving it from the disclosing Party; (3) was supplied to the receiving Party without restriction by a third party, who, to the knowledge of the receiving Party after due inquiry, was under no obligation to the disclosing Party to keep such information confidential; (4) was independently developed by the receiving Party without reference to Confidential Information of the disclosing Party; (5) is, or becomes, publicly known, through no wrongful act or omission of the receiving Party or Breach of the Large Load Interconnection Agreement; or (6) is required, in accordance with LLIP Section 7.1.6, Order of Disclosure, to be disclosed by any Governmental Authority or is otherwise required to be disclosed by law or subpoena, or is necessary in any legal proceeding establishing rights and obligations under the Large Load Interconnection Agreement. Information designated as Confidential Information will no longer be deemed confidential if the Party that designated the information as confidential notifies the other Party that it no longer is confidential.

7.1.2  Release of Confidential Information.  Neither Party shall release or disclose Confidential Information to any other person, except to its Affiliates (limited by the Standards of Conduct requirements), employees, consultants, Affected System Operators, the Large Load Customer, or to parties who may be providing or considering providing financing to or equity participation with Large Load Interconnection Customer or the Large Load Customer, or to potential purchasers or assignees of Large Load Interconnection Customer or the Large Load Customer, on a need- to-know basis in connection with these procedures, unless such person has first been advised of the confidentiality provisions of this LLIP Section 7.1 and has agreed to comply with such provisions.  Notwithstanding the foregoing, a Party providing Confidential Information to any person shall remain primarily responsible for any release of Confidential Information in contravention of this LLIP Section 7.1.

7.1.3 Rights.  Each Party retains all rights, title, and interest in the Confidential Information that each Party discloses to the other Party.  The disclosure by each Party to the other Party of Confidential Information shall not be deemed a waiver by either Party or any other person or entity of the right to protect the Confidential Information from public disclosure.

7.1.4 No Warranties.  By providing Confidential Information, neither Party makes any warranties or representations as to its accuracy or completeness.  In addition, by supplying Confidential Information, neither

PDF page 319

30

Party obligates itself to provide any particular information or Confidential Information to the other Party nor to enter into any further agreements or proceed with any other relationship or joint venture.

7.1.5 Standard of Care.  Each Party shall use at least the same standard of care to protect Confidential Information it receives as it uses to protect its own Confidential Information from unauthorized disclosure, publication or dissemination.  Each Party may use Confidential Information solely to fulfill its obligations to the other Party under these procedures or its regulatory requirements.

7.1.6 Order of Disclosure.  If a court or a Governmental Authority or entity with the right, power, and apparent authority to do so requests or requires either Party, by subpoena, oral deposition, interrogatories, requests for production of documents, administrative order, or otherwise, to disclose Confidential Information, that Party shall provide the other Party with prompt notice of such request(s) or requirement(s) so that the other Party may seek an appropriate protective order or waive compliance with the terms of these confidentiality provisions. Notwithstanding the absence of a protective order or waiver, the Party may disclose such Confidential Information which, in the opinion of its counsel, the Party is legally compelled to disclose.  Each Party will use Reasonable Efforts to obtain reliable assurance that confidential treatment will be accorded any Confidential Information so furnished.

7.1.7 Remedies.  The Parties agree that monetary damages would be inadequate to compensate a Party for the other Party's Breach of its obligations under this LLIP Section 7.1.  Each Party accordingly agrees that the other Party shall be entitled to equitable relief, by way of injunction or otherwise, if the first Party Breaches or threatens to Breach its obligations under this LLIP Section 7.1, which equitable relief shall be granted without bond or proof of damages, and the receiving Party shall not plead in defense that there would be an adequate remedy at law.  Such remedy shall not be deemed an exclusive remedy for the Breach of this LLIP Section 7.1, but shall be in addition to all other remedies available at law or in equity.  The Parties further acknowledge and agree that the covenants contained herein are necessary for the protection of legitimate business interests and are reasonable in scope.  No Party, however, shall be liable for indirect, incidental, or consequential or punitive damages of any nature or kind resulting from or arising in connection with this LLIP Section 7.1.

7.1.8 Disclosure to FERC, its Staff, or a State.  Notwithstanding anything in this LLIP Section 7.1 to the contrary, and pursuant to 18 CFR section 1b.20, if FERC or its staff, during the course of an investigation or otherwise, requests information from one of the Parties that is otherwise required to be maintained in confidence pursuant to the LLIP, the Party shall provide the requested information to FERC or its staff, within the

PDF page 320

31

time provided for in the request for information.  In providing the information to FERC or its staff, the Party must, consistent with 18 CFR section 388.112, request that the information be treated as confidential and non-public by FERC and its staff and that the information be withheld from public disclosure.  Parties are prohibited from notifying the other Party prior to the release of the Confidential Information to FERC or its staff.  The Party shall notify the other Party to the Large Load Interconnection Agreement when it is notified by FERC or its staff that a request to release Confidential Information has been received by FERC, at which time either of the Parties may respond before such information would be made public, pursuant to 18 CFR section 388.112.  Requests from a state regulatory body conducting a confidential investigation shall be treated in a similar manner, consistent with applicable state rules and regulations.

7.1.9 Subject to the exception in LLIP Section 7.1.8, any information that a Party claims is competitively sensitive, commercial or financial information ("Confidential Information") shall not be disclosed by the other Party to any person not employed or retained by the other Party, except to the extent disclosure is (i) required by law; (ii) reasonably deemed by the disclosing Party to be required to be disclosed in connection with a dispute between or among the Parties, or the defense of litigation or dispute; (iii) otherwise permitted by consent of the other Party, such consent not to be unreasonably withheld;  or (iv) necessary to fulfill its obligations under this LLIP or as a Participating TO, including disclosing the Confidential Information to the ISO or to a subregional, regional or national reliability organization or planning group.  The Party asserting confidentiality shall notify the other Party in writing of the information it claims is confidential.  Prior to any disclosures of the other Party's Confidential Information under this subparagraph, or if any third party or Governmental Authority makes any request or demand for any of the information described in this subparagraph, the disclosing Party agrees to promptly notify the other Party in writing and agrees to assert confidentiality and cooperate with the other Party in seeking to protect the Confidential Information from public disclosure by confidentiality agreement, protective order or other reasonable measures.

7.1.10 This provision shall not apply to any information that was or is hereafter in the public domain (except as a result of a Breach of this provision).

7.1.11 Participating TO shall, at Large Load Interconnection Customer's election, destroy, in a confidential manner, or return the Confidential Information provided at the time the Confidential Information is no longer needed.

7.2
Disputes

Such dispute or claim shall be resolved in accordance with Section 14 of the TO Tariff, including, as applicable, the ISO ADR Procedures; provided, however, that disputes arising under a Cost Recovery Agreement shall be resolved under the dispute resolution provisions of that agreement.

III. FERC SHOW CAUSE ORDERS
8
Q.
PLEASE DESCRIBE FERC’S SHOW CAUSE ORDERS.
9
A. On June 18, 2026, the FERC instituted show cause proceedings under section 206 of the 10

Federal Power Act through six show cause orders addressing the integration of large and 11

co-located loads onto the interstate transmission system. Each order is directed to an 12

Independent System Operator or Regional Transmission Organization ("ISO/RTO") and, 13

where applicable, its transmission-owning members.3 The orders arise from the 14

Commission's preliminary finding that the existing tariffs on file with the Commission 15

appear to be unjust, unreasonable, or unduly discriminatory or preferential because they 16

lack sufficiently clear and consistent provisions to address the unique challenges posed 17

by the rapid growth of large loads (such as data centers) and co-location arrangements 18

between loads and generating facilities.4
19

3 Cal. Indep. Sys. Op. Corp., 195 FERC ¶ 61,214 (2026) (relevant CAISO Show Cause Order) (“Show Cause Order”).
4 Show Cause Order at P 38.

PDF page 333

Page 4

Each respondent is directed, within 60 days of the date of the order, to either (1) show 1

cause why its tariff remains just and reasonable and not unduly discriminatory or 2

preferential without the provisions identified by the Commission, or (2) explain what 3

tariff changes would remedy the identified concerns if the Commission were to determine 4

that the tariff is unjust and unreasonable and proceeds to establish a replacement tariff.5 5

As relevant here, the show cause order directed to CAISO and its Participating 6

Transmission Owners (the “Show Cause Order”) required respondents to show cause or 7

propose remedial tariff revisions to (1) ensure transparency concerning the network 8

upgrade costs to provide transmission service to Eligible Customers on behalf of large 9

loads, (2) establish a pro forma cost recovery agreement to mitigate the risk of cost 10

shifting among transmission customers, and (3) ensure such payments are appropriately 11

credited toward transmission owners' transmission revenue requirements consistent with 12

the Commission's cost-of-service regulations.6
13
Q.
DID FERC PROVIDE ANY ADDITIONAL GUIDANCE ON HOW THE COST
14
RECOVERY ASPECTS SHOULD BE ADDRESSED BY RESPONDENTS?
15
A. Yes, Commissioners Chang and See each issued separate concurrences providing 16

additional guidance on how cost recovery for large-load-driven transmission costs should 17

be approached. Commissioner Chang supported the Commission's two-part preliminary 18

solution — public posting of information on new large loads, associated network 19

upgrades identified in local transmission planning, and the costs of those upgrades, 20

together with a pro forma cost recovery agreement to help ensure that Eligible Customers 21

serving large loads bear the risk and are ultimately responsible for the costs incurred to 22

5 Id. at Ordering Para. B.
6 Id. at P 3.

PDF page 334

Page 5

provide transmission service — as a necessary step to protect wholesale customers 1

against unjustified cost shifts.7 Commissioner Chang also cautioned that bilateral 2

agreements that simply provide transmission revenue contributions "untethered from any 3

assessment of the actual cost of providing transmission service induced by individual 4

large loads" may be insufficient to protect other customers against unjustified cost shifts.8 5

She urged that the resulting regulatory and rate design not be limited to a pre-defined 6

solution set, encouraging parties to submit comprehensive records outlining various and 7

innovative approaches to customer protection, including but not limited to the Ratepayer 8

Protection Pledge.9
9
Commissioner See likewise described the Commission's findings on cost recovery 10

agreements between transmission owners and Eligible Customers as "an important step in 11

mitigating the risks of stranded assets if large loads prove speculative," and she 12

highlighted the Commission's decision to reduce unnecessary redundancy by allowing an 13

Eligible Customer to satisfy its financial security requirement by relying on credit support 14

or other financial security that a large load customer may have posted under a retail 15

agreement.10 She signaled that, as the Commission gains more experience, further 16

streamlining may be appropriate — suggesting, for example, that a cost recovery 17

agreement between a transmission owner and a large load customer itself could 18

potentially satisfy an Eligible Customer's financial security requirement — and stated that 19

she would be looking to ensure cost recovery agreements contain "fair and transparent 20

7 Show Cause Order, Comm’n. Chang Concurrence at PP 6-9.
8 Id. at P 8.
9  Id. at PP 8-9.
10 Show Cause Order, Comm’n. See Concurrence at P 9.

PDF page 335

Page 6

measures to ensure the right costs get on the right bills."11 More broadly, Commissioner 1

See reaffirmed the Commission's policy of rolling most network upgrade costs — 2

including those driven by large loads — into the embedded-cost rate paid by transmission 3

customers, but conditioned that approach on a commitment to evaluating alternative 4

transmission technologies and other tools (including alternative cost allocation structures, 5

greater clarity in transmission service agreements, accounting for state large-load-specific 6

tariffs, and direct assignment in appropriate cases) that can keep costs at a responsible 7

and accountable level and ensure retail consumers do not bear costs incommensurate with 8

the benefits they receive.12
9

10

18
The meetings addressed applications and studies, operational requirements, cost-shift 19

prevention, flexible interconnection service options, and co-located resources. The 20

meetings provided an opportunity to examine the respective roles and responsibilities of 21
