22

11 Id.
12 Id. at P 9.

PDF page 336

Page 7

LSPGC also participated in CAISO’s public stakeholder meetings on August 19, October 1

1, and October 19, 2026, and submitted written comments on CAISO’s Straw Proposal, 2

Draft Final Proposal, and Draft Tariff Language. Those comments asked CAISO to 3

recognize non-load-serving and affected PTOs expressly, preserve a meaningful role for 4

those PTOs in the interconnection and study process, and address coordination and cost 5

recovery.
6
Q.
HOW DID THIS COORDINATION ADDRESS THE CIRCUMSTANCES OF
7
NON-LOAD-SERVING PTOS SUCH AS LSPGC?
8
A. LSPGC coordination highlighted the need to ensure that an affected PTO has a 9

meaningful role in the process even when that PTO does not provide retail service to the 10

requesting load. LSPGC is also a non-load-serving PTO. The CRA therefore establishes 11

obligations with the Eligible Customer taking wholesale transmission service on behalf of 12

the Large Load Customer, while leaving retail recovery to applicable state law and the 13

Eligible Customer’s retail arrangements.
14
From July through September 2026, the coordination included additional meetings with 15

non-load-serving PTOs and the broader California PTO group. The discussions addressed 16

participation in the application and study process, access to information, technical 17

coordination, timing, and allocation and recovery of costs. The coordination supported 18

the development of positions reflecting the circumstances of non-load-serving PTOs for 19

consideration by the broader PTO group.
20
The coordination also included meetings with Pacific Gas and Electric Company 21

(“PG&E”) in August 2026 to discuss notice, information sharing, technical consultation, 22

and coordination when a large-load request affects a non-load-serving PTO’s facilities. 23

PDF page 337

Page 8

These efforts emphasized the distinct roles of load-serving, non-load-serving, and 1

affected PTOs throughout the process.
2
From October through November 2026, the coordination included joint cost-recovery 3

meetings with load-serving PTOs to examine potential approaches and implementation 4

considerations, including the circumstances of non-load-serving and affected PTOs. 5

The coordination provided an opportunity to evaluate different approaches and identify 6

implementation considerations relevant to non-load-serving PTOs. LSPGC’s CRA 7

addresses the cost-recovery and financial-security requirements associated with large 8

loads affecting LSPGC’s facilities.
9
Q.
BASED ON THIS PROCESS, WHAT IS LSPGC’S PROPOSED APPROACH?
10
A. LSPGC proposes a pro forma CRA that establishes the Eligible Customer’s minimum 11

revenue contribution and related security obligations for Covered Upgrades. The CRA is 12

the form of agreement contemplated by Section 10.9 and 10A.6 of LSPGC’s 13

Transmission Owner Tariff (“TO Tariff”). The applicable interconnection agreement 14

governs physical interconnection and associated responsibilities. As Mr. Arzola explains, 15

LSPGC also proposes revisions to the definition of the TRBAA and Section 5.3 of the 16

TO Tariff to pass the CRA-related credits through to transmission customers. 17

V. COST RECOVERY AGREEMENT
18
Q.
PLEASE DESCRIBE THE CRA.
19
A. The CRA is a pro forma contract between LSPGC, as Transmission Owner, and the 20

Eligible Customer taking wholesale transmission service under the CAISO Tariff on 21

behalf of a Large Load Customer. The Large Load Customer executes a Limited 22

Acknowledgment for the information, authorization, notice, and cooperation purposes 23

stated therein, including cooperation in a transition to a Successor Eligible Customer. The 24

PDF page 338

Page 9

Large Load Customer is not itself a party to the wholesale payment obligations. The CRA 1

establishes the Eligible Customer’s wholesale revenue-support and security obligations 2

associated with the Large Load Facility and the Covered Upgrades, without establishing 3

or prescribing the retail rates, charges, or recovery arrangements between the Eligible 4

Customer and the Large Load Customer. Covered Upgrades include the Large Load- 5

Driven Upgrades and any LSPGC-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project share 6

identified in the applicable Schedules for which an Annual MTRC is established. 7

Affected System Upgrades and regional project shares owned by another entity are 8

addressed through separate arrangements under Sections 4.2 and 6.3.6, rather than 9

included in LSPGC’s MTRC absent the authorization and conditions specified in the 10

CRA.
11
Q.
HOW DOES THE CRA ALIGN WITH THE COST RECOVERY PRINCIPLES
12
OUTLINED IN THE SHOW CAUSE ORDER?
13
A. At a high level, the CRA responds directly to the three cost-recovery concerns identified 14

in the Show Cause Order by: (i) establishing a pro forma contract between LSPGC and 15

the Eligible Customer that formalizes cost responsibility for the Covered Upgrades; (ii) 16

establishing payment and security obligations designed to mitigate cost shifting to other 17

transmission customers; and (iii) providing credits to transmission customers through the 18

TRBAA to prevent duplicate recovery. The CRA does not change CAISO cost allocation. 19

TRBAA Credits are passed through the TRBAA, not as Formula Rate revenue credits. 20

LSPGC’s existing Attachment 1a identifies project plant, and its existing Attachment 9, 21

Line 18 removes revenue credits included in the TRBAA from Formula Rate revenue 22

credits. These existing provisions prevent the same amounts from being credited twice 23

and require no change to the Formula Rate Template.
24

PDF page 339

Page 10

Q.
PLEASE DESCRIBE HOW THE CRA CALCULATES THE ELIGIBLE
1
CUSTOMER'S PAYMENT OBLIGATION.
2
A. The core payment mechanism is the Supplemental Minimum Transmission Revenue 3

Contribution (the "Supplemental MTRC") in Section 6.4 of the CRA. Under Section 6.3 4

and Schedule 5, LSPGC calculates a separate Annual MTRC for each Covered Upgrade 5

over a twenty-year MTRC Term beginning on its applicable in-service date and ending 6

on the day immediately preceding the twentieth anniversary of that date, with partial Rate 7

Years prorated. The Estimated Annual MTRC uses then-current estimated capital cost. 8

Within one year after the Covered Upgrade enters service, when actual capital cost is 9

known and all costs associated with its estimated capital cost have been accounted for, 10

LSPGC establishes the Final Annual MTRC as the final incurred capital cost multiplied 11

by the Project-Specific Levelized Fixed Charge Rate calculated under Schedule 5. 12

Schedule 5 applies a fixed five-percent annual levelized-depreciation schedule over the 13

twenty-year MTRC Term to calculate present-worth federal and state income-tax 14

depreciation benefits; that schedule is not a separate annual MTRC charge. The allocation 15

methodology assigns each Large Load Customer a share of the upgrade costs, taking into 16

account other large loads and any portion serving other transmission system needs or 17

customers. That allocation percentage is applied only once in calculating the customer’s 18

Annual MTRC.
19
LSPGC separately calculates Qualifying Transmission Revenues ("QTR") under Section 20

6.2 and Schedule 2. QTR consists of qualifying wholesale transmission revenues 21

attributable to service to the Large Load Facility that reduce transmission revenues 22

otherwise collected from CAISO customers or directly credit LSPGC’s Net Revenue 23

Requirement. Qualifying Transmission Revenues include the portion of TAC revenues 24

PDF page 340

Page 11

attributable to the Large Load Facility and allocable to LSPGC, together with qualifying 1

wheeling and other wholesale transmission revenues that reduce the amounts otherwise 2

collected from transmission customers. Each revenue amount is counted only once. The 3

Eligible Customer pays the greater of zero and the applicable Annual MTRC less QTR. 4

Under Section 6.5, LSPGC invoices forecast Supplemental MTRC, including any prior- 5

period true-up adjustment, in monthly installments payable within thirty Calendar Days 6

after receipt. Upon initial finalization, the Final Annual MTRC applies back to the in- 7

service date, with estimated charges reconciled in the next Reconciliation Statement, 8

provided as Ex. LSC-102 to my testimony. QTR attributable to a specific Covered 9

Upgrade is applied to that upgrade; other QTR is allocated among Covered Upgrades 10

then in their MTRC Terms pro rata based on their respective prorated Annual MTRCs for 11

the applicable period. If an upgrade enters service during a Rate Year without a projected 12

Supplemental MTRC in the Annual Projection, LSPGC promptly forecasts and invoices 13

the prorated amount for the remainder of that year under Section 6.5. The Invoice 14

Statement is provided as Ex. LSC-103 to my testimony.
15
Q.
WHY WAS THE SUPPLEMENTAL MTRC DESIGNED IN THIS WAY?
16
A. The Annual MTRC operates as a revenue floor, not an additive charge. Under Section 17

6.6, LSPGC serves a Reconciliation Statement, provided as Ex. LSC-102 to my 18

testimony, concurrently with each Annual Update using actual QTR and the applicable 19

Annual MTRC. The reconciliation identifies billings, receipts, Applied Security 20

Proceeds, the updated Cumulative Revenue Ledger, Required Operational Security, and 21

the reconciliation of forecast TRBAA credits to amounts recorded in the Transmission 22

Revenue Balancing Account ("TRBA"). It does not annually reopen final capital cost or 23

Formula Rate inputs. The Final Annual MTRC may change only as permitted by Section 24

PDF page 341

Page 12

6.3.1, including correction of error or fraud, effective prospective reassignment, a final 1

FERC order applicable to the calculation, or prospective changes resulting from a final 2

FERC order changing LSPGC’s Formula Rate return on equity or from subsequently 3

enacted tax rates or tax law. Dispute determinations are implemented under Section 11.5. 4

When a permitted recalculation is made under Section 6.3.1, LSPGC updates and serves 5

the MTRC Calculation Template on the Eligible Customer; the replacement template 6

becomes effective on service, subject to the Eligible Customer’s Article 11 dispute rights. 7

This design responds to Commissioner Chang’s concern about contributions untethered 8

from the costs induced by individual large loads. The CRA ties the minimum contribution 9

to the allocated capital cost and Schedule 5 inputs for identified Covered Upgrades while 10

establishing a defined twenty-year commitment. Annual reconciliation tests the revenue 11

shortfall against that commitment rather than recalculating an annual Formula Rate-based 12

upgrade revenue requirement.
13
Q.
HOW DOES THE CRA ADDRESS STRANDED-ASSET RISK?
14
A. The CRA addresses stranded-asset risk through a twenty-year revenue commitment, 15

construction and operational security, and termination provisions that preserve the 16

Eligible Customer’s responsibility if the Large Load fails to materialize or departs the 17

system.
18
First, Section 6.7 establishes the Total Required Revenue Commitment for each Covered 19

Upgrade as the Final Annual MTRC multiplied by twenty, subject to the adjustments 20

specified in that section. Before final determination, the projected commitment uses the 21

Estimated Annual MTRC. The Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility equals 22

that commitment less cumulative QTR, Supplemental MTRC Receipts, Applied Security 23

Proceeds, effective prospective reassignment credits, and cash amounts received and 24

PDF page 342

Page 13

applied toward the Termination Fee, excluding any portion already counted as Applied 1

Security Proceeds. Each dollar is counted only once. LSPGC updates an upgrade-specific 2

Cumulative Revenue Ledger at least annually and includes it with each Reconciliation 3

Statement. When a final Reconciliation Statement determines that the remaining 4

responsibility is zero (“Break-Even”), operational payment and security obligations 5

terminate. Once finally satisfied, the responsibility does not revive because of later 6

changes in Formula Rate inputs, subject to final true-ups and correction of errors or fraud 7

for periods before satisfaction.
8
Second, Sections 3.3.4 and 3.5 provide that cancellation, downsizing, curtailment, or 9

cessation after an applicable in-service date does not itself eliminate payment or security 10

obligations. Upon effective termination of the CRA or permanent cessation of service 11

after that date, the Eligible Customer owes a lump-sum Termination Fee equal to the 12

Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility for the in-service Covered Upgrades, less 13

any Supplemental MTRC billed and unpaid as of the effective termination date, due thirty 14

Calendar Days after LSPGC’s invoice. The excluded unpaid Supplemental MTRC 15

remains separately payable and secured; its later payment, satisfaction through Applied 16

Security Proceeds, or direct recovery reduces the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility. 17

Payment in full discharges only the remaining contractual responsibility included in the 18

fee, and no further Supplemental MTRC accrues for periods after the effective 19

termination or cessation date, subject to final reconciliation for earlier periods. LSPGC 20

holds each Termination Fee, together with any Operational Security proceeds applied to 21

it, in a separate interest-bearing Termination Fee Account. For each Covered Upgrade, 22

the Annual Termination Fee Credit for each remaining Rate Year equals the applicable 23

PDF page 343

Page 14

Annual MTRC, prorated for a partial Rate Year, but may not exceed the available 1

Termination Fee Balance. In the final Rate Year of the MTRC Term, the credit includes 2

the entire remaining balance, including earnings, so the account is reduced to zero, 3

subject to release and adjustment if a prospective reassignment occurs. The uncredited 4

balance is separately accounted for as a deferred liability, is not included in rate base, and 5

is not itself a TRBAA Credit. These credits do not reduce rate base.
6
Third, Article 7 requires Construction Security for incurred and irrevocably committed 7

costs and Pre-In-Service Termination Costs, including applicable cancellation, 8

demobilization, and restoration costs and properly accrued allowance for funds used 9

during construction ("AFUDC"). Construction Security continues separately for upgrades 10

not yet in service. Operational Security must be delivered no later than thirty Calendar 11

Days before the earliest expected applicable in-service date and be fully effective before 12

service. Under Section 7.3, it equals one hundred percent of the nominal Remaining 13

Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, reduced only by currently certified Qualifying 14

Retail Security Support.  Security is recalculated at the events specified in Section 7.4, 15

including annual projections and reconciliations, final MTRC determination, permitted 16

recalculations, reassignment, and credit review events. Security does not cap the Eligible 17

Customer’s underlying liability. For a LSPGC-owned CAISO-Assigned Regional Project, 18

the CRA does not independently impose pre-service Construction Security solely because 19

CAISO has assigned a project share; any such requirement must arise under the 20

applicable tariff, interconnection agreement, or other Commission-accepted arrangement. 21

Together, these provisions protect against stranded-asset exposure through an identified 22

revenue commitment and enforceable payment and security obligations. Sections 4.2 and 23

PDF page 344

Page 15

6.3.6 also require separate affected-system and third-party regional-project arrangements 1

and evidence of compliance. These provisions support the Show Cause Order’s objective 2

of mitigating the risk of stranded assets if large loads prove speculative. 3

Q.
HOW DOES THE CRA AVOID REDUNDANT FINANCIAL SECURITY
4
REQUIREMENTS?
5
A. Section 7.1 recognizes Construction Security provided under an interconnection or 6

engineering and procurement agreement for the same costs, avoiding duplication. Under 7

Article 7 and Schedule 3, a currently effective Retail Security Certification reduces direct 8

Operational Security dollar-for-dollar by certified Qualifying Retail Security Support. 9

This does not reduce the underlying wholesale obligation and does not apply to 10

Construction Security or Pre-In-Service Termination Costs. The certification remains 11

effective until superseded, withdrawn, or deemed ineffective; no annual recertification is 12

required unless an update-triggering event occurs. Direct Security may be cash, a 13

qualifying Letter of Credit, or another form accepted by LSPGC in its sole discretion. 14

There is no automatic unsecured allowance for an investment-grade utility. 15

Section 6.10 allows transfer to a Successor Eligible Customer through an assumption 16

agreement and required Security before the prior Eligible Customer is released. Section 17

6.9 permits prospective reassignment to a subsequent large load at another facility only 18

when an objective study demonstrates reliance on identifiable incremental upgrade 19

capability that avoids or reduces otherwise-needed upgrades. The reassignment is 20

bounded by the avoided upgrade cost, capability-supported amount, and remaining 21

allocated net plant. A reduction becomes effective only after the service, agreement, 22

security, and approval conditions are satisfied, and does not reopen prior years. A sale or 23

PDF page 345

Page 16

transfer of the same Large Load Facility is governed by Section 6.10 rather than the 1

reassignment study process.
2
Q.
HOW DOES THE CRA HELP ENSURE THAT PAYMENTS ARE
3
APPROPRIATELY CREDITED TO TRANSMISSION CUSTOMERS?
4
A. Section 6.8 establishes TRBAA Credits rather than Formula Rate revenue credits. In each 5

annual TRBAA, LSPGC includes as a Forecast TRBAA Credit the projected 6

Supplemental MTRC for the following Rate Year, increased or reduced by any prior- 7

period true-up adjustment, plus any Annual Termination Fee Credit scheduled for that 8

year under Section 3.3.4.  Actual Supplemental MTRC Receipts and Applied Security 9

Proceeds are recorded in the TRBA when received or applied, except that proceeds 10

applied to a Termination Fee are credited through the Termination Fee’s annual crediting 11

schedule. Differences between the forecast credit and the applicable actual amounts flow 12

through a subsequent TRBAA. The TRBA also reflects any adjustments necessary to 13

prevent duplicate recovery of amounts collected under the CRA. Each dollar is counted 14

once; QTR is not credited again, and posting, drawing, or holding Security does not itself 15

produce a credit. LSPGC maintains supporting workpapers and includes sufficient 16

workpapers with each TRBAA filing to permit verification, subject to appropriate 17

confidentiality protections.
18
This mechanism provides transmission customers the benefit of the CRA’s payments 19

without changing the Formula Rate calculation or recovering the same amount twice. 20

Interest on the TRBA balance is governed by Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff. By contrast, 21

Supplemental MTRC invoices, refunds, and prior-period adjustments between LSPGC 22

and the Eligible Customer do not accrue interest under Section 6.6.
23

PDF page 346

Page 17

VI. TARIFF IMPLEMENTATION AND REVIEW
1
Q.
PLEASE DESCRIBE THE ASSOCIATED TARIFF REVISIONS.
2
A. The pro forma CRA establishes the contractual revenue-support and security obligations 3

contemplated by Section 10.9 and 10A.6 of the TO Tariff. LSPGC also proposes to revise 4

the definition of the TRBAA and Section 5.3 so that amounts required to be credited 5

under a CRA flow through to transmission customers. The revisions provide for the 6

forecast credits and the amounts recorded in the Transmission Revenue Balancing 7

Account (“TRBA”), with interest on the TRBA balance governed by Section 5.3. As Mr. 8

Arzola explains, no changes are required to the Formula Rate Template, the Protocols, or 9

Appendix I of the TO Tariff.
10
Q.
HOW DOES THE CRA RELATE TO INTERCONNECTION AND AFFECTED
11
SYSTEM OBLIGATIONS?
12
A. The CRA contemplates an Eligible Customer for Large Load (“ECLL”) or Transmission 13

System Wholesale Load (“TSWL”) Interconnection Agreement. Under Section 12.3, the 14

applicable interconnection agreement governs physical interconnection, design, 15

engineering, procurement, construction, testing, energization, and operating 16

requirements, except to the extent the CRA expressly establishes or supplements 17

construction-cost, pre-service termination-cost, revenue-support, or security obligations. 18

Section 4.2 requires the Eligible Customer to enter into and maintain arrangements with 19

each applicable Affected System Owner, including the required payment and security 20

obligations, and to provide evidence of compliance before LSPGC undertakes the 21

construction or material commitments specified in that section. Costs and security under 22

those arrangements are administered separately, as provided in the CRA. 23

PDF page 347

Page 18

Q.
WHY ARE NO CHANGES TO THE FORMULA RATE TEMPLATE OR
1
PROTOCOLS NEEDED?
2
A. LSPGC’s existing Attachment 1a – Project Plant Detail Worksheet reports plant 3

separately by project, and Attachment 1 – Project Revenue Requirement Worksheet 4

calculates each project’s revenue requirement using the applicable allocation factors. 5

Covered Upgrades can be reported as separate projects on those worksheets. Attachment 6

9 – Revenue Credit Detail, Line 18, “Less: Revenue Credits included in the TRBAA,” 7

removes any TRBAA Credits otherwise recorded in the revenue accounts included on 8

Attachment 9. The full eligible cost of each Covered Upgrade therefore remains in the 9

Base Transmission Revenue Requirement, while CRA credits flow through the TRBAA. 10

The existing Protocols govern the supporting documentation, information exchange, 11

review, challenge, and true-up of Formula Rate inputs, including Covered Upgrade plant 12

and the amounts removed on Attachment 9, Line 18. CRA-related TRBAA Credits are 13

also supported and reviewed in LSPGC’s annual TRBAA filing under Section 205 of the 14

Federal Power Act. Mr. Arzola describes those processes in Exhibit No. LSC-200. 15

Q.
HOW WILL THE PRO FORMA CRA AND INDIVIDUAL AGREEMENTS BE
16
IMPLEMENTED?
17
A. Article 5 identifies the CRA as the form of agreement required under Section 10.9 and 18

10A.6 of the TO Tariff as a condition of LSPGC’s design, procurement, construction, and 19

operation of Large Load-Driven Upgrades. Once the pro forma is accepted, a 20

conforming, executed CRA with populated Schedules generally will not require an 21

individual Section 205 filing. LSPGC must file unexecuted agreements, agreements 22

containing material deviations, and any other agreement required by Commission rule or 23

order. Schedule 5 contains the upgrade-specific MTRC calculation and Cumulative 24

PDF page 348

Page 19

Revenue Ledger; it does not itself impose a separate FERC filing requirement. These 1

provisions do not eliminate LSPGC’s annual Section 205 filing of its TRBAA. 2

Q.
HOW ARE COVERED UPGRADES TREATED FOR PURPOSES OF RETURN
3
ON EQUITY, CAPITAL STRUCTURE, AND COST CONTAINMENT?
4
A. As Mr. Arzola explains, Covered Upgrades are subject to LSPGC’s Commission- 5

accepted Formula Rate and the return on equity (“ROE”) and capital structure reflected in 6

the effective Formula Rate. The commitments to limit ROE to 9.8%, inclusive of 7

incentive ROE adders, and the equity component of capital structure to 45% were made 8

in connection with the competitive solicitations for the Gates and Round Mountain 9

projects and are reflected in the Approved Project Sponsor Agreements for those projects. 10

LSPGC is not proposing to extend those project-specific commitments to Covered 11

Upgrades. Covered Upgrade costs will be separately identified on Attachment 1a and 12

excluded from the cost containment worksheets in Attachments 11 and 12; they will not 13

be subject to the capital cost caps or annual revenue requirement caps applicable to 14

LSPGC’s competitively selected projects.
15
LSPGC is not requesting a different ROE, a different capital structure, or a new incentive 16

ROE adder in this proceeding. LSPGC retains the right, subject to applicable 17

Commission requirements, to seek different treatment for Covered Upgrades in a future 18

Section 205 filing. Unless and until the Commission authorizes different treatment, the 19

effective Formula Rate governs. Any recalculation of a Final Annual MTRC remains 20

subject to the express limitations in CRA Section 6.3.1.
21
Q.
HOW DOES THE CRA INTERACT WITH THE FORMULA RATE AND
22
PROTOCOLS?
23
A. Under Sections 6.1 and 12.3, the Formula Rate and Protocols continue to govern the 24

calculation, information exchange, and true-up of LSPGC’s Net Revenue Requirement 25

PDF page 349

Page 20

and the rate-base treatment of Covered Upgrades. LSPGC posts its Annual Projection by 1

October 5 for the following Rate Year and its Annual Update by July 1 after each Rate 2

Year. The CRA follows that calendar for forecasting and reconciling Supplemental 3

MTRC. It preserves recovery of eligible upgrade costs through the Formula Rate and 4

does not require the Eligible Customer to reimburse LSPGC directly for their capital 5

costs. Supplemental MTRC payments support the minimum revenue commitment and are 6

credited to transmission customers through the TRBAA.
7
Schedule 5, rather than a new Formula Rate calculation, controls the Estimated Annual 8

MTRC and Final Annual MTRC. The CRA controls Supplemental MTRC, the
9
Cumulative Revenue Responsibility, Operational Security, and prospective reassignment. 10

With each Annual Projection, Section 6.5 requires upgrade-specific workpapers showing 11

the Annual MTRC, forecast QTR, projected Supplemental MTRC, prior-period true-up 12

adjustment, Forecast TRBAA Credit, Total Required Revenue Commitment, and 13

Required Operational Security. With each Annual Update, Sections 6.6 and 6.7 require 14

service of the Reconciliation Statement, updated Cumulative Revenue Ledger, 15

recalculated security, TRBA reconciliation, and supporting workpapers. 16

Section 6.8 governs credits through the TRBAA, and Article 11 governs disputes arising 17

under the CRA. The Annual MTRC and related contractual obligations are administered 18

under the CRA separately from the Formula Rate calculation.
19
Q.
HOW DOES THE CRA ADDRESS ANNUAL TRUE-UPS AND FINALITY?
20
A. Section 6.6 carries an under-billed or over-billed Supplemental MTRC amount into 21

billing following the next Annual Projection. If no further Supplemental MTRC will be 22

invoiced, LSPGC separately invoices an under-billed amount, payable within thirty 23

Calendar Days after receipt, or refunds an over-billed amount within thirty Calendar 24

PDF page 350

Page 21

Days after service of the Reconciliation Statement. These adjustments between the 1

Parties bear no interest. The Reconciliation Statement becomes final upon written 2

acceptance or ninety Calendar Days after service, whichever occurs first, unless a timely 3

Notice of Dispute identifies disputed items. Undisputed portions become final on the 4

otherwise applicable date; disputed items become final upon the applicable Dispute 5

Determination.
6
Under Section 11.5, LSPGC implements a final Dispute Determination by updating the 7

affected calculations, Schedules, ledger, reconciliation, and Security, as applicable, and 8

invoicing or refunding amounts between the Parties within thirty Calendar Days, without 9

interest. Any resulting adjustment affecting amounts credited to or recovered from 10

transmission customers is recorded in the TRBA and flows through the TRBAA, with 11

interest on amounts returned to transmission customers as provided in Section 5.3 of the 12

TO Tariff.
13
Q.
HOW DOES THE CRA SUPPORT COST AND BILLING TRANSPARENCY?
14
A. The Annual Projection workpapers and annual Reconciliation Statement make the 15

upgrade-specific minimum contribution, forecast and actual QTR, Supplemental MTRC, 16

cumulative commitment, security, and TRBAA credit calculations available to the 17

Eligible Customer. Schedule 5 records the MTRC calculation and Cumulative Revenue 18

Ledger. Section 6.8 also requires workpapers with each TRBAA filing sufficient for 19

transmission customers to verify the credits, with aggregation or privileged treatment 20

available to protect customer-specific information. LSPGC’s annual TRBAA filing is 21

subject to Commission review and permits interested parties to intervene, comment, and 22

protest, as Mr. Arzola explains.
23

PDF page 351

Page 22

Section 6.12 provides the Eligible Customer access, upon reasonable request, to the 1

information needed to verify the identification of upgrades and the CRA’s calculations. 2

Article 11 provides informal resolution, alternative dispute resolution, and access to 3

FERC. The Large Load Customer and any Successor Eligible Customer may participate 4

to the extent a dispute affects its obligations under the CRA or Limited Acknowledgment. 5

Disputes over CAISO’s regional project designation or assignment are resolved under the 6

applicable CAISO Tariff procedures.
7
The CRA’s confidentiality provisions protect customer-specific information while 8

preserving verification and dispute rights. These contractual records and the TRBAA 9

filing workpapers provide a basis to trace payments and credits and test whether the same 10

obligation has been counted more than once.
11
Q.
DOES THIS CONCLUDE YOUR TESTIMONY?
12
A. Yes, it does.
13

18
A. I have a Bachelor of Arts degree in Economics from Rice University in Houston, Texas, 19

and a Graduate Certificate in Energy Policy and Management from Portland State 20

University in Portland, Oregon. I have over 22 years of experience in the energy industry, 21

PDF page 366

2

4
II. PURPOSE AND SUMMARY OF TESTIMONY
5
Q.
WHAT IS THE PURPOSE OF YOUR TESTIMONY?
6
A. The purpose of my testimony is to explain how LSPGC will recover the cost of 7

transmission facilities built to serve large loads and covered by a Large Load Cost 8

Recovery Agreement (“CRA”) (such facilities, “Covered Upgrades”), and how it will 9

credit the amounts Eligible Customers pay under their CRAs to transmission customers. 10

The proposal responds to the Commission’s June 18, 2026, order in Docket No. EL26- 11

71-000 (“Show Cause Order”). Specifically, my testimony (i) describes the large load 12

cost recovery framework; (ii) explains how the amounts LSPGC receives under its CRAs 13

will be credited to customers through LSPGC’s Transmission Revenue Balancing 14

Account Adjustment (“TRBAA”), and the related revisions to LSPGC’s Transmission 15

Owner Tariff (“TO Tariff”); (iii) explains why no changes are needed to LSPGC’s 16

Formula Rate Template, its Formula Rate Implementation Protocols, or Appendix I of the 17

TO Tariff; (iv) describes the exhibits I sponsor, which are two illustrative examples; and 18

(v) explains why the proposal is just and reasonable. The pro forma CRA, including how 19

each Eligible Customer’s annual Minimum Transmission Revenue Contribution is 20

established, is sponsored by LSPGC witness Mr. Hayes.
21
Q.
PLEASE SUMMARIZE THE PROPOSAL.
22
A. LSPGC proposes to credit the amounts it receives under its CRAs to transmission 23

customers through its TRBAA, the existing mechanism LSPGC uses to pass revenue that 24

PDF page 367

3

it does not receive through the California Independent System Operator Corporation 1

(“CAISO”) Transmission Access Charge (“TAC”) back to customers. To implement this 2

proposal, LSPGC proposes to revise the definition of the TRBAA and Section 5.3 of the 3

TO Tariff so that the TRBAA also flows through to transmission customers the amounts 4

a CRA requires to be credited through it, including TRBAA Credits that LSPGC receives 5

directly from or on behalf of an Eligible Customer. No changes to LSPGC’s Formula 6

Rate Template or Formula Rate Implementation Protocols are needed.
7
Q.
DOES THE PROPOSAL CHANGE HOW LSPGC’S REVENUE REQUIREMENT
8
IS CALCULATED?
9
A. No. LSPGC’s Formula Rate is unchanged. Covered Upgrades are included in LSPGC’s 10

rate base and revenue requirement in the same manner as any other LSPGC transmission 11

plant, and are reported as separate projects on the existing Attachment 1a – Project Plant 12

Detail Worksheet and Attachment 1 – Project Revenue Requirement Worksheet. The 13

existing Attachment 9, Line 18, “Less: Revenue Credits included in the TRBAA,” 14

ensures that TRBAA Credits are credited only once. Entry into a CRA and receipt of 15

amounts under it will not require any modification of the Formula Rate. 16

17
EXHIBITS?

III. LSPGC AND FORMULA RATE BACKGROUND
20
Q.
PLEASE PROVIDE AN OVERVIEW OF LSPGC AND ITS FORMULA RATE.
21
A. LSPGC is a transmission-only Participating Transmission Owner in the CAISO. LSPGC 22

owns the Gates and Round Mountain projects, which the CAISO selected LSPGC to 23

PDF page 368

4

develop through its competitive solicitation process, and is developing additional 1

competitively selected projects. LSPGC’s formula rate consists of its Formula Rate 2

Template (Appendix III of the TO Tariff) and its Formula Rate Implementation Protocols 3

(Appendix IV of the TO Tariff) (together, the “Formula Rate”), which were originally 4

accepted by the Commission in Docket No. ER21-195 and most recently revised in 5

Docket No. ER26-2665. LSPGC’s revenue requirement is recovered through the CAISO 6

TAC.
7
Q.
HOW DOES THE FORMULA RATE OPERATE EACH YEAR?
8
A. By October 5, LSPGC posts an Annual Projection of its net revenue requirement for the 9

upcoming Rate Year. By July 1 after each Rate Year, LSPGC posts an Annual Update 10

using actual FERC Form No. 1 or other Company data. The difference between the 11

projected and actual net revenue requirement, with interest, is the True-Up Adjustment, 12

which is included in a later Annual Projection.
13
Q.
HOW IS LSPGC’S TRANSMISSION REVENUE REQUIREMENT
14
DETERMINED?
15
A. As provided in Appendix I of the TO Tariff, LSPGC’s Transmission Revenue 16

Requirement (“TRR”) is the sum of its Base Transmission Revenue Requirement (“Base 17

TRR”), determined under the Formula Rate, and its TRBAA. LSPGC files to update its 18

TRBAA each October, effective January 1. Under Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff, the 19

TRBAA equals the principal balance in LSPGC’s Transmission Revenue Balancing 20

Account (“TRBA”) as of September 30 (“Cr”), plus a forecast of Transmission Revenue 21

Credits for the following calendar year (“Cf”), plus the interest balance for the TRBA 22

(“I”). The TRBA compares LSPGC’s Base TRR to the amounts it actually receives, so 23

that LSPGC collects no more and no less than its Base TRR. LSPGC already uses the 24

PDF page 369

5

TRBAA to credit customers with revenue it receives outside the TAC, such as Generator 1

Interconnection Process Forfeited Deposit Allocations.
2
Q.
WHY IS LSPGC MAKING THIS FILING?
3
A. In the Show Cause Order, the Commission directed the CAISO and its transmission 4

owners to show cause why their tariffs remain just and reasonable or to propose changes 5

for integrating large loads. Among other things, the Commission called for a mechanism 6

to ensure that large load payments are “credited toward transmission owners’ 7

transmission revenue requirements.” LSPGC’s TO Tariff limits Transmission Revenue 8

Credits to proceeds received from the CAISO, so its TRBAA does not currently provide 9

for amounts LSPGC receives directly from or on behalf of an Eligible Customer. The 10

revisions I describe provide the mechanism the Commission called for. 11

IV. THE LARGE LOAD COST RECOVERY FRAMEWORK
12
Q.
WHAT IS A CRA?
13
A. A CRA is an agreement between LSPGC and an Eligible Customer taking transmission 14

service on behalf of a large load. Under its CRA, the Eligible Customer commits to an 15

annual Minimum Transmission Revenue Contribution (“Annual MTRC”) for each 16

Covered Upgrade built to serve the large load, for a fixed twenty-year term beginning on 17

the applicable in-service date, with partial Rate Years prorated as provided in the CRA. 18

The pro forma CRA is set forth in Appendix V of the TO Tariff. The Annual MTRC is 19

calculated solely under the MTRC Calculation Template in Schedule 5 of the CRA and is 20

not an input to, or a calculation made under, the Formula Rate. The terms of the pro 21

forma CRA, including how the Annual MTRC is established, termination, and credit 22

support requirements, are described in the transmittal letter and the testimony of Mr. 23

Hayes.
24

PDF page 370

6

Q.
HOW ARE THE COSTS OF COVERED UPGRADES RECOVERED?
1
A. Each Covered Upgrade placed under the CAISO’s Operational Control is included in 2

LSPGC’s rate base and Base TRR and recovered through the CAISO TAC together with 3

the cost of LSPGC’s other transmission facilities. The Eligible Customer pays the TAC 4

for the service it takes, like any other transmission customer. The portion of those TAC 5

payments allocable to LSPGC, determined under Schedule 2 of the CRA, counts toward 6

the Eligible Customer’s Annual MTRC as “Qualifying Transmission Revenues.” Under 7

Section 6.2, Qualifying Transmission Revenues also include qualifying wheeling and 8

other wholesale transmission revenues attributable to service to the Large Load Facility, 9

to the extent they reduce transmission revenues otherwise collected from CAISO 10

customers or directly credit LSPGC’s Net Revenue Requirement, without duplicate 11

recognition.
12
Q.
WHAT IS THE SUPPLEMENTAL MTRC?
13
A. The Supplemental MTRC is calculated separately for each Covered Upgrade as the 14

amount, if any, by which the applicable Annual MTRC, prorated for a partial Rate Year, 15

exceeds the Qualifying Transmission Revenues allocated to that upgrade under Section 16

6.4. It ensures that the Eligible Customer pays at least its committed Annual MTRC each 17

year, even if its load develops more slowly than expected. The Supplemental MTRC is 18

never less than zero.
19
Q.
HOW IS THE SUPPLEMENTAL MTRC BILLED AND RECONCILED?
20
A. The Supplemental MTRC follows the same calendar as the Formula Rate. With each 21

Annual Projection, LSPGC forecasts the Eligible Customer’s Qualifying Transmission 22

Revenues and resulting Supplemental MTRC for the Rate Year and invoices that amount, 23

increased or reduced by any prior-period true-up adjustment, to the Eligible Customer in 24

PDF page 371

7

equal monthly installments. In connection with each Annual Update, LSPGC determines 1

the actual Supplemental MTRC using actual Qualifying Transmission Revenues and 2

serves the Eligible Customer a Reconciliation Statement. The difference between the 3

Supplemental MTRC billed for a Rate Year, excluding any prior-period true-up 4

adjustment included in that billing, and the actual Supplemental MTRC for that Rate 5

Year is a prior-period true-up adjustment, which increases or reduces the Supplemental 6

MTRC invoiced following the next Annual Projection. The adjustment is invoiced or 7

refunded separately only if no further Supplemental MTRC will be invoiced. No interest 8

accrues on the Supplemental MTRC or on any prior-period true-up adjustment between 9

LSPGC and the Eligible Customer.
10
Q.
WHAT HAPPENS IF AN ELIGIBLE CUSTOMER DOES NOT PAY OR
11
TERMINATES ITS CRA?
12
A. If an Eligible Customer does not pay, LSPGC may draw on the Eligible Customer’s 13

Operational Security and apply the proceeds to the unpaid amount (“Applied Security 14

Proceeds”). Upon effective termination of the CRA or permanent cessation of service by 15

the Large Load Facility after the applicable in-service date, the Eligible Customer pays a 16

lump-sum Termination Fee equal to the Remaining Cumulative Revenue Responsibility 17

for each in-service Covered Upgrade, less any Supplemental MTRC billed and unpaid as 18

of the effective termination or cessation date. The excluded unpaid amounts remain 19

separately payable and secured; their later payment, satisfaction through Applied Security 20

Proceeds, or direct recovery reduces the Cumulative Revenue Responsibility. Full 21

payment of the Termination Fee discharges only the responsibility included in that fee, as 22

provided in Section 3.3.4. LSPGC will hold the Termination Fee in a separately 23

identified, interest-bearing Termination Fee Account for each Covered Upgrade and 24

PDF page 372

8

credit the balance, including earnings, to transmission customers through the TRBAA in 1

annual amounts equal to the Annual MTRC (“Annual Termination Fee Credits”), for the 2

Rate Years remaining in the applicable MTRC Term, prorated for partial Rate Years, 3

with each year’s credit limited to the available balance and the final year’s credit 4

including any remaining balance, including earnings, so that the account is reduced to 5

zero, subject to the prospective reassignment and release provisions of Section 3.3.4. The 6

uncredited Termination Fee Balance is separately accounted for as a deferred liability and 7

is not itself a TRBAA Credit or Formula Rate input. The Covered Upgrade remains in 8

rate base. The terms governing security and termination are described in the testimony of 9

Mr. Hayes.
10
Q.
WHAT ARE TRBAA CREDITS?
11
A. The CRA uses the term “TRBAA Credits” for the Forecast TRBAA Credit and the 12

amounts LSPGC records in the TRBA and credits to transmission customers through the 13

TRBAA: payments of Supplemental MTRC received from or on behalf of the Eligible 14

Customer (“Supplemental MTRC Receipts”), Applied Security Proceeds, Annual 15

Termination Fee Credits, and any credit required to prevent LSPGC from recovering the 16

same cost twice. The projected Supplemental MTRC, increased or reduced by any prior- 17

period true-up adjustment, together with any Annual Termination Fee Credit scheduled 18

for the following year, included in the TRBAA is the “Forecast TRBAA Credit.” 19

Consistent with the CRA, TRBAA Credits are credited solely through the TRBAA and 20

are not inputs to the Formula Rate. Qualifying Transmission Revenues are not TRBAA 21

Credits because they are already reflected in calculating the Supplemental MTRC. 22

PDF page 373

9

Q.
HOW ARE COVERED UPGRADES TREATED FOR PURPOSES OF RETURN
1
ON EQUITY, CAPITAL STRUCTURE, AND COST CONTAINMENT?
2
A. Covered Upgrades will be subject to LSPGC’s Commission-accepted Formula Rate and 3

the return on equity (“ROE”) and capital structure reflected in the effective Formula Rate. 4

LSPGC’s commitments to limit its ROE to 9.8%, inclusive of incentive ROE adders, and 5

to limit the equity component of its capital structure to 45% were made in connection 6

with the competitive solicitations for the Gates and Round Mountain projects and are 7

reflected in the Approved Project Sponsor Agreements for those projects. LSPGC is not 8

proposing in this proceeding to extend those project-specific competitive commitments to 9

Covered Upgrades.
10
Covered Upgrade costs will be separately identified on Attachment 1a and will not be 11

included in the cost containment worksheets LSPGC provides under Sections 1.c and 12

3.d(vi) of the Formula Rate Implementation Protocols (Attachments 11 and 12 of the 13

Formula Rate Template). Those costs will not be subject to the capital cost caps or annual 14

revenue requirement caps applicable to LSPGC’s competitively selected projects. 15

LSPGC is not requesting a different ROE, a different capital structure, or any new 16

incentive ROE adder in this proceeding. LSPGC retains the right, subject to applicable 17

Commission requirements, to seek a different ROE, capital structure, or incentive 18

treatment for Covered Upgrades in a future filing under Section 205 of the Federal Power 19

Act. Unless and until the Commission authorizes different treatment, the ROE, capital 20

structure, and any applicable incentive treatment reflected in the effective Formula Rate 21

will govern.
22

PDF page 374

10

V. CREDITING CRA AMOUNTS TO CUSTOMERS THROUGH THE TRBAA
1
Q.
HOW WILL LSPGC CREDIT TRBAA CREDITS TO CUSTOMERS?
2
A. Through the TRBAA. Each October, LSPGC will include the Forecast TRBAA Credit 3

for the following calendar year in Cf. Under the CRA, the Forecast TRBAA Credit equals 4

the projected Supplemental MTRC, increased or reduced by any prior-period true-up 5

adjustment, which is the same amount LSPGC will invoice the Eligible Customer, plus 6

any Annual Termination Fee Credit scheduled for that year under Section 3.3.4. The 7

Forecast TRBAA Credit lowers the TRR effective January 1 and, through the TAC, the 8

transmission charges paid by all customers. During the year, the CAISO pays LSPGC the 9

lower TRR, and the Eligible Customer pays the Supplemental MTRC directly to LSPGC. 10

The TRBA records both. For these CRA amounts, a TRBA difference arises when the 11

amounts LSPGC records in the TRBA differ from the corresponding credits reflected in 12

CAISO payments, for example because of late payment or non-payment, the application 13

of security, a termination, or the timing of monthly installments. Any such difference is 14

included in the September 30 TRBA balance and returned to, or recovered from, 15

customers through Cr in the following TRBAA. Amounts later refunded or reversed, and 16

adjustments resulting from the resolution of a dispute under the CRA, are also recorded 17

in the TRBA.
18
Q.
WHY IS THE TRBAA THE APPROPRIATE MECHANISM?
19
A. There are several reasons. First, the TRBAA already exists to credit customers with 20

revenue LSPGC receives outside the TAC, and the Commission and stakeholders review 21

it each year. Second, because the Supplemental MTRC is invoiced on a forecast basis, 22

including the Forecast TRBAA Credit in Cf gives customers the credit in the TRR 23

effective January 1, rather than a year later. Third, the TRBA ensures LSPGC collects no 24

PDF page 375

11

more and no less than its Base TRR, so LSPGC retains none of the TRBAA Credits. 1

Fourth, crediting the revenue through the TRBAA keeps the full cost of each Covered 2

Upgrade in the Base TRR, consistent with the CAISO’s requirement that facilities under 3

its Operational Control be included in the TRR, while the TRBAA shows the credit 4

transparently and separately.
5
Q.
DOES USING THE TRBAA LIMIT THE ABILITY OF CUSTOMERS AND
6
OTHER INTERESTED PARTIES TO REVIEW OR CHALLENGE THESE
7
CREDITS?
8
A. No. LSPGC’s annual TRBAA filing is made under Section 205 of the Federal Power Act. 9

Each filing is noticed by the Commission, is open to intervention, comment, and protest, 10

and is subject to Commission review and acceptance. LSPGC will include in each filing 11

the calculations, work papers, and testimony supporting the TRBAA Credits, so 12

interested parties can review and challenge those amounts in that proceeding. In addition, 13

the inputs to the Formula Rate Template, including the Covered Upgrade plant reported 14

on Attachment 1a and the amounts removed on Attachment 9, Line 18, are subject to the 15

existing information exchange, review, and challenge procedures of the Formula Rate 16

Implementation Protocols. As a result, every amount affected by the proposal remains 17

subject to review and challenge in one or both of these processes.
18
Q.
WHAT CHANGES TO THE TO TARIFF ARE NEEDED TO IMPLEMENT THIS
19
APPROACH?
20
A. Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff currently provides for the TRBAA to flow Transmission 21

Revenue Credits through to transmission customers. The TO Tariff defines Transmission 22

Revenue Credits as the proceeds received from the CAISO, other than for the recovery of 23

LSPGC’s Regional Transmission Revenue Requirement through the Regional 24

Transmission Access Charges. Because CRA-related credits include amounts received 25

PDF page 376

12

directly from or on behalf of the Eligible Customer and Annual Termination Fee Credits 1

scheduled under Section 3.3.4, LSPGC proposes to revise the definition of the TRBAA 2

and Section 5.3 so that the TRBAA also flows through to transmission customers all 3

amounts a CRA requires to be credited through it. The revisions add amounts recorded in 4

the TRBA under a CRA to Cr, add any forecast credit a CRA requires to Cf, and include 5

amounts recorded in the TRBA under a CRA in the TRBA principal balance used to 6

calculate interest. The revisions also clarify that Section 5.3 does not require the payment 7

of interest between LSPGC and an Eligible Customer, which is governed solely by the 8

CRA. The definition of Transmission Revenue Credits is unchanged.
9
Q.
HOW WILL TRBAA CREDITS BE REFLECTED IN THE ANNUAL TRBAA
10
FILING?
11
A. LSPGC will add the following lines to the TRBA and TRBAA calculation it files each 12

year, as shown in Exhibit No. LSC-202:
13
• Lines 7a through 7d record Supplemental MTRC Receipts, Applied Security 14

Proceeds, Annual Termination Fee Credits, and duplicate-recovery credits and 15

other adjustments, including reversals of amounts later refunded and adjustments 16

resulting from the resolution of disputes under the CRA. Applied Security 17

Proceeds used to pay a Termination Fee are held in the Termination Fee Account 18

and credited through the Annual Termination Fee Credits on Line 7c, rather than 19

on Line 7b. These amounts are included in Net Payment on Line 8; and 20

• Cf on Line 18 is separated into other Transmission Revenue Credits (Line 18a) 21

and the Forecast TRBAA Credit (Line 18b), which equals the projected 22

Supplemental MTRC for the following calendar year, increased or reduced by any 23

PDF page 377

13

prior-period true-up adjustment, plus any Annual Termination Fee Credit 1

scheduled for that year.
2
Because TRBAA Credits are included in Net Payment, any difference between the credit 3

reflected in CAISO payments and the TRBAA Credits recorded becomes part of the 4

TRBA balance and accrues interest in the same manner as other TRBA balances. Absent 5

TRBAA Credits, these lines are zero and the calculation is unchanged. 6

Q.
WHY DO TRBA BALANCES RELATED TO TRBAA CREDITS ACCRUE
7
INTEREST WHILE PRIOR-PERIOD TRUE-UP ADJUSTMENTS DO NOT?
8
A. Interest applies when LSPGC holds funds that belong to transmission customers, or when 9

transmission customers hold funds owed to LSPGC. A TRBA balance created by a late 10

payment, a non-payment before security is applied, or the timing of monthly installments 11

reflects an actual over- or under-recovery by LSPGC, so it accrues interest like any other 12

TRBA balance. A prior-period true-up adjustment is different. When further 13

Supplemental MTRC will be invoiced, the adjustment is added to the Supplemental 14

MTRC invoiced for a later Rate Year and to the Forecast TRBAA Credit for that same 15

Rate Year, so the matched adjustment does not itself create an over- or under-recovery by 16

LSPGC. Differences between the credit reflected in CAISO payments and actual receipts 17

or applied amounts remain subject to TRBA interest. When no further Supplemental 18

MTRC will be invoiced, Section 6.6 instead requires a separate invoice or refund, with 19

the receipt or refund recorded in the TRBA when received or paid. Consistent with 20

Section 6.6 of the CRA and Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff, no interest accrues on a prior- 21

period true-up adjustment between LSPGC and the Eligible Customer; interest on any 22

resulting TRBA balance is calculated separately.
23

PDF page 378

14

Q.
WILL LSPGC SUPPORT THESE AMOUNTS IN EACH ANNUAL TRBAA
1
FILING?
2
A. Yes. In each annual TRBAA filing, LSPGC will provide the TRBA and TRBAA 3

calculation substantially in the form of Exhibit No. LSC-202, together with work papers 4

supporting the Forecast TRBAA Credit and the TRBAA Credits recorded during the 5

TRBA year, and its witness will testify to those amounts. As provided in Section 6.8 of 6

the CRA, those work papers may present customer-specific information on an aggregated 7

basis or be submitted with a request for privileged treatment under 18 C.F.R. § 388.112. 8

Q.
ARE ANY CHANGES NEEDED TO APPENDIX I OF THE TO TARIFF?
9
A. No. Appendix I states that LSPGC’s TRR equals its Base TRR plus its TRBAA, and that 10

the TRBAA is computed in accordance with Section 5.3 of the TO Tariff and the CAISO 11

Tariff. TRBAA Credits are included in the TRBAA amount stated in Appendix I each 12

year, so no change to its terms is required.
13
VI. LSPGC’S FORMULA RATE TEMPLATE
14
Q.
ARE ANY CHANGES TO LSPGC’S FORMULA RATE TEMPLATE NEEDED
15
TO IMPLEMENT THE PROPOSAL?
16
A. No. LSPGC’s Formula Rate Template already contains the features the proposal requires: 17

• Attachment 1a – Project Plant Detail Worksheet reports monthly transmission 18

gross plant, accumulated depreciation, Construction Work in Progress (“CWIP”), 19

and unamortized abandoned plant, and the resulting net plant, separately for each 20

project. A Covered Upgrade is reported as its own project on Attachment 1a, and 21

the Attachment 1a totals tie to Attachment 4 and Appendix III;
22
• Attachment 1 – Project Revenue Requirement Worksheet calculates the revenue 23

requirement of each project using the same allocation factors applied to all 24

PDF page 379

15

LSPGC transmission plant, so each Covered Upgrade’s revenue requirement 1

reflects its share of total plant; and
2
• Attachment 9 – Revenue Credit Detail, Line 18, “Less: Revenue Credits included 3

in the TRBAA,” removes from Formula Rate revenue credits any amounts 4

credited to customers through the TRBAA.
5
Additional project lines may be added to Attachments 1a and 1 as Covered Upgrades are 6

placed in service. Because the Attachment 1a totals tie to Attachment 4 and Appendix III, 7

reporting plant by project does not change total plant or the total revenue requirement. 8

Q.
HOW DOES ATTACHMENT 9, LINE 18 APPLY TO TRBAA CREDITS?
9
A. Consistent with Section 6.8 of the CRA, TRBAA Credits are credited solely through the 10

TRBAA and are not Formula Rate revenue credits. Line 18 is a safeguard: to the extent 11

any TRBAA Credits are recorded in the revenue accounts included on Attachment 9, 12

Line 15, Line 18 removes them, so they are credited to customers once, through the 13

TRBAA, and not also through the Formula Rate.
14
Q.
ARE ANY CHANGES NEEDED TO THE FORMULA RATE
15
IMPLEMENTATION PROTOCOLS?
16
A. No. The Formula Rate Implementation Protocols already require LSPGC to provide 17

supporting documentation for data used in the Formula Rate that are not stated in the 18

FERC Form No. 1, and to include a variance analysis of revenue credits with an 19

explanation of material changes, which will include the amounts on Attachment 9, Line 20

18. The existing posting, review, challenge, and true-up procedures apply to Covered 21

Upgrades reported on Attachment 1a without change. TRBAA Credits are reviewed in 22

LSPGC’s annual TRBAA filing under Section 205 of the Federal Power Act, as I 23

described above.
24

PDF page 380

16

VII. ILLUSTRATIVE EXHIBITS
1
Q.
PLEASE DESCRIBE EXHIBIT NO. LSC-201.
2
A. Exhibit No. LSC-201 is LSPGC’s currently effective Formula Rate Template populated 3

with hypothetical data for the 2030 Rate Year to illustrate the treatment of a Covered 4

Upgrade. The example includes the Gates and Round Mountain projects and one $60 5

million Covered Upgrade placed in service January 1, 2030, reported as Project C on 6

Attachments 1a and 1, consistent with the illustrative CRA worksheet sponsored by Mr. 7

Hayes (Exhibit No. LSC-101). In that worksheet, the Covered Upgrade has an Annual 8

MTRC of $10,278,360 and projected Qualifying Transmission Revenues of $3,363,840, 9

for a Supplemental MTRC of $6,914,520. In Exhibit No. LSC-201:
10
• Because the Covered Upgrade is placed in service January 1, 2030, it is not 11

included in the December 2029 plant balance, and its 13-month average gross 12

plant is $55.38 million. The Covered Upgrade’s revenue requirement on 13

Attachment 1 is $10.21 million and the total Base TRR is $34.91 million; 14

• TRBAA Credits of $6,914,520 are assumed to be recorded in a revenue account 15

reported on Attachment 9, Line 14, and are removed on Line 18, so they do not 16

reduce the Base TRR; without Line 18, the Base TRR would be $28.00 million 17

and the amounts would be credited twice;
18
• The Covered Upgrade’s first-year Formula Rate revenue requirement of $10.21 19

million differs from its Annual MTRC of $10.28 million because the two are 20

calculated separately and on different bases. The Annual MTRC is calculated 21

under Schedule 5 of the CRA and, once finalized, is fixed subject to the 22

adjustments expressly permitted by Section 6.3.1. Its levelized calculation uses a 23

twenty-year MTRC Term to recover the allocated capital cost and the return, 24

PDF page 381

17

taxes, and fixed expenses reflected in Schedule 5. The Formula Rate revenue 1

requirement is recalculated each year using 13-month average balances and book 2

depreciation rates, so this January 1 in-service example includes a zero December 3

2029 plant balance in the 13-month average even though the upgrade is in service 4

for all of 2030. All else equal, the capital-related revenue requirement declines as 5

the plant depreciates. The difference does not affect LSPGC’s revenue: the 6

Covered Upgrade is recovered through the Base TRR, and all TRBAA Credits are 7

returned to customers through the TRBAA; and
8
• The example does not model the cost containment adjustments for the Gates and 9

Round Mountain projects, which apply only to those projects and do not affect the 10

Covered Upgrade.
11
The illustrative data are hypothetical and are not LSPGC or customer data. 12

Q.
PLEASE DESCRIBE EXHIBIT NO. LSC-202.
13
A. Exhibit No. LSC-202 illustrates the TRBA and TRBAA calculation for two consecutive 14

TRBA years ending September 30, 2030 and 2031, using the Supplemental MTRC from 15

Exhibit No. LSC-101 and the Base TRR from Exhibit No. LSC-201. For trade months 16

before January 2030, the example uses a Base TRR that excludes the Covered Upgrade; 17

the 2030 Base TRR is then held constant so the TRBA balances shown result only from 18

the TRBAA Credits. Two years are shown because the TRBAA operates on a lag: a 19

credit included in Cf in one filing is compared to actual receipts in the following TRBA 20

year, and any difference is returned in the filing after that.
21
• In the TRBA year ending September 30, 2030, the annual $6,914,520 credit takes 22

effect January 1, 2030, and Supplemental MTRC Receipts replace the reduction 23

PDF page 382

18

in CAISO payments. Only a small timing difference remains, because the 1

Supplemental MTRC is invoiced in equal monthly installments while CAISO 2

payments reflect the days in each month. The resulting TRBA balance of $6,833 3

and interest of $355 are returned to customers, and the TRBAA effective January 4

1, 2031 is a credit of $6,921,708.
5
• In the TRBA year ending September 30, 2031, the example assumes two 6

hypothetical events. First, the 2030 Reconciliation Statement shows that the 7

Eligible Customer was under-billed by $100,000. Under the CRA, that prior- 8

period true-up adjustment is added to the Supplemental MTRC invoiced for 2032 9

and to the 2032 Forecast TRBAA Credit on Line 18b of $7,014,520, so it does not 10

create a TRBA balance and carries no interest. Second, the Eligible Customer 11

pays its installment for August 2031 service after September 30. Together with 12

the remaining timing difference, the late payment produces a TRBA under- 13

collection of $573,097, which is included in Cr with interest of $1,407 and 14

reverses when the payment is received in the next TRBA year. The TRBAA 15

effective January 1, 2032 is a credit of $6,440,016.
16
The illustrative data are hypothetical and are not LSPGC or customer data. 17

VIII. CONCLUSION
18
Q.
WHY ARE THE PROPOSED REVISIONS JUST AND REASONABLE?
19
A. The revisions implement the Commission’s direction that large load payments be credited 20

toward transmission owners’ revenue requirements, using LSPGC’s existing TRBAA 21

mechanism. Each Eligible Customer pays at least its committed Annual MTRC for each 22

Covered Upgrade, and TRBAA Credits reduce the TRR paid by all customers through the 23

TAC. LSPGC retains none of the TRBAA Credits, and Attachment 9, Line 18 ensures 24

PDF page 383

19

they are credited only once. LSPGC’s Formula Rate is unchanged, and Covered 1

Upgrades are reported as separate projects on the existing Attachment 1a. The existing 2

Formula Rate review procedures and LSPGC’s annual TRBAA filing provide full 3

transparency for the new calculations.
4
Q.
DOES THIS CONCLUDE YOUR TESTIMONY?
5
A. Yes.
